...the absence of a calculated aspect. For example, if the **Observer** <note>original: "drasta"</note> is the **Moon** at 1s 3° 20' 50" and the **Visible Object** <note>original: "drishya"</note> is **Mercury** at 2s 3° 20' 50", or Mercury at 6s 3° 20' 50", or Mercury at 8s 3° 20' 50". Such instances are possible, as stated in other similar texts.

### The Degrees of Brilliance

Now, the **Degrees of Brilliance** <term>Diptansha—the "orb" or range of degrees within which a planet’s influence is active</term> for the Sun and others are stated. 

*   For the **Sun**: Fifteen (15) 
*   For the **Moon**: Twelve (12)
*   For **Mars**: Eight (8)
*   For **Mercury**: Seven (7)
*   For **Jupiter**: Nine (9)
*   For **Venus**: Seven (7)
*   For **Saturn**: Nine (9)

These "degrees of brilliance" should be known for the Sun and the other planets.

### Special Rules Regarding Aspects

Now, a specific detail regarding **Aspects** <term>Drishti—the "glance" or influence one planet casts upon another</term> is described. 

Within the **Zodiac** <note>original: "Chakra"</note>, or the wheel of the twelve signs starting from the Ascendant, the **Leftward Aspect** <note>original: "vama-drig"</note> should be understood as more powerful. This is in comparison to the "rightward" aspect. The "leftward aspect" refers to the gaze of a planet situated in a "left" position upon a planet in a "right" position. 

The "South" or "Right" division consists of the signs from the 1st (Ascendant) to the end of the 6th house. The "Left" division consists of the signs from the 7th house to the end of the 12th house. 

This is also stated in the **Brihat Jataka** <note>A foundational 6th-century Sanskrit text on astronomy and astrology by Varahamihira</note> in the verse beginning with "Kantagit..." etc. **Bhattotpala** <note>A famous 10th-century commentator on Indian astronomical texts</note> explains that the "left" part refers to the visible portion of the zodiac—the signs that have already risen and are positioned in the 12th, 11th, 10th, 9th, and 8th houses, as well as the portion of the 7th house that corresponds to the degree of the Ascendant. This entire visible part is the "left" division. Conversely, the "right" or "southern" division consists of the invisible signs—those that have not yet risen—namely the remaining portion of the Ascendant, and the 2nd, 3rd, 4th, 5th, and 6th houses, along with the corresponding portion of the 7th house. 

Regarding the statement that the "leftward aspect" is more powerful in the circle, one example is provided: **From the Midheaven** <note>original: "Madhya," the 10th house</note>. The aspect cast from the 10th house (the highest point in the sky) upon the 4th house (the "Nadir" or the point directly below) is said to be very powerful. This implies that the gaze of a planet in the 10th house upon a planet in the 4th house is strong, while the gaze of a planet in the 4th house upon one in the 10th house is weak.

This means that the aspect of planets situated in the 7th through 12th houses cast upon planets in the 1st through 6th houses is designated as "powerful." This is also the view held by **Samarasimha** <note>A 13th-century authority on Tajika (Perso-Arabic) astrology in India</note>. He states: "In the zodiac, the leftward aspect should be known as more powerful than the rightward aspect. Specifically, the aspect cast from the Midheaven upon the Nadir is strong; this principle should be inferred for all positions."

The term "Leftward Aspect" <note>original: "vama-drishti"</note> is a compound word. It means "the gaze from the left" or "the gaze upon the left." The meaning is that the gaze cast by planets in the left division upon planets in the right division is stronger than the gaze cast from the right to the left. 

To illustrate: the aspect from the **Midheaven** (10th house) upon the **Nadir** (4th house) is powerful. This principle applies everywhere from the 7th...

<summary>This page defines the Diptansha (orbs of influence) for each planet and explains the "Leftward Aspect" rule, which dictates that planets in the upper/visible houses (7-12) exert a stronger influence on those in the lower/invisible houses (1-6).</summary>

<meta>This page continues the technical exposition on planetary aspects (drishti) and orbs of influence (diptansha), moving from general rules of strength to the specific introduction of the sixteen yogas (astrological configurations) central to the Tajika system. The text cites Keshava Daivajna, a renowned 15th-century authority.</meta>

### Strength of Aspects Based on Position

The aspect of planets situated in the houses from the **twelfth** to the **first** <note>original: "lagnadi," referring to the Ascendant</note> upon planets situated from the **first** to the **sixth** house is to be considered powerful. This is the matter to be reflected upon. 

This is also the opinion of the illustrious **Keshava Daivajna** <note>A famous astronomer-astrologer of the Nandigrama school</note>. As it is said: "The aspect of a planet in the latter half is greater than the sine of the first half..." 

To clarify: the "aspect of a planet in the latter half" refers to the houses from the **seventh** to the **twelfth**. The "first half" refers to the houses from the **Ascendant** <note>1st house</note> to the **sixth**.

### On Aspects Within the Same Sign

Now, another opinion regarding an aspect occurring within a single house is stated. Even in a single sign <note>original: "ekarkshe-pi"</note>, the mutual aspect between the **Visible Object** <note>original: "drishya" - the planet being looked at</note> and the **Observer** <note>original: "drasta" - the planet casting the aspect</note> when both are located in the same zodiac sign is known as **Wealth-Generating**. Such a configuration is understood by scholars to be a giver of auspicious results and extreme profit. Thus do some sages and experts of **Tajika Shastra** <term>A branch of Indian astrology that integrated Persian and Arabic techniques, often used for annual horoscopy</term> understand it. || 13 ||

### The Application of Orbs of Influence

Now, the purpose of the previously mentioned **Degrees of Brilliance** <term>Diptansha—the "orb" or range of degrees within which a planet’s influence remains active</term> is stated:

->A planet bestows a specific and excellent result of its aspect upon a planet located either in front of it or behind it, provided it is within its own **degrees of brilliance**. If these degrees are exceeded, the experts know the result of the aspect to be merely middling (ordinary). || 14 ||<-

->First is **Iqbal**, then **Induvara**, likewise **Itthashala**, and another is **Israf**. Then comes **Nakta**, then **Yamaya**, **Manau**, **Kamboola**, and then **Gairi-Kamboola** is mentioned. || 15 ||<-

->**Khallasara**, **Radda**, then **Iduphalikuttha**, the one named **Dutthotthadibira**, **Tambira**, **Kuttha**, and **Durapha**—these are the sixteen Yogas. I shall now describe their characteristics. || 16 ||<-

The planet gives the full result of its aspect within those degrees. If those **degrees of brilliance** are exceeded, it is understood that it gives only a "middling" or average result. || 14 ||

### The Sixteen Yogas of Tajika

Now, the **Sixteen Yogas** <term>Yoga—a specific planetary combination or configuration</term> are explained. Their names are given in the two preceding verses.

1.  **Iqbal** <note>Sanskrit: Ikkavala</note>
2.  **Induvara**
3.  **Itthashala** <note>Also known as "Ithasala," a key configuration involving the application of aspects</note>
4.  **Israf** <note>Sanskrit: Isarapha - refers to the separation of aspects</note>
5.  **Nakta**
6.  **Yamaya**
7.  **Manau** <note>Sanskrit: Mana-u</note>
8.  **Kamboola**
9.  **Gairi-Kamboola**
10. **Khallasara**
11. **Radda** <note>Sanskrit: Rada</note>
12. **Iduphalikuttha** <note>Sanskrit: Oduphalikuttha</note>
13. **Dutthotthadibira**
14. **Tambira**
15. **Kuttha**
16. **Durapha**

These are the sixteen yogas. I shall now describe the specific characteristics and definitions of these, beginning with **Iqbal**. || 17 || 16 ||

->॥ छ ॥ ॥ छ ॥<-

<meta>This page continues the discussion on the strength of planetary aspects from page 21. It transitions from the physical positioning of planets to the qualitative analysis of aspects (drishti) and specific configurations (yogas) that determine the success or failure of an inquiry. The text specifically focuses on the relationship between the Lord of the Ascendant (the inquirer) and the Lord of the Task (the matter being asked about).</meta>

### Now, the Examination of Aspects is Discussed || "Lagnēśa," etc. ||

**The Lord of the Ascendant** <note>original: "Lagnēśa" - the planet ruling the rising sign</note>, the **Lord of the Task** <note>original: "Kāryādhipa" - the planet ruling the house associated with the subject of the question</note>, and their "helpers"—meaning the friend of the Ascendant Lord and the friend of the Task Lord—these four are the focus. When they are situated in a zodiac sign, and the ruler of that sign or a **benefic planet** <note>original: "saumya" - an auspicious planet like Jupiter or Venus</note> aspects them, then the previously mentioned **Muthaśila configuration** <term>original: "Muthaśila-yoga" - A Tajika astrological aspect where a faster planet approaches a slower one, indicating the realization of a goal</term> is said to be very powerful and a giver of excellent results. Among these, the wise say that when the configuration is viewed with a **friendly aspect** <note>original: "snēha-dr̥ṣṭi" - specifically the 3rd, 5th, 9th, or 11th house aspects</note>, it is especially strong. || 21 ||

Now, another consideration of the results is stated || "Svakṣētra," etc. || If the Lord of the Ascendant and the Lord of the Task are in "their own signs and so on"—meaning their own home, their sign of exaltation, their "term" (hadda), or their "decan" (drekkana)—or in other **auspicious locations** <note>the houses of benefic planets</note>, and are joined with or aspected by benefic planets, then it should be said that in the past, a full auspicious result was produced.

-> When the Lord of the Ascendant, the Lord of the Task, and their helpers are in a sign aspected by its Lord or a benefic, || <-
-> Then the sages call that configuration powerful, especially if seen with a friendly aspect. || 21 || <-

-> If they are in their own signs or other good places, and are joined or aspected by benefics, or are approaching such a state, || <-
-> Then auspiciousness was fully present in the past, will be in the future, or exists now. || 22 || <-

-> The opposite is true in the reverse case—when a planet moves from a desired sign into an undesirable house. || <-
-> In the past it was auspicious, but now it is inauspicious; this should be spoken of for one wishing to know what is yet to come. || 23 || <-

If the Lord of the Ascendant and the Lord of the Task are moving toward their own signs or good locations and are joined or aspected by benefics, then it should be said that the auspicious result **will happen in the future**. If the Lord of the Ascendant and the Lord of the Task are *currently* in their own signs or good locations and are joined or aspected by benefic planets, then it must be said that the auspicious result **is happening right now**. || 22 ||

### Now, the Inauspicious Result is Described || "The Opposite," etc. ||

"The opposite" refers to a reversal of the previously mentioned state of being in one's own sign or a good location. In such an undesirable state, the meaning is changed. For example: if the Lord of the Ascendant or the Lord of the Task has entered an enemy's sign, or is in its **sign of debilitation** <note>original: "nīcha" - the point where a planet is weakest</note>, or is in an enemy's term (hadda), or is in the house of a **malefic planet** <note>original: "pāpa-graha" - naturally inauspicious planets like Mars or Saturn</note>, and is joined or aspected by malefic planets, then in the past, an inauspicious result occurred. If the Lord of the Ascendant or the Lord of the Task is currently in such a bad location and is joined or aspected by malefic planets, then it should be said that misfortune is **occurring right now**.

<summary>This page describes how the strength and aspects of the Lord of the Ascendant and the Lord of the Task determine whether an event has already happened, is currently happening, or will happen in the future. It emphasizes that placement in "own signs" and aspects from "benefic planets" are essential for a successful outcome in Tajika astrology.</summary>
<keywords>Lagnēśa (Ascendant Lord), Kāryādhipa (Significator), Muthaśila-yoga, friendly aspect, benefic planets, malefic planets, Tajika Nilakanthi, planetary strength, future prediction</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical analysis of Tajika astrological configurations (yogas). The previous page concluded the discussion on the Muthaśila yoga, focusing on how the dignity of planets in their own signs or friendly signs strengthens the outcome. This page transitions into more complex scenarios: when a planet moves from a good sign to a bad one, and the introduction of the Isarapha (separation) and Nakta (translation of light) configurations.</meta>

CC-0. Courtesy Sanjay Raina, Jammu. Digitized by eGangotri

Now, the specific consideration of the results of the third **Muthaśila configuration** is discussed. "Now" refers to when the **Lord of the Ascendant** <note>original: "Lagnēśa" — the planet ruling the rising sign representing the inquirer</note> or the **Lord of the Task** <note>original: "Kāryēśa" — the planet ruling the matter in question</note> has reached a desired sign, but moves from a **friend’s sign** <note>a zodiac sign ruled by a planet friendly to it</note> toward an **undesirable planet’s sign** or an **enemy’s sign**. In such a case, it should be said that previously there was a "good or auspicious result," but now "inauspicious results" prevail. This is particularly relevant for one intending to set out on a journey. For example: if the Lord of the Ascendant or the Lord of the Task is currently in a friendly house but will soon enter an enemy’s house, then one must predict that the inauspicious result is "impending" or "will occur in the future." || 23 || 

### The Definition of the Isarapha Configuration

## When a fast-moving planet approaches even one degree past a slow-moving one, that is Isarapha. Failure of the task occurs in Musaripha when arising from malefic planets; according to the school of Hillaja, this should be considered with benefic planets. || 24 ||

## There is no mutual aspect between the Lord of the Ascendant and the Task Lord, but a fast-moving planet is situated between them. Taking the light if positioned behind, it should then be placed elsewhere; this is the Nakta yoga. || 25 ||

Regarding the connection not being formed: when the **Isarapha configuration** <term>original: "Isarapha-yoga" — from the Persian/Arabic 'Israf' meaning dissipation or separation; occurs when a faster planet passes a slower one</term> occurs, there is a "destruction of the task" <note>failure of the desired outcome</note>. In this Isarapha yoga, which is also called by the synonym **Musaripha** <term>original: "Mūsaripha"</term>, if it involves **malefic planets** <note>original: "pāpayōḥ" — naturally harmful planets like Mars or Saturn</note>, the desired work will be destroyed. However, if this configuration arises from **benefic planets** <note>original: "saumya" — auspicious planets like Jupiter or Venus</note>, the task is not destroyed. This specific result should be understood according to the school of **Hillaja** <note>a famous authority in Tajika astrology</note>. || 24 ||

Now, the characteristics of the **Nakta configuration** <term>original: "Nakta-yoga" — from the Persian 'Naql', meaning the translation or transfer of light</term> are stated. "Lagnēśa," etc. The Lord of the Ascendant and the Lord of the Task have no mutual aspect <note>they are in signs that cannot "see" each other according to standard rules</note>. However, if there is a **fast-moving planet** <note>such as the Moon</note> located between them—meaning between the Lord of the Ascendant and the Lord of the Task—such a middle planet takes the **light** <note>original: "tējaḥ" — the influence or power of a planet</note> from the fast-moving planet situated behind and "places" or "deposits" it upon the other slow-moving planet. This is the Nakta configuration. || 25 ||

<summary>This page defines the Isarapha (separation) and Nakta (translation of light) yogas in Tajika astrology, explaining how the movement of planets between friendly and hostile signs or the intervention of a third planet determines the success or failure of a query.</summary>
<keywords>Tajika astrology, Isarapha yoga, Musaripha, Nakta yoga, Hillaja, translation of light, planetary aspects, Lord of the Ascendant, malefic planets, benefic planets</keywords>

These concepts are borrowed from Perso-Arabic astrology (Tajika) and integrated into the Sanskrit tradition.</meta>

<margin>Tajika Neelakanthi <note>The abbreviated title of the manuscript: Tā. Nī.</note></margin>
<margin>23</margin>

Now, he states the example of this <term>Nakta Yoga</term> <note>Nakta, meaning 'translation' or 'connection,' occurs when two planets do not aspect each other, but a faster planet (usually the Moon) connects them by transferring 'light' from one to the other.</note>. In the verse beginning with **Obtaining a wife** and **Sagittarius**, he explains: Suppose an inquirer comes at a particular time and asks the question: "Will I obtain a wife or not?" At that moment, the **Ascendant** <note>original: "Tanu-lagna" — the rising sign representing the inquirer</note> is **Virgo** <note>original: "Kanyā"</note>. Its lord, **Mercury** <note>original: "Budha"</note>, is situated in **Leo** <note>original: "Simha"</note> at ten degrees. Meanwhile, the divine preceptor **Jupiter** <note>original: "Bṛhaspati"</note> is at twelve degrees in the seventh house <note>the house of marriage</note>. Because these two, Mercury and Jupiter, are in a **six-eight relationship** <note>original: "ṣaḍaṣṭa-sthāna" — an inauspicious position where planets cannot 'see' or aspect each other</note> to one another, there is no aspect between them at all. 

However, the **Moon** <note>original: "Candra"</note>, being a fast-moving planet, is situated between Mercury and Jupiter in **Sagittarius** <note>original: "Cāpa"</note> or **Taurus** <note>original: "Vṛṣabha"</note>. What is the Moon’s nature here? It is **visible to both** <note>original: "ubhaya-dṛśya-mūrti"</note>, meaning its form is aspected by both Mercury and Jupiter. Furthermore, the Moon is at eight or eleven degrees. Such a Moon, having **received the brilliance** <note>original: "tejaḥ-sāmarthyaṃ" — the power or light</note> from Mercury, gives it to Jupiter. Therefore, even though Mercury (the lord of the inquirer) lost its strength, it should be predicted that the acquisition of a wife will occur through one's own efforts. Why? Because the Moon, acting as a **middle-man** <note>original: "tṛtīya-madhyastha" — a third-party mediator</note>, has transferred the brilliance; thus, the wife is obtained through the efforts of a third party. || **This is Nakta Yoga.** || 26–27.

->A query for a wife: the Ascendant is Virgo, its lord Mercury is in Leo at ten degrees.<-
->Jupiter is in the house of marriage; there is no mutual aspect between them. But the Moon,<-
->in Sagittarius or Taurus, visible to both and moving fast at eight or eleven degrees,<-
->takes the brilliance from Mercury and gives it to Jupiter. Success comes through another.<- 26–27.

->A slow-moving planet situated between two others aspects both with its rays.<-
->Taking the brilliance from the faster one, it gives it to the slower for the failure of the task; this is Yamaya.<- 28.

### The Definition of Yamaya Yoga

Now he states the definition of <term>Yamaya Yoga</term> <note>From 'Yama,' the god of restraint or death; this yoga indicates a blockage or failure of the intended goal.</note>. In the phrase **Situated within**, he explains: The Lord of the Ascendant and the Lord of the Task <note>the planet representing the matter being asked about</note> do not aspect each other. Situated between these two is a **slow-moving planet** <note>original: "manda-gati-graha," usually Saturn or Jupiter</note>. By its own brilliance and position, it aspects both the Lord of the Ascendant and the Lord of the Task. Then, that slow-moving planet takes [the light] from the faster Lord of the Ascendant...

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of Tajika astrology. Having finished the explanation of Nakta Yoga (Translation of Light) on the previous page, the author now defines and illustrates Yamaya Yoga (Collection of Light). Both are configurations used to determine if a specific question asked of an astrologer will result in success or failure.</meta>

...between the Lord of the Ascendant and the Lord of the Matter, whichever is the fast-moving planet, taking the splendor or light from that one, it is given to the slower-moving planet. To whichever is the slower-moving planet among the Lord of the Ascendant and the Lord of the Matter, [a third planet] gives or bestows [the light]; this is called **Yamaya Yoga**. It is prescribed and declared for the successful completion of a desired task. || 28 || 

### **Now, the example of this Yamaya Yoga**

Regarding the verse beginning with **Obtaining a kingdom** and **Ten degrees**: 

In a query or question asking, "Will I obtain a kingdom (high office) or not?" the **Ascendant** <note>original: "Lagna" — the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the time of the question</note> is **Libra** <note>original: "Tulā"</note>. Its lord and master is **Venus** <note>original: "Sita" or "Śukra"</note>. He is situated in the seventh house in **Aries** <note>original: "Meṣa"</note>, marked at eight degrees. Likewise, the Lord of the Kingdom—the tenth house being the house of kingdom/rank—its lord and master is the **Moon** <note>original: "Candra"</note>, who, marked by six-and-twenty <note>The text uses "rasa-aṃśaiḥ" where 'rasa' signifies 6 and 'viṃśati' is implied or 'twenty' is understood from context, totaling 26 degrees</note> degrees, is in **Taurus** <note>original: "Vṛṣabha"</note>, situated in the eighth house. Because these two, the Lord of the Ascendant and the Lord of the Kingdom, are situated in a **two-twelve relationship** <note>original: "dvi-dvādaśa-sthāna" — an arrangement where planets are in adjacent signs and cannot 'see' or aspect each other</note>, there is no aspect between them at all. 

> Concerning a query on obtaining a kingdom: the Ascendant is Libra, and Venus is in Aries. If the Moon, as the Lord of the Kingdom, is in Taurus at twenty-six degrees, and no aspect exists between the two; but **Jupiter** <note>original: "Guru"</note>, being slow, || 29 ||
> 
> situated in **Cancer** <note>original: "Karka"</note> at ten degrees, beholds them both with his great light, and takes the Moon's light and gives it to Venus—then the gain of the position/rank will happen through a **minister** <note>original: "Amātya"</note>. This should be declared after careful consideration. || 30 ||

**Jupiter** <note>original: "Bṛhaspati"</note> is a slow-moving planet, measured at ten degrees and situated in Cancer, which is the tenth house. From that position, he beholds both the Lord of the Ascendant and the Lord of the Matter <note>Venus and Moon</note> through a positional aspect. Having more "light-degrees" than both the Moon and Venus, he takes the splendor or light belonging to the Moon and gives it to Venus. For this reason, the gain of the "rank" or "kingdom" will occur through a minister or counselor. This is how the judgment should be spoken after reflecting on it. This is **Yamaya Yoga**. || 29 || || 30 ||

<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of the sixteen Tajika Yogas—specific planetary configurations used in medieval Indian horary astrology (predicting the outcome of a question). Having previously discussed Nakta and Yamaya Yogas, which are favorable, the author here introduces Manao Yoga, the first of the configurations that signify obstruction or failure.</meta>

<margin>Tajika Nilakanthi || 24 ||</margin>
### **Now, the definition of Manao Yoga is stated.**

Regarding the verse beginning with **"A retrograde planet"** and **"He shall steal the splendor"**: In this **Manao Yoga** <note>derived from the Arabic *man'*, meaning prohibition or prevention</note>, the mutual placement and aspects of the Lord of the Ascendant and the Lord of the Matter are required. If, relative to a **fast-moving planet** <note>original: "śīghrakheta" — a planet with a higher daily velocity</note>—specifically between the Lord of the
<margin>Chapter 1 || 24 ||</margin>
Ascendant and the Lord of the Matter—whichever is the **fast-moving planet** at a certain degree and minute, and if behind or in front of it, **retrograde Mars or Saturn** is situated; such a **Mars or Saturn**, through a **fourth-house aspect** <note>original: "turyadṛṣṭyā" — a specific aspect where a planet influences the house four places ahead of it</note> or from the same sign or the seventh sign, or by a specific look <note>original: "daśādṛṣṭyā"</note> with degrees that are either more or less—that is to say, relative to the degrees of the **fast-moving planet**, with degrees that are higher or lower but **falling within the range of the fast-moving planet’s orb of influence** <note>original: "dīptāṃśa-madhya-varti" — the "shining degrees" or orb within which a planet's power is active</note>—it steals the splendor of that **fast-moving planet**. What is the condition of this planet? It is **approaching the Lord of the Matter** <note>original: "kāryapadetthaśālī" — in a state of Itthashala, or a perfecting aspect, with the planet representing the goal</note>. The **Place of Matter** refers to the house of the desired result. This means that if the Lord of the Matter

> If a retrograde planet or **Saturn** is situated behind or ahead of a **fast-moving planet** and looks at it with a **fourth-house aspect**,
> Or from the same sign or the seventh sign, with degrees that are more or less [than the fast planet], || 31 ||
> It steals its splendor; though it may be in a state of **Itthashala** <note>an applying aspect</note> with the **Lord of the Matter**, this **Manao** configuration is not auspicious.
> Example: A **query about obtaining a wife**. The **Ascendant** is **Virgo**; here, **Mercury** is at ten degrees, and **Jupiter** is at fifteen. || 32 ||
> **Mars** is in the **tenth house** at eleven degrees. First, **Mercury** has its splendor destroyed by **Mars**;
> Then it meets **Jupiter**. Because of this, or if [Mars] is behind it, there is no gain of a woman. || 33 ||

is in a state of **Muthashila** <note>original: "muthaśilakārī" — the Tajika term for an applying aspect where a faster planet catches up to a slower one</note>, even then, if this [interference] occurs, this is called **Manao Yoga**. It is not auspicious. The meaning is that the person will not achieve their goal. 

### **Now, the example**

Regarding the verse beginning with **"Obtaining a wife"**: In a query asking, "Will I obtain a wife or not?" the **Ascendant** is **Virgo**. Its lord is **Mercury**. Here, in **Virgo**, he is marked by ten degrees. Likewise, **Jupiter** <note>original: "surejya"</note>, marked by fifteen degrees, is **going to the house of the wife**—meaning he is in the seventh house (the house of marriage). Furthermore, **Mars** <note>original: "avanija"</note> is in the **tenth house** <note>original: "khe" — literally 'in the sky'</note> marked by eleven degrees. 

Now, **Mars** looks at **Mercury** with a **fourth-house aspect**. Because of this, having become one degree higher than **Mercury** and standing in its way, **Mercury** has its splendor "killed" by **Mars**. Thus, **Mercury**, deprived of its splendor, first having made...

<summary>This page defines and illustrates Manao Yoga, a configuration where a third planet (usually Mars or Saturn) obstructs the positive connection between the Lord of the Ascendant and the Lord of the Matter. Using a marriage query as an example, the text explains how Mars "steals the light" of Mercury before it can complete its aspect with Jupiter, resulting in a failed outcome.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of the sixteen Tajika Yogas, focusing specifically on Manao Yoga (the Yoga of Prohibition). This configuration describes a situation where a positive outcome is promised by the main planets but is ultimately "stolen" or blocked by the interference of a malefic planet like Mars or Saturn.</meta>

Afterward, because of its fast-moving nature, the planet meets with the Lord of the Matter. For this reason, it should be said that there is no "gain of a wife" <note>This refers to the specific question used as an example, where the seeker asks if they will marry</note>; the acquisition does not occur. This is one variation of the configuration. Now, for another: alternatively, as established in the previous configuration, if **Mars** is situated at fewer degrees relative to **Mercury** and is behind it, and if this **Mars** remains behind, this **Manao Yoga** becomes the "destroyer of the work." This is **Manao Yoga**.

### 31, 32, 33. Now, the varieties of Manao Yoga are stated.

**If there is an Itthashala** <note>original: "itthaśāla" — an applying aspect where a faster planet approaches a slower one</note> **between the two—the Lord of the Ascendant and the Lord of the Matter—and Saturn or the Son of the Earth (Mars) are in the same sign; if they are situated between the Lord of the Ascendant and the Lord of the Matter, within the range of their own orbs of influence...** <note>original: "svadīptīṃśa" — the 'shining degrees' or planetary orb within which a planet’s influence is felt</note>. By the word "own," the Lord of the Ascendant and the Lord of the Matter are meant. If they are within the degrees of their orbs, whether by fewer or more degrees, falling in the middle of those orbs:

### This is Manao Yoga

> **If an applying aspect exists between the two, and Saturn or Mars are in the same sign within the orb of their splendor, they are declared the stealers of splendor and the destroyers of the matter.** — Verse 34

> **In a query regarding the acquisition of a kingdom: the Lord of the Libra Ascendant is Venus, located in Cancer at fifteen degrees. The Moon is in Taurus at ten degrees, and Mars—at sixteen degrees in Taurus—takes away the splendor of both and thus steals the kingdom.** — Verse 35

By the degrees of Saturn and Mars, they become the **stealers of splendor** <note>original: "tejoharau" — planets that strip the power or light from a positive astrological aspect</note> for the Lord of the Ascendant and the Lord of the Matter; thus, they are declared the **destroyers of the matter**.

#### Now, an example:

Regarding the **Query on the Acquisition of a Kingdom**: In the case of a question asking "Will I obtain a kingdom or not?", if the Ascendant is **Libra** <note>Tula-lagna</note>, its ruler is **Venus** <note>Sita/Shukra</note>. Venus is located in **Cancer** <note>Karka</note>—the tenth house—at fifteen degrees. The Lord of the House of Kingship (the 10th house) is the **Moon** <note>Shashi/Chandra</note>, who is exalted in **Taurus** <note>Vrisha</note> at ten degrees. **Mars** <note>Kuja</note> is also in Taurus at sixteen degrees. It should be understood that by the word "and," **Saturn** <note>Shani</note> is also in Taurus at sixteen degrees. Even though there is an **Itthashala** <note>an applying aspect</note> between the Lord of the Ascendant and the Lord of the Matter, Mars steals the splendor and thus "steals the kingdom." Again, this is **Manao Yoga**. — 35

***

<summary>This page defines Manao Yoga, a configuration in Tajika astrology where the presence of Mars or Saturn "steals the splendor" of an otherwise positive aspect, leading to the failure of the seeker's objective. An example is provided concerning a royal query where despite a strong Moon and Venus, the presence of Mars and Saturn in Taurus prevents the acquisition of a kingdom.</summary>
<keywords>Manao Yoga, Tajika Shastra, Itthashala, planetary orbs, Tula Lagna, Venus, Moon, Mars, Saturn, horary astrology, obstruction</keywords>

Now, wishing to speak of the **Kamboola Yoga** <note>original: "kambūlayoga" — a configuration where the Moon acts as a bridge between two planets</note>, which is divided into eight or sixteen varieties, the author first states the general definition of **Kamboola Yoga**: "Arising from the Lord of the Ascendant and the Lord of the Matter..." Here, when there is an **Itthashala** <note>an applying aspect</note> between the Lord of the Ascendant <note>the planet representing the seeker</note> and the Lord of the Matter <note>the planet representing the object of the query</note>, if the Moon <note>original: "indu"</note> also forms an **Itthashala** with either one of them, then it is **Kamboola**. It is said that **Kamboola** occurs when the Moon is in a technical aspect with either the Lord of the Ascendant or the Lord of the Matter.

As stated in the **Tajika Bhushana** <note>The Ornament of Tajika Astrology, a classical reference text</note>: "If the Night-Maker (the Moon) forms an **Itthashala** with even one of the two planets forming a yoga, then the **Kamboola Yoga** is declared. It is of three types: Full, Medium, and Weak." These three are calculated based on the strength of the planets involved. Another source says: "When the Moon is in an **Itthashala** with one of two planets who are already in an **Itthashala** with each other, that is **Kamboola Yoga**."

# From the Itthashala of the Lord of the Ascendant and the Lord of the Matter, and then from an Itthashala with the Moon, Kamboola is known to be of various kinds, such as Best, Medium, and others. || 36 ||

This **Kamboola** is generally of three types: Best, Medium, and "others," meaning Weak. Through these variations, it is remembered as being of many kinds. Based on the four types of "authority" <note>original: "adhikāra" — refers to the strength a planet gains from its position in the zodiac, such as being in its own sign or exaltation</note> of the Lord of the Ascendant, the Lord of the Matter, and the Moon—

<margin>
This means there are sixteen variations based on the differences in authority. They are as follows:
1. Best of the Best
2. Best of the Medium
3. Best
4. Best of the Weak
5. Medium of the Best
6. Medium of the Medium
7. Medium
8. Medium of the Weak
9. Best
10. Medium
11. Medium of the Medium
12. Weak
13. Weak of the Best
14. Weak of the Medium
15. Weak
16. Weak of the Weak
A chart is written below to clarify these. In this context, being in one's own house or sign of exaltation is considered "Best."
</margin>

| | Lords of Ascendant & Matter | Lords of Ascendant & Matter | In the Authority of the Ascendant | In the Authority of the Ascendant |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Moon in Best Authority** | Both in Medium Authority | Both in Medium Authority | Both in Equal Authority | Both in Weak Authority |
| **Moon in Medium Authority** | Both in Best Authority | Both in Medium Authority | Both in Equal Authority | Both in Weak Authority |
| **Moon in Equal Authority** | Both in Best Authority | Both in Medium Authority | Both in Equal Authority | Both in Weak Authority |

<summary>This page defines Kamboola Yoga, a configuration where the Moon facilitates an aspect between the Ascendant Lord and the Lord of the Matter. It details sixteen sub-types of this yoga based on the relative strength and "authority" of the planets involved.</summary>

--- Page 54 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical classification of Kamboola Yoga, focusing on how the dignity (strength) of the involved planets—the Moon, the Lord of the Ascendant, and the Lord of the Matter—determines the quality of the astrological result. It transitions from general definitions to specific hierarchical categories like "Superior-Superior."</meta>

Dignity is considered **Medium** <note>original: "madhyama"</note> when a planet is in its own **Hadda** <note>a specific divisional degree similar to 'bounds' or 'terms' in Western astrology</note>, its own **Drekkana** <note>a ten-degree subdivision or 'decanate'</note>, or its own **Navamsha** <note>the ninth-part divisional chart, crucial for determining planetary strength</note>. It is considered **Low** <note>original: "adhama"</note> when the planet is in the sign of an enemy or in its sign of debilitation. The absence of these three specific conditions results in **Equal** dignity. 

It is stated in the **Manushya Jataka** <note>A classical text on human horoscopy</note> regarding the **Kamboola** <note>the 'bridge' yoga involving the Moon</note>: "If the Moon also forms a **Mutthashila** <note>an applying aspect within a specific range of degrees</note> while two other planets are already in an **Itthashala** <note>a general term for a harmonious applying aspect</note>, it creates the yoga. If the planets are mutually in their own houses or exaltation signs, it is **Principal** (Superior); if in the aforementioned divisions, it is **Middle**; and if in enemy signs, it is **Low**." 

This is also stated by **Samarasimha** <note>A renowned 13th-century authority on Tajika astrology</note>. || 36 ||

The author now describes these same variations using highly specific titles, starting with the definition of **Superior-Superior Kamboola**:

<insert>Superior-Middle Kamboola</insert>

->If the Moon is placed in its own house or its sign of exaltation, and the Lord of the Ascendant and the Lord of the Matter are situated similarly,<-

->the resulting **Itthashala** is called **Kamboola**, and it is declared to be **Superior-Superior**. || 37 ||<-

->When the aspect is formed by planets residing in their own **Hadda**, **Drekkana**, or **Navamsha** divisions,<-

->it is **Middle-Superior Kamboola**; it is simply **Superior** when the dignity is lesser. || 38 ||<-

This occurs when the Moon forms a **Mutthashila** aspect while being in its own house or exaltation. In such a case, the **Kamboola** is called **Superior-Superior** because all three participating planets have attained the highest level of dignity. This is the first variation. 

Among these sixteen varieties of **Kamboola Yoga**, it is a rule that the Lord of the Ascendant <note>original: "lagnesha," representing the person asking the question</note> and the Lord of the Matter <note>original: "karyesha," representing the object of the inquiry</note> must possess some form of dignity and be in a mutual **Mutthashila** aspect. The Moon's **Mutthashila** is required with at least one of these two planets. || 37 ||

Next, the author explains the definitions of **Middle-Superior** and **Simple-Superior Kamboola**: 

If the Lord of the Ascendant and the Lord of the Matter form a mutual **Mutthashila** while located in their own **Hadda**, **Drekkana**, or **Navamsha** divisions, and the Moon—placed in its own house or exaltation—then forms a **Mutthashila** with them, it is **Middle-Superior Kamboola**. This is because while the Moon has attained superior dignity, the other two planets have only attained medium dignity...

<summary>This page defines the hierarchy of Kamboola Yoga based on planetary "dignities" (strength based on zodiacal position). It introduces "Superior-Superior" and "Middle-Superior" categories, emphasizing the Moon's role in connecting the Lord of the Ascendant and the Lord of the Matter.</summary>

<keywords>Kamboola Yoga, Tajika astrology, Mutthashila, Itthashala, planetary dignity, Hadda, Drekkana, Navamsha, Samarasimha, Moon aspects</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical classification of Kamboola Yoga from the Tajika Nilakanthi, a classic text on Persian-influenced Sanskrit astrology. It focuses on the "Superior-Medium" and "Superior-Inferior" variations of the yoga, illustrating how the strength (dignity) of the Moon can compensate for the weakness of other planets to ensure a successful outcome for the querent.</meta>

### Collection of 81 Year Old Dina Nath Raina, Jammu. Retired Priest.
**Reference: Pra. 1**

<margin>Tajika Nilakanthi Page 26</margin>
...because [the Moon] has attained dignity. The author, however, referred to this as **Medium-Superior** <note>original: "madhyama-uttamam"</note> simply to avoid breaking the poetic meter of the verse. 

**An Illustration:**
In a query regarding "Will I have good fortune or not?", the Ascendant is Libra. Its lord, Venus <note>original: "Bhrigu"</note>, is in the tenth house in its own **Hadda** <note>original: "dasham-svahaddayam"; specific degrees of a sign governed by a planet, similar to 'bounds' in Western astrology</note> at 10 degrees. The lord of the House of Fortune (the 9th), Mercury <note>original: "Budha"</note>, is in the seventh house in its own **Hadda** at 14 degrees. These two are in a mutual **Mutthashila** <note>an applying aspect where a faster planet reaches a slower one</note>. The Moon is in Cancer, its own house, at 18 degrees, forming a **Mutthashila** with them. This is a **Superior-Medium Kamboola** yoga. The result is that fortune will be achieved with very little effort. Similar examples should be sought for planets residing in their own **Drekkana** <note>ten-degree subdivision</note> or **Navamsha** <note>ninth-part subdivision</note>. 

Now, regarding those who lack such dignity: if the **Lord of the Ascendant** <note>representing the querent</note> and the **Lord of the Matter** <note>representing the goal</note> are in mutual **Mutthashila** but lack dignity, yet the Moon—placed in its own house or exaltation—forms a **Mutthashila** with them, then it is considered a **Superior Kamboola**. This is because the Moon alone has attained **Superior Dignity**, while the other two have attained **Equal** (neutral) dignity.

**Example:**
In a query about "attaining a kingdom," the Ascendant is Gemini; its lord, Mercury, resides in a neutral house. 

->**If [the lords] are in debilitation or enemy houses, but the Moon is in its own Decanate or divisional part, or is placed in its sign of exaltation and forms an Itthashala aspect...** || 39 ||<-

The lord of the House of Kingdom (the 10th) is Jupiter <note>original: "Guru"</note>, who is in a neutral sign, Virgo. The Moon, however, is in Taurus <note>its sign of exaltation</note>. Since there is a mutual **Mutthashila** aspect between all three, it becomes a **Superior Kamboola**. Therefore, the attainment of the kingdom should be declared as "Superior" (highly successful). || 38 ||

Now, in the next half-verse, the author describes the characteristics of the **Superior-Inferior Kamboola**: 

When the Lord of the Ascendant and the Lord of the Matter are in their signs of debilitation or in the signs of their enemies, but are in a mutual **Mutthashila** aspect, and the Moon—placed in its own house or exaltation sign—forms a **Mutthashila** with either of them, it is called **Superior-Inferior Kamboola**. This is because the Moon has attained **Superior Dignity**, while the other two have attained **Inferior (Low) Dignity**. 

**An Illustration:**
In a query about "obtaining a wife," the Ascendant is Libra. Its lord, Venus, is at 10 degrees in Virgo, its sign of debilitation. The lord of the House of Wives (the 7th) is Mars <note>original: "Bhauma"</note>, who is at 15 degrees in Cancer, its sign of debilitation. The Moon also...

<meta>This page continues the technical classification of Kamboola Yogas from the Tajika Nilakanthi, specifically defining the 'Medium-Superior' and 'Medium-Medium' variations based on the planetary dignities of the Ascendant Lord, the significator, and the Moon. It builds on the previous page's discussion of Superior variations.</meta>

# Collection of 81 Year Old Dina Nath Raina, Jammu. Retired Priest.
## Reference: Pra. 1

...in the 12th division <term>original: "dvadasamsa"; a 2.5-degree subdivision of a zodiac sign</term> of Cancer, its own house. A mutual **Mutthashila** <term>an applying aspect between two planets where the faster one approaches the slower one</term> is formed between them. This is known as **Superior-Inferior Kamboola** <note>original: "uttama-adhama-kamboola"</note>; in this case, obtaining a woman <note>this refers to a question about marriage or finding a partner</note> is accomplished through gifts and great effort. 

**Now, the definition of Medium-Superior Kamboola:**
The author says: "In one's own decan..." <note>original: "sva-drekkana"</note>. If the **Lord of the Ascendant** <note>the planet ruling the sign rising on the eastern horizon at the time of the query, representing the seeker</note> or the **Lord of the Matter** <note>the planet ruling the house associated with the question</note> is situated in its own **Drekkana** <term>a ten-degree subdivision of a sign</term> or its own **Navamsha** <term>a ninth-part subdivision of a sign, roughly 3 degrees and 20 minutes</term>, and forms a **Mutthashila** with a planet that is in its own house or exaltation, and if the Moon also forms an **Itthashala** <term>a specific type of favorable applying aspect</term>, then this is called **Medium-Superior Kamboola**. Why? Because the Moon has attained "medium" dignity while the other planets have attained "superior" dignity. This **Medium-Superior Kamboola** is not considered significantly different in its results from the previously mentioned **Superior-Medium Kamboola**. 

**An Illustration:**
In a query regarding "Will I obtain a wife?", the Ascendant is Libra. Its lord is Venus <note>original: "Shukra"</note>, located in the Ascendant (its own house) at 24 degrees. The seventh house <note>the house of marriage and partners</note> is Aries; its lord is Mars <note>original: "Bhauma"</note>, located in Aries (its own house) at 28 degrees. The Moon, excluding the signs of Taurus and Cancer, is in its own **Navamsha** in Capricorn at 22 degrees. A mutual **Mutthashila** occurs between them; this is the **Medium-Superior Kamboola**. In this case, the attainment of a wife is excellent.

->This Medium-Superior [yoga] is not distinct from the former. With planets in their own **Hadda** or similar positions, it is **Medium-Medium Kamboola**. || 40 ||<-

Now, with half a verse, the author defines **Medium-Medium Kamboola**: 
If the **Lord of the Ascendant** and the **Lord of the Matter** are situated in their own **Hadda** <term>a specific degree-based dignity similar to 'terms' or 'bounds' in Western astrology</term>, **Drekkana**, or **Navamsha**, and they form a **Mutthashila**, and the Moon—also situated in its own **Drekkana** or **Navamsha**—forms a **Mutthashila** with them, then it is **Medium-Medium Kamboola**. Why? Because all participating planets have attained "medium" dignity. 

**An Illustration:**
In a query regarding children, the Ascendant is Gemini. Its lord is Mercury <note>original: "Budha"</note>, located in Libra in its own **Hadda** at 8 degrees. The lord of the house of children <note>the 5th house</note> is Venus, located in Gemini in its own **Hadda** at 8 degrees. The Moon is in the first **Drekkana** of Cancer and in its own **Navamsha**. Because of the mutual **Mutthashila** between all three, this is **Medium-Medium Kamboola**. By this, the attainment of children is "medium-medium"—meaning it is possible but requires significant effort. || 39 || || 40 ||

Now, with a half-verse, he explains the definition of **Medium Kamboola** <term>a planetary configuration where the Moon links the Ascendant lord and the significator, specifically of "medium" strength</term>. He says: "Medium..." The term "devoid of lower" <note>original: "hina-rahita"</note> refers to those planets that lack superior or medium dignity, meaning they have attained **Neutral Dignity** <note>original: "sama-adhikara"; planets in signs where they are neither exalted, in their own house, nor debilitated</note>. When a **Mutthashila** <term>an applying aspect</term> is formed between the **Lord of the Ascendant** <note>the planet representing the seeker</note> and the **Lord of the Matter** <note>the planet representing the object of the question</note> who are in such neutral positions, and if the Moon—situated in its own **Medium Dignity** <note>such as being in its own Decan or Term</note>—also forms a Mutthashila with them, then it is **Medium Kamboola**. Why? Because the Moon has attained medium dignity while the others have attained neutral/equal dignity.

**Here is an example:** In a question regarding **progeny** <note>original: "santati-prashne"</note>, suppose the Ascendant is Taurus and its Lord, **Venus**, is in the **Hadda** <term>a "term" or Egyptian bound; a 5-7 degree subdivision of a sign</term> of a neutral planet at 4 degrees in Capricorn. The Lord of the 5th House <note>the house of children</note>, **Mercury**, is in a neutral Hadda at 5 degrees in Libra. The Moon is in its own **Drekkana** <term>a 10-degree subdivision</term> in Cancer. Because a mutual **Mutthashila**

->Medium Kamboola arises from planets of lower dignity. Medium-Inferior Kamboola arises from planets situated in signs of debilitation or enemy signs. || 41 ||<-
->Even if the Moon lacks specific dignity, if it joins a planet in its own sign or exaltation, that is Superior; if it joins one in its own Hadda/Term, it is called Medium as before. || 42 ||<-

<margin>Pra.</margin> <note>Abbreviation for Prakarana, meaning 'Section' or 'Chapter'.</note>

is formed among these, it results in **Medium Kamboola**. This means the birth of children is accomplished only through great effort <note>original: "ayasa-sadhya"</note>.

Now, with a half-verse, he explains the definition of **Medium-Inferior Kamboola**. He says: "Medium-Inferior..." When the Lord of the Ascendant and the Lord of the Matter are situated in their **Signs of Debilitation** <term>original: "nicha-kshetra"; the zodiac sign where a planet is weakest</term> or in **Enemy Signs**, and they form a mutual Mutthashila, and if the Moon—situated in its own Drekkana or **Navamsha** <term>a 3-degree 20-minute subdivision</term>—forms a Mutthashila with them, it is called **Medium-Inferior Kamboola**. Why? Because the Moon has medium dignity while the others have "inferior" <note>adhama</note> dignity.

**Here is an example:** In a question regarding **attaining fortune** <note>original: "bhagya-prapti"</note>, suppose the Ascendant Lord, **Mars**, is in Cancer (his sign of debilitation), and the 9th House Lord <note>the house of fortune</note>, **Jupiter**, is in Capricorn (his sign of debilitation). The Moon is in Cancer in its own Drekkana. Because there is a mutual Mutthashila yoga among them, it is **Medium-Inferior Kamboola**. Fortune will be attained, but only with great difficulty. || 41 ||

Rather, it is in a neutral state. If such a Moon is with a planet in its own sign or exal...

<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of Kamboola Yogas from the Tajika Nilakanthi. # Collection of 81 Year Old Dina Nath Raina, Jammu. Retired Priest.

If the **Lord of the Ascendant** <note>original: "Lagnadhisha"; the planet representing the person asking the question</note> and the **Lord of the Matter** <note>original: "Karyadhisha"; the planet representing the object of the query</note> are both in their signs of exaltation and are in a mutual **Mutthashila** <term>an applying aspect or configuration where a faster planet approaches a slower one</term>, it becomes a **Kamboola** <term>a configuration where the Moon acts as a bridge between two planets in aspect</term>. In such a case, it is considered **Excellent** <note>original: "Uttama"</note>. Why? Even if the Moon lacks specific dignity, the other two planets have attained the highest dignity (exaltation). 

**Here is an example:** In a question regarding the **acquisition of wealth** <note>original: "dhana-labha-prashne"</note>, suppose the Ascendant is **Libra** <note>original: "Tula"</note>. The Lord of the Ascendant, **Venus**, is in Libra, which is its own home. The Lord of Wealth (the 2nd house), **Mars**, is in **Capricorn**, where it is exalted. The **Moon** is in a neutral **Decan** <term>Drekkana; a 10-degree subdivision of a sign</term> in **Gemini**. Because there is a mutual Mutthashila yoga among them, a "Superior-Neutral" Kamboola is formed, and the attainment of fortune will be excellent.

Now, with a half-verse, he explains the definition of another **Medium Kamboola**: "In its own Hadda..." If the Lord of the Ascendant and the Lord of the Matter are situated in their own **Hadda** <term>also known as "Terms" or "Bounds"; a Perso-Arabic subdivision of a sign used in Tajika astrology</term>, or their own Decan or **Navamsha** <term>a 1/9th divisional chart</term>, and form a mutual Mutthashila, and the Moon—possessing equal or medium dignity—also forms a Mutthashila with them, then it is a **Medium Kamboola**. This is because the Moon lacks high dignity while the others have attained only medium dignity. This yields a result similar to the Medium Kamboola previously described.

**Example:** In a question of wealth, the Ascendant is Libra; its lord is Venus. Venus is in **Leo** at 10 degrees in its own Hadda.

->Even with three-quarters strength, it is considered "Medium"; this is perceived as logical. An "Inferior Kamboola" is said to occur when the configuration happens in debilitation or an enemy's sign. (Verse 43)<-

The Lord of the House of Wealth, Mars, is in the "body" (the 1st house) at 10 degrees in its own Hadda. The Moon is in a medium Decan in Gemini at 10 degrees. There is a mutual Mutthashila yoga between them. Therefore, this is a Medium Kamboola, and the acquisition of wealth will be of a medium nature. || 42 ||

Again, he explains another variety of Medium Kamboola with a half-verse: "By a quarter less..." If the Lord of the Ascendant and the Lord of the Matter lack both superior and medium dignity, but form a mutual Mutthashila, and the Moon—possessing equal/neutral dignity—also forms a Mutthashila, then we consider it a Medium Kamboola. This is logical because all the planets have attained equal (neutral) dignity.

**Example:** In a question of wealth, the Ascendant is **Aries**. Its lord, Mars, is in Leo at 10 degrees. The Lord of Wealth, Venus, is in **Aquarius** at 10 degrees. The Moon is in Libra at 10 degrees. When a mutual Mutthashila yoga is formed among these, it is a Medium Kamboola. By this, the gain is medium—meaning it is neither excellent nor very poor. 

Now, he describes the **Inferior Kamboola** <note>original: "Adhama-kamboola"</note> with a half-verse: "In debilitation or an enemy's sign..." When the Lord of the Ascendant is in its sign of debilitation or in the sign of its enemy...

<meta>This page continues the discussion of Kamboola Yogas from the Tajika Nilakanthi, specifically focusing on combinations involving "Adhama" (Inferior) conditions where one or more planets lack dignity. It follows the previous page's explanation of superior and medium configurations, transitioning here into how debilitated planets or the Moon affect the outcome of a query.</meta>

# Collection of 81 Year Old Dina Nath Raina, Jammu. Retired Priest.

<margin>T. N. <note>original: "Ta. Ni."; abbreviation for the text title, Tajika Nilakanthi</note>
|| 28 ||</margin>

If the **Lord of the Matter** <note>original: "Karyadhisha"; the planet representing the object of the query</note> and the **Lord of the Ascendant** <note>original: "Lagnadhisha"</note> have formed a mutual **Mutthashila** <term>an applying aspect where a faster planet approaches a slower one</term>, and the **Moon**, being placed in a position of authority, also forms a Mutthashila with them, then it is called an **Inferior Kamboola** <note>original: "Adhama-khyam Kamboolam"</note>. Why? Because while the Moon has attained authority, the other two planets have attained "Inferior" <note>original: "Adhama"</note> status (lack of dignity). 

**An Example:** In a question regarding **children** <note>original: "Putra-prashne"</note>, the Ascendant is **Gemini** <note>original: "Mithuna"</note>. Its Lord, **Mercury**, is in **Pisces**, where it is **Debilitated** <note>original: "Neecha"; the sign where a planet is at its weakest</note>. The Lord of the House of Children (the 5th house) is **Venus**, which is in **Virgo**, also **Debilitated**. The Moon, however, is without specific authority here. Because there is a mutual Mutthashila yoga among these three, an **Inferior Kamboola** is formed. Therefore, the concern regarding the birth of a son will have a very poor result; it means it will be accomplished with slight difficulty. || 43 ||

Now, he explains the definition of the **Inferior-Superior Kamboola** <note>original: "Adhamottama Kamboola"</note> with the verse beginning "Neecha-shatru" (Debilitated or Enemy):

If the **Moon** is in its sign of debilitation or in the sign of an enemy, but it forms a Mutthashila with the Lord of the Ascendant and the Lord of the Matter—who are themselves in their own homes or in their signs of exaltation—then it is an **Inferior-Superior Kamboola**. Why? Because the Moon has attained Inferior status, while the other two have attained Superior status. This configuration gives a result equal to the previously mentioned [superior] Kamboola yoga.

**An Example:** In a question regarding the **attainment of happiness** <note>original: "Sukha-prapti-prashne"</note>, the Ascendant is **Leo** <note>original: "Simha" </note>.

<margin>Question
?</margin>
-> If the Moon is in its sign of debilitation or an enemy's house, but forms a configuration with planets in their own homes or exalted, it is the **Inferior-Superior Kamboola**, which grants results equal to the former [superior] types. || 44 || <-

-> If the Moon is in its sign of debilitation or an enemy's house, and is not in its own Hadda or similar division, but forms a configuration, that is called **Inferior-Medium Kamboola**. || 45 || <-

<margin>|| 28 ||</margin>
Its lord is the **Sun**, which is in **Aries**, its sign of **Exaltation** <note>original: "Ucha"</note>. The Lord of the House of Happiness (the 4th house) is **Mars**, which is in **Capricorn**, also **Exaltation**. The **Moon** is in **Scorpio**, its sign of **Debilitation**. In this way, when a mutual Mutthashila occurs among these three, an **Inferior-Superior Kamboola** is formed. The attainment of happiness will be accomplished with a small amount of effort. || 44 ||

Now, he explains the definition of the **Inferior-Medium Kamboola** <note>original: "Adhamamadhyama Kamboola"</note>: "The Moon..." etc. If the Moon is in its sign of debilitation or an enemy's house, but the Lord of the Ascendant and the Lord of the Matter—who are in their own **Hadda** <term>a subdivision of a sign also known as "Bounds" or "Terms"</term>, or by the word "etc." in their own **Decan** <note>original: "Drekkana"</note> or **Navamsha** <note>original: "1/9th division"</note>—form a mutual Mutthashila (also called **Itthashala**) with it, then it is called an **Inferior-Medium Kamboola**. Why? Because the Moon has Inferior status and the other two have attained Medium status.

**An Example:** In a question regarding the **attainment of children**, the Ascendant is **Virgo** <note>original: "Kanya"</note>. Its lord is **Mercury**, which is at 5 degrees of **Capricorn**, in its own **Hadda**. The Lord of the House of Children, **Saturn**, is at 5 degrees of **Pisces**, in its own **Hadda**. The **Moon** is at 5 degrees of **Scorpio**, its sign of **Debilitation**. When a mutual Mutthashila yoga is formed among these three, an **Inferior-Medium Kamboola** occurs. Therefore, the attainment of offspring should be known as being achievable only with great effort. || 45 ||

<summary>This page defines three specific types of Kamboola configurations (Inferior, Inferior-Superior, and Inferior-Medium) based on whether the Moon and the planets involved are exalted, debilitated, or in their own minor astrological divisions. It provides practical examples for queries regarding children and happiness to illustrate how these configurations determine the ease or difficulty of achieving a goal.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the technical classification of Kamboola Yogas from the Tajika Nilakanthi, a classic text on Persian-influenced Indian horary astrology. It picks up from the previous discussion on "Inferior-Superior" configurations, moving into the specific definitions of "Inferior" (Adhama), "Lowest-of-the-Low" (Adhama-adhama), and "Highest-of-the-High" (Uttama-uttama) planetary combinations.</meta>

# Collection of 81 Year Old Dina Nath Raina, Jammu. Retired Priest.

Now he states the definition of another [type], the **Inferior Kamboola** <note>original: "Adhama-Kamboola"</note>. "Indu" refers to the Moon. If the **Moon** is in its sign of **Debilitation** <term>Neecha; the zodiac sign where a planet's power is at its lowest</term> or in the sign of its enemy, and if it forms a **Mutthashila** <term>an applying aspect where a faster planet approaches a slower one</term> with the **Lord of the Ascendant** <note>original: "Lagnadhisha"</note> and the **Lord of the Matter** <note>original: "Karyadhisha"</note>—both of whom are <term>Padona</term> <note>meaning "lacking a quarter," referring here to planets lacking essential dignities or "authority"</note>—then it should be known as an **Inferior Kamboola**. Why? Because the Moon has attained an "Inferior" status, and the other two have likewise attained similar statuses. This yields a result equal to the previously mentioned Kamboola yoga. 

**An Example:** In a query regarding the **acquisition of a kingdom** <note>original: "Rajya-prapti-prashne"</note>, the Ascendant is **Taurus** <note>original: "Vrusha"</note>. Its Lord is **Venus**, located at the **6th degree** <term>Shad-amsha</term> of **Leo**. The Lord of the Kingdom (the 10th house) is **Saturn**, located at the **10th degree** <term>Dasha-amsha</term> of **Taurus**. The Moon, however, is at the **28th degree of Scorpio**, its sign of Debilitation. Since a Mutthashila yoga is formed among these, it is an **Inferior Kamboola**; therefore, the acquisition of the kingdom will be accomplished only with great difficulty. || 46 || 

Now he states the definition of the **Lowest-of-the-Low Kamboola**:

> If the Moon is in its sign of fall or an enemy’s sign, and forms an aspect with planets that lack dignity, it is known as a **Medium Kamboola**, giving results similar to what was previously stated. || 46 ||
> When a planet situated in its sign of fall or an enemy’s sign forms a **Mutthashila** with the Moon, which is also in its sign of fall or an enemy’s sign, that **Kamboola** is called the **Lowest-of-the-Low**. || 47 ||
> If the Sun or Mars is in Aries, or the Moon is in Taurus or Cancer, and they form a **Mutthashila**, that **Kamboola** brings about the **Highest-of-the-High** results in all matters. || 48 ||

When the Moon forms a Mutthashila with the Lord of the Ascendant and the Lord of the Matter, and all three are in their respective signs of **Debilitation** or in the signs of their enemies, then the Kamboola is called **Lowest-of-the-Low** <note>original: "Adhama-adhama"</note>. Why? Because the Moon and the other two planets have all attained "Inferior" status. 

**An Example:** In a query regarding the **attainment of children** <note>original: "Putra-labha-prashne"</note>, the Ascendant is **Sagittarius** <note>original: "Dhanu"</note>. Its Lord is **Jupiter** <note>original: "Guru"</note>, which is **Debilitated** in **Capricorn**. The Lord of the House of Children (the 5th house) is **Mars** <note>original: "Bhauma"</note>, which is **Debilitated** in **Cancer**. The Moon is also at the **28th degree of Pisces**. Here, because there is a mutual Mutthashila yoga among these three, it is the "Lowest-of-the-Low" Kamboola. Therefore, one should declare that there will be **no attainment of progeny at all**. || 47 || 

Thus, the variations of the sixteen types of Kamboola have been stated. Now, among the previously mentioned variations, he gives an example of the **Highest-of-the-High Kamboola** yoga. "In Aries," etc. 

**An Example:** In a query regarding **offspring** <note>original: "Apatya-prashne"</note>, the Ascendant is **Aries** <note>original: "Mesha"</note>. Its Lord is **Mars**, which is in **Aries**, its own house. The Lord of the 5th House is the **Sun** <note>original: "Ravi"</note>, which is in **Aries**, the sign of its **Exaltation** <term>Uccha; the zodiac sign where a planet's influence is at its peak</term>. While there is a mutual Mutthashila yoga between these two (the Lord of the Ascendant and the Lord of the Matter), if the Moon—being situated in either **Taurus** (its sign of Exaltation) or **Cancer** (its own house)—also forms a Mutthashila, then that **Kamboola** is the **Highest-of-the-High**, bringing about the desired fruit or success in the matter. || 48 ||

<summary>This page defines three grades of the Kamboola yoga—Inferior, Lowest-of-the-Low, and Highest-of-the-High—based on whether the participating planets and the Moon are in signs of strength (exaltation/own house) or weakness (debilitation/enemy signs). It provides specific examples related to politics (kingdom acquisition) and family (birth of children) to illustrate how these planetary dignities determine the success or failure of a query.</summary>

# Collection of 81 Year Old Dina Nath Raina, Jammu. Retired Priest.

<margin>Ta. Ni. <note>Abbreviation for the text "Tajika Nilakanthi"</note></margin>
<margin>॥ 29 ॥</margin>

Now he states the example of the final **Lowest-of-the-Low Kamboola yoga** <note>original: "Adhama-adhama-kamboola"</note>. He says, "Being in Scorpio..." In a query regarding the **birth of a son** <note>original: "putra-labha-prashne"</note>, the Lord of the House of Children <term>the 5th house</term> is **Jupiter**, who is in **Capricorn**, his sign of **Debilitation** <term>Neecha; the sign where a planet's influence is weakest</term>. The Lord of the Ascendant is **Mars**, who is in **Cancer**, his sign of Debilitation. The **Moon** is in **Scorpio**, her sign of Debilitation. In this case, because of the **Mutthashila** <term>an applying astrological aspect</term> between the Moon and Mars, and between Jupiter and Mars, the resulting **Kamboola** is called the "Lowest-of-the-Low." This brings about the **destruction of the desired goal** and causes sorrow. ॥ 49 ॥ 

Then he says that examples for the various intermediate types of Kamboola—situated between the first and last types—should be inferred. He says, "In this way..." In this way, experts in astrology <note>original: "daivajña"</note> should apply these examples by connecting the defined characteristics previously explained. These examples are not explicitly stated here because the definitions themselves are clear in meaning. ॥ 50 ॥

Now, regarding the definition of Kamboola, some scholars say that the **Mutthashila yoga** occurs only when the fast-moving planet and the slow-moving planet are located in the same zodiac sign. He presents this view: "The Moon in Aries..." When the **Moon** is in **Aries** and forms a Mutthashila with **Saturn**, who is also in **Aries**, the Kamboola produced is called **Average-Inferior** <note>original: "Madhyama-adhama"</note>.

> ->**The Moon in Scorpio and Mars in Cancer: from their applying aspect <note>original: "Itthashala"</note>, the Lowest-of-the-Low Kamboola is formed, which causes the destruction of work and brings sorrow. ॥ 49 ॥ In this way, the application of examples for the previously mentioned types should be inferred by the wise, based on the connection to the stated characteristics. ॥ 50 ॥ Others have said that the applying aspects <note>original: "Itthashala"</note> occur when a slow and a fast planet are situated in the same sign, such as the Moon in Aries with Saturn. ॥ 51 ॥**<-

<margin>Pr. 1</margin>
<margin>॥ 29 ॥</margin>

He explains the view of these other teachers: When the **Moon** <note>original: "abja"</note> is in **Aries**, and it forms a Mutthashila with **Saturn** who is also in **Aries**, the resulting Kamboola is known as **Average-Inferior**. This is used as an illustration because both the fast and slow planets are in the same sign. Others say the same regarding the Mutthashila and other aspects. The word "other" refers to aspects like the **Esarapha** <term>a separating aspect, from the Persian 'Ittisal' and 'Inshiraf'</term>. 

Furthermore, **Samarasimha** <note>A famous 13th-century authority on Tajika astrology</note> says: "When the Moon is in Aries with Saturn; or in Cancer with Mars; or in Virgo with Venus; or in Capricorn with Jupiter; or in Pisces with Mercury—it is not auspicious." ॥ 1 ॥ 

The meaning is this: If the **Moon** is in **Aries** and forms a Mutthashila with **Saturn** (who is also in Aries, as no other location is mentioned), an **Average-Inferior** Kamboola is formed. Why? Because here the Moon has no dignity <note>original: "adhikara"</note> in Aries, such as being in her own house or exaltation; nor is it her **Decan** <term>Drekkana; a 10-degree division</term>, though she may be in her own **Ninth-Division** <term>Navamsha</term>. Saturn, meanwhile, is in his sign of **Debilitation**. 

Similarly, if the **Moon** is in **Cancer** (her own house) and forms a Mutthashila with **Mars** who is also in **Cancer** (his sign of Debilitation), the **Highest-Inferior** <note>original: "Uttama-adhama"</note> Kamboola is formed. Why? Because here the Moon is in her own house, but Mars is debilitated. Similarly, in **Virgo** <note>original: "striyam," literally 'the woman'</note>...

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of the "Middle-Low" (Madhyamadhama) Kamboola yoga, picking up from the debate regarding the placement of planets in specific signs and sub-divisional charts. It transitions from listing specific planetary combinations to a polemic against the view that astrological aspects only function when planets occupy the same zodiac sign.</meta>

# Collection of 81 Year Old Dina Nath Raina, Jammu. Retired Priest.

When the **Moon** is in **Virgo** along with **Venus**, who is in her sign of **Debilitation** <term>Neecha; the sign where a planet's influence is weakest</term>—namely Virgo—and a **Mutthashila** <term>an applying astrological aspect between a faster and slower planet</term> is formed, it results in a **"Middle-Low" Kamboola** <note>original: "Madhyamadhama-kamboola"</note>. Why? Because the Moon is in its own **Navamsha** <term>a one-ninth divisional chart used to determine a planet's hidden strength</term>, but Venus is debilitated. Here also, the Moon's presence in her own **Drekkana** <term>a one-third divisional chart</term> is not possible.

Similarly, when the **Moon** is in **Capricorn** with **Jupiter**, who is in his sign of **Debilitation**—namely Capricorn—and a Mutthashila is formed, it results in a **"Middle-Low" Kamboola**. Why? Because here the Moon is in her own Drekkana and her own Navamsha, but Jupiter is debilitated. Here too, the Moon's own Drekkana is not possible <note>This likely refers to specific degrees within the sign</note>.

Similarly, when the **Moon** is in **Pisces** with **Mercury**, who is in his sign of **Debilitation**—namely Pisces—and a Mutthashila is formed, it results in a **"Middle-Low" Kamboola**. Why? Because here the Moon is in her own Drekkana and her own Navamsha, but Mercury is debilitated. These five examples are given to show that such combinations are inauspicious and do not yield good results. ॥ 51 ॥

Now, the author refutes the opinion of others by providing an illustration. He says, "That is improper..."

->That is improper; for a planet in Debilitation, with a debilitated planet or an enemy, even if an **Itthashala** <term>the Tajika term for an applying aspect or "Mutthashila"</term> is formed, it causes the destruction of the task; this is clearly stated. ॥ 52 ॥<-

The opinion of some—that the Mutthashila and other aspects should only be considered when the fast-moving planet and the slow-moving planet are in the **same sign**—is **improper**. This means it does not stand to reason. Why? He explains: "For a planet in debilitation..."

Between the **Lord of the Ascendant** <term>Lagnadheesha; the planet ruling the rising sign</term> and the **Lord of the Task** <term>Karyadheesha; the planet ruling the house associated with the question</term>, if one planet enters its sign of debilitation and forms an **Itthashala** with the other planet (who is also in its sign of debilitation), it is said to cause the **destruction of the task**. It is not the case that both must share the same debilitation sign. 

Similarly, if there is mutual enmity between the Lord of the Ascendant and the Lord of the Task, the Mutthashila is said to cause the destruction of the task. This is because it is clearly and explicitly stated in the **Tajika Samjna Tantra** <note>A foundational manual on Tajika astrology</note> composed by **Samarasimha**. That verse states: 

"If a debilitated planet forms a Mutthashila with another debilitated planet, or if an enemy forms one with an enemy, that **Kamboola** is not desired; even the **Moon** acts as a destroyer in this situation." ॥ 1 ॥

Regarding this, the second yoga <note>the one involving enemies</note> can also occur when planets are in the same sign, so it should not be presented as a flaw in the logic. The author has written this by referencing the context of original source texts. ॥ 52 ॥

<summary>This page defines specific "Middle-Low" Kamboola yogas involving debilitated planets (Venus, Jupiter, and Mercury) and refutes the sectarian view that astrological aspects only occur within the same zodiac sign.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the technical exploration of Kamboola yoga from page 29. While the previous page focused on "Middle-Low" variations involving debilitated planets, this page introduces a further variation where the planetary lords lack essential dignities, and it concludes by defining the "Void of Course" (Shunya Marga) planet.</meta>

<margin>Tajika Neelakanthi || 30 ||</margin>
Now, the author explains another variation of the **Kamboola yoga** <term>a configuration where the Moon transfers light between two planets in aspect</term> for the purpose of understanding how results are produced. 

**"The Lord of the Ascendant and the Work..."** <note>Refers to the opening of Verse 53</note> Previously, it was stated that **Kamboola** occurs when the Moon forms a **Mutthashila** <term>an applying aspect between a faster and slower planet</term> with the Lord of the Ascendant <term>Lagnadhipa; the planet ruling the rising sign</term> and the Lord of the Matter <term>Karyadhipa; the planet ruling the house of the question</term> when they share the same authority.

Now, however, a difference in their authority is described. In a situation where a **Mutthashila** aspect between the Lord of the Ascendant and the Lord of the Matter is being considered, if one of them is in its **Debilitation** <term>Neecha; the sign where a planet is weakest</term> and the other (the second one) is devoid of dignities such as being in its own sign <note>The "five dignities" are: own sign, exaltation, term (hadda), decan (drekkana), and the one-ninth division (navamsha)</note>—meaning it is not in its own house, its exaltation, its own **Hadda** <term>Egyptian 'terms' or bounds</term>, its own **Drekkana** <term>ten-degree decan</term>, or its own **Navamsha** <term>one-ninth divisional chart</term>, nor is it in its debilitation or a literal enemy's sign—it is essentially in a state of neutral or "average" authority. In this case, if the **Moon**, possessing average authority itself, forms a Mutthashila with them, then this too is called **Kamboola** yoga. ॥ 53 ॥

Next, the author explains the result of this yoga with an analogy. **"In that case..."** <note>Refers to Verse 54</note> In such a Kamboola yoga, one should understand that the outcome of the work will be minimal. An analogy is provided: just as a man of one caste seeking wealth from a man of a different caste <note>original: "anyajati"</note> receives only a small amount of money—this is the implied meaning—so too do the poets <note>original: "kavayah," meaning scholars or wise astrologers</note> understand this Kamboola. This means that when one asks for something from one's own kin, they may give much out of a sense of duty or shame, but from a stranger, the gain is small. ॥ 54 ॥

<margin>Chapter 1 || 30 ||</margin>
> If there is an **Itthashala** <term>an applying aspect</term> between the lords of the Ascendant and the Work, and one is in debilitation while the other is devoid of dignities like its own sign, and the Moon here forms a **Kamboola** yoga— ॥ 53 ॥
> In that case, the realization of the work should be known as minimal. The wise know this to be like a man of one caste seeking wealth from another caste; so it is with this yoga. ॥ 54 ॥
> A planet that has no dignity in its own sign or others, neither auspicious nor even inauspicious, and is not seen by any other planet, is considered a **"Traveler of the Empty Path"** <term>Shunya-adhvaga; equivalent to the Western 'Void of Course'</term>. ॥ 55 ॥

The commentary continues: Wishing to speak of the **Gairi-Kamboola** <note>A variation of the yoga</note>, the author first defines the "Planet of the Empty Path" (Shunya Marga) as it is useful for that definition. **"Of whom..."** <note>Refers to Verse 55</note> For whichever planet there is no "auspicious authority"—meaning it is not in its own house, exaltation, Hadda, Drekkana, or Navamsha—nor is there "inauspicious authority"—meaning it is not in its sign of debilitation or an enemy's sign—and furthermore, its form is "not seen" by any other planet, whether auspicious or inauspicious... <note>The text cuts off here, continuing the definition of a Void of Course planet</note>
