<summary>This page describes a weak version of the Kamboola yoga where the primary planets lack essential dignities, resulting in minimal success. It also introduces the definition of a "Void of Course" planet, referred to here as a "Traveler of the Empty Path."</summary>
<keywords>Tajika Neelakanthi, Kamboola Yoga, Itthashala, planetary dignity, debilitation, Void of Course, Shunya-adhvaga, Sanskrit astrology, lunar aspects</keywords>

--- Page 64 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of the Kamboola yoga, specifically defining the condition of a "Void of Course" planet and a specialized configuration called Gairi Kamboola. It transitions from general rules to a specific worked example involving a question about happiness.</meta>

The "Seen Void-Traveler" or "Void Path" <note>original: *shunya-marga*</note> is now described. Regarding this matter, there is no consensus among authorities because the sage Samarasimha has not spoken of it. ॥ 55 ॥

After establishing the characteristics of the **Void Path**, the author describes the characteristics of the **Gairi Kamboola** <term>a variation of the Kamboola yoga where the Moon connects to a third planet after changing signs</term> in two verses.

### The Characteristics of the Void Path and Gairi Kamboola

**"The Lord of the Ascendant and the Matter..."** <note>Refers to the opening of Verse 66</note> When an **Itthashala** <term>an applying aspect between two planets</term> exists between the Lord of the Ascendant and the Lord of the Matter, if the Moon is a "Void-Traveler" <note>original: *shunyadhvaga*</note>, it is called a **Void Path**. 

How does a **Void Path** occur? It is said: "because it is void of positions such as Exaltation." By the phrase "exaltation etc.," it means the planet lacks any essential dignity, such as being in its own sign, its own **Drekkana** <term>a ten-degree decan</term>, its own **Navamsha** <term>a one-ninth divisional chart</term>, or even being in its sign of debilitation or an enemy’s sign—it is essentially lacking any specific standing in its current location. ॥ 56 ॥

Therefore, for such a Moon, if there is no **Mutthashila** <term>an aspect indicating the completion of an event</term> with either the Lord of the Ascendant or the Lord of the Matter, the aspect is considered "void." 

However, if such a Moon is situated at the very end of a zodiac sign and is about to enter the next sign, and if the planet that rules that next sign (either as its own home or its place of exaltation) is actually present in that sign, and the Moon forms a **Mutthashila** with that planet—then the configuration named **Gairi Kamboola** occurs. This **Gairi Kamboola** should be understood to yield results similar to the previously described types of **Kamboola**.

There is a specific variation: if the Moon enters the next sign and forms an aspect with the ruler of that sign, but that ruler itself lacks authority <note>meaning it is not in its own house or exaltation</note>, then that **Gairi Kamboola** is considered inauspicious and will grant undesirable results. ॥ 57 ॥ 

Now, the author provides an example of this **Gairi Kamboola** in three verses:

> When the Moon is a "Void-Traveler" while an **Itthashala** exists between the Lords of the Ascendant and Matter; because it lacks positions like exaltation, it has no aspect with anyone. ॥ 66 ॥ 
> 
> If the Moon enters another sign and forms an **Itthashala** with the ruler of that sign (in its own house or exaltation), this is **Gairi Kamboola**. However, if it lacks such a position, it is considered inauspicious. ॥ 67 ॥ 
> 
> For example, in the question **"Will I obtain happiness?"**, Leo is the Ascendant. The **Sun** <note>ruler of Leo</note> is in **Aries** at eight degrees. The Lord of Happiness <note>the 4th house, Scorpio</note> is **Mars**, located in **Aquarius** at twelve degrees. ॥ 68 ॥ 
> 
> There is an **Itthashala** between them <note>Sun and Mars</note>. At that time, the **Moon** is in the final degree of **Virgo**. Lacking any dignity in its own sign etc., it has no aspect with anyone. ॥ 59 ॥ <note>Note: Verse numbering in original manuscript fluctuates here.</note>

This describes a situation where the Moon is in the 30th degree of Virgo, lacking any authority such as its own sign, and currently holds no **Mutthashila** aspect with either the Sun (Lord of the Ascendant) or Mars (Lord of the Matter).

<summary>This page defines the 'Void of Course' (Shunya Marga) Moon and the Gairi Kamboola yoga, where the Moon completes a planetary connection only after moving into a new sign. It introduces a practical example of a horary question regarding the attainment of happiness.</summary>

<keywords>Shunya Marga, Void of Course, Gairi Kamboola, Itthashala, Mutthashila, Sun, Mars, Moon, Leo Ascendant, Aries, Aquarius, Virgo, horary astrology, essential dignity</keywords>

--- Page 65 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical differentiation of the Kamboola yoga variations. Specifically, it concludes the discussion on Gairi Kamboola and introduces the Khallasara and Radyoga configurations, which generally indicate failure or obstruction in astrological queries.</meta>

# The Gairi Kamboola and Khallasara Yogas

But the Moon desires to go to the sign of Libra. Libra is the sign of exaltation for Saturn. Therefore, because of its fast motion <note>original: *shighragamitva*</note>, the Moon moves to that other sign—Libra—and forms a **Mutthashila** <term>an aspect indicating the completion of an event</term> with Saturn, who is situated there. This specific planetary arrangement is known as **Gairi Kamboola**. 

Now, its result is stated: It grants gain in the form of the fulfillment of the desired task through the assistance of another, a third party.

An example chart <note>referring to the diamond-shaped diagram</note> is provided for this. In this same configuration, if Mercury or other planets were in the sign of Libra, but lacked even a single dignity <note>original: *adhikara*, referring to essential dignities like domicile or exaltation</note>—whether favorable or unfavorable—and the Moon, having gone there, formed a **Mutthashila** with Mercury or the others, then that **Gairi Kamboola** should be understood as yielding undesirable results. In this example, there is no consensus because it was not mentioned by Samarasimha <margin>original: *Samarasimhenapyuktatvat*; However, some copies suggest it *was* mentioned by Samarasimha.</margin> ॥ 58 ॥ 59 ॥ 60 ॥

Now, the author explains the characteristics of the **Khallasara** yoga. In the verse "Void," the word "by any one" should be supplied.

->If the Moon enters its exaltation or another sign and forms an **Itthashala** <term>an applying aspect</term>, it is **Gairi Kamboola**; it grants gain through help from others. ॥ 60 ॥<-
->If the Moon is a **Void-Traveler** <note>original: *shunyadhvaga*; a planet making no aspects in its current sign</note> and forms no **Itthashala** or connection <note>original: *yuti*</note> with either [of the two main planets], it is **Khallasara**. This is inauspicious and destroys the results of a **Kamboola**. ॥ 61 ॥<-
->If a planet that is set <term>combust by the sun</term>, in debilitation, in an enemy’s sign, or retrograde is weak and forms a **Mutthashila**, it is unable to carry the matter forward. Because its light is at its end or in its "face" <note>original: *mukha*; referring to its current degree-position</note>, it does not accomplish the task. ॥ 62 ॥<-

<margin>Or because of the lack of logic, it destroys the result of the Kamboola yoga. ॥ 61 ॥</margin> A **Mutthashila** yoga between the Lord of the Ascendant <note>representing the querent</note> and the Lord of the Matter <note>representing the object of the question</note> is indeed expected. In that context, according to the previously mentioned characteristics, the Moon is a "Void-Traveler" on a void path. Such a Moon does not possess a **Mutthashila** yoga with either the Lord of the Ascendant or the Lord of the Matter. Alternatively, if such a Moon does not even have a connection <note>original: *vayu* or *yuti*</note> with those two, the yoga is known as **Khallasara**. This is not auspicious. In what way? It "destroys the result of the Kamboola." When there is a **Mutthashila** or just a simple yoga, the **Kamboola** is fulfilled. But here, due to the lack of a **Mutthashila** with anyone, it destroys the result. ॥ 61 ॥

Now the characteristics of the **Radyoga** are explained. "Set" etc.: If a planet is set <term>invisible due to the Sun's proximity</term>, or in its sign of debilitation, or in the house of its enemy, or is retrograde, or has "diminished light"—meaning it is devoid of brilliance—such a planet is considered "weak." If a weak planet forms a **Mutthashila** with any lord of a house, that planet, whether at the "face" <note>beginning of a sign</note> or the end, is not a fulfiller of that house's task...

Gairi-kamboola-pa... <note>Fragment of the yoga name</note>

<summary>This page defines the Gairi Kamboola yoga, where the Moon completes a task via a third planet in its next sign, and the Khallasara and Radyogas, which represent failed outcomes due to a weak Moon or debilitated planets.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of Tajika astrology, specifically focusing on the outcomes of the Rad yoga and introducing the configurations for the Dushphalikuttha and Dutthotthadivira yogas. These yogas describe how the strength and position of planets determine whether a goal is achieved and who provides the necessary assistance.</meta>

...does not occur. This is because this planet is not capable or powerful enough to bring forth great splendor. ॥ 62 ॥ 

Now, the author explains the ripening of results for such a weak planet known as **Rad**, depending on its specific position in the houses and the timing:

"Situated in an angle" <term>Kendra: the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses, which are the most powerful positions in a chart</term>: If the Lord of the Task <note>the planet representing the object of the query</note> is a weak planet and is situated in an angle, and it joins <term>Mutthashila: an applying astrological aspect</term> with the Lord of the Ascendant <note>the planet representing the person asking the question</note> which has moved to a cadent house <term>Apoklima: the 3rd, 6th, 9th, or 12th houses, considered weak positions</term>, then the specific task will first begin to manifest but will eventually fail or be destroyed in the end. 

If, however, a planet situated in a cadent house joins with a planet situated in an angular house, then it should be said that the task first meets with failure, but is later accomplished at the very end. ॥ 63 ॥

Now, the author explains the characteristics of the **Dushphalikuttha** yoga:

"Slow...": If a slow-moving planet <note>such as Saturn or Jupiter</note> is situated in its own sign, its sign of exaltation, or its own "dignities" <note>original: *svabhoccādi-pada*; referring to the five essential dignities including its own decan or division</note>, and it forms an **Itthashala** <term>an aspect indicating the connection of two planets</term> with a fast-moving planet <note>such as the Moon or Mercury</note> that lacks its own sign or exaltation, then it should be said that the desired task will still be accomplished. This holds true provided the fast-moving planet is not in a "powerless position," such as being retrograde <term>Vakra: appearing to move backward, which weakens a planet's influence</term> or being "combust" by the Sun as previously described. ॥ 64 ॥ 

Now, the author explains the **Dutthotthadivira** yoga:

"Devoid of strength...": When both the Lord of the Ascendant and the Lord of the Task are "devoid of strength"—meaning they are weak because they are in their signs of fall, are retrograde, or are in the houses of enemies—and if one of these two forms a **Mutthashila** with a third planet that is well-placed in its own sign or exaltation, then the wise <note>original: *santah*; literally "the good" or "the experts"</note> desire the task to be accomplished through the assistance of that third party <note>a helper or intermediary</note>. 

Alternatively, if any two other planets are strong (being in their own signs or exaltations) and they join with either the Lord of the Ascendant or the Lord of the Task, the wise also declare that the task will be achieved through the help of others. ॥ 65 ॥

> ->A planet in an angle joining one in a cadent house: the task begins but fails at the end. If one in a cadent house joins one in an angle: it fails first but succeeds in the end. ॥ 63 ॥<-
> ->If a slow planet is in its own sign or exaltation and forms an aspect with a fast planet lacking such dignity, the task still occurs—unless the fast one is weak through retrogradation. ॥ 64 ॥<-
> ->When the Ascendant and Task lords are weak, if one joins a third planet in its own sign, the wise say the task is won through another's help. If two other strong planets join one of the lords, help from others still brings success. ॥ 65 ॥<-

<summary>This page details the outcomes of specific Tajika astrological yogas (Rad, Dushphalikuttha, and Dutthotthadivira), emphasizing how planetary strength and house placement determine if a task succeeds or fails, and whether success requires outside help.</summary>
<keywords>Rad yoga, Dushphalikuttha yoga, Dutthotthadivira yoga, Kendra (Angle), Apoklima (Cadent), Mutthashila, Itthashala, planetary strength, assistance from others</keywords>

--- Page 67 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of Tajika astrology from the 'Tajika Neelakanthi' by Neelakantha Bhatta. Following the discussion of the Rad and Dushphalikuttha yogas, it now defines the Tambira yoga—centered on the transfer of planetary influence—and provides a comprehensive definition of what constitutes a 'strong planet' (Kuttha) according to the Persian-influenced Tajika tradition.</meta>

<margin>
Tajika Neelakanthi
|| 32 ||
</margin>

Now, the author explains the characteristics of the **Tambira** yoga. He says "Strong..." and so on. When a **Mutthashila** <term>an applying aspect where a faster planet approaches a slower one</term> does not occur between the Lord of the Ascendant <note>the planet ruling the sign rising on the eastern horizon</note> and the Lord of the Task <note>the planet representing the object of the query</note>, but there is a certain strong planet situated at the very end of a sign—specifically residing in the 29th degree <gloss>the final portion of the 30 degrees of a zodiac sign</gloss>—and it is about to enter the next sign, it gives its great splendor through its **orbs of light** <term>diptansha: the range of degrees within which a planet’s influence is active</term> to another planet already residing in that next sign. This configuration is called **Tambira** yoga. If the planet that has entered the next sign is endowed with strength by being in its own house and so on, then this yoga becomes a "doer of the task," meaning it fulfills the desired outcome. ॥ 66 ॥

Now, the author explains the characteristics of **Kuttha**. Here, the word **Kuttha** is used in the **Yavana language** <note>original: *yavanabhāṣayā*; referring to the Persian-Arabic astrological tradition from which Tajika astrology is derived</note> to mean a strong planet. There are many types of strength; the author explains some of these types in the first verse. "In the Ascendant..." and so on. The connection here is that a planet is "strong." Among all positions, a planet situated in the Ascendant <note>the 1st house</note> is the strongest. In its absence, a planet in an **angle** <term>Kendra: the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses</term> is strong.

<margin>
Chapter 1
|| 32 ||
</margin>

->A strong planet at the end of a sign, about to enter the next sign, gives its splendor through its orbs of light to another; this is **Tambira**, which accomplishes the task for the Lords of the Ascendant and the Task. ॥ 66 ॥<-

->In the Ascendant, or in an angle, or near it, or aspecting the Ascendant; in its own house, exaltation, decan, **Musallaha**, or its own **Hadda**; a planet that is of moderate speed and not fast is considered strong. ॥ 67 ॥<-

However, a planet in the other angles is of lesser strength compared to one in the Ascendant. In the absence of that, a planet is strong if it is "near the angle," meaning in the **succedent houses** <term>Panaphara: the 2nd, 5th, 8th, and 11th houses</term>. These are of lesser strength than the angles. In the absence of that, a planet is strong if it is "aspecting the Ascendant" <note>original: *vilagnadarśī*</note>, meaning it is situated in the 3rd or 9th houses. 

Alternatively, the phrase "near it or aspecting the Ascendant" can be taken as a single qualification. It means a planet is strong if it is situated in the succedent or **cadent houses** <term>Apoklima: the 3rd, 6th, 9th, and 12th houses</term> while also aspecting the Ascendant—specifically those in the 3rd, 5th, 9th, or 11th houses. By the word "near," the succedent and cadent houses are included, excluding the 6th, 8th, and 12th <note>traditionally considered the 'dusthana' or difficult houses</note>. Some say that even in the 6th house and others, a planet is somewhat strong if it possesses a mathematically calculated aspect. 

Furthermore, a planet is strong when residing in its own house, its sign of exaltation, its **decan** <term>Drekkana: a 10-degree division of a sign</term>, its **Musallaha** <note>a Tajika dignity similar to the Greek 'triplicity'</note>, its **Navansha** <term>a 3.33-degree division; one-ninth of a sign</term>, or its own **Hadda** <term>Hrida: a Persian-derived system of planetary 'bounds' or 'terms'</term>. Likewise, a planet is strong if it has "moderate motion," meaning its true daily motion is equal to the average motion defined in texts like the *Nandakshabhujagarave*. Also, a planet is strong when it is "not fast" <note>original: *aśīghro*</note>, meaning its motion is slower than its average speed. ॥ 67 ॥

<summary>This page defines the Tambira yoga, where a planet at the end of a sign transfers its influence to a planet in the next sign to ensure success, and provides a detailed hierarchy of planetary strength (Kuttha) based on house position, zodiacal dignities, and speed.</summary>
<keywords>Tambira yoga, Kuttha, planetary strength, Tajika astrology, Mutthashila, Kendra, Panaphara, Apoklima, Hadda, Musallaha, planetary motion</keywords>

--- Page 68 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical classification of planetary strengths (Kuttha) within the Tajika system of astrology. Following the previous page's introduction of the term 'Kuttha' as a loanword from the Persian tradition meaning 'strength,' the text now enumerates the specific conditions—astronomical, temporal, and zodiacal—that render a planet powerful enough to fulfill the 'task' or query of the seeker.</meta>

Now, the author explains other types of planetary strength. He begins with: "A planet that has risen..."

A planet that has **risen** <term>kritodaya: having emerged from the Sun's rays and thus visible</term> is strong. Similarly, a planet in **direct motion** <term>margi: moving forward through the zodiac rather than appearing to move backward or 'retrograde'</term> is strong. Likewise, one that is joined with or **aspected** <note>the 'glance' or mathematical relationship between planets</note> by a **benefic planet** <note>typically Jupiter, Venus, or a well-placed Mercury and Moon</note> is strong. 

Furthermore, a planet is strong if it is not overtaken or destroyed by the **Kshuta** aspect <note>original: *kṣuta*; a specific technical aspect in Tajika astrology involving the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses</note> of a **cruel planet** <note>a malefic planet like Saturn or Mars</note>, and if it is not physically joined with such a cruel planet.

Having thus stated general planetary strength, he now speaks of **temporal strength** <term>samayabala: strength derived from the time of day or night</term>, saying: "In the evening, Venus, the Moon, and Mars..." This connects to the following verse: ॥ 68 ॥

### ->A planet that has risen, is in direct motion, and is joined or aspected by a benefic is strong; so is one not overtaken by the 'Kshuta' aspect of a cruel planet, nor joined with one. Venus, the Moon, and Mars are strong in the evening. ॥ 68 ॥<-

### ->Jupiter and Saturn are strong in the later part of the night; the male planets are powerful during the day. The others are strong at night, especially when not in the Sun's division and when placed in fixed signs. ॥ 69 ॥<-

Now he explains this further. "When..." and so on. **Venus, the Moon, and Mars** are strong when they rise during the **evening twilight**. **Jupiter and Saturn** are strong during the later part of the night, specifically the period following midnight. Similarly, the **male planets**—namely the **Sun, Mars, and Jupiter**—are strong during the **daytime**. The other planets—**the Moon, Mercury, Venus, and Saturn**—are strongest during the **night**.

Additionally, planets are strong when they are "not in the same division" as the **Sun** <note>original: *inasya naikabhāgasthitāḥ*</note>. This means that if the planets are not in the exact same **Navamsha** <term>a one-ninth division of a zodiac sign</term> where the Sun is currently located, they possess strength. 

Therefore, the reading "situated in a different division from the Sun" is the most correct. There is an alternative reading that says "situated in the same division as the Sun," but this is refuted. For instance, in the works of **Samarasimha** <note>a famous 13th-century authority on Tajika astrology</note>, some see the phrase "in the same division" and interpret it to mean being in the same sign or division as the Sun. However, that cannot be correct, because a planet in the same degree as the Sun would be **combust** <note>hidden by the Sun's glare</note>, and no text describes a combust planet as strong. On the contrary, earlier in this very book, it is stated that such a planet is weak. Therefore, the reading "not in the same division as the Sun" is the logical one. 

Finally, planets situated in **fixed signs** <term>sthira rashi: Taurus, Leo, Scorpio, and Aquarius</term> are to be understood as being endowed with strength. ॥ 69 ॥

<summary>This page details the specific conditions for planetary strength (Kuttha) in Tajika astrology, including direct motion, being aspected by benefics, and temporal factors like day/night strength. It specifically argues for a textual correction regarding planetary strength relative to the Sun's position to avoid the weakness of combustion.</summary>
<keywords>Tajika Neelakanthi, planetary strength, Kuttha, temporal strength, fixed signs, Samarasimha, Navamsha, combustion, malefic aspects</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical classification of planetary conditions in the Tajika system, specifically moving from types of strength to the definitions of planetary weakness. It maintains the structure of citing a root verse (sutra) followed by a detailed commentary explaining the Sanskrit and technical Persian-derived terminology.</meta>

<sig>Manuscript Page 1</sig>

<margin>Tajika Nilakanthi</margin>
<margin>॥ 33 ॥</margin>

Now, the author describes other types of planetary strength. Regarding "female" planets: female planets are considered **strong** <term>valinaḥ: possessing strength or 'bala'</term> when they are located in the six signs starting from the **fourth house** <note>original: *caturthāt*; the fourth house of a horoscope</note>. This means they are strong while situated between the fourth and the ninth houses.

**Male planets** are strong when they are located in the six signs starting from the **tenth house** <term>viyad-bhāva: the 'sky-house' or 10th house</term>. This means they are strong while situated between the tenth and the third houses. 

Furthermore, male planets are most powerful when placed in **odd signs** <term>ojabhagāḥ: odd-numbered signs like Aries or Gemini</term>. Conversely, female planets are strong when placed in **even signs** <note>original: *same*; even-numbered signs like Taurus or Cancer</note>. After carefully considering these various distinctions in strength and identifying which planet has the most auspicious qualities, one should declare the resulting prediction. ॥ 70 ॥

### ->Female planets are strong from the fourth house, and male planets from the tenth, across six signs. Male planets are strong in odd signs, others in even. Having weighed these differences, one should speak the results. ॥ 70 ॥<-

## Definition of Durapha (Weakness)

Now the author explains the characteristics of **Durapha** <term>durapha: a Tajika term derived from the Arabic 'du'f', meaning weakness</term>. In the **Yavana language** <note>the language of the 'foreigners' or 'Greeks', referring here to the Perso-Arabic astrological tradition</note>, the word *Durapha* signifies "powerlessness" or "debility." This condition is of many types. The author describes several of these types in the following verse:

> **If a planet is in the sixth, eighth, or twelfth house from the Ascendant; if it is not in direct motion, is in an enemy’s sign, or its sign of debilitation; if it is turning retrograde or is joined with cruel planets; if it is combust, or if it forms a 'Muthashila' aspect with planets that are cruel, debilitated, or in enemy signs; if it is aspected by the 'Kshuta' glance of a cruel planet; if it is seeking an 'Itthashala' with planets in the twelfth, sixth, or eighth houses; or if it resides in the tail or mouth of Rahu—then that planet is considered weak. ॥ 71 ॥**

<margin>Manuscript Page 1</margin>
<margin>॥ 33 ॥</margin>

Regarding the phrase "from the Ascendant": here it refers to the condition of being **weak** <note>original: *nirbala*</note>. Any planet becomes weak if it is situated in the **sixth, eighth, or the last (twelfth) house** from the **Ascendant** <term>lagna: the rising sign at the time of the month, day, or query</term>.

Similarly:
*   A planet is weak if it is **not direct** <term>anṛju: specifically meaning retrograde motion</term>.
*   A planet is weak if it is in an **enemy's house** <note>original: *arigṛhagaḥ*</note>.
*   A planet is weak if it is in its **sign of debilitation** <note>original: *nīcagaḥ*</note>.
*   A planet is weak if it is **turning retrograde** <note>original: *vakragāmī*</note>.
*   A planet is weak if it is **joined with cruel planets** <note>malefics like Saturn or Mars</note>.
*   A planet is weak if it is **combust** <term>astagaḥ: hidden by the Sun's light</term>.
*   A planet is weak if it forms a **Muthashila** <term>muṭhaśila: a technical aspect of application between two planets</term> with a cruel planet, or with a planet in its sign of debilitation or an enemy’s sign.
*   A planet is weak if it is **aspected** <note>the 'glance' or mathematical relationship</note> by a cruel planet via the **Kshuta-drishti** <note>a specific technical aspect involving the 4th, 10th, and 7th houses</note>.
*   A planet is weak if it is located in the **twelfth, sixth, or eighth houses**...

--- Page 70 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical classification of planetary conditions in the Tajika system, transitioning from the general definitions of strength to the specific conditions that render a planet "nirbala" or weak. It specifically addresses the "Durapha" (weakness) states, including the influence of Rahu and the specific debilities of the Moon.</meta>

<header>Collection of 81 Year Old Dina Nath Raina, Jammu. Retired Priest.</header>

If a planet desires to form an **Itthashala** <term>itthashala: a favorable aspect where a faster planet approaches a slower one</term> or while it is forming a **Muthashila** <term>muthashila: another term for the Itthashala aspect in Tajika astrology</term>, it is considered **weak** <note>original: "nirbala"</note>. Likewise, a planet suffers from **Nagabhaga** <note>original: "nagabhaga"; literally 'mountain-part', here referring to being in the 7th house from its own sign</note> when it is in the seventh house from its own home. For example, if the sign occupied by a planet is the seventh from its own ruling sign, it is understood to be weak. Furthermore, a planet is weak when it resides in the "Tail" or "Mouth" of **Rahu** <note>the North Node of the Moon</note>. The "Tail" refers to the degrees already consumed by Rahu, and the "Mouth" refers to the degrees Rahu is about to consume. A planet positioned there is powerless. ॥ 71 ॥ 

Now, the author describes other types of inauspicious conditions. Regarding "Not aspecting": A planet that does not "see" or aspect the **Ascendant** <term>tanu-lagna: the first house, representing the body or the self</term> is weak. 

Similarly, a planet is weak if it is situated in the **descending portion** <note>original: "astabhāga"; the setting or 7th house region</note>. Specifically, if a planet is in the seventh **Navamsha** <term>navamsha: a one-ninth divisional chart</term> from the Navamsha occupied by the Sun, it is weak. This was also stated by **Samarasimha** <note>a famous 13th-century authority on Tajika astrology</note>, who noted: "Situated in the setting portion of the Sun." Additionally, a planet is weak when it is "void":

> **One who does not aspect the Ascendant, resides in the setting portion, and is void of dignities such as exaltation, is weak.**
> **Joined with a harsh Saraphina aspect and lacking in power, such a planet is not capable of accomplishing the intended task. ॥ 72 ॥**

> **The Moon is lost when in the twelfth house from the Sun, or in the first half of Scorpio, and especially at the end of Libra.**
> **If its form is not seen by the Lord of its sign, nor seen by any other planets, it is known as "Shunyamarga" and is deficient in strength. ॥ 73 ॥**

A planet is weak when it is "void" <note>original: "shūnya"</note>, meaning it lacks its **essential dignities** <note>original: "svoccādi-padaiḥ"</note> such as its own house, its sign of exaltation, its **Hadda** <term>hadda: a divisional 'limit' or 'term' similar to Egyptian bounds</term>, its **Drekkana** <term>drekkana: a ten-degree decanate division</term>, or its own Navamsha. Furthermore, if a planet forms a **Saraphina** <term>saraphina: a separating aspect, often considered unfavorable for immediate results</term> with a malefic planet, it will not possess strength. Because of this, the planet is not "Vibhu"—it is not capable or powerful enough to bring the desired work to fruition. ॥ 72 ॥ 

Having thus spoken generally about the weaknesses <note>original: "durappha", from Persian 'du'af'</note> of all planets, the author now describes the specific, extreme weaknesses of the **Moon**: 

Regarding the Moon: If the Moon is in the twelfth house from the sign occupied by the Sun, it is weak. Likewise, it is "lost" or produces inauspicious results and is weak when in the first half of **Scorpio** <note>original: "vṛścika"</note>. It is especially "lost" or weak in the latter half of **Libra** <note>original: "tulā"</note>. Furthermore, the Moon is weak if its "form" is not seen (aspected) by the Lord of the sign in which the Moon resides, nor is it aspected by any other planets. Finally, if the Moon is characterized by the previously mentioned "Void Path" <term>shūnyamārga: "void of course," where the Moon makes no further aspects before leaving its sign</term>, it should be known as weak and "deficient by a quarter" <note>original: "pādonah"</note>. ॥ 73 ॥

<meta>This page continues the technical classification of planetary conditions in the Tajika system, specifically focusing on the "Durapha" (weakness) condition of the Moon. It concludes the section on the sixteen yogas and begins the discussion of the "Harsha-sthanas" or places of joy for the planets.</meta>

<page-num>34</page-num>

<margin>Tajika Nilakanthi
॥ 34 ॥</margin>

<margin>Chapter
1
॥ 34 ॥</margin>

Now, he speaks of another **Durapha** <term>durapha: a condition of planetary weakness or inauspiciousness in Tajika astrology</term> regarding the Moon. **"Waning"** <note>original: "kshina"</note>: The word "Moon" is implied here. A "waning" Moon is defined as being from the eighth day of the dark fortnight <note>the period of the waning moon</note> until the eighth day of the bright fortnight <note>the period of the waxing moon</note>. Some say it is from the eleventh day of the dark fortnight until the New Moon. Likewise, if it is at the "end of a sign" <note>original: "bhante"</note>—meaning it resides in the final degrees of a zodiac sign—it is **not auspicious**; it gives bad results and is "powerless" <note>original: "nirbala"</note>. In this manner, the Moon should be considered both at the time of birth and in a **horary question** <term>prashna-lagna: the ascendant calculated for the moment a question is asked</term>.

Furthermore, if in the **bright fortnight** <note>original: "shukte"</note>, **Mars** <note>original: "bhauma"</note>, or in the **dark fortnight**, the **son of the Sun** <note>original: "arkasunu"; refers to Saturn</note>, looks at the Moon with a **"mean glance"** <note>original: "kshud-drishtya"; refers to specific aspects like the 4th, 10th, 7th, or 1st house</note>, then that Moon is **not auspicious** and will not provide good results. ॥ 74 ॥

Now, he describes the reduction of the defect <note>original: "dosha"</note> even for a Moon in Durapha through a specific combination. **"In the bright..."**: In the bright fortnight, during the day, if the **son of the Sun** (Saturn) is situated in a **masculine sign** <note>original: "nṛ-gṛha-ga"; literally 'man-house-goer', referring to Aries, Gemini, Leo, etc.</note> and looks at the Moon <note>original: "shashanka"</note>—

->A Moon at the end of a sign is not auspicious at the time of birth or a query; the Moon should be considered thus. If in the bright fortnight Mars, or in the dark fortnight Saturn, looks at the Moon with a mean glance, it is not auspicious. ॥ 74 ॥ If in the bright fortnight during the day Saturn, in a masculine sign, looks at the Moon; or in the dark fortnight at night Mars, in an even sign, looks at it—this spreads a reduction of the defect; otherwise, it is abundant. This should be inferred by the wise in a query or at birth. ॥ 75 ॥ Thus conclude the sixteen yogas.<-

—then this spreads a **reduction** or lessening of the **Durapha defect**. Otherwise, if Saturn does not look at it, it spreads an **abundance** or increase of the Durapha defect. Likewise, in the **dark fortnight**, at **night**, if **Mars** <note>original: "kuja"</note> is situated in an **even sign** <note>original: "samarksha-gata"; referring to Taurus, Cancer, Virgo, etc.</note> and looks at the Moon, it spreads a reduction of the Durapha defect. If Mars does not look, it spreads an abundance of the defect. All this should be **inferred** or contemplated by the wise in the ascendant of a query or in the birth chart. This means it should also be considered in the context of the **Harsha-labha** <note>attainment of joy</note> and other conditions. Thus, the **sixteen yogas** are completed. ॥ 77 ॥

Now, he describes the four **Harsha-sthanas** <term>harsha-sthana: 'place of joy', specific house positions where a planet becomes exceptionally strong or 'happy'</term> for all the planets. **"Nanda, Tri, Shat..."**: Here, the first "place of joy" is described. Starting from the **Sun** <note>original: "ina"</note>, the joyful positions for all planets occur in the **Nanda** <gloss>9</gloss>, **Tri** <gloss>3</gloss>, **Shat** <gloss>6</gloss>, **Lagna** <gloss>1</gloss>, **Bhava** <gloss>11</gloss>, **Putra** <gloss>5</gloss>, and **Vyaya** <gloss>12</gloss> houses respectively, beginning from the Ascendant. Furthermore, their own signs and exaltation signs are also considered. These apply to women and men, during night and day, in that order. ॥ 76 ॥

This is explained as follows: From the Ascendant—

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of Tajika astrology, moving from the Moon’s weaknesses to the "Places of Joy" (Harsha Sthanas) for all planets. It details the specific conditions—position, dignity, gender-alignment, and time—that grant a planet strength and "happiness."</meta>

<header>Collection of 81 Year Old Dina Nath Raina, Jammu. Retired Priest.</header>

The **Sun** in the **9th house** is exalted <note>original: "darpitah"; literally 'proud' or 'boastful'</note>. The **Moon** in the **3rd house**. **Mars** in the **6th house** <note>original: "prishthastha"; literally 'situated at the back'</note>. **Mercury** in the **1st house** <note>original: "lagnastha"; the Ascendant</note>. **Jupiter** in the **11th house**. **Venus** in the **5th house**. **Saturn** in the **12th house** <note>original: "vyayastha"</note>. These are the conditions that grant **Joy** <term>Harsha: a state of planetary strength where the planet is said to be "happy" and thus more beneficial</term>.

Now, the **second place of Joy** is described: **"Own sign or exaltation."** When a planet is in its own home <note>the zodiac sign it rules</note> or in its sign of exaltation, it is considered **Joyful**. The strength in one's own house is the same as in exaltation; in both cases, the strength is calculated as five **Vishopakas** <term>Vishopaka: a traditional unit of astrological strength, where 20 units represent a full point of power</term>.

Now, the **third place of Joy** is described: **"The Three-Sign groups."** From the **Ascendant** <note>original: "Lagna"</note>, groups of three zodiac signs in sequence are favorable for **female planets** <note>original: "stri-graha"</note> and **male planets** <note>original: "purusha-graha"</note>. This is the meaning:
*   In the **2nd and 3rd signs** from the Ascendant, female planets are joyful. 
*   In the next three—the **4th, 5th, and 6th houses**—male planets are joyful. 
*   Again, in the **7th, 8th, and 9th houses**, female planets are joyful. 
*   Finally, in the **10th, 11th, and 12th houses**, male planets are joyful.

Now, the **fourth place of Joy** is described: **"Night and Day."** This refers to the joy of these female and male planets based on the time of day. This is the meaning: If the **Year-Entrance** <term>Varshapravesha: the moment the Sun returns to its birth position, used to calculate a yearly horoscope</term> occurs at **night**, female planets are joyful. If the Year-Entrance occurs during the **day**, male planets are joyful. 

Planets situated in these four places become **strong**. In this **Tajika system** <term>Tajika: a branch of Indian astrology with Persian/Arabic influence, distinct from the older Parashari system</term>, the **Moon, Saturn, Venus, and Mercury** are considered **feminine** <note>note: in other systems, Saturn and Mercury are often neutral or masculine</note>. The **Sun, Mars, and Jupiter** are **masculine**. There are no "neuter" planets here. Here, **20 Vishopakas** is the maximum strength. Because there are four 

| | Sun | Moon | Mars | Merc. | Jup. | Ven. | Sat. |
| :--- | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: |
| **Position** | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| **Exalt/Own** | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 5 | 0 | 5 |
| **Sign Group** | 5 | 0 | 0 | 5 | 0 | 5 | 5 |
| **Day/Night** | 5 | 0 | 5 | 0 | 5 | 0 | 0 |
| **Total Joy:** | 10 | 0 | 5 | 5 | 10 | 5 | 10 |

places of Joy, the total is divided by four, resulting in **5 Vishopakas** for each. Thus, for each of the four categories, a strength of 5 Vishopakas should be assigned. 

**Here is an example:** 
The Sun and other planets are not in the houses mentioned in the first Joy (the 9th, etc.). Therefore, the **first Joy** is not obtained. Here, **Jupiter** is in its own house and **Saturn** is in its exaltation; therefore, the **second Joy** is obtained, and five Vishopakas are assigned to them. **Saturn, Venus, and Mercury** (the female planets) are in the 7th, 8th, or 9th houses, and the **Sun** (a male planet) is in the 10th house; therefore, they receive the **third Joy** and five Vishopakas. In this chart, the Year-Entrance is during the **day**; therefore, the male planets (**Sun, Mars, and Jupiter**) receive the **fourth Joy** and five Vishopakas. 

In this way, the places of joy for all planets and their respective strengths and totals are written. ॥ 76 ॥

The ornament of the lineage of Garga was Chintamani, a master of calculation. His son was Ananta, a man of infinite intellect, who composed the *Janu Paddhati* to dispel the views of the wicked. His son is indeed the wise Nilakantha, who, by the command of the learned Shiva, has explained the sixteen yogas and the states of Joy <note>original: "Harsha-bhani"</note> in this *Samjna Viveka* <note>a section of the larger work, the Tajika Nilakanthi</note>. — 77

## || 4 || Now, the method for calculating the Fortunate Point is described: "By the Sun..." ||

If the birth <term>Varshapravesha</term><note>The solar return or the entry into a new year</note> occurs during the **day**, the position of the **Sun** should be subtracted from the **Moon**. If the birth occurs at **night**, the **Moon** should be subtracted from the **Sun**. To this result, the **Ascendant** <note>Lagna</note> is added. The resulting figure, consisting of signs and degrees, is known as the **Fortunate Point** <note>Punya Sahama</note>.

Now, a special refinement for calculating all sensitive points <note>Sahamas</note> is explained: "Between the subtrahend..." If the Ascendant sign does not fall between the sign of the planet being subtracted <note>shodhya</note> and the sign of the planet it is being subtracted from <note>shuddhyashraya</note>, then **one additional sign** (30 degrees) must be added to the calculated point.

To clarify: The planet that is being subtracted (made lesser) is the 'subtrahend.' The 'source of purification' is the planet from which the subtraction is made. If the Ascendant does not lie between these two, you must add one sign to the resulting point. 

If, however, the Ascendant *does* fall between the subtrahend and the source planet, then one sign is *not* to be added. This rule applies to all the sensitive points mentioned hereafter. As stated later: "from the point of fortune, Mars..."—this indicates the various houses and planetary owners which will be used as the Ascendant or subtrahends in further calculations.

43. **Valour** <note>Shaurya</note>, 44. **Means** <note>Upaya</note>, 45. **Poverty** <note>Daridratva</note>, 46. **Greatness** <note>Gurutam</note>, 47. **Misfortune** <note>Dupatha</note>, 48. **Bondage** <note>Bandhanam</note>, 49. **Daughter** <note>Duhita</note>, 50. **Mother-in-law** <note>Shvashru</note>.

> These are the fifty sensitive points. || 4 || During the day, add the Ascendant to the Moon minus the Sun; at night, add the Ascendant to the Sun minus the Moon to find the Fortunate Point. If the Ascendant is not between the subtrahend and the source planet, add one sign. || 6 ||

| Purified Planets | | | | | | | |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Sun** | **Moon** | **Mars** | **Merc.** | **Jup.** | **Ven.** | **Sat.** | **Asc.** |
| 9 | 5 | 8 | 8 | 8 | 7 | 6 | 0 |
| 7 | 22 | 14 | 12 | 29 | 24 | 6 | 18 |
| 30 | 9 | 32 | 16 | 35 | 31 | 46 | 3 |
| 6 | 47 | 36 | 8 | 13 | 48 | 44 | 54 |

**Calculation Example:**
**Sun:** 9s 7° 30' 6" subtracted from **Moon:** 5s 22° 9' 47". 
The remainder is: 8s 14° 39' 41". 
Adding the **Ascendant:** 0s 18° 3' 54", 
the resulting **Fortunate Point** <note>Punya Sahama</note> is: 9s 24° 33' 35". || 5 ||

### Now, the method for calculating the Lots of Teacher, Knowledge, and Fame is described:

**"Opposite..."** means that the calculation for these two lots—the **Lot of the Teacher** <note>Guru Sahama</note> and the **Lot of Knowledge** <note>Vidya Sahama</note>—is performed in the reverse manner of the Lot of Fortune <note>Punya Sahama</note>. It is as follows:

During the **day**, the **Moon** should be subtracted from the **Sun**, and the result added to the **Ascendant** <note>Lagna</note>. If the **Yearly Entrance** <note>Varshapravesha</note> occurs at **night**, then the **Sun** should be subtracted from the **Moon**, and the result added to the **Ascendant**. These results constitute the Lots of the Teacher and Knowledge. 

For the **Lot of Fame** <note>Yashas Sahama</note>: During the **day**, **Jupiter** <note>Surejya</note> should be subtracted from the **Lot of Fortune**. At **night**, it is the reverse: the **Lot of Fortune** should be subtracted from **Jupiter**. As before, the result is added to the **Ascendant**. The refinement involving the "addition of one sign" <note>Saikata; adding 30 degrees if the Ascendant does not fall between the two points</note> must be applied to all these Lots.

**Example Calculation:**
The **Moon** (7 signs, 22 degrees, 9 minutes, 47 seconds) is subtracted from the **Sun** (9s 7° 30' 6"). The remainder is 3s 15° 20' 19". When the **Ascendant** (4s 3° 24' 13") is added, and after applying the "plus one sign" rule, the result is the **Lot of the Teacher**: 5s 3° 24' 13". This same value serves as the **Lot of Knowledge**. 

To find the **Lot of Fame**: The **Lot of Fortune** (9s 2° 43' 35") is subtracted from **Jupiter** (8s 29° 35' 13"). The remainder is 11s 16° 55' 38". When added to the **Ascendant**, the **Lot of Fame** becomes 0s 24° 55' 32". || 6 ||

> The calculation for the Teacher and Knowledge points is the reverse of the previous; subtract Jupiter from the Lot of Fortune. || In the day, it is reversed at night; following the previous rules, the wise call this the Lot of Fame. || 6 ||
> Subtract the Lot of Fortune from the Lot of the Teacher; at night, reverse this and add the position of Venus. || Applying the "plus one sign" rule as previously taught, the learned call this the Lot of Friends. || 7 ||
> Subtract Mars from the Lot of Fortune as described to find Greatness; at night, reverse this. || Subtract Saturn from Venus during the day, and reverse at night, for the Lot of Hope; the remainder should be calculated as stated. || 8 ||

### Now, the method for calculating the Lot of Friends is described:

**"From the Lot of Fortune..."** During the **day**, subtract the **Lot of Fortune** from the **Lot of the Teacher**. At **night**, it is reversed: subtract the **Lot of the Teacher** from the **Lot of Fortune**. Then, the position of **Venus** <note>Shukra</note> must be added. 

**Note:** In this specific calculation, do *not* add the Ascendant. Because Venus is added instead, the "plus one sign" rule <note>Saikata</note> is applied based on whether Venus falls between the subtrahend and the source point. The learned call this the **Lot of Friends** <note>Mitra Sahama</note>.

**Example:**
Subtract the **Lot of Fortune** (9s 2° 43' 35") from the **Lot of the Teacher** (5s 3° 24' 13"). The remainder (8s 0° 40' 38") is added to **Venus** (7s 24° 31' 48"). After applying the "plus one sign" rule, the **Lot of Friends** is 3s 25° 12' 26". || 7 || 

### Now, the calculation for the Lots of Greatness and Hope is described:

**"From Fortune..."** During the **day**, subtract **Mars** <note>Bhauma</note> from the **Lot of Fortune**. Then, add the **Ascendant** and apply the "plus one sign" rule as described before. This result is the **Lot of Greatness** <note>Mahatmya Sahama</note>. At **night**, this is reversed...

...the inverse of that <note>original: *vilo-mam*</note>. During the day, subtract the **Lot of Fortune** <note>Punya Sahama</note> from **Mars** <note>Bhauma</note>. This results in the **Lot of Greatness** <note>Mahatmya Sahama; signifies one's magnanimity or high status</note>.

Now, during the **day**, subtract **Venus** <note>Shukra</note> from **Saturn** <note>Shani/Manda</note>. At **night**, it is the reverse: subtract **Saturn** from **Venus**. The remainder should be added to the **Ascendant** <note>Lagna</note> according to the established rules <note>including the adjustment of adding one sign if the Ascendant falls outside the arc</note>. This results in the **Lot of Hope** <note>Asha Sahama; also known as the Lot of Desire or Ambition</note>.

**Calculation Example:**
The **Lot of Fortune** (9 signs, 24 degrees, 3 minutes, 35 seconds) is subtracted from **Mars** (8s 14° 3' 36"), leaving a remainder of 0s 9° 8' 0". When the **Ascendant** is added along with the one-sign adjustment, the result for the **Lot of Greatness** is 2s 6° 14' 53".
To find the **Lot of Hope**: **Venus** (7s 24° 3' 48") is subtracted from **Saturn** (6s 6° 46' 54"), leaving a remainder of 10s 12° 15' 6". Added to the **Ascendant**, the **Lot of Hope** is 11s 0° 19' 0".

### Now, the method for calculating the Lot of Power and the Lot of Brother is stated:

To find the **Lot of Power** <note>Samarthya Sahama; representing one's capability or strength</note>: During the day, subtract the **Lord of the Ascendant** <note>Tanupa; the planet ruling the rising sign</note> from **Mars** <note>Ara</note>. At night, it is the reverse: subtract **Mars** from the **Lord of the Ascendant**. Add this to the **Ascendant** as before. However, if **Mars** happens to be the Lord of the Ascendant, then subtract **Jupiter** <note>Jiva</note> instead; this rule applies both day and night to determine the **Lot of Power**.

->Subtract the Lord of the Ascendant from Mars, and at night reverse it; but if Mars is the Lord of the Ascendant, subtract from Jupiter always. To find the Lot of Brother, subtract Saturn from the Sun’s position as refined by previous calculations. || 9 ||<-

->By day, subtract the Moon from Jupiter; at night, reverse the order of the Sun and Moon. Following the stated method, the Lot of Respect is found. By subtracting the Sun from Saturn, and reversing it at night, the Lot of the King and Father is known. || 10 ||<-

The **Lot of Brother** <note>Bhratri Sahama</note> is calculated at all times (day or night) by subtracting **Saturn** <note>Arka</note> from the specific mathematical point <note>Dhruva</note> associated with the brother, using the same refinements as before.

**Calculation Example:**
The rule states to subtract the Lord of the Ascendant from Mars. In this case, the **Lord of the Ascendant** is Mars itself. Therefore, we subtract **Mars** (8s 14° 3' 36") from **Jupiter** (8s 29° 35' 13"). The remainder is 0s 15° 31' 37". Adding the **Ascendant** and the one-sign adjustment, the **Lot of Power** is 2s 3° 6' 31".
For the **Lot of Brother**: Subtract **Saturn** (6s 6° 46' 54") from **Jupiter** (8s 29° 35' 13"). The remainder is 2s 22° 48' 19". Adding the **Ascendant** and the one-sign adjustment, the **Lot of Brother** is 4s 10° 52' 13".

Now, the method for finding the **Lot of Respect** and the **Lot of the King and Father** is stated. During the **day**, subtract the **Moon** from **Jupiter**, ignoring the Sun's position. At **night**, subtract the **Sun** from **Jupiter** and add the **Moon**. Using the established method <note>adding one sign</note>, this becomes the **Lot of Respect** <note>Gaurava Sahama</note>.

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for calculating Sahamas (Astrological Lots). The previous page concluded with the Lot of Power and the Lot of Brother; this page moves into the Lots related to parents, children, longevity, and professional life.</meta>

<margin>Tajika Nilakanthi Page 37</margin>
If the birth or the year-entry occurs at night and the Sun and Moon are not in their required positions <note>referring to whether the planet to be subtracted is within the arc of the Ascendant</note>, then the "plus-one" rule <note>original: *saikata*; a mathematical adjustment where one full sign (30 degrees) is added to the calculation</note> should be applied. 

For a daytime year-entry, the **Lot of the Father** <note>Tata Sahama</note> and the **Lot of the King** <note>Raja Sahama</note> are found by subtracting **Saturn** <note>Shani</note> from the **Sun** <note>Arka</note>. At night, this is reversed: subtract the **Sun** from **Saturn**. Add the result to the **Ascendant** <note>Lagna</note> and apply the plus-one rule. <margin>Instruction 1</margin>

**Calculation Example:**
**Jupiter** <note>Guru</note> is at 8s 29° 35' 13". The **Moon** <note>Chandra</note> is at 5s 22° 9' 47". When the Moon is subtracted from Jupiter, the remainder is 3s 7° 25' 26". Adding the **Sun** (9s 7° 30' 6") and applying the plus-one rule, we get the **Lot of Respect** <note>Gaurava Sahama</note> at 1s 14° 55' 32". 
To find the **Lot of the King**: Subtract the **Sun** (9s 7° 30' 6") from **Saturn** (6s 6° 46' 54"). The remainder is 8s 19° 18' 48". Adding the **Ascendant** results in the **Lot of the King** at 9s 7° 22' 42". The **Lot of the Father** is the same: 9s 7° 22' 42". || 10 ||

### Now, the method for calculating the Lots of Mother, Son, Life, and Water is stated:

To find the **Lot of the Mother** <note>Matru Sahama</note>: During the **day**, subtract **Venus** <note>Sita/Shukra</note> from the **Moon** <note>Indu</note>. At **night**, it is reversed: subtract the **Moon** from **Venus**. Add this to the **Ascendant** and apply the plus-one rule. <margin>37</margin>

-> For the Mother, subtract Venus from the Moon; reverse this at night. For the Son, subtract Jupiter from the Moon during the day or night. The Lot of Life is found by subtracting Jupiter from Saturn; reverse this at night. The Lot of Water is equal to the Mother's. || 11 || <-

-> For Action, subtract Mars from Mercury; reverse this at night. The Lot of Disease is always from Mercury. For Love, subtract the Lord of the Ascendant from the Moon during the day; at night, reverse it and subtract from the Sun. || 12 || <-

Whether it is day or night, for the **Lot of the Son** <note>Putra Sahama</note>: subtract **Jupiter** <note>Ijya</note> from the **Moon**. Add the result to the **Ascendant** and apply the plus-one rule. 
Now, for the **Lot of Life** <note>Jivita Sahama</note>: during the **day**, subtract **Jupiter** from **Saturn**. At **night**, it is reversed: subtract **Saturn** from **Jupiter**. Add the result to the **Ascendant** and apply the plus-one rule. 
The **Lot of Water** <note>Ambu Sahama; representing the home, comforts, or fixed assets</note> is exactly the same as the **Lot of the Mother**.

**Calculation Example:**
**Venus** (7s 24° 31' 48") is subtracted from the **Moon** (5s 22° 9' 47"), leaving 9s 27° 37' 59". Adding the **Ascendant** gives the **Lot of the Mother** at 10s 15° 41' 53".
Subtracting the **Moon** from **Jupiter** leaves 3s 7° 25' 26". Adding the **Ascendant** and the plus-one adjustment gives the **Lot of the Son** at 4s 25° 29' 20".
Subtracting **Jupiter** from **Saturn** leaves 9s 7° 11' 41". Adding the **Ascendant** results in the **Lot of Life** at 9s 25° 15' 35".
The **Lot of Water** is the same as the Mother: 10s 15° 41' 53". || 11 ||

### Now, the method for calculating the Lots of Action, Disease, and Love is stated:

To find the **Lot of Action** <note>Karma Sahama; representing one's profession or career</note>: during the **day**, subtract **Mercury** <note>Budha/Jna</note> from **Mars** <note>Bhauma/Ara</note>. At **night**, it is reversed: subtract **Mars** from **Mercury**. Add the result to the **Ascendant** and apply the plus-one rule. Always...

<meta>This page continues the detailed mathematical instructions for calculating Sahamas (Astrological Lots) in the Tajika system. While the previous page focused on family and status (Father, King, Mother), this page moves into more personal and circumstantial Lots, including those for health, romance, strife, knowledge, and death.</meta>

<header>Collection of 81 Year Old Dina Nath Raina, Jammu. Retired Priest.</header>

Both day and night, from the <term>Ascendant</term> <note>Lagna; the rising sign</note> the <term>Moon</term> should be subtracted. If the "plus-one" rule <note>original: saikata; a mathematical adjustment where one sign is added to the result under specific conditions</note> is applied to the sum with the Ascendant, it becomes the **Lot of Disease** <note>Roga Sahama</note>. During the day, the <term>Lord of the Ascendant</term> <note>Lagnasvami</note> should be subtracted from the Moon. At night, this is reversed: subtract the Moon from the Lord of the Ascendant. Adding this result to the Ascendant and applying the plus-one rule results in the **Lot of Love** <note>Madana or Manmatha Sahama; associated with desire and the god of love</note>. If the Lord of the Ascendant is the Moon itself, then day or night, subtract the Lord of the Ascendant (the Moon) from the **Sun** <note>Arka</note>; adding this to the Ascendant with the plus-one rule results in the Lot of Love.

**Example Calculations:**
When **Mercury** <note>Budha</note> is subtracted from **Mars** <note>Bhauma</note>, the remainder is 0s 2° 16' 28". Adding the Ascendant and the "plus-one" adjustment, the **Lot of Birth Rituals** <note>Jatakarma Sahama</note> is 1s 20° 20' 22".
When the Moon is subtracted from the Ascendant, the remainder is 6s 25° 54' 7". Adding the Ascendant, the **Lot of Disease** becomes 7s 13° 58' 1".
When the Lord of the Ascendant (Mars) is subtracted from the Moon, the remainder is 9s 7° 37' 11". Adding the Ascendant, the **Lot of Love** <note>Madana Sahama</note> becomes 9s 25° 41' 5". || 12 ||

### Now, the method for bringing forth the Lots of Strife, Forgiveness, and Knowledge is stated:

Regarding the **Lot of Strife** <note>Kali Sahama</note> and **Lot of Forgiveness** <note>Kshama Sahama</note>: During the **day**, subtract Mars from **Jupiter** <note>Guru</note>. At **night**, this is reversed: subtract Jupiter from Mars as per the previous method. Adding the result to the Ascendant and applying the plus-one rule yields the Lot of Strife and the Lot of Forgiveness.

> During the day, subtract Mars from Jupiter; at night, reverse this as before for the Lots of Strife and Forgiveness. ||
> For the **Lot of Knowledge** <note>Shastra Sahama</note>, subtract **Saturn** <note>Sauri</note> from Jupiter during the day. At night, reverse this and add the position of **Mercury** <note>Jna</note> as before. || 13 ||
> Day or night, subtract the Moon from Mercury for the **Lot of Relatives** <note>Bandhu Sahama</note>. At night, this same calculation becomes the **Lot of Captivity** <note>Bandaka Sahama</note>. ||
> For the **Lot of Death** <note>Mrityu Sahama</note>, reverse this during the day. Subtract the Moon from the **Eighth House** <note>Ashtamarksha</note> and add the position of Saturn as stated. || 14 ||

During the day, subtract Saturn from Jupiter. At night, subtract Jupiter from Saturn. To this, add the position of Mercury. As before, if Mercury does not fall within the interval of the subtraction, apply the "plus-one" rule. This becomes the **Lot of Knowledge**.

**Example Calculations:**
Subtracting Mars from Jupiter, the remainder is 0s 15° 2' 37". Adding the Ascendant and the "plus-one" adjustment, the **Lot of Strife** is 2s 3° 6' 31", and the **Lot of Forgiveness** is also 2s 3° 6' 31".
Subtracting Saturn from Jupiter, the remainder is 2s 22° 48' 19". Adding Mercury, the **Lot of Knowledge** becomes 11s 5° 4' 27". || 13 ||

### Now, the method for bringing forth the Lots of Relatives, Captivity, and Death is stated:

Regarding "Day and Night": If the year-entry occurs during the day or night, subtract the Moon from Mercury. Add this to the Ascendant and apply the plus-one rule; this becomes the **Lot of Relatives**. If the year-entry occurs at night, this same calculation serves as the **Lot of Captivity**. During the day, to find the Lot of Captivity, do the reverse: subtract Mercury from the Moon and add the result to the Ascendant with the plus-one rule.
For the **Lot of Death**, regardless of day or night year-entry, subtract the Moon from the cusp of the **Eighth House**. To this, add Saturn and apply the plus-one rule as previously described.

### On the Calculation of the Lots of Foreign Lands and Wealth

**Regarding Foreign Lands:**
Always, whether the <term>Solar Return</term> <note>Varshapravesha; the chart for the beginning of the year</note> occurs by day or night, subtract the <term>Lord of the Ninth House</term> <note>Dharmeshvara; the ruler of the house of fortune and travel</note> from the Ninth House itself. As previously described, add the Ascendant and the "plus-one" adjustment; this shall be the **Lot of Foreign Lands** <note>Deshantara Sahama</note>.

**Regarding Wealth:**
Day or night, at the moment of the Solar Return, subtract the <term>Lord of Wealth</term> <note>Vittapa; the ruler of the second house</note> from the **Second House** <note>Artha Bhava</note>. As before, add the Ascendant and the "plus-one" adjustment; this shall be the **Lot of Wealth** <note>Artha Sahama</note>.

**Example:**
In the Ninth House (8s 9° 26' 38")...
<note>The text here repeats the rule: To find the "Foreign" Lot, subtract the Lord of Fortune from the Ninth House.</note>
...Day or night, subtract the Lord of Wealth from the Second House for the Wealth Lot. ॥ 15 ॥

*Subtract the Sun from Venus, then for the other, always as before;*
*Then for the other deed, subtract Saturn from the Moon, or in the night, the reverse;*
*Always calculate the Merchant's Lot using the method described for Captivity.* ॥ 16 ॥

Subtracting the Lord of the Ninth, **Jupiter** <note>Guru</note> (8s 29° 35' 13"), from the Ninth House, the remainder is 11s 9° 55' 25". Adding the Ascendant, the **Lot of Foreign Lands** becomes 1s 27° 55' 19".
From the Second House (11s 3° 49' 30"), subtract the Lord of the Second House, **Venus** <note>Shukra</note>. The remainder is 5s 19° 17' 42". Adding the Ascendant, the **Lot of Wealth** <note>Dravya Sahama</note> becomes 6s 7° 21' 36". ॥ 15 ॥

### On the Lots of Adultery, Other Deeds, and Trade

**Regarding Adultery:**
"Subtract the Sun from Venus"—Always, day or night, subtract the **Sun** <note>Arka</note> from **Venus** <note>Sita</note>. As before, add the Ascendant and apply the "plus-one" rule. This is the **Lot of Adultery** <note>Anyadara-ahvaya Sahama; literally 'the Lot named another's wife'</note>.

**Regarding Other Deeds:**
During the day, subtract Saturn from the Moon. Conversely, at night, subtract the Moon from Saturn. Add the Ascendant and the "plus-one" rule to find the **Lot of Other Deeds** <note>Anyakarma Sahama</note>.

**Regarding Trade:**
Always, day or night, this is to be calculated using the method for the "Lot of Captivity" <note>Vandaka Sahama</note>. That is: at the Solar Return, subtract Mercury from the Moon, add the Ascendant and the "plus-one" rule. This becomes the **Lot of Trade** <note>Vanik Sahama</note>.

**Example:**
When the Sun is subtracted from Venus, the remainder is 10s 17° 14' 2". Adding the Ascendant, the **Lot of Adultery** <note>Parastri Sahama</note> becomes 11s 5° 55' 36". When Saturn is subtrac... <note>The text cuts off mid-calculation for the next point.</note>

### Calculations for Success, Marriage, and Birth

Now, the author describes the methods for bringing forth the Lots of Wealth, Success in Work, and Marriage. Regarding the verse beginning with "From Saturn": During the day, subtract the <term>Sun</term> <note>Surya</note> from <term>Saturn</term> <note>Shani</note> and add the position of the ruler of the Sun's sign. At night, subtract the Moon from Saturn and add the ruler of the Moon's sign.

When the "plus-one" adjustment <note>saika; a rule in Tajika astrology where 30 degrees are added to the calculation if the Ascendant does not lie between the two points being measured</note> is applied, that position becomes the **Lot of Success in Work** <note>Karya-siddhi Sahama</note>. For the **Lot of Marriage** <note>Vivaha Sahama</note>, always—whether the <term>Solar Return</term> <note>Varshapravesha</note> occurs by day or night—subtract Saturn from <term>Venus</term> <note>Shukra</note> and apply the "plus-one" adjustment.

When Saturn is subtracted from Venus, the remainder is 11s 7° 44' 54". Adding the Ascendant and the "plus-one" adjustment results in the **Lot of Marriage** at 3s 5° 4' 8".

-> ...results in the Lot of Faith. Following the direction previously stated, the Lot of Merit is considered. Thus, the Lot named Love is described. For Strength, the Body, and for Fame, subtract Jupiter from Mars. ॥ 19 ॥ <-

**Example Calculation:**
When the mean position of <term>Mars</term> <note>original: "Bhauma Madhya"</note> is subtracted from the required point, the remainder is 2s 7° 45' 42". Now, the method for finding the **Lot of Poverty** <note>Daridra Sahama</note> and the **Lot of Greatness** <note>Guruta Sahama</note> is described:

"In reverse" <note>vamam</note>. During the day, subtract <term>Jupiter</term> <note>original: "Ijya" or "Guru"</note> from <term>Saturn</term> <note>Shani</note>. At night, subtract Saturn from Jupiter. Add the Ascendant and the "plus-one" adjustment; this becomes the **Lot of Strategy**.

At night, subtract the **Moon** <note>Chandra</note> from the **Moon's Exaltation Point** <note>Chandrocha</note>. Following the previous instructions, add the Ascendant and the "plus-one" adjustment; this becomes the **Lot of Greatness**.

Now, the method for finding the **Lot of Water-Travels** <note>Jalapatha Sahama; indicating sea voyages or paths over water</note> and the **Lot of Bondage** <note>Bandhana Sahama; indicating imprisonment or being constrained</note> is described:

Regarding "Half-Cancer" <note>Karkardha</note>: During the day, subtract Saturn from the value of three and a half signs (3s 15°). Otherwise, at night, subtract three and a half signs (3s 15°) from Saturn. Add the Ascendant and the "plus-one" adjustment; this shall be the **Lot of Water-Travels**.

During the day, subtract Saturn from the **Lot of Fortune**. At night, subtract the **Lot of Fortune** from Saturn. Add the Ascendant and the "plus-one" adjustment; this becomes the **Lot of Bondage**.

...having held the remainder 9s 8° 13' 6". When added to the <term>Ascendant</term> <note>Lagna; the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon</note>, it becomes the **Lot of Sea Voyages** <note>original: "Jalapatha Sahama"; used to predict travels across water</note>: 9s 26° 17' 0". When <term>Saturn</term> <note>Shani</note> is subtracted from the **Lot of Fortune** <note>Punya Sahama</note>, the remainder is 2s 25° 56' 41". When added to the Ascendant along with the "plus-one" adjustment <note>saika; adding 30 degrees if the Ascendant does not fall between the two points being subtracted</note>, it becomes the **Lot of Imprisonment** <note>original: "Bandhana Sahama"</note>: 4s 14° 0° 35". ॥ 23 ॥

Now, he describes the method for bringing forth the **Lot of the Daughter** <note>Kanya Sahama</note> and the **Lot of the Horse** <note>Ashva Sahama</note>. Regarding the "Moon": Always, whether by day or night, <term>the Moon</term> <note>Chandra</note> is to be subtracted from the position of <term>Venus</term> <note>Shukra</note>. As previously stated, the sum is added to the Ascendant with the "plus-one" adjustment; that shall be the **Lot of the Daughter**. For the Lot of the Horse: during the day, the <term>Sun</term> <note>Surya</note> is to be subtracted from the Lot of Fortune. At night, the Lot of Fortune is to be subtracted from the Sun. The <term>Eleventh House</term> <note>Aya-bhava; the house of gains and income</note> is then added to this, along with the "plus-one" adjustment; that shall be the **Lot of the Horse**. 

**Example:**
When the Moon is subtracted from Venus, the remainder is 2s 2° 22' 1". This, added to the Ascendant with the "plus-one" adjustment, becomes the **Lot of the Daughter**: 3s 20° 25' 55". When the Sun is subtracted from the Lot of Fortune, the remainder is 11s 25° 13' 29". This, added to the Eleventh House (10s 9° 26' 38"), becomes the **Lot of the Horse**: 10s 4° 40' 7". 

Regarding these two Lots, there is a lack of consensus <note>sammatir-nasti</note> because they were not mentioned by <term>Samarasingha</term> <note>A famous 13th-century authority on Tajika astrology</note>. In this *Samjna-tantra* <note>The first section of the Tajika Nilakanthi</note>, it is stated that if the Ascendant does not fall between the minuend and the subtrahend, then one sign (30 degrees) should be added to the Lot. However, no supporting statement for this specific rule can be found elsewhere. Even in the system of the <term>Yavanas</term> <note>original: "Yavana"; referring to the Greco-Arabic astrological tradition from which Tajika originated</note>, this "plus-one" adjustment does not exist. In the writings of Samarasingha, there is also a reading that suggests counting "from the second house of the Ascendant." In that reading, the "plus-one" adjustment does not arise. Here, the author of this text has taken the "from the second house..."

<margin>
Question 1
Page 40
</margin>

> **Subtract the Moon from Venus; this is always called the Lot of the Daughter. Subtract the Sun from the Lot of Fortune and add the House of Gains for the Lot of the Horse; at night, this is reversed. ॥ 24 ॥**

...reading as the basis for the "plus-one" adjustment in the Lots. However, in the works of Samarasingha, as well as in the *Yavana Tajika* and the *Manvattha Tajika*, the reading "from the second house of the Ascendant" is indeed present. In that specific reading, the "plus-one" adjustment is not used. Taking this into account, all the great teachers have omitted the "plus-one" adjustment for these Lots. 

Here is an example of what Samarasingha says: "During the day, combine the degrees to be traversed by the Lord of the Day <note>the Sun</note> with the degrees already traversed by the Moon. After calculating the intervening signs and making each of them thirty degrees... ॥ 1 ॥ If the Ascendant does not fall within those intervening signs, then its traversed degrees should be added to the middle; but if it does fall within them, the degrees to be traversed should be subtracted. From the second house of the Ascendant, calculating thirty degrees for each sign, where the calculation comes to rest, that point is known as the Lot of Fortune." 

In the case of a daytime <term>Solar Return</term> <note>Varshapravesha; the chart cast for the moment the Sun returns to its birth position</note>, one should set aside the Sun's sign and use only the degrees and minutes. The degrees should be subtracted from thirty, and the minutes from sixty. To the remainder, the degrees and minutes of the Moon (having discarded the sign) should be added. After that, one counts from the sign immediately following the one occupied by the Sun, determining how many signs it is from the sign preceding the one occupied by the Moon...

<meta>The text ends with the catchword "previous sign" (purvarashi), indicating the discussion of counting methods between planetary positions continues on the next page.</meta>
