<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of "Sahamas" or Astrological Lots, specifically focusing on the mathematical derivation of the "Punya Sahama" (Lot of Merit or Fortune). It transitions from the reference table on the previous page to the underlying logic and debate regarding its calculation.</meta>

<margin>Sā. Nī.</margin> <margin>|| 41 ||</margin>
...it occurs. Those quantities should be placed above the previously calculated degrees. Following that, for each [sign], thirty degrees should be allocated for each respective zodiac sign. These are then added to the degrees placed below. 

Next, one must determine if the <term>Lagna</term><note>The Ascendant; the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the time of birth.</note> falls between the <term>Sun</term><note>Surya</note> and the <term>Moon</term><note>Chandra</note>. If the Ascendant sign is between them, then one should discard the sign of the Ascendant and add the degrees. If the Ascendant sign is not between them, then the <insert>remaining degrees</insert> <margin>Prathamā 1</margin> of the Ascendant should be subtracted. 

Following this, when the <term>elapsed degrees</term><note>Bhukta-ansha; the portion of a sign already traversed by a planet.</note> are added, the counting begins from the Ascendant. However, when the <term>remaining degrees</term><note>Bhogya-ansha; the portion of a sign yet to be traversed.</note> of the Ascendant are subtracted, the counting begins from the <term>Second House</term><note>Dhana Bhava; the house of wealth.</note>. By calculating in this manner, one subtracts thirty for each sign; the sign in which thirty cannot be subtracted is where the **Lot of Merit** <note>original: "Punya Sahama"</note> is known to be located. 

In the reading "from the second house from the Ascendant," the Ascendant falls between the Sun and the Moon. Alternatively, if it does, the counting proceeds specifically from the Second House and not from the Ascendant itself.

### **Example Calculation**

**Input Data:**
*   **Sun:** 9 signs, 7 degrees, 39 minutes, 6 seconds
*   **Moon:** 5 signs, 22 degrees, 9 minutes, 47 seconds
*   **Ascendant:** 1 sign, 8 degrees, 3 minutes, 54 seconds

The **remaining degrees** of the Sun are 22° 29' 54". To this, the **elapsed degrees** of the Moon, 22° 9' 47", are added, resulting in 44° 39' 41". When the signs between the Sun and Moon are added, the value becomes 3 signs, 14° 39' 41". Converting this entirely into degrees results in 104° 39' 41". 

Because the Ascendant is NOT between the Sun and the Moon, it is added to the elapsed degrees of the Ascendant (18° 3' 54"), resulting in 1 sign, 32° 43' 35". When thirty is subtracted for each sign starting from the Ascendant, the **Lot of Merit** is found in **Leo** <note>Simha Rashi</note> at 4s 2° 43' 35". If counting from the Second House, the **Lot of Merit** is found in **Virgo** <note>Kanya Rashi</note> at 5s 2° 43' 35".

In this way, a single result is reached. There is a dispute regarding these readings. In the reading "from the second house from the Ascendant," the logic is this: when the elapsed degrees of the Ascendant are added, the counting starts from the Ascendant because a connection to the Ascendant remains. But when the remaining degrees are subtracted, there is no connection to the Ascendant. Therefore, the counting starts from the Second House. In this case, nothing remains of the Ascendant. This is the logic provided. There is general consensus in all texts; however, due to some lack of agreement, the reading "from the second house from the Ascendant" is also used.

As stated in the **Manushya Jataka** <note>A classical text on human horoscopy</note>:
<margin>|| 41 ||</margin>

> "The remaining degrees are spoken of as these, as far as the total elapsed degrees within; when it has gone outside, the remaining degrees are added to the elapsed. Having calculated the signs, one should speak of them from the second from the Ascendant; from there the Lots such as **Merit** occur, where the degrees reach their conclusion." || 24 ||

<summary>This page explains the mathematical formulas for calculating the "Punya Sahama" (Lot of Merit), specifically addressing the debate over whether to count from the Ascendant or the Second House based on the positions of the Sun and Moon.</summary>
<keywords>Punya Sahama, Lot of Merit, Ascendant, Lagna, Sun, Moon, calculation, zodiac, degrees, Manushya Jataka, astrological math</keywords>

--- Page 86 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of "Sahamas" or Astrological Lots. While the previous page focused on the mathematical derivation of the "Punya Sahama" (Lot of Merit), this page explains the "Phalapaka" or the timing of when these results will actually manifest in a person's life during the solar year.</meta>

Collection of 81 Year Old Dina Nath Raina, Jammu. Retired Priest.

Now, having explained the origin of the <term>Lots</term><note>Sahamas; specific sensitive points in a horoscope used in Tajika/Annual astrology.</note> and intending to describe their results, the author addresses the question of how many days after the <term>Solar Return</term><note>Varshapravesha; the moment the Sun returns to its exact birth position, marking the start of a new year.</note> the results of a Lot will be obtained. He explains the method for calculating these days in the verse beginning with "Subtract its own lord."

For whichever Lot one wishes to know the day of obtaining good or bad results, the position of that Lot (in signs, degrees, etc.) should be set down. One should subtract the position of its own <term>Lord</term><note>Svanatha; the planet that rules the zodiac sign where the Lot is located.</note> from it. The remaining value should be converted into total degrees. These degrees are then multiplied by the <term>Rising Time</term><note>Udaya; the specific time it takes for a zodiac sign to rise over the horizon at a particular latitude.</note> of the sign where the Lot is situated. The product is then divided by three hundred. The resulting number represents the days, and these days constitute the <term>Ripening Time</term><note>Phalapakakala; the period when the predicted fruit or outcome of the Lot manifests.</note> of those Lots. 

In this regard, some say that by dividing the resulting days by thirty, and adding the result to the <term>Sun's position</term> at the time of the Solar Return, the time of fruition occurs when the Sun reaches that specific point in the zodiac. Others say that the results manifest after the calculated number of days starting from the beginning of the <term>Planetary Period</term><note>Mahadasha</note> of the Lot’s ruler, either within that period or a sub-period. 

Now, if a Lot and its Lord are at the exact same degree and sign, there would be no difference between them, and thus no days could be calculated. To address how one might know the timing of results in such a case, the author says:

-> **Subtract its own ruler from the Lot; multiply the remaining degrees by its sign's rising value, and divide by three hundred.** <-
-> **The ripening of that Lot occurs after those many days, or during the Lot ruler's planetary period should the former be impossible. [Verse 26]** <-

"During the Lot ruler's planetary period" means that if it is impossible to calculate the days (due to a lack of distance between the Lot and its ruler), the fruit of the Lot will be obtained during the ruler's own <term>Dasha</term><note>Planetary period</note>. The specific timing within that period would be determined by the sub-periods (*Antardasha* or *Vidasha*). 

**Calculation Example:**
The **Lot of Merit** <note>original: "Punya Sahama"</note> is at 5 signs, 5 degrees, 25 minutes, and 1 second.
Subtract its ruler, **Mercury**, which is at 3 signs, 6 degrees, 14 minutes, and 12 seconds.
The remainder is 2 signs, 2 degrees, 10 minutes, and 49 seconds. 
The total degrees of this remainder are 59° 10' 49". 
Since the Lot is in Virgo, this is multiplied by the rising value of Virgo (335), resulting in 19825 | 23 | 35. 
Divided by 300, we get 66 days, 9 <note>A ghati is 24 minutes.</note> *ghatis*, and 4 <note>A pala is 24 seconds.</note> *palas*. 
Therefore, the result of the Lot of Merit will ripen 66 days after the Solar Return. Alternatively, if the ruler's period is long, the results manifest after this many days have passed from the start of Mercury's planetary period... [Verse 27]

<summary>This page details the mathematical formula for predicting exactly when a "Lot" (Sahama) will yield its results during the year by subtracting the ruler's position from the Lot's position and adjusting for local rising times. It provides a concrete example using the Lot of Merit and its ruler, Mercury.</summary>
<keywords>Sahama calculation, timing of results, Punya Sahama, Lot of Merit, Solar Return, Varshapravesha, planetary periods, Dasha, Mercury, Virgo rising value</keywords>

# Now the Results of the Lots are Explained

Regarding the phrase "In its own exaltation, etc.": The <term>Lord of the Lot</term><note>Sahamapa; the planet that rules the sign where the Sahama/Lot is located.</note>, if it is positioned in its own house, its own exaltation, its own <term>Term</term><note>Hadda; a subdivision of a zodiac sign used extensively in Tajika and Hellenistic astrology.</note>, its own <term>Triplicity</term><note>Trairashika; a division of three signs grouped by element.</note>, or in its <term>Musallaha</term><note>A technical dignity in Tajika astrology indicating a well-placed or "armed" planet.</note>—meaning it is in a "Good Path"—and if it is in a favorable house, or by extension, in the house of a friendly planet, or is associated with or aspected by benefic planets, and if it **looks at the Ascendant** (aspected by the Lagna), then it is said to be **endowed with vigor**. This means it is powerful.

If the Lord of the Lot, even if positioned in its own exaltation or a "Good Path," **does not look at the Ascendant**, then it is considered **not powerful**. This means it is weak.

Regarding the phrase "The Sun and Moon": The lords of the signs occupied by the Sun and the Moon are considered as follows. First, the lord of the sign where the Sun was located at the time of birth. Second, the lord of the sign where the Moon was located at the time of birth. Third, the lord of the Ascendant calculated for the moment of the last New Moon or Full Moon prior to birth. Fourth, the lord of the Ascendant at the time of birth. The strength of these four should be evaluated in this way.

In the <term>Hillaja</term><note>Hyleg; a Persian/Arabic term for the "Life-Giver" or the planet used to calculate the lifespan.</note> system for calculating the span of life, and in general human horoscopy, the consideration of the strength of these points is useful. Although it is not strictly required for the present topic of annual lots, it is mentioned here in passing. || 26 ||

> If the Lord of the Lot is in its own exaltation or a good position and aspects the Ascendant, it is endowed with vigor. If it does not look at the Ascendant, it is not strong.
> One should similarly consider the strength of the Lords of the signs occupied by the Sun and Moon, and the Ascendant, for the purpose of such evaluation. || 26 ||

> One who is deficient in the **Five-fold Strength**, who is not situated in a **Place of Joy**, 
> And who does not aspect the Ascendant, is considered powerless; but if even a small portion of these factors exists, the planet has some strength. || 27 ||

Now, the author describes the characteristics of being weak or strong. Regarding "Five-fold": The <term>Five-fold Strength</term><note>Pancha-vargiya-bala; a specific scoring system in Tajika astrology that evaluates a planet based on five criteria: House, Exaltation, Triplicity, Term, and Decan.</note> was mentioned previously. If a planet is deficient in this, it is called "weak in vigor." The four <term>Places of Joy</term><note>Harshasthana; specific houses or conditions where a particular planet feels "happy" or strengthened, such as the Sun being joyful in the 9th house.</note> were also mentioned previously. If a planet is not in even one of these Places of Joy, and if it **does not look at the Ascendant**, then that planet should be known as **powerless** (weak).

However, if even a small degree of these dignities exists, it is "strong." For example:
If a planet is in its own house or its exaltation, it has **Great Authority**.
If a planet is in its own <term>Term</term> (Hadda), it has **Medium Authority**.
If it is in its own <term>Triplicity</term> (Trairashika) or its own...

Pr.

1

</margin>

It also briefly touches upon the Hillaja (Hyleg) system used for determining longevity.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the technical evaluation of the "Sahamas" (astrological Lots), moving from the mathematical calculation of their timing to the qualitative assessment of their strength. It specifically addresses how the Lord of the Eighth House—traditionally associated with obstacles and loss—and malefic planets can negate the positive effects of a Lot.</meta>

When the Lord of the Lot is present, it possesses a certain degree of authority. Therefore, the wise have described a hierarchy of planetary strength: "Thirty [units of strength] in its own sign, twenty in its own exaltation," and so forth. This refers to its **positional strength**. Thus, even if its natural share of authority is small, if the planet is situated in a place called the <term>Musallaha</term> <note>original: "musahala-akhye"; a technical dignity in Persian-influenced Tajika astrology indicating a planet is well-placed or 'armed'</note> and aspects its source—the Lot itself—then that planet should be known as **strong**. || 27 ||

Having thus understood the strength or weakness of the Lord of the Lot, he now describes the growth or decline of the Lot itself. Regarding the phrase "By its own master": If a Lot is joined with or aspected by its own master, or is joined with or aspected by benefic planets, and if the Lord of the Lot is strong according to the methods previously mentioned, then there will be **growth** of that Lot, meaning it will have the power to grant its results. If a Lot is not joined with or aspected by its master or by benefic planets, then the manifestation of that Lot's results will not occur.

### ->If it is joined or aspected by its own lord and by benefic planets, and if the lord is strong, then there is an increase in the power of that Lot. If it is not joined or aspected by its lord and benefics, its manifestation will not occur;<-
### ->this should be considered first. [28] If the Lot is joined or aspected by the Lord of the Eighth House, or joined or aspected by malefic planets, or forms an <term>Itthashala</term> <note>A configuration where a faster planet approaches a slower one, indicating the fulfillment of an event</note> with them, then even if its realization was possible, it meets with destruction. Therefore, in this life, let this be examined first.<-
### ->|| 29 || First, having deliberated upon the strength and weakness of all the Lots in the birth chart, one should then consider in the annual chart those Lots whose manifestation is possible. || 30 ||<-

The meaning is that the Lot will not give the results corresponding to its name. This must be investigated first. || 28 ||

Now he describes another characteristic of the non-occurrence of a Lot's results. Regarding the phrase "The Eighth": If a Lot is joined with or aspected by the **Lord of the Eighth House** <note>Ashtamadhipati; the planet ruling the 8th sign from the annual ascendant, usually signifying death, delays, or obstacles</note> calculated from the annual chart, or is joined or aspected by a malefic planet, or forms an <term>Itthashala</term> or <term>Muthashila</term> <note>Persian-derived terms for planetary aspects</note> with the Lord of the Eighth or with malefic planets, then even if the Lot was capable of yielding fruit because it was joined by its own lord or benefics, that result is **destroyed**. This means it does not give the result promised by the Lot’s name. For that reason, one should first examine and deliberate upon this strength or weakness of the Lot in the <term>Janma</term> <note>the original natal birth chart</note>. || 29 ||

Next, regarding the method of determining the strong Lots and rejecting the weak ones, he says: "At the beginning..."

<vocab>सहम (Sahama): An astrological Lot or sensitive point, बलाबल (Balabala): Strength and weakness, इत्थशाल (Itthashala): A specific planetary aspect, मुथशिल (Muthashila): A synonymous term for Itthashala, अष्टमाधिपति (Ashtamadhipati): Lord of the Eighth House, फलदातृत्व (Phaladatritva): The capacity to grant results.</vocab>

It instructs the astrologer to first evaluate the Lot's strength in the birth chart before looking at the annual progression.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the technical evaluation of the Sahamas (astrological Lots) from the 'Tajika Nilakanthi'. Having established how to determine the strength of a Lot's planetary ruler on the previous page, the text now transitions into the specific effects of these points, beginning with the most significant one: the Punya Sahama (Fortune Point).</meta>

->La. Ni.<-
->Section 1<-

First, at the time of birth, one should evaluate and understand the strength and weakness of all fifty <term>Lots</term> <note>original: "sahamas"; mathematical points in the horoscope calculated by adding and subtracting the longitudes of planets and the ascendant</note>. One should only consider the results for the year for those Lots where the attainment of results is actually possible. This means that if the <term>Lord of the Lot</term> <note>the planet that rules the sign where the Lot is located</note> is found to be weak in every way, one should never even consider those Lots for that specific year. || 30 ||

Thus, the method for the Lots being capable of giving results when they are strong has been explained. Now, the results of all the Lots are being stated. Among them, he first describes the result of the **Fortune Point**. Regarding the phrase "When strong": When the **Fortune Point** is strong, or when it is aspected by or joined with benefic planets or its own Lord, then there is the fulfillment of <term>Dharma</term> <note>merit, religious duty, or right action</note> and the **arrival of wealth**. In the opposite case—meaning if the Lot is weak or aspected by/joined with malefic planets—the wise say the result is the opposite: the destruction of merit and the loss of wealth. || 31 ||

Now he states an exception to these inauspicious results. Regarding the phrase "From the Ascendant": When the Fortune Point is situated in the sixth, eighth, or twelfth houses from the **Yearly Ascendant**, then it causes the destruction of merit, fortune, and fame.

### ### When the Fortune Point is strong, there is fulfillment of duty and arrival of wealth. When joined or aspected by its auspicious Lord, [it is good]; the wise know the opposite is true if the opposite occurs. || 31 ||
### ### Situated in the sixth, eighth, or twelfth from the Ascendant, it destroys merit, fortune, and fame. If occupied or aspected by its auspicious Lord, the possibility of happiness and merit arises. || 32 ||
### ### If joined by a malefic but aspected by a benefic, it is inauspicious at first and naturally auspicious [later]. If joined by a benefic but aspected by a malefic, it is auspicious at first and unfavorable afterward. || 33 ||
### ### In whatever year the Fortune Point is auspicious, that year brings good fortune. If this is unfavorable, no good comes; therefore, one should first evaluate the Fortune Point. || 34 ||

Even if it is situated in those [bad] houses, if it is aspected by a benefic planet or its own Lord, then at the "point of arrival" <note>original: "akrante"; referring to the time the planet transits or the period matures</note> during the year, there will be the possibility—the attainment—of happiness, merit, and so on. This implies that there is an inauspicious result in the first half of the year [due to the house placement] and a good result later [due to the aspect]. || 32 ||

Now he speaks of the results of the conjunctions and aspects involving both malefic and benefic planets. Regarding the phrase "Joined by a malefic": If that Fortune Point is joined by a malefic but aspected by a benefic, then in the first half of the year it will be inauspicious, but in the second half of the year it will be auspicious. However, when the Fortune Point is joined by a benefic but aspected by a malefic, then it will be auspicious at the beginning (the first half of the year) and "unfavorable"—meaning inauspicious—in the later second half of the year. It follows logically that if it is both joined and aspected by malefics, the entire year will be inauspicious. If it is both joined and aspected by benefics, the auspicious result lasts throughout the entire year. || 33 ||

Now he offers praise for the Fortune Point. Regarding the phrase "In whatever year": In whatever year the Fortune Point is auspicious—that is, it produces auspicious results—that year...

<summary>This page discusses the Punya Sahama (Fortune Point), explaining how its strength and the aspects it receives from benefic or malefic planets determine the timing and quality of wealth and success during the year.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the technical evaluation of the Sahamas (astrological Lots) in the Tajika system. The previous page discussed the general strength of the Lot's ruler; this page focuses on the specific outcomes for the Punya Sahama (Fortune Point) and introduces the "Rule of Reversal" for inauspicious Lots such as those for death or enemies.</meta>

<insert>Shiva</insert> <note>original: "śiva"; an invocation to the deity Shiva, commonly placed at the start or significant breaks in a manuscript</note>

...that year is auspicious. It should be understood that in that year, it will grant good results. In a year where the **Fortune Point** <note>original: "punya-sahama"</note> is in an unfavorable position, it is truly inauspicious and does not grant good results during the course of the year. Because it only yields bad results in such a state, one should first and foremost investigate the Fortune Point. || 34 ||

Now, he describes the inauspicious results when the Fortune Point is placed in unfavorable houses in the birth chart:

->If situated in the sixth, eighth, or twelfth houses <note>original: "rihpha-sthana" refers to the 12th house</note> at birth, and in the yearly chart it is again seized by malefic planets <note>joined or aspected by them</note>, then it destroys merit, wealth, and happiness. The same result—the destruction of merit, wealth, and happiness—occurs if the **Lord of the Fortune Point** is combust <note>original: "dagdha," literally "burnt," referring to a planet being so close to the Sun that its influence is neutralized</note>. || 35 ||<-

->In this manner, one should carefully consider all the Lots in both the birth chart and the yearly chart. For those Lots related to **disease**, **enemies**, **strife**, and **death**, one should predict the results in reverse. || 36 ||<-

->The **Lot of Success** <note>original: "karyasiddhi-sahama"</note>, when joined with or aspected by benefic planets and forming a <term>Muthasila</term> <note>a Tajik aspect where a faster planet moves toward a slower one, indicating the event is coming to fruition</note>, grants victory. The wise say that even if afflicted by malefic aspects in battle, victory is achieved through great struggle. || 37 ||<-

**Disease** <note>original: "māndya"</note> refers to illness. **Enemy** <note>original: "ari"</note> means a foe. **Strife** <note>original: "kali"</note> refers to a quarrel. **Death** <note>original: "mṛtyu"</note> is a well-known term. Because these are examples of inauspicious Lots, the **Lot of Poverty** is also included.

This is why the author will say further on: "In the cases of poverty, death, disease, enemies, and strife, the reverse is stated." Regarding these Lots, one should declare a result that is the opposite of the Fortune Point’s result. 

To explain: Starting from the verse "As with the Fortune Point, if it has reached a high position..." and continuing through "situated in the sixth, eighth, or twelfth houses," when the investigation is performed, if a "good" result is calculated for the Lots of disease, enemies, strife, or death, then one must actually predict an **inauspicious** result. If an "inauspicious" result is calculated, then one should predict a **good** result <note>In this context, a "good" result for the Lot of Death would mean the person avoids death, hence the "reversal" logic</note>. || 38 ||

Next, he states the auspicious and inauspicious results of the **Lot of Success**. If the Lot of Success is joined with or aspected by benefic planets, or if a benefic planet forms a **Muthasila** yoga with it, then it grants victory in battle and the destruction of enemies.

<summary>This page explains the conditions that ruin the benefits of the Fortune Point, such as placement in malefic houses or combustion, and introduces the "reversal rule" for negative Lots like death or poverty. It also describes how the Lot of Success predicts victory in conflict through specific planetary aspects.</summary>

> **If the Lord of the Fame Lot is in the eighth house and joined or aspected by a malefic, fame is destroyed. If earned through sin, ill-repute follows; if the planet lacks strength, the family's reputation is ruined. || 40 ||**

If it is joined or aspected by a mixture of planets, the marriage is achieved only through great hardship and distress. However, if the Lot of Marriage is joined or aspected by cruel malefics, or is <gloss>eclipsed</gloss> <note>original: "payalikrta"; likely referring to a planet being combustion or otherwise weakened</note>, or if it forms a Muthasila with the **Lord of the Eighth House** <note>the ruler of the house of death and endings</note>—either from the yearly ascendant <note>original: "varsha-lagna"</note> or from the Lot itself—then marriage will definitely not happen in that year. || 39 ||

# Collection of 81 Year Old Dina Nath Raina, Jammu. Retired Priest.

The destruction of fame earned over a long time may occur. However, in connection with sinful deeds, there is a "gain" of ill-fame <note>original: "ayashas"; notoriety or infamy</note>. If that Lot is situated in the eighth house <note>the house associated with transformations, hidden things, and death</note>, or if it has lost its vitality or is combust <note>rendered invisible by the Sun's proximity</note>, then the fame of the entire lineage will be destroyed.
|| 40 ||

Now, he speaks again of the auspicious and inauspicious results of the **Lot of Fame** <note>original: "yasha-sahama"</note>.

If the Lord of the Lot of Fame is in a favorable <term>Itthashala</term> <note>a Tajika aspect where a faster planet approaches a slower one, indicating the manifestation of an event</note> with a benefic planet, or is aspected by a benefic planet while possessing strength, then fame will increase. Likewise, there will be victory in battle. Furthermore, there will be the acquisition of vehicles such as elephants and horses, and of weapons. However, if the Lord of the Lot of Fame is in an <term>Isarapha</term> <note>a separating aspect where the faster planet moves away from the slower one, indicating the fading or loss of an event</note> with a malefic planet, then both fame and wealth will be destroyed. || 41 ||

Now, he speaks of the auspicious and inauspicious results of the **Lot of Hope** <note>original: "aasha-sahama"</note>.

The Lot of Hope should not be situated in the sixth, eighth, or twelfth houses from the Ascendant <note>these are known as 'dusthanas' or houses of suffering</note>. Furthermore—

-> **If the Lord of the Lot of Fame is in a favorable Itthashala, aspected by benefics, or is possessed of strength, there is an increase in fame. Victory in battle and the gain of vehicles and weapons occurs; but in a malefic Isarapha, fame and wealth are destroyed. || 41 ||** <-
-> **The Lot of Hope and its Lord should be free from the sixth, eighth, and twelfth houses, and should be joined or aspected by benefics. Then one gains desired objects, gold, clothing, and vehicles; but if joined or seen by a malefic, there is great sorrow. || 42 ||** <-
-> **The Lord of Illness, if it is itself a malefic planet and joined or aspected by malefics, is considered a cause of disease. If it forms an Itthashala with the Lord of Death, then death occurs; if the Lord of Illness is weak, it happens with great suffering. || 43 ||** <-

—the Lord of the Lot of Hope should also be free from these [negative] positions. If both the Lot and its Lord are joined with or aspected by benefic planets, then one shall gain desired objects, wealth such as gold, clothing, and vehicles. But if they are seen by or joined with a malefic planet, then there is great sorrow regarding those desired objects. || 42 ||

Now he speaks of the results of the **Lot of Illness** <note>original: "roga-sahama"</note>.

If the Lord of Illness <note>original: "mandyadhipa"; 'mandya' here refers to the Lot of sluggishness or disease</note> is itself a malefic planet and is joined with or aspected by other malefic planets, it should be known as a cause of disease. If the Lord of the Lot of Illness forms an <term>Itthashala</term> <note>an applying aspect</note> with the Lord of Death <note>the planet ruling the eighth house from the Ascendant</note>, then death will occur. If such a Lord of the Lot of Illness is weak in strength, then death will occur with great difficulty and suffering. || 43 ||

# Tajika Nilakanthi
### Section 1, Page 45

<margin>
Tajika Nilakanthi
45
</margin>

Now, he speaks again regarding the favorable and unfavorable results of the **Lot of Illness** <note>original: "mandya-sahama"; also refers to the Lot of Sloth or Debility</note>. 

If the Lot of Illness is aspected <note>viewed through a planetary "glance"</note> by its own Lord or by benefic planets, or if the Lord of the Lot is strong and located outside of the "evil houses"—meaning the sixth, eighth, and twelfth houses—then illness will certainly not occur. However, if the Lot is joined with or aspected by a mixture of both benefic and malefic planets, one should understand that there will be some slight fear of disease or physical distress. || 44 ||

Now, he describes the favorable and unfavorable results of the **Lot of Wealth** <note>original: "artha-sahama"</note>. 

The Lot of Wealth, if aspected by or joined with a benefic Lord, grants happiness through the acquisition of riches.

**If the Lot of Illness is aspected by its own Lord or a benefic, and the Lord is strong and situated outside the eighth, sixth, or final [twelfth] houses, then illness does not occur. If it is joined or aspected by a mixture [of benefics and malefics], there is some fear of disease. || 44 ||**

**The Lot of Wealth, if seen or joined by a benefic Lord, brings happiness through the arrival of riches. If seen or joined by malefics, it causes the destruction of wealth. If it is joined by malefics but aspected by benefics, and forms an <term>Itthashala</term> with a benefic, it first destroys wealth and afterwards brings the emergence of wealth and happiness. The reverse occurs if the conditions are swapped. || 45 ||**

**Through an aspect of an enemy, there is fear of the enemy and loss of wealth to thieves and the like. Through an aspect of a friend, there is the gain of wealth, honor, fame, and happiness through the association with friends. || 46 ||**

<margin>
Ma.
1
45
</margin>

When the Lot is aspected or joined by malefic planets, it causes the destruction of wealth or property. Now, regarding the Lot of Wealth being joined by malefics while simultaneously being aspected by benefics: if it also forms an <term>Itthashala</term> <note>a Tajika aspect where a faster planet approaches a slower one, indicating the manifestation of an event</note> with a benefic planet, then at first, all accumulated wealth may be destroyed, but later, it will bring about the emergence of wealth accompanied by happiness. 

"The reverse occurs if swapped" <note>original: "vyatyasato vyatyayah"</note> means: if the Lot of Wealth is joined by benefics but aspected by malefics, or if it forms an <term>Itthashala</term> with malefics, then there will largely be auspicious results, but perhaps some slight loss of wealth will occur at the end. || 45 ||

Now, he speaks of the results of aspects from enemies and friends. Regarding whichever Lot is being considered: if it is aspected by an enemy <note>a planet in a hostile relationship or position</note>, whether that planet is a benefic or a malefic, there will be fear originating from enemies and loss of wealth to thieves and such. If the Lot is aspected by a friend, then through the union with friends, one attains wealth, honor, fame, and happiness.

# Tajika Nilakanthi

**Now, he describes the favorable and unfavorable results of the Lot of Children.** <note>original: "putra-sahama"</note>

Regarding the Lot being aspected by its good Lord: if the Lot of Children is aspected by or joined with benefic <note>gentle or auspicious</note> planets and its own Lord, then it grants the gain of a son, the birth of a child, and the happiness resulting from that. If the Lot of Children is joined with malefic <note>harsh or inauspicious</note> planets, but forms an <term>Itthashala</term> <note>a favorable planetary configuration where a faster planet approaches a slower one</note> with benefic planets, then first there is sorrow regarding the child, but afterwards there is happiness and well-being for the son. || 47 ||

**Now, he describes again the favorable and unfavorable results of the Lot of Children.**

Regarding being joined with malefics: if the Lot of Children is joined with malefic planets and a <term>Sarafa</term> <note>an unfavorable configuration or separation</note> is formed by malefics, while the Lord of the Lot of Children is "robbed of its light" <note>original: "gataujasi"; refers to being weak, powerless, or combust by the Sun</note> or is set <note>astrologically hidden</note>, then:

> The Lot of Children, if seen or joined by its good Lord, grants the gain of a child and happiness. ||
> If joined with malefics but in <term>Itthashala</term> with a benefic planet, it first gives pain but later happiness regarding the son. || 47 ||
> If joined with malefics and forming a <term>Sarafa</term> through malefics, it leads to the destruction of the son if the Lord of the Lot is weak. ||
> If the Lord of the Fifth House <note>the house of children</note> at birth is also the Lord of the Lot during the year, and is seen by a favorable friend, it leads to the obtaining of a son. || 48 ||
> The Lot of the Father <note>original: "pitrya-sahama"</note>, if joined or aspected by benefics and its Lord, grants the father wealth, clothing, honor, and happiness. ||
> If the Lord is weak, in the house of death <note>the 8th house</note>, and there is a malefic <term>Musharapha</term> <note>a configuration indicating separation or loss</note>, the father’s destruction occurs due to traveling to a foreign land, especially if in a movable sign. || 49 ||
> If there is a benefic <term>Itthashala</term> but a malefic planet is joined, there will first be great anger or trouble. ||
> Afterward, one finds happiness; if the Lord is in full strength, there is honor from kings and an increase in fame and glory. || 50 ||

...there is the destruction of the son. If, at the time of the "Year-Entrance" <note>the solar return or annual chart</note>, the Lord of the Lot of Children is also the Lord of the Fifth House from the time of birth, and is further aspected by a friendly benefic planet, then it leads to the attainment or "finding" of a son. || 48 ||

**Now, he describes the favorable and unfavorable results of the Lot of the Father.** <note>original: "pitru-sahama"</note>

Regarding the paternal: if the Paternal Lot (Lot of the Father) is seen or joined by benefic planets, or is joined or aspected by its own Lord, it grants the father wealth, clothing, honor, and happiness. If the Lord of the Lot of the Father is weak or "set," or is located in the house of death <note>the 8th house, counted from the Ascendant or the Lot</note>, and if a <term>Musharapha</term> yoga is made by a malefic planet—and especially if this occurs in a "movable sign" <note>Aries, Cancer, Libra, or Capricorn</note>—then there will be the death or destruction of the father during a journey to a foreign land. || 49 ||

**Now, he describes again the favorable and unfavorable results of the Lot of the Father.**

Regarding the benefic: In the Lot of the Father, by extension [this applies to the] son...

If the Lord of the Lot of Fear <note>original: "trasahamesha"; refers to a point in the chart associated with anxiety or illness</note> forms an <term>Itthashala</term> <note>a favorable planetary configuration where a faster planet approaches a slower one</note> with a benefic <note>auspicious</note> planet, but also forms a connection with a malefic <note>harsh</note> planet, then in the first half of the year there will be a severe outbreak of illness and an increase in undesirable events. Afterward, in the second half of the year, one finds and attains happiness. This means the person becomes free from disease. When the Lord of the Father’s Lot is at full strength—specifically when it possesses a power rating of more than fifteen <term>vishvapakas</term> <note>a numerical measure of a planet's strength</note>—there will be an increase in honor and fame received from the King. || 50 ||

**Now, he describes the favorable and unfavorable results of the Lot of Imprisonment.** <note>original: "bandhana-sahama"</note>

Regarding the Lot of Imprisonment: if it is joined with or aspected <note>viewed</note> by its own Lord, then there is no experience of imprisonment or jail. If that Lot of Imprisonment is aspected or joined by a malefic planet, then there is imprisonment. Furthermore, regarding the Lot—

->If the Lot known as Imprisonment is joined or aspected by its Lord, there is no imprisonment. If it is aspected or joined by a malefic, there is imprisonment, especially if a malefic <term>Muthashila</term> <note>an astrological configuration similar to Itthashala</note> is formed. || 51 ||<-

->If the Lot known as Honor is joined or aspected by its Lord or by benefic planets, it leads to the attainment of happiness. It brings royal honor, fame, and fine clothing; but if aspected or joined by malefics, there is a loss of position. || 52 ||<-

->If the Lot is aspected or joined by both benefics and malefics, and an <term>Itthashala</term> is formed with the malefics, it causes the destruction of wealth and honor in the first half of the year. If a benefic <term>Itthashala</term> follows in the latter half, it grants happiness and the gain of vehicles and tools. || 53 ||<-

->If the Lords of the Lot of Action and the House of Career are joined with benefics and their own Lords, and are endowed with strength through <term>Muthashila</term> and other factors, they grant the gain of gold, horses, elephants, and land. If joined or aspected by malefics, they grant unfavorable results. || 54 ||<-

If a <term>Muthashila</term> configuration is formed by a malefic planet with the Lord of the Lot of Imprisonment, then imprisonment is particularly certain. || 51 ||

**Now, he describes the favorable and unfavorable results of the Lot of Honor.** <note>original: "gaurava-sahama"</note>

If the Lot of Honor is joined or aspected by its own Lord or by benefic planets, it results in the attainment of happiness. There will be the acquisition of royal honor, fame, and fine clothing <note>original: "ambara"; often signifies status through dress</note>. If the Lot of Honor is joined or aspected by malefic planets, there will be a loss or destruction of one's position or authority. || 52 ||

**Now, he further describes the unfavorable results of the Lot of Honor.**

If the Lot of Honor is aspected or joined by both benefics and malefics, and if an <term>Muthashila</term> is formed by malefic planets, then in the first half of the year, it causes <note>original: "vidhatte"</note> the destruction of wealth and honor. In the second half of the year, if an <term>Muthashila</term> is formed with benefic planets for the Lot of Honor, then it brings happiness and the gain of vehicles and weapons. || 53 ||

**Now, he describes the favorable and unfavorable results of the Lot of Action.** <note>original: "karma-sahama"; also relates to one's career or deeds</note>

It transitions from the Lot of Imprisonment discussed on the previous page to the outcomes of the Lot of Action.</meta>

<page-num>41</page-num>

**Regarding the Lot of Action:** The term "Lords of the state of action" <note>original: "karmabhava"</note> refers to four specific factors: the Lord of the 10th house, the planet occupying the 10th house, the Lord of the Lot of Action <note>original: "karma-sahama"</note>, and the planet occupying that Lot. If these four are strong and form a <term>Muthashila</term> <note>a favorable planetary aspect where a faster planet reaches a slower one</note> with auspicious planets or with their own house lords, then they grant the acquisition of gold, horses, elephants, and land. However, if these Lords of the Lot of Action are viewed or joined by malefic <note>unfavorable</note> planets, they produce inauspicious results. || 54 ||

Now, he describes the unfavorable results for the Lords of the Lot of Action when they are weakened. **Regarding combustion and retrogradation:** If the Lords of the Lot of Action are "set" <note>original: "astangata"; hidden by the sun's light/combust</note> or are "retrograde" <note>original: "vakra"; appearing to move backward</note>, they cause the failure of actions. This means that any auspicious work or career venture that has been started will remain unaccomplished. If these same Lords are joined with or viewed by <term>Sauri</term> <note>Saturn</note>, then the failure of actions is particularly certain. Likewise, if the Lord of Royal Action <note>the Lot concerning the King or government</note> and the Lord of the 10th house form a <term>Musaripha</term> <note>an unfavorable configuration where a faster planet moves away from a slower one, indicating separation or loss</note> with a malefic planet, then there is a fall from power <note>original: "raja-bhransha"</note>, the loss of a kingdom, or the destruction of one's professional undertakings.

### If the Lords of Action are combust or retrograde, they cause the failure of actions; if they are joined or viewed by Saturn, this failure is especially certain. If the Lords of the State and Action form a Musaripha with a malefic, there is loss of status and destruction of career. || 55 ||

### The Teacher represents the Guru; Knowledge is the Lot of Learning; Scripture represents the Revealed and Remembered texts; Delusion is Stupidity; Strength represents the Army; the Body is the Limb; Water represents Radiance. || 56 ||

### Greatness represents the Master of a Circle; Honor represents Dignity; the King is the Lord of Punishment and Favor, who possesses the Insignia such as the Royal Parasol. || 57 ||

Now, he clarifies the meanings of certain Lots that might be ambiguous. **Regarding the "Teacher":** One who instructs in virtues or teaches the *Gayatri* mantra <note>a sacred Vedic chant</note> is called the "Guru" or "Teacher" <note>original: "upadeshta"</note> here. Although the *Amarakosha* <note>a famous Sanskrit dictionary</note> defines "Guru" as a father or a planet (Jupiter), here it specifically refers to the teacher, because the Lot of the Father is treated as a separate category. It cannot refer to the planet Jupiter here because no specific result would follow from that interpretation. Therefore, the "Guru" is the instructor.

Likewise, the "Lot of Learning" <note>original: "vidya-sahama"</note> signifies Knowledge. The word "Scripture" <note>original: "shastra"</note> signifies the *Shruti* <note>revealed texts like the Vedas</note> and *Smriti* <note>traditional law codes</note>. The word "Stupidity" <note>original: "jadya"</note> signifies Delusion <note>original: "moha"</note>, or ignorance. The word "Strength" <note>original: "bala"</note> is well-known to mean both the army and physical power; he clarifies here that in this context, it means the Army. "Body" <note>original: "deha"</note> refers to the "Limb" <note>original: "anga"</note> or the physical frame. "Water" <note>original: "jala"</note> signifies "Radiance" <note>original: "dyuti"</note> or luster. || 56 ||

Furthermore, when the suffix for "state" is added to the word for "Great," it results in the terms "Greatness" <note>original: "gurutva"</note> and "Honor" <note>original: "gaurava"</note>. Between these—

<header>Chapter 1</header>
<sig>Ta. Ni.</sig>

Thinking that it would be improper to speak of these separately if their meanings were identical, he explains the difference between the two. **Regarding Greatness:** Here, the distinction follows worldly convention. By the word "Greatness" <note>original: "gurutā"</note>, one refers to being one among hundreds in a circle or assembly. By the word "Dignity of the Master" <note>original: "svāmigaurava"</note>, one refers to a person characterized by great honor and an abundance of prestige. Now, the word "King" <note>original: "rāja"</note> is well-known to signify a member of the warrior caste <note>original: "kṣatriya"</note>; he provides the specific definition of its meaning here with the half-verse beginning with "Restraint." In this context, by worldly convention, the word "King" refers only to a "Lord of the Earth." He gives the characteristics of such a person: **Restraint and favor.** "Restraint" <note>original: "nigraha"</note> means the infliction of what is undesirable, and "favor" <note>original: "anugraha"</note> means the granting of what is desired through one's word. The one who is the "Lord" <note>original: "vibhu"</note> of these two is the one who is capable of them. What kind of person is he? One who "possesses the signs such as the parasol." The "parasol" <note>original: "chhatra"</note> is well-known; by the word "such as," the fly-whisk <note>original: "chāmara"</note> and other regalia are included. He who possesses or enjoys these signs is a King. || 57 || Next, he explains the meanings of Majesty, Capability, and Valor. **Majesty:** The state of being great is Majesty, or greatness. **Depth of counsel:** Counsel <note>original: "mantra"</note> is the deliberation conducted in private; its "depth" <note>original: "gāmbhīrya"</note> refers to its quality of not being disclosed.

---

### Majesty, depth of counsel, fortitude, and the possession of intellect; || Capability is the strength born of the body; Valor is the effort in subduing enemies. || 58 || Hope is said to be desire; Faith is a mind inclined toward virtue; Bondage is dependence on others; || The falling of water is rain, or a sudden immersion in water. || 59 || Mental distress, physical disease, and agony are called Sluggishness; Kinsmen are known as "those of the same funeral cake"; || Truth and falsehood together are the practice of commerce; Conception is known as "placement." || 60 ||

---

Now, one might ask how "non-disclosure" occurs; he replies: **Fortitude.** Fortitude <note>original: "dhṛti"</note> means courage. "Intellect" <note>original: "buddhi"</note> is wisdom born of the consideration of consequences. By these two, one "possesses" <note>original: "śālitā"</note>, meaning one is endowed with them. The essence of this is that "possession" of these qualities constitutes Majesty. By the word **Capability**, he means strength born of the body. By the word **Valor**, he means the exertion or effort used in the "restraint" <note>subdual</note> of enemies. || 58 || **Regarding Hope:** By the word "Hope" <note>original: "āśā"</note>, desire is meant. By the word "Faith" <note>original: "śraddhā"</note>, a mind inclined toward <term>Dharma</term> <note>righteousness or religious duty</note> is meant. By the word "Bondage" <note>original: "bandha"</note>, dependence on others or a state of being subject to another is meant. By the phrase "falling of water," he means rain; or alternatively, a sudden immersion into water. || 59 || **Regarding Mental Distress:** By the word "Agony" <note>original: "tāpa"</note>, he means "Mental Distress" <note>original: "ādhi"</note>. "Sluggishness" <note>original: "māndya"</note> is the name for "Physical Disease" <note>original: "vyādhi"</note>, which causes pain to the body. Regarding the word "Kinsmen" <note>original: "bandhu/sapiṇḍa"</note>...

## ...the close relatives <note>original: "sapindas"</note> are declared. By the word "merchant" <note>original: "vaṇik"</note>, a livelihood of "truth and falsehood" <note>original: "satyālīka-vṛtti"</note> is meant. This refers to living through a combination of honest trade and the flexible ethics of the marketplace. By the word "progeny" <note>original: "prasava"</note>, the act of conception <note>original: "ādhānam"</note> is meant. || 60 || "Servitude" <note>original: "dāsatvam"</note> is expressed by the term "working for others" <note>original: "para-karma"</note>. Other terms are
## clear from their own names. They should be described according to lineage, caste, and nature as appropriate. || 61 || Regarding "favorable connection" <note>original: "śubhayoga"</note>: when a Lot <note>original: "sahama"</note> is seen or joined by a beneficial planet, then there is happiness for the lord <note>of the Lot</note>. The happiness produced by that Lot is known according to the strength of the lord of the Lot. Specifically: when the lord of the Lot is strong, there is great happiness. When it has moderate strength, there is moderate happiness. When it is weak, there is sorrow.

# Servitude is called "working for others" <note>original: "para-karma"</note>; other terms are clear by their own names. They should be described as appropriate to the lineage, caste, and nature <note>of the person</note>. || 61 || From favorable connections or aspects, happiness comes according to the 
# strength of the lord <note>of the Lot</note>. Poverty, extreme dullness, enemies, and strife indicate the opposite. || 62 || Even at the time of a query, the Lot should be considered according to the inquirer's desire. The use of all these 
# is for the various things that people ask about. || 63 || Thus ends the consideration of the fruits of the Lots. Having determined the precise planets and the Ascendant, excluding the signs <note>original: "rāśīn vinā"</note>, keep only the smallest degrees. 
# Arrange them in increasing order of degrees; this becomes the sequence of the planetary periods <note>original: "daśā-krama"</note> for the year. || 1 ||

## This is the meaning. Poverty, extreme dullness, enemies, and strife indicate the opposite. The fruit of these Lots should be understood as inverted. For instance, when a good result is indicated, it <note>under bad conditions</note> should be known as bad; when an unfavorable result is indicated, it should be known as a good
## result. || 62 || Regarding how the consideration of good and bad Lots in the birth chart and the fifth house should be performed, he says: "At the time of query" <note>original: "praśna-kāle"</note>. The word "Lot" <note>original: "sahama"</note> is used here in a collective sense meaning "all Lots."
## Those Lots, such as the Lot of Merit <note>original: "Puṇya-sahama"</note>, should be considered according to the wish of the inquirer <note>the person asking the astrologer a question</note>. Here, there is a use for all the Lots because people ask about various and diverse things. || 63 || Thus ends the consideration of the fruits of the Lots. Now, the calculation of the planetary periods <note>original: "daśā-nayana"</note> is stated.
## First, he explains the calculation of the "Minute Degrees" <note>original: "kṛśāṃśa"</note>. "The precise ones" <note>original: "spaṣṭān"</note>: at the time of the solar return <note>original: "varṣa-praveśa"</note>, having fixed the precise planets and the Ascendant, and setting aside the signs, all the degrees and minutes should be placed. Among them, the smallest degrees...

<summary>This page concludes the definitions of various "Lots" (Sahamas), emphasizing that their results depend on the strength of their ruling planets and can be used for specific birth-chart analysis or answering specific questions (horary astrology). --- Page 99 ---
<meta>This page transitions from the general definitions of astrological points (Sahamas) to the technical calculation of planetary periods (Dashas) for the year. It explains the specific Tajika method of "Patyamsha," which partitions the year based on the longitudinal degrees of the planets.</meta>

The planet used for subtraction should be placed first. Then, place the planets in increasing order of their degrees. Specifically, however many degrees a planet has, they should be arranged in that increasing order along with the rising sign <note>original: "lagna"</note>. This establishes the order of the planetary periods <note>original: "dasha"</note> for the year. || 1 || Now, the author describes the calculation of the "Portion-Degrees" <note>original: "pātyāṃśa"</note>. "The lower," etc. The lower or smaller degree reached by a planet should be subtracted sequentially from the planet with higher degrees. 

For example: first, the planets are arranged in order of their degrees. Then, they are subtracted in the order of first, second, third, and so on. After that, when the remainders of the purified degrees <note>original: "viśuddhāṃśa"</note> are totaled one by one after mutual subtraction, the sum of those remainders will be exactly equal to the highest degree among all planets (the final planet). This is the universal rule. Subsequently, the year—whether the **Solar** <note>original: "saurī"</note> year of 360 days or the **Civil** <note>original: "sāvanī"</note> year of 365 days, 15 minutes, 31 seconds, and 30 thirds—is the dividend to be divided by this sum of remainder degrees. The result obtained...

## Sequentially subtract the lower from the higher; the sum of the remainders of the purified degrees <note>original: "viśuddhāṃśaka"</note> will be equal to the highest degree of all. The duration of the year is the dividend for this sum. || 2 ||

## Multiply those purified degrees by this obtained constant <note>original: "dhruvāṅka"</note>; this will indeed be the duration of that planet’s period <note>original: "daśā"</note> in days and so on. One should then declare the results, whether good or bad, from the scriptures. || 3 ||

## If the purified degrees are equal, the period of the stronger planet comes first. If the strength <note>original: "bala"</note> is equal, then the one with fewer degrees <note>original: "alpagati"</note> comes first. If those are equal, the period is considered based on the planet's relationship to the rising sign <note>original: "vilagna"</note> or the strength of the lord of the rising sign. || 4 ||

...is the **constant** <note>original: "dhruvāṅka"</note>. || 2 || Now he describes the method for calculating the days. "The purified degrees," etc. All the purified degrees should be multiplied by this obtained constant. By doing this, the measure of the period <note>original: "daśā"</note> in days and so on for each respective planet is found. Having calculated the Dasha days in this way, one should state the auspicious or inauspicious results according to the scriptures. || 3 || 

Now he describes the determination of the period when degrees are equal. "In the equality of purified degrees," etc. When the degrees of two planets are equal, the one that is stronger in the **five-fold strength** <note>original: "pañca-vargī-bala"; a specific Tajika method of weighing planetary power</note> calculation has the first period. 

<margin>Note: Here, the reading "equality of purified degrees" <note>original: "śuddhāṃśa-sāmye"</note> is mentioned in the text, but the reading "equality of lower degrees" <note>original: "hīnāṃśa-sāmye"</note> is more logical, consistent with the mathematical rules, and accepted by all authorities.</margin>

In this system, the order of the periods is determined by the lower degrees first. After that, the rule is that the "purified degrees" are formed through the process of subtraction. If the purified degrees of two planets are equal, the one with more strength has the first period. After that, the second. How should this be handled? He says: "In equality of strength..."

<summary>This page explains the mathematical method for calculating "Patyamsha Dashas," which are specific time periods assigned to planets during a single year. It details how to subtract planetary degrees to find "purified degrees" and how to use a mathematical constant to convert these degrees into a specific number of days.</summary>
<keywords>Patyamsha Dasha, planetary periods, Shuddhamsha, purified degrees, Dhruvanka, mathematical constant, Pancha-vargi-bala, five-fold strength, Tajika astrology</keywords>

One should know that the first period belongs to the planet with the lowest motion <note>or degree</note>. In the event that a planet and the Ascendant <note>original: "vilagna"</note> have the exact same degrees, the text says this: regarding the equality of degrees between a planet <note>original: "khaga"</note> and the Ascendant, one must consider factors such as strength, or the speed of the planet belonging to the Lord of the Ascendant.

In the calculation of Solar Periods <note>original: "saura-daśā"</note>, the entry into the first period occurs exactly at the moment of the Year-Entry <note>original: "varṣapraveśa"</note>. Below that calculation, the precise position of the Sun <note>original: "spaṣṭa-sūrya"</note> at the time of Year-Entry should be written. To find the starting time for subsequent periods, the days of the first period must be added to the Sun's position at the time of the entry.

It is done as follows: The degrees of the Sun should be treated as days to be added; minutes <note>original: "kalā"</note> should be treated as *ghatikas* <note>units of 24 minutes</note> to be added; and seconds <note>original: "vikalā"</note> should be treated as *palas* <note>units of 24 seconds</note> to be added. Seconds divided by sixty should have their quotient added to the minutes. Minutes divided by sixty should have their quotient added to the degrees. Degrees divided by thirty should have their quotient added to the zodiac signs...

<meta>This page continues the technical explanation of Tajika astrology, specifically focusing on the calculation of planetary periods (Dashas) for a solar return chart. It bridges the theoretical rules for Solar and Civil year-measures with a concrete mathematical example using specific planetary positions.</meta>

<margin>Ta. Ni.</margin>
<margin>॥ 49 ॥</margin>
...should be applied. If the number of signs <note>original: "rāśayaḥ"</note> exceeds twelve, they should be divided by twelve, and the remaining signs should then be added to the Sun's position at the time of entry into the second period. This same method applies to the Sun's position at the entry of the second period and so on for the subsequent ones. By adding in this manner, the final position of the Sun will equal the Sun’s position at the moment the year began <note>original: "varṣapraveśa"</note>. If they match, then the sequence of the **Solar Period** <note>original: "sauradaśā"</note> is correct; otherwise, it is incorrect.

Now, regarding the **Civil Period** <note>original: "sāvanadaśā"</note>: at the moment the year begins, the first Civil Period commences. One should add the days of the first Civil Period to the weekday and time of the year's commencement; the entry into the second period occurs on that specific weekday and time within that month. Then, the days of the second period are added to the weekday and time of the second period's entry to find the entry of the third period, and so on. If the final calculation results in the weekday and time of the following year's commencement, then the period entries are correct;
<margin>Section</margin>
<margin>1</margin>
otherwise, they are incorrect.

**Example** <note>original: "udāharaṇam"</note>:
Saturn has the fewest degrees <note>original: "aṃśa"</note> among all the factors. Therefore, ignoring the signs, Saturn is placed first at 6|48|54. Then the Sun <note>original: "arka"</note> at 7|30|6; then Mercury at 12|26|18; then Mars at 14|39|36; then the Ascendant <note>original: "lagna"</note> at 18|3|54; then the Moon at 22|9|47; then Venus at 24|31|48; and finally Jupiter at 29|35|13.

First, the degrees of Saturn are taken as they are: 6|48|54. These are subtracted from the Sun’s degrees (7|30|6) to yield the Sun’s **Portion-Degrees** <note>original: "pātyāṃśāḥ"</note> of 0|41|12. In this way, each preceding value is subtracted from the next to find the Portion-Degrees for all: Mercury is 4|46|2; Mars is 2|16|18; the Ascendant is 3|31|18; the Moon is 4|5|53; Venus is 2|22|1; and Jupiter is 5|3|25. The sum of these is 29|35|13.

With a year-measure of 360 days <note>the "Civil" or Savana year</note>, converted into minutes <note>original: "ṣaṣṭisavarṇitau," referring to base-60 mathematical reduction</note>, the dividend and divisor are 1,296,000. Dividing by the total degrees (converted to a common denominator) yields a **multiplier** <note>original: "guṇaka"</note> of 12|10|3. Multiplying Saturn’s Portion-Degrees (6|48|54) by this multiplier results in **Saturn’s period days**: 82|55|18.

Alternatively, using the **Solar year-measure** of 365 days, 15 minutes, and 31 seconds <note>original: "365|15|31"</note> divided by the total Portion-Degrees (29|35|13), the multiplier becomes 12|20|42. Multiplying Saturn's Portion-Degrees (6|48|54) by this results in **Saturn’s period days**: 84|7|52. The days for all other planetary periods are calculated in this same manner. 4.
<margin>॥ 49 ॥</margin>

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for Tajika astrology, moving from the calculation of major planetary periods (Dashas) to the calculation of sub-periods (Antardashas) and the specific moments when months and days begin within the solar return year.</meta>

# Now, the Calculation of Sub-periods is Explained

Now, the method for bringing forth the sub-periods <note>original: "antardaśānayana"</note> is explained. Regarding the "measure of the periods" <note>original: "daśāmānām"</note>: the meaning is clear. Within whichever planet's major period a sub-period is being calculated, that period's duration—consisting of days, *ghatis*, and *palas* <note>traditional Indian units of time: 1 ghati = 24 minutes; 1 pala = 24 seconds</note>—should be treated as the whole, and the sub-period should be calculated according to the rule previously stated. 

**Example** <note>original: "udāharaṇam"</note>:
Saturn's major period <note>original: "śanidaśā"</note> is 82 | 55 | 18. This is divided by the sum of the fractional degrees <note>original: "pātyāṃśayogena"</note>, which is 29 | 35 | 13. The resulting multiplier <note>original: "guṇaka"</note> is 2 | 48 | 9. By multiplying Saturn’s specific fractional degrees by this multiplier, the sub-period of Saturn within its own major period is found to be 19 | 55 | 58. In this same manner, the sub-periods for all other planets within Saturn’s major period are calculated. || 5 ||

# Now, the Calculation of the Entry into Months and Days is Explained

Now, the method for calculating the entry into months and days is explained. Regarding the phrase "one by one" <note>original: "ekaiketi"</note>: The position of the Sun and other planets at the time of birth is given in signs, degrees, etc. This position is set down in eleven places. By adding one, two, or three signs respectively, one finds the time (in months, *ghatis*, and *palas*) when the Sun reaches those exact degrees and minutes. That time is known as the "Month-Entry" <note>original: "māsapraveśa"</note>. This is what is meant:

-> Considering the measure of the major periods as the whole, the sub-periods should be calculated by the path previously described, according to the fractional degrees. Initially, the sub-period belongs to the lord of the major period, and the others follow in order. || 5 || <-

-> If the Sun <note>original: "ravi"</note> reaches the same degrees and minutes through the increase of one sign at a time, that is known as the Month-Entry; if it matches the minutes [by adding degrees], it is the Day-Entry. || 6 || <-

-> Calculate the difference between the Sun's position for that month and the position of the Sun in the nearest row of the almanac. Convert this to minutes <note>original: "kalīkṛtya"</note> and, using the rate of motion, add or subtract it from the days and times. || 7 || <-

-> Find the weekday associated with that row. If the month’s Sun is more or less than the almanac’s Sun, the entry into the month or the entry into the day occurs on that weekday and time. || 8 || <-

The first "Month-Entry" is simply the "Year-Entry" <note>original: "varṣapraveśa"</note> itself. In that case, the Sun is at the exact same position as it was at birth. If one is calculating the second Month-Entry, one adds a single sign <note>30 degrees</note> to the birth position. The day and time when the Sun reaches those exact degrees, minutes, and seconds is the moment of the second Month-Entry. This should be understood for the subsequent months as well. If calculating the "Day-Entry" <note>original: "dinapraveśa"</note>, one adds one degree at a time; the moment the Sun reaches that position is the Day-Entry. 

Now, the method for deriving the *ghatis* and other units for the Month-Entry from the almanac <note>original: "pañcāṅga"</note> is described: "Of the Month-Sun..." <note>original: "māsārkasya"</note>. The Sun for the Month-Entry is produced by the addition of one sign. Using the Sun's position found in the nearest entry of the almanac <unclear>...</unclear>

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for Tajika astrology, specifically focusing on the precise calculation of the "Month-Entry" (Masapravesha). It discusses the mathematical corrections required to adjust calculations from standard almanacs (like those based on the Kashi/Varanasi meridian) to the specific birthplace of the individual.</meta>

<margin>
Note: 50
</margin>

The difference between the two Suns <note>the Sun's position at birth versus its position in the current almanac</note> should be calculated. Convert that difference into minutes of arc <note>original: "kalā"</note>. This should be divided by the daily motion of the Sun at that time. The resulting figures—consisting of days, *ghatis*, and *palas* <note>traditional units of time: 1 ghati = 24 minutes; 1 pala = 24 seconds</note>—should be taken. This result must be added to or subtracted from the day and time of the reference point.

To explain: If the Sun in the almanac at the reference time is greater than the required Month-Entry Sun, then the result should be added to the day and time of the reference point. If it is less, it should be subtracted. At that resulting time (expressed in weekdays, *ghatis*, and *palas*), the Month-Entry <note>original: "māsapraveśa"</note> occurs. 

In the same way, the entry into half-months <note>fortnights</note> and days should be understood. Regarding the phrase "from the day and time of the reference point": the *ghatis* of the reference point should not be taken directly. Why? Because the almanac <note>original: "pañcāṅga"</note> of a specific village or town already includes the corrections for local longitude and ascensional difference <note>original: "cara-deśāntara-saṃskāra"</note>. For this reason, one must take the "Year-lord" <term>Abdapa<gloss>The planetary ruler of the solar return year</gloss></term> as it is, free from those local corrections, in days, *ghatis*, and *palas*. 

This is how it is done: The Year-lord should be calculated from the same astronomical treatise <note>original: "grantha"</note> used to determine the Sun's position in the almanac. However, there is a specific distinction: if the treatise's calculation of the Year-lord already includes corrections for the equation of time, the solar cycle, or the local ascensional difference <note>original: "udayantara, bhuja-phala, cara-saṃskāra"</note>, then the Year-lord must be rendered "correction-free." For example, in the treatise *Ramavinoda*, the Year-lord calculation already includes corrections for the equation of time, solar cycle, and the local latitude of Kashi <note>modern-day Varanasi</note>. Similarly, in the *Makaranda* treatise, the Year-lord calculation includes the correction for Kashi's latitude. 

Therefore, one must first obtain the Year-lord without these corrections. After that, for the person whose Month-Entry is being calculated, one must apply the specific corrections for their *birthplace*—namely the local longitude, latitude/ascensional difference, and the equation of time. That Year-lord then becomes "precise" <note>original: "sūkṣma"</note>. These four corrections should be applied to the Year-lord in the same way they were applied to the Sun at the time of birth. 

Then, the difference previously calculated between the Month-Entry Sun and the reference Sun should be added to or subtracted from this Year-lord as described. This yields the precise *ghatis* of the Month-Entry. If the birth Sun was not calculated using *Ramavinoda*, but the reference Sun in the almanac *was* calculated from *Ramavinoda*—or if both birth and reference Suns were calculated using *Makaranda*—then the result is precise. If different treatises are mixed, the result will be approximate <note>original: "sthūla"</note>. 

### An Example Calculation
According to the treatise *Grahalaghava*, the true Sun at birth is 9 signs, 7 degrees, 30 minutes, and 6 seconds. Adding one sign, the second Month-Entry Sun becomes 10s 7° 30' 6". The closest date to this is the 9th day of the dark fortnight of the month Phalguna <note>original: "phālguna-kṛṣṇa-navamī"</note>. In the *Ramavinoda*-based almanac, the reference Sun for this date is 10s 10° 1' 38". The difference between them is 0s 2° 31' 32". This converted into minutes is 151 minutes and 32 seconds. This is divided by the Sun's daily motion, which is 60 minutes and 31 seconds...

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for determining the Lord of the Year (Abdapa). While the previous page established that the Lord of the Year must be calculated without local geographic corrections (like longitude), this page provides the specific mathematical steps using the "Ramavinoda" tables, an astronomical handbook composed around 1590 CE by Ramachandra for the Mughal official Raja Man Singh.</meta>

### Calculation of the Lord of the Year

The dividend and divisor have been standardized: the dividend is 90.92 and the divisor is 36.31. Dividing the dividend by the divisor, the result is 2 days, 30 *ghatis*, and 13 *palas* <note>2 | 30 | 13. Traditional time: 1 ghati = 24 minutes, 1 pala = 24 seconds</note>.

Now, the method for finding the <term>Abdapa<gloss>Lord of the Year; the planet ruling the moment the Sun enters Aries for that year</gloss></term> is described, specifically when using the planets as positioned in the **Ramavinoda** almanac. 

**The Calculation:**
The year in which the *Ramavinoda* was composed is the <term>Saka Era<gloss>A historical calendar era beginning in 78 CE</gloss></term> 1512 <note>roughly 1590 CE</note>. This is subtracted from the desired Saka year of 1550 <note>roughly 1628 CE</note>. The remainder is 38 "elapsed years" <note>original: "gatābdāḥ"</note>. 

The specific constant for this calculation is 1 | 15 | 31 | 24 <note>This represents the weekday and time increment for one solar year</note>. Multiplying this constant by the 38 elapsed years results in 47 | 49 | 57 | 53 | 12. To this, the "interpolation value" <note>original: "vyamkṣepa"</note> of 0 | 24 | 23 is added, totaling 48 | 14 | 20 | 53 | 12. 

Dividing the top number by seven <note>to find the day of the week</note>, the remainder indicates the Lord of the Year is 5 | 56 | 57 <note>The 5th day, which is Thursday, at specific time units</note>. However, because this value already includes corrections for the "Equation of Time," "Equation of the Center," and "Ascensional Difference," these must now be removed to find the "raw" Year-Lord.

---

->Earth (1), Five-Moons (15), Earth-Qualities (31), Earth-Ramas (31), and the Perfected (24) multiplied by the elapsed years... combined with the zeros, the oceans (4), and the Twin gods (2)...<-
->multiplied by the seven fires (37?), this is how the Lord of the Year is known starting from the Sun.<-

---

Regarding the first correction, the "Equation of Time" <note>original: "udayāntara"</note>: In the year Saka 1512, at the moment of the <term>Mesha Sankranti<gloss>The Sun's entry into the zodiac sign of Aries</gloss></term>, the Sun was at 0 degrees. The <term>Ayanamsha<gloss>The value for the precession of the equinoxes</gloss></term> was 16° 24' 54". Based on the *Brahma-tulya* system, the Equation of Time was calculated as a negative 13 *palas*. 

However, it is stated that for the Lord of the Year, the correction is applied in reverse. Thus, the 13 *palas* become positive. Subtracting this from the previously calculated Lord of the Year, the value without the Equation of Time is 6 | 14 | 8. 

Next is the "Equation of the Center" <note>original: "bhujaphala"</note>: The Sun’s corrective value is 2 | 7. Converting this to minutes of arc gives 127. Dividing by 360, the result in *ghatis* is a positive 0 | 21. Applying the "reverse" rule, this becomes a negative 0 | 21. Adding this to the previous figure of 6 | 14 | 8 results in 6 | 14 | 29. 

Finally, the "Ascensional Difference" <note>original: "carasaṃskāra"</note>: For the city of **Kashi** <note>Modern Varanasi</note>, the latitude <note>original: "palaṃkṣā"</note> is 5 | 45. The correction factors are 57 | 46 | 19, resulting in a time correction of 31. Again, applying the "reverse" rule, this becomes a positive 31. Adding this to the previous figure of 6 | 14 | 29 gives the final corrected Lord of the Year: 6 | 15 | 58. 

In this manner, the Lord of the Year is correctly determined with all necessary adjustments.

This much has been demonstrated for the sake of providing an example. Now, the **easy method** is described. 

Having derived the values for the <term>Equation of Time<gloss>udayāntara; the difference between apparent solar time and mean solar time</gloss></term>, the <term>Solar Correction<gloss>su-phala; a correction for the sun's position</gloss></term>, and the <term>Ascensional Difference<gloss>cara; correction for the observer's latitude</gloss></term> from the previous text, and having performed the mutual <term>refinement<gloss>saṃskāra; mathematical correction</gloss></term> of these three, a value of 23 <term>palas<gloss>units of time equal to 24 seconds</gloss></term> is produced as a <term>negative value<gloss>ṛṇa; literally 'debt,' meaning it must be subtracted</gloss></term>. These 23 palas are fixed. 

In the desired year, if the Lord of the Year is calculated by the previously mentioned verse, then these 23 palas should be subtracted from the year-units of that calculation. Thus, the Lord of the Year for the desired year becomes free of these corrections. For example, the previously calculated Lord of the Year was 6 | 24 | 21 <note>6 days, 24 ghatis, 21 palas</note>. When 23 palas are subtracted from this, the resulting Lord of the Year, refined of these corrections, is 6 | 13 | 58.

### Alternatively: Method from the Ramavinoda Almanac

Alternatively, the author describes how to derive the Lord of the Year, free of the three corrections, for a desired year using the **Ramavinoda** <note>A famous astronomical table composed by Ramachandra in 1590 CE for Raja Man Singh</note>. He says: "From the total years..." The year in which the *Ramavinoda* was composed is <term>Saka Era<gloss>A historical calendar era starting in 78 CE</gloss></term> 1512. This is subtracted from the desired Saka year 1550.

> ->Multiply the elapsed years by 207, and again multiply the<-
> ->elapsed years by 14. Divide by 45. To the resulting ghatis,<-
> ->add 320. Divide the sum by 800. Add the elapsed years to<-
> ->this result and divide by seven; the remainder is the Lord of the Year starting from Aries.<-

The elapsed years are 38. These are multiplied by the constant 207, resulting in 7866. The elapsed years (38) are then multiplied by 14, resulting in 532. This is divided by 45, yielding 11 <term>ghatis<gloss>24-minute units</gloss></term> and 49 palas. This is added to the previous figure of 7866, making it 7866 | 11 | 49. To this top number, the constant 320 is added, totaling 8186 | 11 | 49. 

This is divided by 800. The quotient is 10 days, 3 ghatis, and 58 palas. To this top number, the 38 elapsed years are added, totaling 48 | 3 | 58. When divided by seven <note>to find the day of the week</note>, the remainder indicates the Lord of the Year is 6 | 3 | 58 <note>Friday at the specified time</note>. 

Because the corrections for the <term>Ascensional Difference<gloss>cara</gloss></term> and <term>Longitude<gloss>deśāntara</gloss></term> were already applied to the Mean Sun at the time of birth, they must be applied to this Lord of the Year for one’s own location. The Ascensional Difference of the Sun is 78 palas. Divided by sixty, this is 1 ghati and 18 palas. Since the rule states this is "inverse," it becomes a negative value of 1 | 18. Subtracting this from the Lord of the Year (6 | 3 | 58), the <term>latitude-corrected<gloss>cara-saṃskṛta</gloss></term> Lord of the Year is 6 | 2 | 40. 

The longitude distance is 30 units. Subtracting one-fourth of this gives 22 palas. Since the rule states this is "inverse," it becomes a negative value of 22 palas. Subtracting these from the latitude-corrected Lord of the Year...
