<meta>This page continues the technical mathematical demonstration for calculating the "Month Entrance" (Māsa-praveśa). It follows a discussion on the Lord of the Year (Abdapa) and addresses a common error where practitioners mix data from different astronomical source texts.</meta>

<header>Collection of 81 Year Old Dina Nath Raina, Jammu. Retired Priest.</header>

From the <term>Lord of the Year<gloss>abdapa; the planetary ruler of the year</gloss></term> (6 | 12 | 18) which has been <term>refined<gloss>saṃskṛta; mathematically corrected</gloss></term> by one's own <term>longitudinal and latitudinal corrections<gloss>cara-deśāntara</gloss></term>, we calculate the interval between the <term>Boundary Sun<gloss>avadhistha-arka; the solar position at the start of the period</gloss></term> and the **Month Entrance Sun**. The previously established value for the day and time units is 2 | 30 | 53. Because the Boundary Sun is less than the Month Sun, the refined Lord of the Year (6 | 12 | 18) is subtracted, resulting in the **Time of Month Entrance**: 4 | 25. Thus, on **Tuesday, the 6th day of the dark fortnight of the month of Phalguna**, the rough <term>ghatikas<gloss>units of 24 minutes</gloss></term> for the second month entrance are 4 | 25. 

Why is this? The solar position at the time of birth was calculated using the **Grahalaghava** <note>A famous astronomical treatise written by Ganesha Daivajna in 1520 CE</note>, whereas the Boundary Sun was taken from an almanac based on the **Ramavinoda** <note>A set of astronomical tables used for creating calendars</note>. If the calculation for both were performed using a single text, then <term>precise ghatikas<gloss>sūkṣma-ghaṭikā</gloss></term> would be produced. 

Therefore, without careful thought, because of the differences between texts, everyone calculates the Month Entrance using the "easy method" based on the <term>Almanac<gloss>pañcāṅga</gloss></term>. **That is incorrect.** 

Now, the author describes the calculation of the **precise ghatikas**. From whichever text the birth-time Sun was calculated—using however many refinements—the Sun for the Month Entrance on the day of entrance must be calculated from that same text using the same refinements. The knowledge of the day of the Month Entrance can be estimated using the Almanac. By adding one <term>Sign<gloss>rāśi; 30 degrees of the zodiac</gloss></term> to the birth Sun, the Sun for the Month Entrance is produced. Then, one must find the difference between the Sun on the day of entrance and that Month Entrance Sun. Convert that difference into <term>minutes of arc<gloss>kalā</gloss></term>. The <term>true daily motion<gloss>spaṣṭa-gati</gloss></term> of the Sun calculated for the time of entrance should then divide those minutes. The resulting quotient, in days, ghatikas, and palas, provides the **precise time units** for the Month Entrance.

**Example:**
The <term>True Sun<gloss>spaṣṭa-arka</gloss></term> refined by longitudinal and latitudinal corrections via the *Grahalaghava* is 9 | 7 | 30 | 6 <note>9 signs, 7 degrees, 30 minutes, 6 seconds</note>. Adding one sign, the Sun for the second month entrance becomes 10 | 7 | 30 | 6. On **Tuesday, the 6th day of the dark fortnight of Phalguna**, the <term>Mean Sun<gloss>madhyamārka</gloss></term> at sunrise according to the *Grahalaghava* day-count (3624) is 10 | 5 | 7 | 55. Adding the longitude correction of 22 <term>seconds of arc<gloss>vikalā</gloss></term>, it becomes 10 | 5 | 8 | 17. The <term>solar anomaly<gloss>manda-kendra</gloss></term> is 4 | 21 | 51 | 43. The <term>equation of center<gloss>manda-phala</gloss></term> is a positive value of 1 | 35 | 49. The refined Sun is 10 | 6 | 44 | 6. Adding the <term>ascensional difference<gloss>cara</gloss></term> of 1 | 24, the True Sun on the day of the month entrance is 10 | 6 | 45 | 30. 

The true daily motion is 60 | 35. The difference between the two [the target Sun and the current Sun] is 0 | 0 | 44 | 36. These minutes are 44 | 36. When divided by the motion of 60 | 35 (after converting both to the same units: dividend 2676 and divisor 3635), the quotient in days, ghatikas, and palas is 0 | 44 | 10. 

Thus, on **Tuesday, the 6th day of the dark fortnight of Phalguna**, the second month entrance occurs at **44 ghatikas and 10 palas** after sunrise. If one calculates in this manner, the ghatikas for the month entrance will be precise. At this moment, the Sun is 10 | 7 | 20 | 6, which is [nearly] equal to the previous value. The same should be understood for future calculations. By this... <note>original: "अनया" (anayā), the catchword for the next page</note>

<meta>This page concludes the introductory section of the Samjna-tantra (System of Definitions). Following the description of his lineage on the previous page, Nilakantha now identifies himself and his parents before the commentator, Vishvanatha, provides the formal closing and date of the commentary.</meta>

...the meaning is "for the sake of." For what purpose? For the **satisfaction of the virtuous** who appreciate merit—specifically, for the satisfaction of astronomers. Similarly, having drawn upon the <term>Manual of Birth Horoscopy<gloss>jātaka-paddhati; a technical treatise used to determine the auspicious and inauspicious results of a child's birth based on the time of delivery</gloss></term>, which is the science that describes favorable and unfavorable outcomes based on the time of birth, he [Nilakantha] has set forth this composition which establishes the mathematical foundations. 

If one asks, "Why did he create this work when manuals by authorities like **Shripati** already exist?" he replies by saying: **"Having refuted flawed views."** This means he has set aside and corrected those doctrines that were contrary to logic by providing specific critiques. || 2 ||

Now, he describes himself and introduces the **System of Definitions** (Samjna-tantra):

By **Padmamba**—this means by the mother named Padma. From the astronomer **Ananta**, a son named **Shri Nilakantha** was born, endowed with the splendor of great scholarship. What sort of son? A wise scholar, devoted to the **Vedas** and the sacred scriptures. He has composed the **Discernment of Definitions** (Samjna-viveka). What kind of work is it? It is:

->This wise **Shri Nilakantha**, devoted to the Vedas and scriptures, was born of **Padma**. He composed the **Discernment of Definitions** together with the **Ornament of the Lot Points**,<-
->which brings joy to the learned and to Lord Shiva. || 3 || Thus ends the System of Definitions in the *Tajika Nilakanthi*. || 1 ||<-

"With the ornament" means the work is adorned by the science describing the <term>Lot Points<gloss>sahama; specific sensitive points in a horoscope calculated by the distance between two planets or points added to the ascendant, a technique characteristic of the Persian-influenced Tajika system</gloss></term>. In this book, he has thoroughly defined the consideration of these Lot Points. Furthermore, what is the nature of this work? It is "pleasing to the learned and to Shiva," meaning it gives joy to the scholar named Shiva <note>likely a reference to a specific colleague or patron</note> or to the deity Lord Shiva himself. || 3 || 

Thus ends the explanation and the examples for the Chapter on Lot Points within the **System of Definitions** (Samjna-tantra), composed by the illustrious astronomer **Nilakantha**, as written by **Vishvanatha**, the son of the astronomer **Divakara**. 

This "Illuminator of the System of Definitions" was created by **Vishvanatha**. 
May this commentary cause the assembly of the virtuous to feel a bond of grace. || 1 ||

In the year **1551** <note>original: "candrabāṇa-śaracandra," a chronogram where Moon=1, Arrows=5, Arrows=5, Moon=1, read right-to-left as 1551</note> of the **Shalivahana era** <note>approximately 1629 CE</note>, on the fifth day of the bright half of the month of **Margashirsha** <note>occurring in November/December</note>, this was completed by the scholar **Vishvanatha**. || 2 ||

# Salutations to Lord Ganesha
<note>The text begins with a traditional invocation. Ganesha is the elephant-headed deity of wisdom and the "remover of obstacles," traditionally invoked at the start of any Sanskrit literary or scientific work.</note>

I remember in my heart that Lord of Obstacles, Ganesha, whose lotus feet are constantly bowed to by gods, perfected beings, and demons for the attainment of their desired goals; who is dear to his devotees, and who has the face of an elephant. || 1 ||

On the banks of the <term>Godavari<gloss>A major river in central-southern India, often called the "Ganges of the South"</gloss></term> shines the very beautiful village of Gola. There lived my ancestors, who were like suns to the lotus of the <term>Siddhanta<gloss>The foundational astronomical treatises that form the basis of the Indian calendar and planetary calculations</gloss></term>. || 2 ||

Divakara was famous on this earth, served by the best of the twice-born <note>original: "dvijendraih", referring to Brahmins</note> skilled in many sciences; he was the greatest among those who know the fates, and had a great taste for the scriptures of <term>Vedanta<gloss>The philosophical school focused on the ultimate nature of reality</gloss></term>. || 3 ||

He had five sons endowed with virtues. The first among them was Krishna, a supreme astrologer, knower of all sacred traditions, constantly devoted to the lotus feet of the Lord of the Universe. || 4 ||

His younger brothers were famous as the "crest-jewels" of scholars, known on earth as Vishnu and Malhari, masters of the <term>Three Branches<gloss>Tri-skandha: refers to the three parts of Jyotisha (astrology/astronomy)—Mathematical Astronomy (Siddhanta), Omenology (Samhita), and Horoscopy (Hora)</gloss></term>, and the elder brothers of the famous Shri Keshava. || 5 ||

His younger brother is Shri Vishvanatha the astrologer, the foremost among the learned in calculation, who has made the merchandise of the scriptures emerge through his service to Lord Malhari. He is a great forest for the growth of the true scriptures and their principles. || 6 ||

There was one named Nilakantha, the best of the twice-born, who had seen the far shore of the "Ocean of Astrology" and was a knower of the commentaries. I, Vishvanatha, having reflected upon it, am writing a commentary on the <term>Varshatantra<gloss>The "Yearly System," which focuses on predicting events for a specific year of a person's life</gloss></term> taught by him. || 7 ||

<note>Having finished the genealogy and the introduction of the author and commentator, the text moves into the core verses of the work.</note>

## Even the hosts of gods headed by Isha (Shiva), O Ruler, are not able to obtain their respective desires without your grace; I bow to that Dundhiraja (Ganesha), the cause of obtaining intelligence. || 1 ||

## The results of the planetary periods mentioned in the birth chart give results only for broad periods of time for men; the mind of the astrologer does not shine clearly there. Therefore, I speak of the yearly results from the original Tajika system. || 2 ||

<note>original: "ādi-tājikāt"</note>

At the beginning of the book, for the sake of the unobstructed completion of what he has begun, he composes a benediction to teach others. He says: **"Even the hosts..."** I, Nilakantha, bow to that Dundhiraja. What kind of Ganesha? He is the **cause of obtaining intelligence**. Intelligence is the intellect; its "attainment" is its acquisition; and he is the cause or reason for that. Why? Because without the grace or favor of this Dundhiraja, the hosts of gods—the groups of deities headed by Isha (the Great God Shiva)—are **not able** to obtain their **respective desires**, meaning their individual heart's wishes. This is said with certainty. This is heard in the Vedas and other scriptures; therefore, the praise of Ganesha is necessary. || 1 ||

Next, he declares the purpose and the beginning of the project with the verse starting **"The results..."** For that reason, the **yearly results** (the good and bad outcomes of the year) are taken from the original <term>Tajika<gloss>A system of Indian astrology influenced by Perso-Arabic traditions, specifically used for annual horoscopes (Solar Returns)</gloss></term> texts. The singular form "from the original Tajika" is used to refer to the entire category of earlier Tajika works...

<summary>This page introduces the Varsha Tantra section of the Tajika Nilakanthi, providing the lineage of the author Nilakantha and the commentator Vishvanatha. It explains that yearly astrology (Tajika) is necessary because standard birth chart readings are too broad for specific annual predictions.</summary>
<keywords>Ganesha, Nilakantha, Vishvanatha, Tajika, Varshatantra, Godavari, astrology, yearly predictions, genealogy</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the transition into the "Yearly System" (Varsha Tantra) of the Tajika Nilakanthi. Following the introductory salutations on the previous page, the commentator Vishvanatha now justifies why this specific branch of astrology—focused on yearly transitions—is necessary to supplement the general life-long predictions found in standard birth charts.</meta>

-> ता० नी० <-
-> ५५ <-
-> Section 2 <-

<meta>The text below explains the rationale for the Tajika system, noting that while general birth horoscopes provide long-term trends, they lack the precision needed for month-to-month or day-to-day life events.</meta>

"From the books" <note>original: "granthebhya"</note> means "I speak" <note>original: "bruve vadami"</note>. From what [cause]? Because the auspicious or inauspicious results of the planetary periods <term>Dasha<gloss>Specific blocks of time in an individual's life ruled by a particular planet</gloss></term> mentioned in the birth horoscopes <note>original: "jataka-shastra"</note> of men provide only general results <note>original: "sthula-kala-phala-dam"</note> over long durations, usually exceeding a year. This is clear. Since the life-giving periods of the planets arrive through calculations as groups of years, the intellect of the astrologers <note>original: "daivavid-matih"</note> does not shine brightly [in predicting] specific moments.

> [3] The passed years, multiplied by sixteen <note>original: "prakriti-ghna", where 'prakriti' numerically represents 16</note>, and added to the [calculated] groups... once the <term>Ghatis<gloss>A traditional Indian unit of time equal to 24 minutes</gloss></term> are obtained and added to the birth weekday and other factors...
> 
> [4] ...The weekday of the Year Entrance <note>original: "abda-pravesha"</note> should be indicated by the remainder after dividing by seven. The years multiplied by eleven, increased by the portion of the Sun, and divided by the remainder...
> 
> [5] ...The entrance of the year occurs on that lunar day when the Sun is exactly equal to the Sun at the time of birth.
> 
> [6] If the Sun is equal in degrees by an increase of one sign each, then it is the "Monthly Entrance." If it is equal in minutes <note>original: "kala-samah"</note>, it is the "Sub-Entrance."
> 
> [7] The wise should then calculate the positions of the planets for that moment, and clearly establish the Ascendant and the various houses <note>original: "bhava-gana"</note>. I shall now explain the reasoning for the strength of all the planets and the Lord of the Year.
> 
> [8] Twice the [Ascendant degrees]... <note>This verse details technical mathematical calculations for the "Munthaha" point.</note>
> 
> [9] The Lord of the Birth Ascendant, the Lord of the Year Ascendant, the Lord of the Progressed Point <term>Munthaha<gloss>A Persian-influenced mathematical point in Tajika astrology that moves exactly one zodiac sign per year</gloss></term>, the Lord of the Sun's sign during the day, and the Lord of the Moon's sign at night: these five should be considered.

What is the result? In a subtle investigation <note>original: "sukshma-vichare"</note>, the intellect is not clear; therefore, I speak this for the sake of knowing the results within the middle of a year. || 2 || The five verses starting with "The passed years" have been explained in the commentary on the "System of Definitions" <note>original: "Samjna Tantra"</note>. || 3 || 4 || 5 || 6 || 7 || 8 ||

Now, through three verses, he explains the determination of the "Year-Lord" <note>original: "varsha-ishvara"</note> which was promised. "Birth..." refers to the strongest planet and the equality of the aspect, and so on. The Lord of whichever sign was the Ascendant at the time of birth [is the first candidate]. The Lord of the Year Ascendant is the Lord of the Ascendant at the moment of the year's entrance...

<summary>This page explains that yearly astrology (Tajika) is needed for precise timing that general birth charts lack. It provides formulas for calculating the 'Yearly Entrance' (Solar Return) and introduces the five candidates for the 'Year Lord,' a key ruler of the person's upcoming year.</summary>

--- Page 114 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for calculating the Year Entrance (Varshapravesha). It focuses specifically on the "Five Officials" (Pancha-adhikari), five planetary candidates from which one is selected as the Year Lord (Varshasvami) to rule over the individual's year.</meta>

### Collection of 81 Year Old Dina Nath Raina, Jammu. Retired Priest.
<meta>CC-0. Courtesy Sanjay Raina, Jammu. Digitized by eGangotri</meta>

The **Lord of the Munthaha** <note>original: "munthahādhipa"</note> refers to the ruler of the specific calculated point <term>Munthaha<gloss>A mathematical point that moves one sign per year from the birth ascendant, unique to the Tajika system</gloss></term> which will be described later. Immediately following this is the **Lord of the Triplicity** <note>original: "trirāśipa"</note>, as defined in the *Sanjna Tantra* <note>a foundational chapter of the Tajika Nilakanthi text</note>. 

Then, if the Year Entrance occurs during the **day**, the **Lord of the Sun's Sign** <note>the ruler of the zodiac sign where the Sun is currently positioned</note> is considered. If the Year Entrance occurs at **night**, the **Lord of the Moon's Sign** <note>the ruler of the sign where the Moon is positioned</note> is considered. 

After examining these five candidates <note>the Lords of the Year Ascendant, the Birth Ascendant, the Munthaha, the Triplicity, and either the Sun or Moon sign</note>, the astrologer should determine which one among them "looks at" <note>aspects</note> the <term>Lagna<gloss>The Ascendant or the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment the year begins</gloss></term>. The one who observes the Ascendant and possesses the highest **Five-Fold Strength** <term>Panchavargi<gloss>A specific Tajika method of calculating planetary strength using five different criteria</gloss></term> becomes the **Year Lord** <note>original: "varshasvami"</note>. 

If a planet does not look at the Year Ascendant, even if it is the strongest, it usually cannot be the Year Lord. If strengths are equal, the one with the superior aspectual strength becomes the ruler of the year.

->The one among these who is strong and looks upon the Ascendant is the Year Lord; if they do not look at the Ascendant, they are not the Year Lord. If strengths are equal, the learned say the choice is made through the superiority of their aspect. [10]<-

->Even if the aspects and other factors are equal, or if all candidates are weak, the **Lord of the Munthaha** shall be the Year Lord. If none of the five officials look upon the Ascendant, the one with the highest strength must be carefully considered as the Year Lord. [11]<-

->If the strength and other factors are equal, some say the Lord of the Sun's sign rules by day and the Moon's sign by night. If the Moon forms an <term>Itthashala<gloss>A Tajika planetary aspect similar to the Persian 'it-tiṣāl', indicating a harmonious application of light between two planets</gloss></term> with the Year Lord, then the Lord of the Moon's sign is chosen; otherwise, it is different. [12]<-

The ancient teachers <note>original: "purātanāḥ ācāryāḥ"</note> have spoken thus. In the case of "equality of aspect" <note>original: "dr̥g-ādi-sāmye"</note>, the word "and others" implies that even when planetary strengths are equal or if the planets are weak and do not "see" the Ascendant, the determination is made through the <term>Panchavargi<gloss>The 25-point scale of five-fold strength</gloss></term>. 

In both such cases, the **Lord of the Munthaha** becomes the Year Lord. However, if all five officials fail to aspect the Year Ascendant, then the one among them who is simply the most powerful must be examined as the Year's master. If their strengths are equal, the Lord of the Munthaha is to be understood as the ruler. [11]

Now, another opinion regarding the specific possibility of the Moon's sign ruler being the Year Lord is given. When there is equality in strength and aspect among the five officials, if the year begins during the **day**, the ruler of the sign occupied by the **Sun** is the final authority and Year Lord. If the year begins at **night**, the ruler of the sign occupied by the **Moon** <note>original: "indurāśīndu"</note> is chosen.

<vocab>Munthaha (calculated year-point), Year Lord, Year Entrance, Five-fold Strength, Itthashala (planetary connection), Zodiac Sign, Ascendant</vocab>

<summary>This page explains the complex rules for selecting the "Year Lord" from five potential planetary candidates, prioritizing those that aspect the Year Ascendant and possess the highest calculated strength.</summary>
<keywords>Tajika Nilakanthi, Year Lord, Munthaha, Panchavargi, Itthashala, Varshapravesha, Hindu Astrology</keywords>

--- Page 115 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical discussion on determining the "Year Lord" (Varshasvami) for an annual horoscope. The previous page established the five candidates for this role; this page refines the selection process based on planetary aspects and strength, then describes the resulting effects on the individual's year.</meta>

<margin>
Tajika Nilakanthi
॥ 56 ॥
</margin>

...among those with authority, that planet becomes the Year Lord; this is what some teachers say. According to the previously mentioned method, if the Moon—having been identified as the Year Lord—is in a **Muthashila** <term>Muthashila<gloss>A Tajika aspect where a faster planet approaches a slower one within a specific range of degrees, indicating the fulfillment of an event</gloss></term> with another planet, then that other planet becomes the Year Lord. If the Moon forms a **Muthashila** with multiple planets, then among them, the one who possesses the greatest strength is chosen. If their strength is equal, the wise say the Year Lord is the ruler of the **Muthashila**. 

Regarding the decision when the Moon lacks a **Muthashila**, it is said: "Otherwise, in the absence of the Moon’s **Muthashila**, the ruler of the zodiac sign occupied by the Moon becomes the **Year Lord** <note>original: "varshēśvara"</note>." In this manner, if the Moon is in Cancer, the Moon itself is to be known as the Year Lord and no other. // 12 //

Next, the text describes the results based on the placement of the Year Lord:

<margin>॥ 56 ॥</margin>
->If the **Year Lord** <term>Abdadhipa<gloss>The planetary ruler of the specific year</gloss></term> is situated in a house other than the 12th, 6th, or 8th <note>these are considered "dusthanas" or difficult houses in astrology</note>, is visible <note>not "combust" or hidden by the Sun's rays</note>, and is equal in strength to its position in the birth chart, it bestows complete and excellent results: physical health, sovereignty, power, and extreme happiness. // 13 //<-

->When the Year Lord is full of strength, the auspicious results are full; when its strength is middling, the results are middling. If it is weak <note>literally "low" or "debased"</note>, there will be various types of sorrow, grief, enemies, and fears. // 14 //<-

The Year Lord is the planet ruling the year. "Situated in houses other than the 12th, 6th, and 8th" means it is placed in locations distinct from those three. Although it was previously stated that a Year Lord must "look at" <note>aspect</note> the Ascendant, it might seem improper to say it cannot be in the 6th, 8th, or 12th houses <note>as planets in these houses usually do not aspect the 1st house in this system</note>. However, even if a planet is in the 6th, 8th, or 12th houses, it is possible for it to be the Year Lord if it aspects the **Ascendant** <note>original: "tanu"</note> in the Rashi chart. Furthermore, its placement in those difficult houses within the **House Chart** <term>Bhava-kundali<gloss>A chart where house boundaries are calculated precisely, which can differ from the sign-based Rashi chart</gloss></term> is a possibility that even the Creator cannot prevent. 

"Is visible" <note>original: "labdhadayaḥ"</note> means that at the time the year begins, the planet has risen and is not "set" <note>astronomically obscured by the Sun</note>. "Equal in strength" refers to the **Five-Fold Strength** <term>Panchavargi Bala<gloss>A specific Tajika method of calculating planetary power using five criteria: residential, exaltation, bounds/hadd, decan, and navamsha</gloss></term> being comparable to its strength at the time of birth; it means the planet is not lacking in power. Such a Year Lord produces complete and excellent results, specifically regarding the "body" and so on, which is clear in the verse. // 13 // 

Next, it is said that the Year Lord's results follow the **Five-Fold Strength**: When the Year Lord is full of this strength, the good results are full; with medium strength, the good is medium...

<summary>This page explains how to finalize the selection of the Year Lord using the Muthashila aspect and describes the auspicious or inauspicious effects based on the planet's strength and house placement.</summary>
<keywords>Year Lord, Varshasvami, Muthashila, Panchavargi Bala, houses, health, prosperity, Tajika astrology</keywords>

--- Page 116 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical analysis of the Year Lord (Varshasvami). While the previous page focused on how to select which planet becomes the ruler of the year based on its strength and aspects to the Moon, this page details the specific life-outcomes (phalam) predicted when the Sun or Moon holds this title, specifically looking at the Sun's three states of strength: strong, medium, and weak.</meta>

...becomes. In the case of low or diminished strength, there is sorrow, disease, and so forth; this is clear. // 14 // Now, the author speaks of the results when the Year Lord, the Sun, is full of strength. Regarding the verse "If the Sun is the Year Lord," the result "destroys" [enemies] is quite clear. This mentioned result should be spoken of in conjunction with the **birth-planets** <term>Janmi-kheta<gloss>The positions and strengths of planets in the original birth chart, which must be compared to the current annual chart</gloss></term> or by considering the planetary influences at the time of birth.

This is what is meant: if the planet that is the Year Lord was of excellent strength at the time of birth and is also of excellent strength here [in the annual chart], then the results are the most excellent. If it was of medium strength at birth but is excellent now, the result is good. If it was of low strength at birth but is excellent now, the result is average. 

If it was of medium strength at birth, then according to its current strength (excellent, medium, or low), the results will be good, average, or poor respectively. If it was of weak strength at birth, then the results will be average, poor, or very poor respectively. // 15 // 

Now, the author describes the results when the Year Lord, the Sun, is of medium strength:

### The Sun
> ->If the Sun is the Year Lord and is strong, there is happiness from the state, children, and wealth; one becomes a leader suited to their family and enjoys family happiness. There is masculine vigor, fame, physical comfort, and various kinds of honors. Enemies are destroyed. This result is determined by the conjunction with birth-time planets. // 15 //<-
> 
> ->When the Sun is of medium strength, all these results are mediocre. There is little happiness, and it is said there are disputes even with one's own people. There may be displacement from one's position, lack of comfort, and physical weakness. There is fear of the ruler, unless there is a **Muthashila** <term>Muthashila<gloss>A Tajika aspect where a faster planet approaches a slower one, signifying the manifestation of an event</gloss></term> with a benefic planet. // 16 //<-
> 
> ->When the Sun, acting as the Year Lord, is devoid of strength, there is travel to foreign lands, loss of wealth, sorrow, fear of enemies, and lethargy. There is fear of public scandal, severe illnesses, and great misery. There is no happiness even from the father or elders, and there is fear regarding sons and friends. // 17 //<-
> 
> ->If the Moon is the Year Lord and forms a **Muthashila**, and if this Year Lord forms a **Kamboola** <term>Kamboola<gloss>A specific yoga where the Moon acts as a bridge between two planets, transferring strength and ensuring a positive outcome</gloss></term> with the Moon, it is the best of years for the person. // 18 //<-

Regarding the "medium" state: the results mentioned previously for the Moon of excellent strength become reduced by a seventh <note>original: "saptamaṃ," implying a significant reduction or a specific fractional division of the result's quality</note> when the strength is medium. The results from "little happiness" to "fear of the ruler" are clear. The unfavorable results would occur unless there is a **Muthashila** of the Year Lord with a benefic planet; meaning, if there is a **Muthashila** with a benefic, the "unfavorable" results are an exception to the rule. // 16 // 

The results of the Sun being devoid of strength are stated in the verse beginning "When the Sun..."; this is clear. // 17 // 

Next, regarding how the "Ruler of the Year" is determined through the process of **Itthashala** <term>Itthashala<gloss>A synonym for Muthashila, indicating a planetary aspect that brings an event to fruition</gloss></term>, the teacher says:

<margin>Tājika Nīlakaṇṭhī <note>A foundational 16th-century Sanskrit text on Tājika astrology, which incorporates Perso-Arabic techniques.</note></margin>
<margin>|| 57 ||</margin>

Speaking again, the author states that if there is a <term>Kambūla<gloss>A specific astrological configuration where the Moon links two planets that do not aspect each other directly</gloss></term> configuration with the Year Lord for a birth occurring at night, that year is excellent. This is what is meant by the phrase "Regarding the Moon." When the Moon is the Year Lord, the planet with which the Moon forms a <term>Muthaśila<gloss>A favorable aspect, similar to an "applying" aspect in Western astrology, where a faster planet approaches a slower one</gloss></term> becomes the ruler. If the Year Lord forms a *Kambūla* with the Moon—meaning the Moon forms a *Muthaśila* with a planet other than itself—and it is a night birth, then that year should be known as excellent. // 18 // Now, he speaks of the results when the Moon possesses full strength. The verse beginning "When the Moon is endowed with power" is clear. // 19 //

->**|| Regarding the Moon ||**<-
->When the Moon is endowed with power, the noble ones say there is varied happiness from wealth, wife, sons, friends, and home. There are the pleasures of garlands, perfumes, pearls, and fine silks, as well as prosperity and the attainment of a status befitting one's lineage, and friendship with kings. // 19 // When the Moon is the Year Lord and possesses medium strength, these results are moderate: there is enmity with the circle of sons and friends, moving to another place, weakness in the body, and diseases arising from <term>Shleshma<gloss>Phlegm or 'Kapha,' one of the three humors in Indian medicine</gloss></term> if there is a <term>Sarāpha<gloss>A 'separating' aspect in Tājika astrology, usually indicating a loss or decline of influence</gloss></term> configuration with a malefic planet. // 20 // When the Moon as Year Lord is "lost" <note>original: 'naṣṭa'—referring to the Moon being 'combust' or too close to the Sun to be visible</note>, there is disease from cold and phlegm, and conflict with one's own kin due to theft and the like. There is travel to distant lands and loss of happiness from sons and wife; if the Moon is extremely weak, there may even be a death-like state. // 21 // **|| Regarding Mars ||** When Mars is the Year Lord and is powerful, fame, victory, and the destruction of enemies are predicted; also, the rank of a general or leadership in battle. There is the gain of wealth suited to one’s family, respect among people, and happiness from friends, sons, wealth, and wife. // 22 //<-

Now the author describes the results when the Moon has medium strength. From the words "When the Moon is the Year Lord" down to "diseases arising from phlegm," the meaning is clear. If the Moon has a *Sarāpha* configuration with a malefic planet, then the production of phlegm occurs. // 20 // Now he speaks of the results when the Moon is "lost" or extremely weak. "Lost" means it has set <note>become invisible</note> due to being overtaken by the Sun. The second half of the verse describes the result of low strength. // 21 // Now he speaks of the result when Mars is full of strength. The verse beginning "When Mars..." is clear. // 22 //

### Regarding Jupiter

> If **Jupiter** is the Lord of the Year and is endowed with strength, there is happiness within the family, religious merit, an inclination toward virtuous qualities, wealth, fame, and the birth of sons. One gains trustworthiness in the world, a noble mind, and valor. One acquires treasure, receives honor from the king, and triumphs over enemies. || 28 ||

> If the **Teacher of the Gods (Jupiter)** is the Year Lord but possesses only medium strength, these results will be moderate, though there may still be encounters with royalty. One gains knowledge of the sciences and scriptures. However, if there is a separating aspect with a malefic planet, there is poverty, the disappearance of wealth, and distress to one's wife. || 29 ||

> If **Jupiter** is the Year Lord and possesses weak strength, there is a loss of wealth, religious merit, and happiness. One is abandoned by sons, friends, and family, including one's wife. There is fear of public scandal, mental agitation, a very difficult livelihood, diseases of <term>phlegm<gloss>Original: Kapha</gloss></term> in the body, fear of enemies, and constant strife. || 30 ||

### Regarding Venus

> If **Venus** is the Year Lord and is powerful, there is freedom from disease, a life of luxury, devotion to good scriptures, and the enjoyment of sweet foods. There is well-being, majesty, victory, and amorous play with women. Wealth and happiness are attained through laughter and the patronage of kings. || 31 ||

> If the **Son of Bhrigu (Venus)** is the Year Lord and possesses medium strength, all these results will be moderate or slightly diminished. There is hidden sorrow despite a steady livelihood. If Venus is aspected by or joined with a malefic or an enemy planet, there will be calamities and the destruction of wealth. || 32 ||

Now he explains the results of the same planet (Venus) when it has medium strength || "Year Lord" etc. When Venus is aspected by or joined with a malefic planet or an enemy, there are calamities and the destruction of wealth. By using the phrase "aspected or joined," it is clarified that one should look for the "aspect" in the <term>Zodiacal Chart<gloss>Original: Rashi-kundali</gloss></term> and the "conjunction" in the <term>House Chart<gloss>Original: Bhava-kundali</gloss></term>. <note>This is a technical distinction in Tajika astrology: aspects (drishti) are calculated based on the signs, while conjunctions (yuti) are determined by the house positions.</note> Otherwise, since the Tajika system primarily defines aspects in the first place, the word "joined" would be redundant if "aspected" already implied conjunction. The rest is clear. || 32 ||

### Regarding Venus (Weak)

> When **Venus** is the Lord of the Year but possesses low strength, there is great mental distress, public ridicule, calamities, and the destruction of one’s own livelihood. ### Regarding Saturn

> When **Saturn** is the Lord of the Year and is powerful, one acquires new lands, houses, and fields, and accumulates wealth from <note>original: "yavana-avanishat"</note> foreign or Western rulers. One builds gardens and water reservoirs, enjoys physical health, attains a status appropriate to one’s lineage, and becomes a leader among the virtuous. || 34 ||

> If the **Son of the Sun (Saturn)** is the Year Lord but has only medium strength, all these results will be moderate, and one obtains food only through great effort. There are gains from servants, camels, and herds of buffaloes through constant exertion. However, the result becomes malefic if there is a conjunction or aspect with a malefic planet. || 35 ||

> When **Saturn** is the Year Lord but is devoid of strength, there is futility in one's actions, the loss of wealth, calamities, and fear of enemies. There is conflict with wife, sons, and friends, and the consumption of poor food. However, if there is a <term>benefic aspect<gloss>Original: Itthashala</gloss></term> <note>Itthashala (from Arabic *ittisāl*) is a Tajika astrological term for a favorable application of light between two planets.</note> with a benefic planet, there may be at least a small amount of happiness in the year. || 36 ||

> Whichever planet becomes the **Year Lord** is to be understood as the ruler of the entire year’s timeline. Its strength must be carefully considered in both the annual chart and the birth chart. If it is endowed with power, the whole year is said to be auspicious; if it is weak, the results are undesirable; if it is of medium strength, the results are balanced. || 37 ||

---

"Saturn" etc. In the case of an <term>Itthashala aspect<gloss>Original: Muthashila</gloss></term> with a benefic planet, or a conjunction with a benefic planet, even a weak Saturn can grant a tiny amount of happiness during the year. The rest is clear. || 36 ||

"Year Lord" etc. The planet that becomes the Year Lord should be known as the ruler of the entire year-long <term>Dasha<gloss>Time-cycle</gloss></term>. This means it is the period-ruler for the full duration of that year. The strength of this Year Lord—specifically its <term>Five-fold Strength<gloss>Original: Pancha-varga-bala</gloss></term>—must be evaluated at both the time of the solar return and the time of birth. # Tajaka Nilakanthi
## Section 2
<page-num>59</page-num>

<margin>Tajaka Nilakanthi || 59 ||</margin>

When the <term>Year Lord<gloss>Original: Varshesha</gloss></term> possesses strength, the results are auspicious. In a situation where there is equality—if the planet is strong in the <term>Birth Chart<gloss>Original: Janmani</gloss></term> but has medium or low strength in the <term>Year Chart<gloss>Original: Varshe</gloss></term>, or if it is weak in the Birth Chart but strong in the Year Chart—the results are considered medium. By comparing these variations, the results range from excellent to poor. In the case of medium strength, the planet gives moderate results. || 37 ||

Regarding the verse beginning with "By which": In the year chart, whichever planet forms a <term>Muthashila<gloss>Original: Muthashila/Itthashala</gloss></term> <note>A Tajika yoga where a faster planet approaches a slower one, indicating the fruition of an event.</note> with the Year Lord, that planet—the "Muthashila-maker"—gives good results according to its own inherent nature as described in the <term>Sanjna-tantra<gloss>The first section of the Tajaka Nilakanthi dealing with basic terminology</gloss></term>. If a <term>Sarapha<gloss>Original: Sarapha/Musaripha</gloss></term> <note>A yoga of separation where planets move apart, usually indicating a lack of fulfillment.</note> is formed with a benefic planet, there is some happiness in that year. However, if a Sarapha is formed with a malefic planet, the result is certainly undesirable or inauspicious. || 38 ||

Regarding the verse beginning with "In the Hadda": Whichever planet is the Year Lord...

->By whatever planet an **Itthashala** is formed with the **Year Lord**,<-
->that planet gives good results according to its own nature.<-
->If there is a **benefic Sarapha**, there is some good;<-
->but in a **malefic Musaripha**, there is only evil. || 38 ||<-

->Whichever planet occupies its **Hadda** and possesses strength here,<-
->if it is also such in the birth chart and the year chart,<-
->then it truly bestows its own fruits. || 39 ||<-

->If a planet is capable of giving fruits in the birth chart,<-
->but a **Musaripha** occurs in the annual chart,<-
->then in that year, those fruits will not manifest. || 40 ||<-

->In the case of an **Itthashala**, the result is reversed (it manifests).<-
->If neither yoga is present, then clearly<-
->one should rely on the birth chart’s status. || 41 ||<-

<margin>Some say the "Year Lord" is meant here even among enemy planets.</margin>
<margin>The **Hadda** <note>A five-fold division of signs, also known as "Terms" or "Bounds" in Western astrology.</note> belongs to the associated friend or enemy planet. Here, in this friend...</margin>

Whichever planet—and also from the friendship or enmity mentioned—possesses the "light" or power in the form of the stated degree, becomes the **Muthashila-maker**. If that Year Lord is similarly placed at the time of birth and at the time of the year's commencement, then it becomes the giver of its own fruits, whether auspicious or inauspicious. || 39 ||

"Or rather" and "in reversal": If a planet is capable by its nature of giving results in the birth chart, but for this planet, a <term>Musaripha<gloss>Original: Musharipha</gloss></term> or <term>Isarapha Yoga<gloss>Separation</gloss></term> arises from the Year-Ascendant Lord or the Year Lord, then in that year, the fruit produced by that house in the birth chart will not be realized. 
<margin>|| 59 ||</margin>

<summary>This page explains how the timing of events in a person's year is determined by the interaction between the Birth Chart and the Annual Chart, specifically through Tajika yogas like Itthashala (conjunction) and Musaripha (separation).</summary>
<keywords>Tajika, Itthashala, Muthashila, Sarapha, Musaripha, Isarapha, Varshesha, Hadda, planetary strength, annual horoscopy</keywords>

--- Page 122 ---
<meta>This page concludes the discussion on the transfer of planetary strength and provides specific examples of how the Tajika yogas—specifically Itthashala (application) and Isarapha (separation)—interact with the natal birth chart to determine the year's outcomes.</meta>

...it means that the year is auspicious. In the case of a reversal—meaning if there is an absence of **Isarapha** <gloss>original: Isarapha; a yoga of separation</gloss>—the results should be predicted based on the planetary positions at the time of birth. When an **Itthashala** <gloss>original: Itthashala; a yoga of application or conjunction</gloss> or **Muthashila** <gloss>an interchangeable term for Itthashala</gloss> occurs, that specific result will manifest with great certainty. If neither **Isarapha** nor **Muthashila** are present, then it is clear that the results occurring from the houses of the birth chart still apply. Since there is no obstructing factor, what could possibly prevent those results? Although mentioning the results under "reversal" might seem repetitive, it is stated here for the sake of clarity; otherwise, one of the three conditions mentioned might be wrongly considered useless. In reality, this is not a mistake by the author. || 40 || 41 ||

To clarify the meaning of the first verse, an example is given: **The Lord of the Son** <gloss>original: Putradhipa; the ruler of the 5th house</gloss>. In the birth chart, if the **Lord of the Son** <term>the planet ruling the 5th house of children</term> aspects the house of children, that planet is capable of granting the birth of a son. However, regardless of where that planet is placed in the year chart...

> **Example: If the Lord of the Son aspects the house of children in the birth chart, it is capable of giving a son. But if in the year chart the Lord of the Son is in Musaripha <gloss>separation</gloss>, then a loss of the son occurs in that year. || 42 ||**
> **If Jupiter is the Year Lord <gloss>original: Abdeshvara</gloss> and the Moon aspects it with a friendly gaze from a friendly Hadda <gloss>original: Haddesha; a specific degree-division of a sign</gloss>, then because of the "Greatness of Light" <note>This refers to the transfer of planetary influence known as Sahah or Tejas</note> during that year, the year is auspicious. || 43 ||**
> **In this same way, the wise should infer other auspicious and inauspicious results by discerning the strength and weakness of the planets through the consideration of the three yogas. || 44 ||**

->इत्यब्दफलाध्यायः॥<-
->**Thus ends the Chapter on the Results of the Year.**<-

The term **Musaripha** <gloss>separation</gloss> indicates that in this specific year, there will be a loss of a son. This same logic should be applied to other houses as well, using the **Year Lord** <gloss>original: Varsha-lagnapatih</gloss> and the **Isarapha** yoga as examples. || 42 ||

The author clarifies the example involving the **Hadda** <gloss>the "Terms" or degree-subdivisions of a sign</gloss> and aspects: If the **Year Lord** <gloss>original: Abdeshvara</gloss> is Jupiter and the Moon, situated in the **Hadda** of its own friend, aspects Jupiter with a friendly gaze, it holds the "Greatness of Light" <gloss>original: Mahat-tejas</gloss>. This means it is a "Muthashila-maker." In this year, the Moon sees Jupiter in a configuration called **Amudda** <note>A Tajika term for a specific auspicious planetary configuration</note>. The meaning is this: if Jupiter is in the **Hadda** of a friend and the Moon forms a **Muthashila** <gloss>application</gloss> with it, then the Moon "carries the light." Because of that Year Lord, the time of the year becomes auspicious. Some suggest that the term "Year Lord" refers to something after the yoga, and that **Amudda** simply means "seeing the Moon," but that is incorrect. Because this is an introductory section of examples, it is impossible for there to be other yogas not already mentioned, and there is no basis for such an interpretation in the original text. || 43 ||

The author concludes: "In this way..." By following the method described, other results...

It concludes a chapter by the author Divakara.</meta>

<page-num>60</page-num>
<header>Collection of 81 Year Old Dina Nath Raina, Jammu. Retired Priest.</header>

<margin>
Tajika Nilakanthi
Page 60
</margin>

The fruit of good and evil should be understood by the wise through an investigation of the three yogas—**Muthashila**, **Kambula**, and **Isarapha** <gloss>original: Muthashila, Kambula, Isarapha; technical terms for planetary configurations in Tajika astrology</gloss>—by discerning the strength and weakness of the planets. || 44 || 

Thus ends the Chapter on the Results of the Year <gloss>original: Varshaphala-adhyaya</gloss> in the work composed by **Divakara** <note>Divakara was a noted commentator and astrologer</note>.

Now, for the purpose of determining the results of the **Muthaha** <gloss>also known as Muntha; a mathematical point that progresses one zodiac sign every year</gloss>, the method for calculating the **Muthaha** is stated: "From one's birth ascendant..." and so on. This is explained clearly in the commentary on the **Sanjnatantra** <note>A foundational text on Tajika astrological terminology</note>. || 45 || Regarding its logic, it is said that it has a motion like the planets: "Every day..." and so on. For every solar day, that **Muntha** increases by five minutes <term>lipta; 1/60th of a degree</term> based on the proportion that it traverses one full zodiac sign <note>30 degrees</note> in three hundred and sixty solar days. By this method, it increases by two and a half degrees in a solar month. Some scholars speak in this manner.

<margin>
Prathama 2
Page 60
</margin>

->### Now, the Description of the Muthaha (Muntha) ###<-
->### The Muthaha resides in the house determined by counting one sign for each year passed since the birth ascendant. ###<-
->### Add the number of elapsed years to the birth ascendant; this becomes the Muthaha here. || 45 || ###<-
->### It increases daily by five minutes according to the calculation. ###<-
->### Other learned men say it moves two and a half degrees per month. || 46 || ###<-
->### Happiness comes from the aspect of its Lord or benefic planets; ###<-
->### fear and illness come from the aspect of "Khuta" <gloss>the six-eight-twelve relationship or "evil eye"</gloss>. ###<-
->### Its results are determined by its conjunction and aspect with the houses. || 47 || ###<-
->### It is inauspicious if placed in the 4th, 7th, 12th, 6th, or 8th houses from the Year Ascendant. ###<-
->### If placed in the house of merit (9th) or the 10th, it grants lordship; elsewhere, it gives wealth through effort. || 48 || ###<-

In their own opinion, since the movement of the **Muntha** was not mentioned in the middle of previous texts, they say that it only moves to the next sign at the end of the year. This is accepted according to the rule that "a statement is as valid as the word itself." || 46 || "The aspect of its Lord or benefics...": happiness is gained from the sight <gloss>drishti; aspect</gloss> of the Lord of the Muntha or benefic planets. From the "Khuta" aspect <note>referring to unfavorable planetary alignments</note>, illness and fear arise. The results of this **Muthaha** are described based on its association with or aspect on the various houses. || 47 || "From the Year Ascendant..." this is clear <note>referring to the house placements mentioned in verse 48</note>. || 48 ||

<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of Tajik astrology (a system of Indian astrology influenced by Perso-Arabic traditions), specifically focusing on the Varshaphala or "Fruit of the Year." Having previously defined the calculation of the Muntha (a sensitive mathematical point that moves one zodiac sign per year), the text now provides the specific divinatory results for the Muntha's placement in the first eight houses of the annual horoscope.</meta>

# Twelve Clear Verses on the Results of the Houses for the Muntha, beginning with "Destruction of Enemies," and so on.

-> छ &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp; छ &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp; छ &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp; छ &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp; छ <-

When the **Muntha** <gloss>original: Muthaha; a mathematical point in Tajik astrology that progresses one sign each year</gloss> is situated in the **first house** <term>tanu-stha; the house of the self or body</term>, it grants the destruction of enemies, mental satisfaction, gains, an increase in valor and glory, and the favor of the king <note>In a modern context, "favor of the king" refers to success with authorities or the state</note>. It also brings physical nourishment and success in various new endeavors and wealth. || 49 ||

If the **Muntha** resides in the **second house** <term>artha-bhava; the house of wealth</term> during the year, there will be the arrival of wealth through one's own enthusiasm, as well as fame, respect from one's kin, and patronage from the state. The person enjoys sweet foods, physical strength, nourishment, and general happiness. || 50 ||

If the **Muntha** is in the **third house** <term>tritiya; the house of siblings and courage</term>, one attains wealth, fame, and happiness through valor. It brings beauty, pleasure, and a devotion to the worship of gods and the learned <note>original: dvija; literally 'twice-born' or Brahmins</note>. The individual becomes helpful to all, enjoys physical health, renown, and royal support. || 51 ||

When the **Muntha** moves into the **fourth house** <term>sukha-bhava; the house of happiness and the home</term>, there may be physical pain, fear of enemies, and enmity with one's own family or social group. It leads to mental distress, a lack of motivation, public scandal, an increase in diseases, and general misery. || 52 ||

If the **Muntha** <gloss>original: itthaha</gloss> is in the **fifth house** <term>panchamaga; the house of children and intellect</term> and is the lord of the year, it brings the gains of good intellect, happiness, children, and wealth. There is an increase in glory, various luxuries and pastimes, the worship of gods and the learned, and the favor of the king. || 53 ||

If the **Muntha** goes to the **sixth house** <term>ari-ga; the house of enemies</term>, there is emaciation of the body, the rise of enemies, and fear from diseases, thieves, or the state. It causes the destruction of one's work and wealth, an increase in wicked thoughts, and remorse for one's own past actions. || 54 ||

If the **Muntha** is in the **seventh house** <term>dyunopaga; the house of marriage and partnerships</term>, it causes distress to the spouse and kin, fear of enemies, a loss of enthusiasm, and the destruction of wealth and righteousness. It leads to disease, delusion of the mind, and actions that are contrary to one's best interests. || 55 ||

If the **Muntha** is situated in the **eighth house** <term>mrityu-sthitha; the house of death and transformations</term>, there is fear from enemies and thieves, the destruction of one's righteousness and wealth, and the onset of bad habits or disease. For men, it signifies a loss of strength and wandering to very distant places. || 56 ||

-> ॥49॥ &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp; ॥50॥ &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp; ॥51॥ &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp; ॥52॥ &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp; ॥53॥ &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp; ॥54॥ &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp; ॥55॥ &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp; ॥56॥ &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp; छ &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp; छ <-

Having covered the first eight houses on the previous page, the text now details the outcomes for the Muntha's placement in the remaining four houses (9th through 12th) and provides general rules for how aspects from malefic or benefic planets modify these results.</meta>

<header>|| 61 ||</header>

<margin>Tajika Nilakanthi</margin>
<margin>|| 61 ||</margin>

Regarding verse 61 <note>original: krurairiti...</note>: When a house <term>bhava; one of the twelve divisions of the horoscope</term>, such as the house of the self, is seen by an unlucky gaze <gloss>asubha-drishti</gloss> from malefic planets, and the **Muntha** <gloss>a mathematical point that moves one sign per year</gloss> is also present there, then the auspicious fruits associated with that house's placement are destroyed. Instead, the inauspicious results connected to that house will increase. || 61 || The author now describes the specific fruits of the remaining houses:

->Ownership, the arrival of wealth from kings, religious festivals, happiness from children and wife, the worship of gods and the learned <term>dvija; literally 'twice-born' or Brahmins</term>, supreme fame, and the dawning of fortune occur when the **Muntha** is in the **ninth house** <term>bhagya-gata; the house of fortune and spirituality</term>. || 57 ||<-

->Favor from the king, helping one's own kin, success in good deeds, devotion to the learned and the gods, the growth of fame, and the gain of various types of wealth are granted by the **Muntha** when it reaches its height in the **tenth house** <term>ambarastha; literally 'staying in the sky,' the house of career and public status</term>. || 58 ||<-

->If the **Muntha** reaches the **eleventh house** <term>labha-gata; the house of gains</term>, there are luxuries, good fortune, freedom from disease, and a peaceful mind. Wealth comes through the shelter of the king, along with good friends, children, and the fulfillment of desires. || 59 ||<-

->Excessive expenditure, association with wicked people, illness of the body, failure in courageous acts, and the loss of both religious merit and wealth occur when the **Muntha** is in the **twelfth house** <term>vyaya-stha; the house of loss and expenditure</term>; at that time, there may also be enmity from the public. || 60 ||<-

If the house where the **Muntha** is located is seen by malefic planets <term>krura; 'cruel' planets like Mars or Saturn</term> with an unfavorable gaze, the good results arising from that house are destroyed, and the evil results increase. || 62 || Thus ends the results of the Muntha in the houses. If the **Muntha** is joined or seen by the lord of the house <term>bhava-swami</term>, or if the lord is strong and has entered into a favorable **Ithashala** <term>a specific Tajik yoga or planetary configuration indicating the blending of influences</term> with a benefic planet, then the good results of that house are nourished. If the situation is the opposite, one should reflect and deduce that the results will be contrary. || 63 ||

...the good results of that house will increase. It will not increase the inauspicious fruits of that house. "Otherwise" means if the lord of the house is not joined with the Muntha, or if there is no **Ithashala** with a benefic lord. If it is seen by malefics or in a malefic **Muthasila** <term>an aspect leading to a specific outcome</term>, the meaning is: in that case, the "contrary state" occurs. This means the inauspicious results of the house increase, and the auspicious results do not. One must carefully deliberate on the aspects of benefics and malefics and the respective configurations.

<margin>Verse 61</margin>

<meta>This page continues the technical analysis of the "Muntha" (the yearly progression point) from the Tajika Nilakanthi. The previous page concluded with the specific results of the Muntha in the 9th, 10th, and 11th houses. We now move to the general rules for how planetary strengths and aspects modify these results, particularly focusing on the "Janur-lagna" (the birth ascendant) in relation to the annual chart.</meta>

<header>Collection of 81 Year Old Dina Nath Raina, Jammu. Retired Priest.</header>

One must understand whether the results will be auspicious or inauspicious by considering the relative strength of the auspicious <term>shubha-graha; benefic planets like Jupiter or Venus</term> and inauspicious <term>ashubha-graha; malefic planets like Mars or Saturn</term> planets. || 62 ||

Regarding the phrase "From the birth ascendant" <note>original: janur-lagnata iti...</note>: If the **Muntha** <gloss>a sensitive mathematical point that moves one sign per year</gloss> is situated in the 7th, 12th, 6th, 8th, or 4th houses from the birth ascendant in the annual chart, and if that **Muntha** is afflicted <gloss>hata; literally 'struck' or 'damaged'</gloss> or joined by malefic planets, then in that year, the house <term>bhava</term> where the **Muntha** resides is destroyed. However, even if it is positioned in these difficult spots, if it is aspected <gloss>drishti; seen or influenced by</gloss> by the lord of that house or by a benefic planet, then the house is not destroyed; rather, it becomes auspicious. || 63 ||

Regarding "When" <note>original: yadeti...</note>: When the **Muntha** is placed in an unfavorable position and afflicted by malefic planets in *both* the birth chart and the annual chart, then the house associated with the **Muntha** will certainly and completely be destroyed. If, conversely, it is well-placed and joined by benefic planets in both charts, that house will grow and prosper exceedingly. || 64 ||

> If the **Muntha** is in the 7th, 12th, 6th, 8th, or 4th houses from the birth ascendant, and is joined by cruel planets, the very house in which it resides is destroyed. If it is aspected by a benefic planet or its own house-lord, there is no destruction; it becomes auspicious. || 63 ||
> 
> When it is afflicted in both the birth and annual charts, the house is destroyed in every way. But if it is auspicious in both, that house prospers exceedingly. || 64 ||
> 
> If it sits in an unfavorable house in the annual chart, or likewise in the birth chart, and is struck by a cruel planet, it destroys that house. If joined by a benefic, it is auspicious. || 65 ||
> 
> If the **Muntha** is in the 4th house from the birth ascendant and is joined by a benefic planet in the annual chart, it grants ancestral wealth. If joined by a malefic, it causes fear of the king and great suffering. A similar analysis should be applied to the 8th house and others. || 66 ||

<gloss>The above section contains astrological verses regarding the placement of the 'Muntha' and its effects on the houses (Bhavas) based on planetary aspects.</gloss>

The explanation for verse 65: If in the annual chart <term>varsha-kundali</term> the **Muntha** is in an unfavorable house (the 4th, 6th, 7th, 8th, or 12th), or likewise in those same houses relative to the birth ascendant, then the house occupied by the **Muntha** is destroyed because of the affliction by malefic planets. If it is joined by a benefic planet in the annual chart, it gives auspicious results. || 65 ||

The author provides an example for verse 66: If it is in the 4th house from the birth ascendant and joined by a benefic planet at the time the year begins <term>varsha-pravesha</term>, then it brings gain of ancestral property <note>literally "father's wealth" (pitu-dravya)</note>. This is an indicator for judging the 4th house, which relates to family matters and happiness. It produces gain. If joined by a malefic planet, it causes fear from the king <note>in a modern context, this might refer to the government or legal authorities</note> and gives results that are very painful. In this same manner, an investigation or deliberation <term>vimarsha</term> should be performed for the 8th house and other unfavorable houses aside from the 4th. One should then declare whether the results of those houses will be gained or lost. || 66 ||

<page-num>६२</page-num>
<header>TA. NI. <note>Abbreviation for Tajika Nilakanthi</note></header>
<sig>Section 2</sig>
<meta>Catchword/Page number repeated in right margin</meta>

Regarding the phrase "In which" <note>original: "yasmimniti"</note>: In the <term>Varsha-lagna-kundali; the annual horoscope chart</term>, in whichever <term>bhava; house</term> this **Muntha** is stationed and is aspected <gloss>drishta; seen</gloss> by its own planetary lord or by a <term>shubha-graha; benefic or "good" planet</term>, the corresponding house in the <term>Janma-kundali; the birth chart</term> is enhanced. 

The meaning is this: look at the zodiac sign where the **Muntha** is located in the annual chart; find where that same sign falls in the original birth chart. The matters associated with that house will see growth and success. If, however, it is aspected by <term>papa-grahas; malefic or "cruel" planets</term>, the destruction of that house's affairs—as established in the birth chart—is predicted. This is how it should be understood. 

Now, the author states an exception to this rule, starting with the word "From strength" <note>original: "viryat"</note>. By the power and strength of the <term>Varsha-swami; the Lord of the Year</term>, one attains happiness alone. The inauspicious results created by the **Muntha** do not come to pass if the Lord of the Year is exceptionally strong; in such a case, the tendency toward bad results from the **Muntha** is nullified.

> If the **Muntha** is in a house and is aspected by its lord or a benefic, there is growth of the house that corresponds to it in the birth chart. Likewise, destruction is spoken of if it is seen by malefics. However, one must deduce that if the Lord of the Year possesses strength, only happiness will prevail. || 67 ||

> If the **Muntha** is situated in the Sun's house <note>the sign of Leo</note> or is joined with the **Sun**, it grants sovereignty, meetings with kings, the highest attainment of good qualities, and a change of location; these results also apply to the Sun's aspect. || 68 ||

> If joined with the **Moon**, in the Moon's house <note>the sign of Cancer</note>, or even aspected by the Moon, it gives an increase in religious merit and fame, health, and great contentment. However, from the aspect of a malefic, it brings extreme sorrow. || 69 ||

> Joined with **Mars**, in a sign ruled by Mars <note>Aries or Scorpio</note>, or aspected by Mars, it causes diseases of heat and bile, injuries from weapons or fire, and disorders of the blood. If it is further aspected by **Saturn** or in Saturn's house <note>Capricorn or Aquarius</note>, these results are especially pronounced. || 70 ||

This is the meaning. || 67 || 

Now, wishing to speak of the results based on the **Muntha's** conjunction with planets, their aspects, and the zodiac signs they occupy, the author first describes the **Sun**. Regarding "If" <note>original: "yadi"</note>: if the **Muntha** is in the Sun's sign, Leo, or is joined with the Sun, then it grants kingdom/power and similar things, the highest excellence of virtues, and the acquisition of a different location. These same results are stated for the aspect <gloss>drishti</gloss> of the Sun as well. || 68 ||

The author then describes the **Moon** <note>original: "chandrasya"</note>. The phrase "it gives" is clear. From the sight of malefic planets, extreme sorrow occurs. || 69 ||

The author then describes **Mars** <note>original: "bhaumasya"</note>. The term "disorder of the blood" is clear. If "aspected by the son of the Sun" <note>original: "saurikshita"; Saturn</note>, meaning if it is seen by Saturn or joined/aspected by Mars while in Saturn's sign, then the previously mentioned [negative] results occur with even greater intensity. || 70 ||

<summary>This page explains how the Muntha's influence is modified by planetary aspects and house rulers, specifically detailing the effects of the Sun, Moon, and Mars. It emphasizes that a strong "Lord of the Year" can override negative Muntha placements.</summary>
<keywords>Muntha, Tajika, Annual Horoscope, Sun, Moon, Mars, Saturn, Planetary Strength, Medical Astrology</keywords>

--- Page 128 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of the "Muntha" (the annual progression point) in Tajika astrology. Having previously discussed the general effects of its placement in various houses, the text now focuses on the specific results triggered when the Muntha interacts with the planets—Mercury, Venus, Jupiter, Saturn, and the lunar node Rahu—through conjunctions or aspects.</meta>

Regarding Mercury and Venus, the author says: "By Mercury..." <note>original: "budheneti"</note>. The meaning up to the word "accomplishes" is clear. From the aspect <gloss>glance</gloss> of malefic planets or from a conjunction with them, difficult results will occur. || 71 || 

Regarding the phrase "Joined..." <note>original: "yuteti"</note>: the meaning up to "enjoyment" is clear. In this context, the previous rules apply: if the Muntha is in a sign ruled by Jupiter and is either joined with or aspected by Jupiter, it produces these effects. From a <term>shubha-muthashila-yoga; a favorable planetary configuration</term>, one attains a kingdom or high authority. || 72 || 

Regarding Saturn, the author says: "Of Saturn..." <note>original: "shaneriti"</note>. The meaning up to the end is clear. Through the aspect of Jupiter, one attains auspicious results instead of the typical hardships associated with Saturn. || 73 || 

Now, the author speaks of the results of the <term>Mutha-ha; the annual progressed point</term> being located in the "Mouth" <gloss>head</gloss> of Rahu. "In the face of darkness..." <note>original: "tamo-mukhe"</note>. This is clear. The word "Pada" refers to rank or position. || 74 || 

Now he defines the characteristics of Rahu's Mouth, Back, and Tail: "The portions to be traveled..." <note>original: "bhogyaiti"</note>. 

-> If the Muntha is joined or aspected by **Mercury** or **Venus**, or if it is placed in their signs, it brings women, friends, profit, and happiness. It also grants incomparable religious merit and fame, though hardship may arise from the aspect or conjunction of malefic planets. || 71 || <-

-> If the Muntha is in the sign of **Jupiter** and is joined or aspected by **Jupiter**, it grants happiness concerning children and spouse. It provides the enjoyment of gold, fine clothing, and gems. From a favorable configuration <gloss>itthashala</gloss> here, one attains sovereign power. || 72 || If it is in the house of **Saturn** <-

-> or joined or aspected by him, it causes disease, loss of prestige, fear of fire, and loss of wealth. However, through the aspect of **Jupiter**, one attains auspiciousness. || 73 || If the Muntha is in the **Face of Rahu**, <-

-> it brings the attainment of wealth, fame, happiness, and religious advancement. From the conjunction or aspect of **Venus** or **Jupiter**, one gains rank and the acquisition of gold, gems, and clothing. || 74 || The degrees of a sign yet to be traveled by Rahu <-

-> are its **Face**; the degrees already passed are its **Back**. The seventh sign from that position is the **Tail**. One should announce the results after such deliberation. || 75 || <-

The degrees in a sign that are yet to be traveled by **Rahu**—due to its <term>vakra-gati; retrograde or backward motion</term>—are designated as the "Face" <gloss>Mouth</gloss>. The degrees already passed <gloss>consumed</gloss> are known as the "Back." 

For example: Suppose Rahu is currently at ten degrees in Virgo, moving retrograde. In this case, the first ten degrees (from 10 down to 0) are designated as the "Face," because those are the degrees he is about to "consume" or travel through. The remaining twenty degrees (from 10 up to 30), which have already been passed, are the "Back." Then, the seventh sign from the sign occupied by Rahu is the "Tail." For instance, if Rahu is in Virgo, the seventh sign is Pisces; therefore, the sign occupied by <term>Ketu; the South Node</term> is designated as the "Tail." One should deliberate and reflect upon these positions before announcing the astrological results. || 75 ||

<summary>This page details the effects of the Muntha (annual point) when influenced by specific planets and defines the calculation for Rahu's "Face" and "Tail" based on its retrograde motion. It emphasizes that Jupiter's influence can mitigate Saturn's negativity and that Rahu's "Face" can actually bring prosperity and status.</summary>

# Page 63
### Ta. Ni., Section 1
**2**

Now, the author speaks of the results of the <term>Muntha; the annual progressed point</term> being located in the "Back" and "Tail" [of the lunar node Rahu]. The verse beginning with "At its back part" <note>original: "tat-prishtha-bhaga"</note> is clear in its meaning. || 76 ||

Now, he speaks of the auspicious and inauspicious results based on the strength of the planets at the time of birth. Regarding the phrase "Those at birth..." <note>original: "ye janme"</note>: those planets which were strong at the time of birth, if they are weak <gloss>powerless</gloss> at the time of the year <note>referring to the annual solar return chart</note>, then from those planets, an inauspicious result should be predicted at the end of the year <gloss>as the year comes to a close</gloss>, during the second half of the year. Meaning: in the first half of the year, the result of that planet is auspicious. Conversely, if the planets are weak at the time of birth but become strong during the year, then in the first half of the year, the result is said to be undesirable. Meaning: in the second half of the year, an auspicious result should be predicted. If there is equality in strength between the birth time and the year time, then the result will be equal. This is the meaning: if those planets are strong in both the birth and annual charts, then there will be auspicious results for the entire year. If they are powerless in both, then there will be inauspicious results for the entire year. || 77 ||

->In its back part, it is not the giver of auspiciousness; from its tail part, there arises fear of enemies and hardship. Due to the aspect <gloss>glance</gloss> of malefic planets, there is a loss of wealth and happiness. If the planets are thus at birth, there is a loss of wealth. || 76 ||<-

->Those planets strong at the time of birth, if weak during the year, cause inauspiciousness at the year's end. In the opposite case, the beginning is said to be undesirable. If they are equal in strength in both, the result is equal. || 77 ||<-

->If the Lord of the Muntha is in the sixth, eighth, or twelfth house, or is combust or retrograde, or joined with or aspected by malefics, or is in the fourth or seventh house from a malefic, it is not favorable; it grants inauspiciousness and loss of wealth. || 78 ||<-

Now, he speaks of the results concerning the Lord of the Muntha being auspicious or otherwise. Regarding the phrase "In the sixth..." <note>original: "shashthe"</note>: if the <term>Muntha-isha; the planetary ruler of the sign where the annual point is located</term> is in the sixth, eighth, or twelfth <note>these are the "dusthanas" or difficult houses in astrology</note>, or in the "earth" <gloss>the fourth house</gloss>. If it is in these unfavorable places. Or, if it is <term>Asta-gata; combust, meaning too close to the sun to be seen</term> or <term>Vakri; retrograde, appearing to move backward</term>, or likewise aspected by or joined with malefic <gloss>sinful</gloss> planets. Similarly, if it is in the fourth or the seventh <gloss>the setting house</gloss> from a malefic planet's position, then it will not be favorable or auspicious. Furthermore, that Lord of the Muntha gives "rujam" <gloss>disease</gloss> and loss of wealth. || 78 ||

<summary>This page explains how the timing of a planet's effects depends on its relative strength in the birth chart versus the annual chart, and details the negative consequences of a poorly placed Muntha Lord.</summary>

--- Page 130 ---
<meta>This page continues the "Varshatantra" (Annual Treatise) of the Tajika Nilakanthi. The previous page discussed how the strength of planets at the time of birth compared to their strength in the annual solar return chart determines whether the first or second half of the year will be favorable. This page moves into the specific inauspicious combinations (yogas) involving the Muntha and its lord, specifically focusing on life-threatening conditions (Arishta).</meta>

Now, the author speaks of other inauspicious results regarding the lord of the <term>Muthaha; the annual progressed point, also known as the Muntha</term>. Regarding the phrase "If the lord of the eighth": if the lord of the eighth house <note>the house of death and transformation</note> from the annual ascendant is a malefic <gloss>unfavorable</gloss> planet and is joined with the lord of the Muthaha, or if it is aspected by the <term>Kshutarvya; a Tajika term for hostile square or opposition aspects</term>—specifically the fourth, seventh, or eleventh house aspects—then it is not auspicious and yields undesirable results. In these two combinations, death occurs. They say that if only one of these two combinations is present, the result is "death-like" <note>referring to severe suffering or critical illness</note>. || 79 ||

Now, he describes the auspicious and inauspicious results of the Muthaha and its lord. Regarding the phrase "The Muthaha": if in the birth chart <note>the original horoscope</note>, the annual Muthaha or its lord is aspected by or joined with benefic planets, it grants an auspicious result at the beginning of the year—that is, in the first half of the year. Each grants its own result. Now, if the opposite is true in the birth chart but in the annual chart <note>the solar return chart</note> the Muthaha or its lord is aspected by benefics, then it grants an auspicious result at the end of the year <gloss>the second half of the year</gloss>.

->If it is joined with or aspected by the lord of the eighth house, or by a hostile aspect, it is inauspicious. In these two combinations, death occurs; if only one combination exists, they say the result is death-like. || 79 ||<-
->If the Muthaha or its lord is aspected or joined by benefics in the birth chart, it brings good at the beginning of the year; if in the annual chart, then at the end. Otherwise, it is inauspicious. || 80 ||<-

# Thus ends the description of the Muthaha.
# Now, the consideration of misfortunes in the year.

->If the lord of the ascendant is in the eighth house, or the lord of the eighth is in the ascendant, or if they are aspected by Mars, and if Mercury and Jupiter are set, there is injury by weapons, calamity, and death. || 81 ||<-

Otherwise, if it is joined with or aspected by malefic planets, it gives inauspicious results in the first or second half of the year respectively, following the order of the birth and annual charts. In the phrase "otherwise," there is an elision of the letter 'a' <note>a linguistic detail regarding the Sanskrit "anyatha-ashubham"</note>. The correct reading is "not favorable." If both birth and annual charts are favorable, the year is successful in its entirety. If neither is favorable, the year is mediocre. In this matter, many variations regarding the intensity of aspects, the strength of planets, and the balance of benefic versus malefic aspects must be understood by the wise through their own intelligence. || 80 ||

Thus ends the chapter on the Muthaha in the commentary on the *Varshatantra*. Regarding this, <term>Manittha; an ancient authority on astrology</term> says that if a fatal combination <note>Arishta</note> is present, the other predicted results are useless. He states: "The results regarding the year's progress are in vain if life itself is at risk from the year's misfortunes. Therefore, I shall first describe the misfortunes as spoken by the ancient masters of the rules." Thus, the chapter on misfortunes is explained. First, regarding the lord of the ascendant...

It concludes by transitioning to the chapter on Arishta (misfortunes), arguing that life-threatening omens must be studied before any other predictions can be made.</summary>
