<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of Tajika astrology, specifically focusing on "Arishta" or life-threatening astrological combinations in the annual solar return chart. The previous page analyzed the strength of the Muntha (the progressed point) and its lord. This page details specific planetary alignments involving the 8th house (death) and the 12th house (loss) that predict physical harm or illness.</meta>

<margin>
Tajika Nilakanthi

|| 64 ||
</margin>

Now, the author describes <term>Arishta; a Sanskrit term for inauspicious planetary combinations that portend misfortune, illness, or death</term>. Regarding the phrase "The Lord of the Ascendant": If the lord of the annual ascendant <note>the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of the solar return</note> is placed in the eighth house <note>traditionally associated with death and chronic illness</note> and is aspected <gloss>viewed</gloss> by Mars. Or, if the lord of the eighth house is placed in the ascendant and is aspected by Mars. In these two <term>Yogas; specific planetary configurations</term>, there will be injury by weapons, suffering from calamities or fevers, or death itself. Alternatively, if <term>Jna; Mercury</term> and Venus are both combust—meaning they have disappeared from view due to their proximity to the Sun—then injury by weapons, calamity, and death occur. || 81 ||

Now, he says something else. Regarding the phrase "In the year": If the lord of the annual ascendant and the lord of the eighth house are placed in the twelfth, eighth, or fourth houses. Furthermore, if they are joined by a <term>Muthahasila; a Tajika astrological aspect where a faster planet "reaches" a slower one, indicating the fruition of an event</term> yoga, then death will occur due to the aggravation of the <term>Dhatus; the primary humors or elements of the body, such as wind (Vata), bile (Pitta), or phlegm (Kapha)</term> associated with those specific planets. These three conditions together form a single combination, not separate ones.

<margin>
Section 2

|| 64 ||
</margin>

> **If the lord of the annual ascendant and the lord of the eighth house are placed in the twelfth, eighth, or fourth houses, and are joined by a Muthahasila yoga, they grant death through the aggravation of the humors. || 82 ||**
> 
> **When the lord of the birth ascendant is without strength and the lord of the eighth house is in the ascendant, and is aspected by the Sun, it brings death, leprosy, itching, and calamities. || 83 ||**

If only one of these factors is present in a combination, then it results merely in distress rather than death. || 82 || Now he says something else. Regarding the phrase "At birth": This refers to the planet that was the lord of the ascendant at the time of the original birth. If, in the annual chart, this planet is "without strength"—meaning it lacks <term>Panchavargiya Bala; a five-fold system of calculating planetary strength unique to Tajika astrology</term>—and if the lord of the eighth house from the annual ascendant is "setting" <gloss>invisible</gloss>. Some scholars say "setting" means it is located in the seventh house. Others say it means it is combust due to proximity to the Sun.

In both of these opinions, if that planet is aspected by the Sun, it brings death. It also brings a collection of misfortunes such as leprosy and itching. In the verse fragment "When the lord of the birth ascendant is weak and the lord of the eighth house is setting..." the reading of the word "setting" <note>original: "astaga"</note> is actually a scribal error. Why? Because if a planet is "setting" (combust), it is traveling with the Sun, and therefore an "aspect" <gloss>a specific geometric gaze</gloss> from the Sun would be impossible. If the planet is in the same place as the Sun, the phrase "aspected by the Sun" would be redundant and meaningless. Furthermore, while a planet can be combust when in the second or twelfth signs from the Sun, an aspect from the Sun in those positions is not possible. Or perhaps the word "setting" should not be interpreted as the seventh house, as there is no foundational text to support such a meaning. In reality, instead of "setting," the text should likely read "Lord of the Ascendant." Thus: if the lord of the eighth house is in the ascendant and is aspected by the Sun, then death occurs. This is also stated by the sage Manittha: "If the lord of the birth chart is without power..." <note>The text breaks off here as the commentator begins a supporting quote from another authority.</note>

The commentator also critiques potential errors in the transmission of the verses, using logic to argue that a planet cannot be simultaneously combust and "aspected" by the Sun.</summary>

...the character of the person is lost. With the body seen by the Sun, there will be various types of injury from weapons, great suffering, and leprosy; the body becomes like that of the dead. || 83 ||

Now, he says something else regarding the phrase: "The two being combust." The **<term>Muthahasila-lagnatha</term>** <note>the lord of the annual ascendant in an applying aspect</note>, the **<term>Muthaha-swami</term>** <note>the lord of the progressed point known as the Muntha</note>, and the **<term>Varsha-lagnaswami</term>** <note>the lord of the year's ascendant</note>—when these two are combust, meaning they have disappeared from the sky due to their proximity to the Sun, and are **<term>Mandeshitau</term>** <note>aspected or seen by Saturn (Manda)</note>, then there will be the destruction of everything including wife, children, and wealth, or even death. It brings death to oneself, suffering, pain, **<term>Adhi</term>** <note>mental anguish or anxiety</note>, **<term>Vyadhi</term>** <note>physical disease or bodily ailment</note>, and fear. Now, he says another thing regarding the word: "Malefic." The **<term>Abdesha</term>** <note>the Year-Lord or ruler of the annual chart</note> becomes a **<term>Krura Musaripha</term>** <note>a "separating" aspect with a malefic planet; in Tajika astrology, this typically indicates a loss of strength or a difficult conclusion to an event</note> when he forms an **<term>Isarapha</term>** <note>the Persian-derived term for a separating aspect</note> with a malefic planet. The **<term>Janmesha</term>** <note>the lord of the ascendant from the person's original birth chart</note> is "afflicted" (cruel) if he has reached a state of weakness due to being combust or other factors. In that year, even if there is a **<term>Kamboola Yoga</term>** <note>a powerful configuration where the Moon transfers light between two planets, usually indicating success</note> with benefic planets...

> When the **<term>Muthaha</term>** and the lord of the ascendant are combust and aspected by Saturn, there is total destruction, death, suffering, mental anguish, physical illness, fear, and disease. || 84 ||
> If the Year-Lord forms a malefic **<term>Musaripha</term>** and the **<term>Janmesha</term>** <note>Birth-Lord</note> is afflicted, then even in a **<term>Kamboola</term>**, there is calamity and death; this is due to the authority of other factors. || 85 ||
> If the malefics are high in strength and the benefics are weak and placed in the **<term>Ripu</term>** <note>6th house of enemies</note> or **<term>Randhra</term>** <note>8th house of death</note>, then there will be mental anguish, disease, fear, **<term>Kali</term>** <note>strife or conflict</note>, and loss. || 86 ||

...the **<term>Muthashila</term>** <note>an applying aspect</note> involving the Moon. This means the Moon forms an applying aspect with them, and one of them also forms a **<term>Muthashila</term>**. Because of the word "even," it implies that even when a benefic **<term>Kamboola Yoga</term>** is present—which usually suggests the absence of misfortune—there will still be calamity and death. One will experience death through the experience of various kinds of grief. How much more so if a benefic **<term>Kamboola</term>** is absent? This is as stated. "Due to the authority of others" means taking the **<term>Muthaha-lord</term>** and others as the authorities in place of the **<term>Varsha-lord</term>**; if the **<term>Janmesha</term>** is afflicted, then calamity and death will occur. 

Here, some explain "even in a **<term>Kamboola</term>** with benefics" as: if the **<term>Year-Lord</term>** and the **<term>Birth-Ascendant-Lord</term>** are in a **<term>Muthashila</term>** yoga, and the Moon also forms a **<term>Muthashila</term>** with them (which constitutes a **<term>Kamboola</term>**). However, that interpretation is not supported because there is no specific word here describing the **<term>Muthashila</term>** between the birth and year lords. Since a **<term>Kamboola</term>** is defined by the Moon’s **<term>Muthashila</term>** with two planets that are already in a **<term>Muthashila</term>** with each other, and since this specific yoga has a different name, that is enough of an explanation. || 87 ||

Now, he says another thing regarding "Malefics." If the **<term>Malefic planets</term>** are high in strength, being powerful according to the **<term>Panchavargi</term>** <note>a five-fold system for calculating planetary strength in Tajika astrology</note>, and the **<term>Benefic planets</term>** are weak in the **<term>Panchavarga</term>**...

<margin>
*Tājika Nīlakaṇṭhī* <note>Original: ता० नी० (Tā. Nī.). This refers to the seminal 16th-century text on Tajika astrology by Nilakantha.</note>

An <term>Isarāpha yoga</term> <note>A separating aspect in Tajika astrology where a faster planet moves past a slower one, generally indicating the fading of an influence.</note> does not occur with the Moon because of the Moon's swift motion; this is because the rules do not state that a malefic planet can create an Isarāpha aspect with the Moon in this specific context. || 94 ||

Now another rule is stated: "The Moon, etc." When the Moon is combust—meaning it has entered the "Circle of the Sun" <note>original: "arkamaṇḍala"; this occurs when the Moon is very close to the Sun, losing its visibility and strength.</note>—and is placed in the sixth, twelfth, eighth, or fourth houses, the person will suffer from various types of diseases arising from an imbalance of the <term>Tridoṣa</term> <note>The three biological humors in Ayurvedic medicine: Vāta (air/ether), Pitta (fire/water), and Kapha (water/earth).</note>. However, if the Moon is aspected by Jupiter <note>original: "ijya-dṛśā"; Jupiter is considered the great benefic or protector.</note>, then there will be health and happiness. Regarding the phrase "placed in the sixth, twelfth, eighth, or fourth," some readings state "placed in the twelfth or eighth." It was also said by the sage Manittha: "When the Lord of the Night (the Moon) stays within the solar circle and resides in the sixth, twelfth, or eighth house, it causes great suffering through many types of humored-based illnesses during its period." || 95 ||

Now another rule is stated: "Haddā, etc." If the <term>Haddā lord</term> <note>Haddā refers to the "Terms" or "Bounds," a subdivision of a zodiac sign common in Perso-Arabic astrology.</note> and the <term>Lagneśa</term> <note>The Lord of the Ascendant or the planet ruling the rising sign of the year.</note> are located in the seventh, eighth, or twelfth houses—

### The Moon in the Sun’s circle, located in the sixth, twelfth, eighth, or fourth houses, causes various diseases from the three humors; if aspected by Jupiter, there is well-being. || 95 || If the Haddā lord and the Year-Ascendant lord are in the seventh, eighth, or twelfth houses joined with malefics—

### —they bring death during their own planetary periods. If aspected by benefics, one should predict favorable results. || 96 || When planets are in the twelfth and second houses from the year-ascendant, one in direct motion and one in retrograde, disease occurs. Likewise, if the Year-Lord, Ascendant-lord, or Birth-lord are so placed, it indicates imprisonment. || 97 ||

### If the Triplicity Lord is in its sign of debilitation and aspected by a malefic, one's work is ruined. If the Year-Lord is in an enemy sign or combust, there is disease and calamity. || 98 ||

—and if they are joined by malefic planets, then those two lords will bring death during their major or minor planetary periods. If they are aspected by benefic planets or by friends, then happiness will occur. || 96 ||

Now another rule is stated: "From the Year-Ascendant, etc." This refers to a "Scissor" combination <note>original: "kartarī yoga"; where a house or planet is hemmed in by others.</note> where planets are in the twelfth and second houses from the Year-Ascendant—one moving forward and one in retrograde. For example, a malefic planet in direct motion in the twelfth, and a malefic planet in retrograde in the second. In such a case, disease will arise. In this same way, if the Lord of the Year, the Lord of the Year-Ascendant, or the Lord of the Birth-Ascendant are caught in this malefic "Scissor" yoga, then <term>imprisonment</term> <note>original: "bandhanam"; signifies literal jail, captivity, or being severely restricted.</note> will occur. || 97 ||

Now another rule is stated: "Debilitated, etc." If the <term>Trirāśipa</term> <note>The Lord of the Triplicity, a set of three signs belonging to the same element.</note> of the Year-Ascendant is in its sign of debilitation and is aspected by malefics, then the work one is concerned about will be destroyed. This is one combination. Now another: if the <term>Itthashesha</term> <note>The planet that is the "Lord of the Application," governing the specific outcome of the year.</note> is in an enemy's sign or is combust, there will be sickness and misfortune.

If the Lord of the <term>Iththashala</term> <note>A planetary aspect in Tajika astrology where a faster planet approaches a slower one.</note>, the Lord of the <term>Munthahā</term> <note>A sensitive point in the annual horoscope that moves one sign per year from the birth ascendant.</note>, or the Lord of the Year is placed in an enemy sign or is combust <note>original: "astaṃyāte"; hidden by the Sun's light.</note> due to proximity to the Sun, there will be a great calamity caused by an excess of sickness and disease. || 98 ||

Now the "affliction of the Moon" <note>original: "candrāriṣṭaṃ"</note> and its exceptions are stated. Regarding the phrase "The Moon, etc.": If the Moon is placed in the twelfth, sixth, eighth, seventh, or the first house <note>original: "bhū"; the author notes this refers to the first house/ascendant here.</note> from the annual ascendant <note>original: "varṣalagna"</note>, and is aspected by <term>malefics</term> <note>Cruel or naturally unfavorable planets like Mars or Saturn.</note> but not by <term>benefics</term> <note>Kind or naturally favorable planets like Jupiter or Venus.</note>, then it produces misfortune or even death. If it is aspected by one or two malefics, it causes misfortune; if aspected by all malefics, it results in death. 

In this context of misfortune, some have interpreted the word "earth" <note>original: "bhū"</note> to mean the fourth house, but that is incorrect. Why? Because the word "earth" is a synonym for the ascendant (first house). Although "earth" is occasionally used for the fourth house, no text describes the Moon in the fourth house as yielding inauspicious results; indeed, all treatises state that the Moon in the fourth house is auspicious. It is said: "The Moon in the ascendant, seventh, twelfth, sixth, or eighth..."

> When the Moon is in the twelfth, sixth, eighth, first, or seventh houses and is aspected by malefics and not by benefics, it creates misfortune or death. If aspected by Mars, there is danger from fire. ||
> If aspected by Saturn, Rahu, or Ketu, there is fear of enemies, wind-born diseases <note>Conditions related to the "Vata" humor, such as joint pain or nervous disorders.</note>, and poverty. If aspected by the Sun, or if it receives a "favorable look" from Jupiter, one should instead predict auspicious results. || 99 ||
> If the Munthahā is joined with or aspected by a malefic, or joined with Saturn, it bestows mental or physical illness. If it is placed in the twelfth, fourth, sixth, eighth, or seventh house of the birth chart...
> ...or in the eighth house of the annual chart, and is joined or aspected by a malefic, the wise should consider this a sign of death. || 100 ||

->### ॥ Thus ends the Chapter on Ill-Fortune ॥<-
<note>original: "ityariṣṭādhyāyaḥ"</note>

Regarding "the wind-traveler" <note>referring to the Moon</note> and other matters: It is also stated that "the Cold-Rayed One" (the Moon) residing in the eighth, first, seventh, sixth, or twelfth houses [is malefic]. This is found in all standard texts. Such a Moon, when aspected by Mars, causes fear of fire or danger from weapons. When aspected by Saturn, Rahu, or Ketu, the Moon brings fear from enemies. When aspected by the Sun, the Moon causes diseases arising from "wind" (Vata), as well as poverty. 

Now for the exception to what was stated: if the Moon receives a "friendly aspect" from the benefics Mercury, Jupiter, or Venus, one should predict auspicious results. Even if it is only aspected by "the Teacher" <note>original: "ijyā"; a name for Jupiter.</note>, one should still declare the result to be auspicious. || 99 ||

Now the misfortune caused by the Munthahā is described. Regarding the phrase "joined with a malefic": If the Munthahā is joined with malefic planets or aspected by or joined with Saturn, it bestows mental and physical illness. || This is one combination. || Now another: If that Munthahā is positioned in the birth ascendant's twelfth, fourth, sixth, eighth, or seventh houses...

<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of Tajika astrology. Having concluded the discussion on combinations for misfortune (Arishta), the text now transitions into the chapter on how these misfortunes are neutralized or cancelled (Arishta-bhanga). This is a standard structure in Indian astrological texts: first defining the problem, then providing the remedies or exceptions.</meta>

Wherever it is situated, if the <term>Munthā</term> <note>A sensitive point in Tajika astrology that moves one zodiac sign per year from the birth ascendant.</note> of the year is placed in the eighth house from the year's ascendant, and especially if it is joined with or aspected by <term>malefics</term> <note>Cruel or naturally unfavorable planets like Mars, Saturn, or Rahu.</note>, it gives death <note>original: "mṛtiṃ maraṇaṃ dante"</note>. This should also be carefully considered by noble astrologers. || 100 || 

Thus ends the Chapter on Misfortunes in the commentary on the *Varṣatantra* <note>A foundational text on Tajika (Perso-Arabic) astrology.</note> composed by Viśvanātha, son of Śrī Divākara.

### ## Now, the Chapter on the Cancellation of Misfortunes is explained.

Regarding the phrase "The Lord of the Ascendant": If the Lord of the yearly <term>Ascendant</term> <note>original: "lagna"; the rising sign at the moment of the year's commencement.</note> is endowed with strength—specifically being strong according to the <term>Pañcavargī</term> <note>A five-fold system for calculating planetary strength unique to Tajika astrology.</note> scoring—and is aspected by or joined with <term>benefics</term> <note>Naturally favorable planets like Jupiter, Venus, or a strong Moon.</note>, or if it is situated in a <term>Trine</term> <note>The 5th or 9th houses, considered highly auspicious.</note> or a <term>Center</term> <note>The 1st, 4, 7th, or 10th houses, representing the pillars of the horoscope.</note>, it destroys misfortune. During its planetary period, it bestows happiness and wealth. || 1 || 

Now another rule is stated regarding "Jupiter": If Jupiter is in a center or a trine, is not aspected by malefics, and is aspected by benefics—such a Jupiter

### ### || Now, the Cancellation of Misfortune ||

-> The Lord of the Ascendant, being strong, and aspected by benefics, <-
-> or placed in a center or a trine, destroys misfortune and gives happiness and wealth. || 1 || <-
-> Jupiter in a center or a trine, not aspected by malefics but aspected by benefics, <-
-> points toward the destruction of misfortunes arising from the Ascendant or the Moon, and grants happiness. || 2 || <-
-> If the Lord of the fourth house is joined with or aspected by benefics, it grants happiness, fame, and wealth. || 3 || <-
-> If Jupiter and the Lord of the birth ascendant are in the first, second, or fourth houses, they grant happiness and wealth. || 3 || <-
-> If the Lord of the seventh house is in the ascendant, and Jupiter is also in the ascendant, <-
-> and they are not aspected by cruel planets but by benefics or friends, this destroys misfortune. <-
-> It points to the attainment of wealth, fame, and happiness; in its period, one receives favors from the king. || 4 || <-

destroys the misfortune arising from the Ascendant or the Moon. It destroys misfortunes born from the <term>Munthā</term>, the Ascendant, or the Moon, and subsequently bestows wealth and happiness. || 2 || 

Now another rule is stated regarding "Happiness": If the fourth house <note>original: "sukha-sthāna"</note> from the yearly ascendant is joined with its own Lord and aspected by <term>benefics</term> <note>original: "sadbhiḥ"; literally "the good ones."</note>, it results in happiness, fame, and wealth. This is one combination. 

Now another: If Jupiter is in the Ascendant, or in the third house, or in the fourth house, and the Lord of the Birth Ascendant <note>original: "janmeṭ"; the planet that ruled the first house at the moment of the individual's birth.</note> is also present, then it gives happiness and wealth. || 3 || 

Now another is stated regarding "In the Ascendant": If the Lord of the seventh house <note>original: "dyuneśaḥ"</note> is in the Ascendant, and Jupiter <note>original: "surejyaḥ"</note> is also in the Ascendant, and they are not aspected by malefics but are aspected by benefics or friendly planets—

### ## The Strength of House Lords

Now, he says something else: "Endowed with strength," etc. If the lords of <term>Fortune</term> <note>The 9th house, known as the Dharma-sthana.</note> and <term>Wealth</term> <note>The 2nd house.</note> are both endowed with strength—meaning they are powerful according to the <term>five-fold strength</term> <note>original: "pañcavargīya-bala"; a specific Tajika system of calculating planetary power using five different criteria.</note>—and are not aspected by <term>malefic</term> <note>original: "krūraiḥ"; literally "cruel" planets like Mars or Saturn.</note> or sinful planets, and if they are situated in the <term>Ascendant</term> <note>original: "tanugau"; literally "going to the body" or the 1st house.</note>, then there is a destruction of misfortune. Mars gives elephants, horses, gold, clothing, land, and an abundance of happiness. || 12 || Now, through the examination of planetary strength and weakness, he describes the auspicious and inauspicious results regarding <term>Rajayoga</term> <note>original: "rājayoga"; a "royal combination" of planets indicating high status, power, or professional success.</note>. "In this way..." <note>original: "ittham"</note>. In the manner described, one should consider the strength and weakness—determined by the <term>five-fold strength</term> <note>original: "pañca-vargīya-bala"; a specific Tajika method of calculating planetary strength based on five distinct criteria.</note>—of the planet forming the Rajayoga at the time of birth and in the yearly chart. One must deliberate whether the planet is in its sign of exaltation and so on, or if it is weak, having lost its power by being in its sign of debilitation and so on; only then should one declare the presence of a Rajayoga or its destruction. || 13 || Now he describes the destruction of a Rajayoga. "The Year-Lord, the Lord of the Munthaha, and other planets..." The Year-Lord is the ruler of the year; the "Lord of the <term>Itthaha</term>" <note>original: "itthahēsha", here synonymous with the Munthahēsha or the lord of the annual progressed point.</note> is the ruler of the Munthaha. By the word "others," the lords of the yearly ascendant and the birth ascendant are meant. If these planets are joined with or aspected by malefic planets, or if the benefic planets...

## Thus, having deliberated upon the strength and weakness of the planet forming the combination at the time of birth and in the year, one should declare the royal combination or its cancellation. || 13 || 
## If the Year-Lord, the Lord of the Munthaha, and other planets are conjoined with or aspected by malefics, or are combust or in debilitation, || 14 || 
## or if the benefics are lacking in strength, then the cancellation of the royal combination occurs, along with the loss of wealth and happiness. || 14 ||
## || Thus ends the Chapter on the Cancellation of Misfortunes || 
## Sri Chintamani, a jewel of the lineage of Garga and a knower of calculation, had a son named Ananta, of infinite intellect, who composed the "Janu-Paddhati" to destroy the darkness of small-mindedness. || 15 ||
## His son, the wise Nilakantha, by the command of the learned Shiva, spoke on the results of the yearly Munthaha, along with the combinations of misfortunes and the like. || 15 ||

...are lacking in strength, then one should predict the destruction of the Rajayoga. This is one combination. Now another: if a planet is <term>combust</term> <note>original: "astaga"; becoming invisible and weak due to being too close to the Sun.</note>, or in its sign of debilitation, or if the benefic planets are weak according to the five-fold strength calculation, then one should predict the destruction of the Rajayoga. In both of these combinations, one should predict the loss of wealth and happiness. If, however, the benefic planets are in their signs of exaltation, their own houses, or situated in the <term>angles</term> <note>original: "kēndra"; the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses.</note>, <term>trines</term> <note>original: "trikōṇa"; the 1st, 5th, and 9th houses.</note>, or the eleventh house, then they become the makers of Rajayoga. Thus says <term>Manittha</term> <note>original: "māṇitthaḥ"; an ancient authority in Indian astrology, often identified by scholars with the Graeco-Egyptian astrologer Manetho.</note>: "If the Moon, Mercury, Jupiter, or Venus are in exaltation or in their own <term>divisions</term> <note>original: "svāṃśa"; specific sub-divisions of a zodiac sign where a planet gains strength.</note>, or if they are in trines, angles, or the eleventh house, aspected by or joined with their own friends and situated in their own divisions... [they grant] elephants, horses, gems, and clothing..."

# ॥ Thus ends the Chapter on the Cancellation of Misfortunes ॥

# ॥ Now the Chapter on the Consideration of the Twelve Houses is Explained ॥

### ॥ Therein, first, the Consideration of the Body-House ॥
<note>original: "tanu-bhāva-vicāraḥ"; the First House or Ascendant, which represents the physical body, health, and general personality.</note>

Regarding the phrase "whichever house..." <note>original: "yo bhāva iti"</note>. Among the twelve houses, whichever house is aspected by or conjoined with its own ruler, or a benefic planet <note>original: "saumya"; planets like Jupiter, Venus, or a well-placed Mercury</note>, or both—that house prospers and grows. However, if a house is aspected by or conjoined with a malefic planet other than its own ruler, then that house suffers destruction. If a house is influenced by a mixture of both benefic and malefic planets, one should declare mixed results—a combination of auspicious and inauspicious effects.

<margin>Garga says:</margin>
"A planet situated in its sign of debilitation or in an enemy’s sign causes the destruction of the house. In a neutral sign, its effect is middling. In a friend's sign, a trine <note>original: "trikoṇa"; specific auspicious divisions of the zodiac</note>, or its sign of exaltation, a planet is..."

## ॥ Now for the Consideration of the Houses ॥

> ॥ Whichever house is aspected by or conjoined with its ruler or a benefic, that house prospers. When aspected by or conjoined with a malefic, it is destroyed; with mixed influences, one should speak of mixed results. ॥ 1 ॥

> When the Lord of the Ascendant <note>original: "lagnādhipa"</note> is endowed with strength, there is happiness, health, the arrival of wealth, and luxury. When it has middling strength, there is moderate happiness and gain of wealth; when it has little strength, there is an abundance of hardship and calamity. ॥ 2 ॥

> If the ruler of the birth-ascendant, the ruler of the year-ascendant, or the ruler of the <term>Munthaha</term> <note>original: "munthahā-pati"; a mathematical point in Tajika astrology that progresses one sign every year</note>, or the Year-Lord, etc., is devoid of strength, he causes the loss of physical health and eyesight, and a lack of enthusiasm. There is debilitation and humiliation even from one's father and mother. If the Moon is weak, there is a loss of work, poverty, defeat, and fear of disputes, diseases, and the like. ॥ 3 ॥

The house prospers or increases its burden. Here, he describes the results for the sixth, eighth, and twelfth houses, which are the opposite of the others:

<margin>Varāha says:</margin>
"Prosperity and calamity are as described for the benefic houses; however, Satyācārya says the results are reversed for the twelfth, sixth, and eighth houses <note>The 6th, 8th, and 12th houses are generally considered 'dusthanas' or houses of suffering; here, malefic influence might 'destroy' the negative indications of these houses, which could be seen as a positive.</note>." ॥ 1 ॥

### ॥ Now he describes the auspicious and inauspicious results of the Lord of the Ascendant ॥

Regarding the "Lord of the Ascendant..." When the Lord of the Ascendant—the ruler of the yearly ascendant—is strong according to the <term>five-fold strength</term> <note>original: "pañca-vargī-bala"; a Tajika method of calculating planetary power through five specific criteria</note>, then auspicious results such as happiness occur. When there is middling strength, the gain of happiness and wealth is small. When there is little strength (weakness), there is an abundance of hardship and calamity.

Now he describes special results when the planetary ruler of the year loses its strength. "The Lord of the Birth and Year..." The rulers of the birth ascendant or the year ascendant, or the Lord of the <term>Munthaha</term>, or the Year-Lord, and so on—the word "etcetera" implies...

It explains how the strength and aspects of house lords and the Munthaha influence a person's health, wealth, and status for the year.</summary>

--- Page 142 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical analysis of the annual horoscope (Varshaphala) according to the Tajika system. Having previously discussed planetary strengths, it now details the specific physical and social misfortunes that occur when the rulers of the year or the triplicities are weak, specifically focusing on the Year Ascendant (Varsha-lagna) and the Munthaha lord.</meta>

Regarding the rulers of the day and night "triplicities" <note>original: "trairāśika"; a division of the twelve signs into four groups of three based on the elements</note>. Among these ruling planets, starting with the Sun, if any are "deprived of strength" <note>original: "naṣṭabala"</note> or possess "fivefold weakness" <note>original: "pañcālpabala"</note>, the author describes their effects.

First, if the **Sun** is weak, it causes the "dissolution of the skin and eyes." "Skin" refers to the body's surface, and its "dissolution" refers to destruction by diseases like leprosy <note>original: "kuṣṭha"</note>. "Dissolution of the eyes" refers to blindness. It also causes a "lack of enthusiasm" <note>original: "nirutsāhatā"</note>, "lowness" <note>original: "nīcatvaṃ"</note>—meaning a life lived through degraded occupations—and "defeat or suffering" <note>original: "abhibhavaḥ"</note> caused by the father or even the mother.

Next, the results of the **Moon** are stated. (The text notes this is clear in the following verse). ॥ 3 ॥

Now, the results of **Mars** and the other planets are stated in two verses. The results for Mars are also clear. ॥ 4 ॥ 5 ॥

Now, the results of the **Ascendant** <note>original: "lagna"</note> are stated. The "Ascendant" refers to the **Year Ascendant** <note>original: "varṣa-lagna"</note>. If it is conjoined with "malefic planets" <note>original: "pāpa-grahaiḥ"; such as Saturn or Mars</note> and is neither aspected <note>original: "dṛṣṭaṃ"; the planetary 'gaze' or influence</note> by nor joined by "benefic planets" <note>original: "saumyaiḥ"; such as Jupiter or Venus</note>, then men suffer disputes and similar misfortunes. ॥ 6 ॥

Next, the results are stated for when the ruling planets are "endowed with strength" <note>original: "baliṣṭhe sati"</note>.

> If **Mars** is weak, there is instability and cowardice; if **Mercury**, then delusion and defeat. If **Jupiter**, there is a decline in righteousness and a livelihood resulting in difficult fruits. ॥ 4 ॥

> If **Venus** is weak, there is the destruction of pleasures and enjoyments, and quarrels with women. If **Saturn**, there is sorrow caused by servants and diseases arising from an imbalance of the "wind" humor. ॥ 5 ॥ <note>original: "vāta-prakopa"; Vata is one of the three bodily humors in Ayurveda, associated with air and movement.</note>

> If the **Year Ascendant** is joined by malefic planets and is not aspected by nor joined by benefic planets, then men suffer disputes, deception, and the eating of "wicked" <note>original: "duṣṭam-aśanaṃ"; i.e., impure or unhealthy</note> food. ॥ 6 ॥

> <margin>The Ruler of the Birth Ascendant and the Ruler of the Year Ascendant</margin> If the ruler of the Birth Ascendant, the Year Ascendant, or the **Munthaha Lord** <term>Munthaha: A sensitive mathematical point in the annual chart that progresses one sign per year from the birth ascendant</term> are endowed with strength, then the year beginning then is said to bring incomparable happiness, fame, and the arrival of wealth. 

> If these are located in the "unfortunate houses" <note>original: "ṣaṣṭhāṣṭāntyagatā"; the 6th, 8th, or 12th houses</note>, they provide sorrow and fear. If they are "powerless" <note>original: "nirvīryā"</note>, this year is declared inauspicious, unless there is a "benefic aspect" <note>original: "śubhekṣāṃ"</note>. ॥ 7 ॥

If the ruler of the eighth house from the Year Ascendant or the Year's Munthaha Lord are strong and not located in the sixth, eighth, or twelfth houses, then the year is described as beautiful, bringing happiness, fame, and wealth. If these strong planets are located in the sixth, eighth, or twelfth houses, then it is established that they provide sorrow. Here again, if those four <note>the birth lord, year lord, etc.</note> are "powerless" (lacking strength) and situated in the sixth, eighth, or twelfth houses, they provide sorrow and fear. If these four are not "aspected" by benefic planets, this year should be called inauspicious. If benefic planets do aspect them, then the previously mentioned auspicious results should be predicted. ॥ 7 ॥

<summary>This page details the negative life outcomes (such as skin disease, cowardice, and poverty) when specific planets are weak in an annual chart, while also defining the conditions for a prosperous year based on the strength of the Year Ascendant and Munthaha lord.</summary>
<keywords>triplicities, planetary weakness, Sun, Moon, Mars, Year Ascendant, Munthaha Lord, unfortunate houses, Tajika astrology, health and status</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical analysis of the annual horoscope (Varshaphala). Having concluded the discussion on the First House (the Body), the text now transitions to the Second House (Wealth and Finances), explaining how the positions of planets in the birth chart interact with their positions in the current year's chart to predict prosperity or loss.</meta>

<margin>Tajika Nilakanthi</margin>
<margin>Section 2</margin>
->Page 70<-

Now, the author describes the **combination for the loss of wealth** <note>original: "dhana-nāśa-yoga"</note>. In the verse beginning with "In the birth...", the "wealth-giving planet" refers to the planet that grants wealth in the birth chart <note>original: "sūtau"; the natal horoscope</note>. If that planet and the ruler of the House of Wealth are both "deprived of strength" <note>original: "naṣṭabalau"</note> in the annual chart, then a loss of wealth occurs. Furthermore, regarding the property of others, he receives an "accusation" <note>original: "abhisāpaṃ"; a curse or legal reproach</note>; this means that even if you have returned a deposit, people will accuse you, saying, "You have not returned it." Conversely, if both planets are strong, they become the givers of wealth. ॥ 8 ॥

Now, he explains a general rule. Following the same logic, the lords of all the houses <note>original: "bhāvas"; the twelve divisions of the horoscope representing different life areas</note> in the birth chart are the givers of the fruits of those houses. If these house-lords are strong in the annual chart, they "nourish" <note>original: "poṣakāḥ"</note> or strengthen those houses. If they are deprived of strength in the annual chart, wise astrologers should understand that those specific houses will face "destruction" <note>original: "nāśāya"</note>. ॥ 9 ॥

**Thus ends the consideration of the House of the Body (First House).**

***

### Now, the House of Wealth (Second House).

### If the wealth-giving planet at birth and the lord of the house of wealth are both
### weak in the year chart, there is loss of wealth and accusations regarding deposits. ॥ 8 ॥
### Thus, for all the houses, the lords at birth are the nourishers;
### if they are weak in the year, their destruction is to be inferred by the wise. ॥ 9 ॥
### This concludes the inquiry into the First House.
### Now begins the inquiry into the House of Wealth.
### If Jupiter is the lord of wealth at birth and resides in the wealth house in the year,
### and forms an "applying aspect" with the lord of the year's Ascendant,
### then there is gain of wealth throughout the entire year;
### but if it forms a "separating aspect" with malefics, wealth and grain are lost. ॥ 1 ॥
### If Jupiter aspects the wealth house at birth and is the strong Ruler of the Year,
### then a great gain of wealth occurs without any effort. ॥ 2 ॥

"At birth" means in the natal chart. If **Jupiter** <note>original: "jīvaḥ"</note> is the lord of the wealth house <note>original: "vittādhipaḥ"</note> and in the annual chart it is "placed in the house of wealth" <note>original: "vittago"; the 2nd house</note>, and it forms an <term>Ithashala Yoga</term> <note>original: "itthaśālī"; a Tajika aspect where a faster planet approaches a slower one, indicating the manifestation of an event</note> with the lord of the year's Ascendant, then there will be a gain of wealth throughout the entire year. However, if Jupiter forms an <term>Isarafa Yoga</term> <note>original: "sarāphe"; a separating aspect where the planets move away from each other</note> with "malefics" <note>original: "krūraiḥ"; planets like Saturn or Mars</note> other than the Ascendant lord, then there will be a loss of wealth and grain for the whole year. ॥ 1 ॥

Now, the second combination for obtaining wealth is stated. If, at the time of birth, Jupiter "aspects the house of wealth" <note>original: "arthāvalokī"</note>—meaning it gazes upon the second house—and in the annual chart it becomes the "Year Lord" <note>original: "abdeśo"</note> and is "strong" <note>original: "balavān"</note>, then in that year, a great gain of wealth occurs without much...

In this way, wealth is obtained without much effort or strain. ॥ 2 ॥ Now, the author extends the previously mentioned principle to all the houses. He says: **"In this way..."** In this manner, whatever house the planet Jupiter <note>original: "Guru Brihaspati"</note> rules in the birth chart—such as the third house of siblings and so on—if that same planet occupies that same house in the annual chart and forms an *Itthashala* <term>Itthashala: a Tajika aspect where a faster planet approaches a slower one, indicating the fulfillment of an event</term> with the Lord of the Ascendant <note>original: "Lagnesha"</note>, then the person attains the happiness associated with that house. ॥ 3 ॥

Now, he states this again: **"Similarly..."** Likewise, whatever house Jupiter aspects <note>looks upon</note> in the birth chart, if Jupiter is the Year-Lord <term>Year-Lord: the "Abdapa," the most influential planet governing the entire year</term> in the annual chart, then the happiness relating to that house is achieved. So say the experts in Tajika astrology. ॥ 4 ॥

> In this way, if the lord of a house at birth is in that same house during the year, and Jupiter forms an *Itthashala* with the Lord of the Ascendant, happiness from that house will manifest. ॥ 3 ॥

If Jupiter is afflicted by malefic planets and is in the Eighth House or the Second House, a punishment <note>original: "daṇḍaḥ"; often a legal fine or government penalty</note> will surely fall. ॥ 5 ॥
>

> In the same way, if Venus <note>original: "Sita"</note> is the Year-Lord, there is an abundance of money and grain. However, the conjunction of the Lord of Wealth and the Lord of the Ascendant destroys the happiness of wealth. ॥ 7 ॥

Now, the author speaks of the combination for meager wealth and the combination for punishment: **"The Lord of the Sixth House at birth..."** If the Lord of the Sixth House from the birth chart is the benefic planet Mercury and he resides in the sixth house of the annual chart, he provides only meager results. If Jupiter is "afflicted" <note>pained or weakened</note> by malefic planets and is placed in the Eighth House <note>original: "randhra"</note> or the House of Wealth <note>the Second House</note> from the annual ascendant, then certainly a punishment or fine imposed by the king <note>the government</note> will occur. ॥ 5 ॥

Now, he describes another combination for the gain of wealth: **"Jupiter..."** If Jupiter is in the House of Wealth and is aspected by or joined with benefic planets, he will grant happiness through the state and through finances. This is one combination. Now another: if a benefic planet aspects the *Muthaha*—the sign occupied by the progressed point—in both the birth chart and the annual chart, it specifically grants power and happiness. ॥ 6 ॥

Now, the combination for the **destruction of wealth**: 
If the Lord of the Wealth House and the Lord of the Annual Ascendant are both located together in the Wealth House, it may cause the destruction of financial happiness. Now, one might argue: "This is incorrect, because the conjunction of the Wealth Lord and the Ascendant Lord in the Wealth House has been described elsewhere as giving auspicious results." To this, the following is the intended meaning: 

A conjunction in a single sign is of three types. The first is the *Muthashila* yoga <term>Muthashila: Also known as Itthashala, a favorable aspect where a faster planet approaches a slower one, indicating success.</term>; the second is the *Ishrafa* yoga <term>Ishrafa: An unfavorable aspect where a faster planet has already passed a slower one, indicating that the opportunity has been missed or the matter will fail.</term>. In the absence of both *Muthashila* and *Ishrafa*, it is a simple conjunction, which is the third type. Among these three, the simple conjunction and the *Muthashila* are auspicious and provide financial happiness, as stated in other texts. Thus, if in the Wealth Lot <term>Saham: A specific point in the horoscope calculated by the distance between two planets added to the ascendant; the Mal-Saham specifically relates to prosperity.</term> or in the Wealth House, there is a conjunction with Mercury, Jupiter, or Venus, or if these three aspect that place, then one attains abundant wealth and reaches a position of state-like power within their family. ॥ 9 ॥ 

Now he describes another combination for the acquisition of wealth: **"Wealth and Wealth..."** If the Lord of the Wealth House and the Lord of the Wealth Lot are aspected by benefic planets with a "friendly gaze" <note>An aspect from a planet in a friendly sign.</note>, then they grant gains with ease. When they are aspected by an "enemy's gaze," then it should be said that gain occurs only with great effort. ॥ 10 ॥

Tā. Nī. <note>original: "Tā. Nī.", referring to the Tajika Nilakanthi, the foundational text for this system of astrology.</note>
॥ 71 ॥
</margin>

### Now, two other yogas for wealth and the yoga for its destruction are explained.

**By the gaze of a friend** and so on: Now, regarding the two components—the term *Artha* <term>Artha: Literally "purpose" or "meaning," but in astrology, it specifically refers to the 2nd House of Wealth and Assets.</term> refers to the House of Wealth, and the term *Anga* <term>Anga: Literally "limb" or "body," referring here to the Annual Ascendant (Varshalagna), which represents the individual in that year.</term> refers to the Annual Ascendant. If the lords of these two houses form a *Muthashila* yoga <term>Muthashila: An auspicious aspect where a faster planet approaches a slower one, indicating the successful completion of an event.</term> while in a friendly aspect with one another, then wealth is acquired easily, without any struggle. 

On the other hand, if those same two—the Lord of Wealth and the Lord of the Ascendant—form a *Musarifa* yoga <term>Musarifa: Also known as Ishrafa, an unfavorable aspect where a faster planet has already passed a slower one, indicating that the opportunity has passed or will result in failure.</term>, there is a destruction of wealth and a fear arising from bad conduct or "wicked policy." This specific meaning was already clarified previously. || 11 ||

### Now, another yoga for wealth is explained.

**In the birth** and so on: If Jupiter <note>original: "Ijya," a name for Brihaspati/Jupiter meaning 'the one to be worshiped.'</note>, in whichever zodiac sign he resides in the natal chart <note>original: "Janmani," referring to the birth horoscope.</note>, becomes the Annual Ascendant for the year, and if that sign is aspected by or joined with benefic planets or its own lord, then the person gains freedom from disease, authority/lordship, and wealth. || 12 ||

### Now, two other yogas for the gain and loss of wealth are explained.

**The son** and so on: If the Sun <note>original: "Ravi"</note>, who was in the 5th house <note>original: "Suta," literally meaning "son" but used here to denote the 5th house of the natal chart.</note> at the time of birth, is situated in the House of Wealth in the annual chart, then he bestows the happiness of wealth. 

> If a *Muthashila* yoga occurs between the lords of wealth and the ascendant under a friendly aspect, wealth comes easily. If they form a *Musarifa*, there is destruction of wealth and fear from bad policy. || 11 || Whichever sign Jupiter resides in at birth, if that sign becomes the Annual Ascendant and is joined or aspected by a benefic or its lord, it bestows health, authority, and wealth. || 12 || If the Sun, who was in the 5th house at birth, is in the house of wealth in the annual chart, he gives wealth and happiness. If Saturn is in the wealth house, there is destruction of work, little gain, and expenditure of wealth. || 13 || Happiness from brothers occurs if Saturn [in the wealth house] is joined with Jupiter; if aspected by benefics, there is prosperity. If aspected by or joined with cruel planets, the result is completely opposite. || 14 || If Jupiter, the lord of wealth at birth, is in the annual chart, whichever house he occupies from the annual ascendant, he gives gains relative to that house. || 15 ||

If Saturn <note>original: "Shani"</note> is the lord of the House of Wealth or resides there, then the destruction of work and other such misfortunes occur. || 13 ||

### Now, the exception to the defect of Saturn and the yoga for the destruction of wealth are explained.

**Happiness from brothers** and so on: If Saturn resides in the House of Wealth but is joined with Jupiter, then happiness derived from one's brothers will occur. **By the aspect of benefics**: if such a Saturn is aspected by benefic planets, then various types of prosperity <note>original: "Bhutayah," meaning riches or divine powers.</note> will manifest. 

In all the previously mentioned yogas for the gain of wealth, if the planets creating the yoga are joined with or aspected by extremely "cruel" <term>Cruel planets: Malefics, such as Mars or the Sun in certain positions, which can ruin the positive potential of a yoga.</term> planets, then the entire result becomes the opposite—meaning, it results in the loss of wealth. || 14 ||

### Now, the results of Jupiter, the lord of the natal wealth house, occupying the twelve houses from the Annual Ascendant are described in four verses.

**As the Lord of Wealth:** If at the time of birth, Jupiter <note>original: "Guru"</note> is the Lord of the Wealth House <term>Dhana-bhava: The 2nd house of the zodiac, governing assets and speech.</term> and, in the annual chart, he also holds the position of the Year Lord <term>Varshesha: The planet that rules the entire year in the Tajika system.</term>, then he grants gains according to whichever house he occupies. This means he bestows the specific fruits of that house. 

He explains this further: **In the Ascendant** and so on. If he is situated in the Ascendant <term>Lagna: The 1st house, representing the physical body and self.</term>, he brings nourishment and strength to the body. || 15 || In the **House of Wealth**, there is an increase of gold, silver, and similar treasures. Situated in the **House of Siblings** <note>original: "sahaja-rksha-ga," referring to the 3rd house.</note>, he brings happiness through brothers and relatives. In the **House of Friends** <term>Suhrid: A synonym for the 4th house, which governs the home, mother, and lands.</term>, he brings happiness derived from the father, mother, and "patience and land"—meaning the village and the joy generated from it. In the sixth, he gives loss and fear from the host of enemies. In the seventh, from women; in the eighth, death; in the ninth, he is the path for the cause of wealth. || 17 || In the tenth, fortune from the king; in the eleventh, wealth from the royal family; in the twelfth, he brings expenditure. Thus the wise should deliberate, as others have also declared. || 18 ||**

This concludes the Deliberation on the House of Wealth. 

### Now, the Deliberation on the House of Siblings.

**If the Lord of the Year, or the Sun, or Venus is strong and free from malefic influence, there will be mutual happiness among siblings. If the opposite is true, one should predict the opposite result. || 1 ||**

In the **ninth house** <note>original: "anka-ge," where 'Anka' represents the number 9.</note> of the annual chart, he is the "path for the cause of wealth"—a producer of income. || 17 ||

In the **eleventh house** <note>original: "āya," the house of gains.</note>, there is an increase of wealth from the royal family. In the **last/twelfth house** <note>original: "antya".</note>, there will be expenditure. The wise astrologer should deliberate and speak in this manner; so have other teachers declared. || 18 || 

This concludes the Deliberation on the House of Wealth.

<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of the "Sahaja Bhava" (the Third House), specifically detailing the Tajika "Yogas" or planetary configurations that dictate the quality of one's relationship with siblings. It moves from the previous page's discussion of the Sun and Venus into specific combinations involving Mars, Saturn, Mercury, and the Moon.</meta>

<page-num>73</page-num>
<header>Tā. Nī. <note>Abbreviation for Tājika Nīlakaṇṭhī</note></header>
<sig>Section 2</sig>

If an *Ithasāla* aspect <term>Muthaśila: Also known as Ithasāla, this is a Tajika aspect where a faster planet approaches a slower one, indicating the coming to fruition of an event.</term> is formed, then there will be mutual happiness and harmony between the brothers. || 4 ||

**Now he explains the combinations for discord among siblings:**

Regarding "Malefics in *Isarāpha*": If a malefic planet forms an *Isarāpha* aspect <term>Isarāpha: A "separating" aspect in Tajika astrology where the faster planet has already passed the degree of the slower planet, often indicating discord, cancellation, or loss.</term> with the Lord of the Third House, then there will be mutual quarreling among brothers. Similarly, if Saturn <note>original: "Śani"</note> is placed in a sign ruled by Mars <note>original: "Bhauma-ṛkṣa," referring to Aries or Scorpio</note> while occupying the Third House, the brothers will suffer from various diseases. Furthermore, if Mars <note>original: "Asṛj/Bhauma"</note> is placed in a sign ruled by Mercury <note>original: "Jña-ṛkṣa," referring to Gemini or Virgo</note> while in the Third House, one should say without a doubt that the younger siblings will clearly suffer from sluggishness, chronic illness, and similar afflictions. || 5 ||

**Now he explains another combination for happiness among siblings:**

Regarding "Mars in the sign of Saturn": If Mars <note>original: "Asṛj"</note> is in a sign of Saturn <note>original: "Manda-ṛkṣa," referring to Capricorn or Aquarius</note> while in the house of siblings (the Third), and Mercury <note>original: "Budha"</note> is in a sign of Mars <note>original: "Kuja-ṛkṣa," referring to Aries or Scorpio</note>—and if they are in the Third House...

> If a malefic forms an *Isarāpha* aspect, there is strife; if Saturn is in a sign of Mars, there is disease. If Mars is in Mercury's sign in the Third, one should clearly predict sluggishness for the younger sibling. || 6 ||
> 
> If Mars is in Saturn's sign and Mercury is in Mars' sign, and the Lord of the Third is joined or aspected by benefics, there is great mutual happiness and comfort for the siblings. || 6 ||
> 
> If Mercury and Venus are the Lords of the Birth and the Year <term>Janmābda-pau: The planetary rulers of the birth chart and the specific solar return year.</term> and are strong in the Third House, they—along with Jupiter—bring happiness to the group of siblings and relatives. If the Moon is endowed with strength and Venus holds authority in both the birth and year charts, it leads to the increase and prosperity of the siblings and kin. || 7 ||

In both of these planetary combinations, if there is a connection <note>conjunction or aspect</note> with benefic planets, there will be great mutual happiness and affection between siblings born of the same womb. Some say this is only one combination, but that is not possible because it is impossible for Saturn and Mars to be in the same place while being in different signs. || 6 ||

**Now he explains two more combinations for sibling harmony:**

Regarding "Lords of the Birth and Year": If Mercury <note>original: "Budha"</note> and Venus <note>original: "Śukra"</note> are strong and placed in the Third House in both the birth and year charts, and if Jupiter <note>original: "Guru/Bṛhaspati"</note> is also strong and situated in the Third House, they will bring happiness to the siblings and the wider circle of kinsmen <note>such as paternal cousins</note>. This is one combination.

However, based on the "Sentence of Samara" <note>original: "Samara-vākya," referring to an authoritative text or astronomer named Samara</note>, it is stated that if Mercury or Venus hold authority <term>Adhikāra: A state where a planet has specific mathematical strength or governance over a chart.</term> in the birth or year ascendant, there will be growth and prosperity among the siblings...

<summary>This page outlines specific astrological configurations (Yogas) that predict either conflict and illness or harmony and prosperity among siblings. It highlights the influence of "malefic" planets like Saturn and Mars in causing discord, while Mercury, Venus, and Jupiter are shown to foster family bonds.</summary>

<keywords>Tajika astrology, Ithasāla aspect, Isarāpha aspect, Third House (Sahaja Bhava), siblings, Mars (Bhauma), Saturn (Śani), Mercury (Budha), Venus (Śukra), Jupiter (Guru)</keywords>

--- Page 150 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of the "Sahaja Bhava" (the Third House), which governs siblings and courage. It references classical authorities like Samarasiṃha and the Yoga-sudhā-nidhi to validate specific planetary placements. The text transitions from general sibling prosperity to specific combinations indicating sorrow, sibling loss, or disputes, concluding the section on the Third House.</meta>

Therefore, the reading "Mercury <note>original: "Budha"</note> and Venus <note>original: "Asita," literally "the white one"</note> being strong in the Third House in both the birth and year charts" is the appropriate interpretation. It is also stated by the authority Samarasiṃha: "If Mercury is in the Ascendant at birth, or if it is the ruler of the year and placed there, there will be an increase in the prosperity of siblings." 

In the text *Yoga-sudhā-nidhi* <note>The "Treasure-ocean of Yoga," a classical astrological treatise</note> it is said: "If Mercury is endowed with authority in both the birth and year charts and is located in the Third House, then there is an increase in relatives in that year." Some accept the reading "Lord of the Birth and Year" <note>original: "Janmābdapā"</note> and explain it as the rulers of the Birth Ascendant and Year Ascendant, but that interpretation lacks textual support here.

Now for another configuration: If Venus <note>original: "Bhṛgu-ja," the son of Bhrigu</note>, being the ruler of the birth and year times, is strong and situated in the same zodiac sign occupied by the Moon, it leads to the increase of one's siblings and relatives. || 7 ||

**Now he describes the configurations that cause sorrow regarding the Third House:**

### If the Third House is joined by a malefic planet and is devoid of the aspect <note>Aspect: In astrology, this refers to the "look" or mathematical relationship between planets.</note> of the Lord of the Sibling-Point or the Lord of the House, there is sorrow regarding siblings. One should say the same if the Sibling-Point itself is joined by malefics; if the lords of these are "combust," they should be considered as causing the destruction of siblings. || 8 ||

Regarding "Joined by malefics": If the Third House <note>original: "Sahaja-bhāva"</note> is occupied by a malefic planet <note>such as Mars or Saturn</note>, and it is not aspected by the Lord of the Sibling-Point <term>Sahottha-sahama: A specific sensitive point in a Tajika horoscope calculated to determine matters regarding siblings.</term> or the Lord of the Third House itself, the individual will experience sorrow concerning their siblings. 

"Similarly": In this same way, if the Sibling-Point is occupied by malefic planets and is not "seen" by its own ruler or the Lord of the Third House, one should predict sorrow for the siblings. Regarding "Those two lords": If the Lord of the Third House and the Lord of the Sibling-Point are "combust" <term>Dagdha: Literally "burnt"; a planet is combust when it is too close to the Sun, losing its power and becoming harmful.</term>, then they must be understood as bringing about the loss of siblings. || 8 ||

### If the Lord of the Year or the Lord of the Ascendant is in the seventh house from the Lord of the Third, there will be a dispute with siblings. If the Lord of the Third at birth is placed in the Third House and aspected by benefics, and the same occurs in the year chart, it leads to the satisfaction of siblings. || 9 || Thus ends the consideration of the Third House.

Now he speaks of the inauspicious and auspicious configurations <note>original: "shubha-ashubha yogas"</note> for siblings. Regarding "From the Lord of the Third": If, relative to the position of the Lord of the Third House, the Lord of the Year is located in the seventh house <note>original: "dyuna-stha"</note>; or if the Lord of the Year's Ascendant is in the seventh house from the Lord of the Third House, then there will be a dispute or quarrel with one's siblings. This is an inauspicious configuration.

Now for the auspicious configuration: If at the time of birth, the Lord of the Third House is situated within that same Third House and is aspected by benefic planets <note>such as Jupiter or Venus</note>; and if a similar condition exists in the annual chart—where the lord is in the Third House and aspected by benefics—then it results in the "satisfaction of siblings," meaning the happiness and well-being of one's brothers. || 9 ||

<summary>This page concludes the analysis of the Third House (siblings), detailing planetary aspects that cause either prosperity, sorrow, or disputes between brothers, specifically using Tajika astrological techniques like Sahamas and combustion.</summary>
<keywords>Third House, Sahaja Bhava, siblings, Tajika astrology, Sahama, Mercury, Venus, combustion, Samarasiṃha, annual horoscope</keywords>

Analysis of the House of Siblings <note>original: "Sahaja-bhava"</note>. **Now, the analysis of the Fourth House.** Regarding the "Turya" <note>The fourth house</note>: If in the fourth house the Sun <note>original: "Ravi"</note> is joined with malefic planets or aspected by malefic planets, then there is suffering for the father. Similarly, if it is the Moon <note>original: "Vidhu"</note> so situated, there will be suffering for the mother. If both planets are thus placed, the meaning is that both parents will suffer. This is one configuration <note>original: "Yoga"</note>. 

Now for another: If Saturn <note>original: "Arka-putra," literally 'son of the Sun'</note>, being the ruler of the year, enters the zodiac sign occupied by the Sun at the time of birth, then there will be humiliation from the world and a loss of honor for the father. Enmity and quarrels with the father may occur. || 1 ||

If Saturn <note>the son of the Sun</note> enters the sign of the birth-Sun, there is humiliation and enmity with the father. || 1 ||

"Now," etc. If the sensitive points <note>original: "Sahama"</note> of the two parents are "burnt" (afflicted by malefic planets), and if this occurs while there is a placement in the fourth house relative to the *Muthaha* <note>A moving mathematical point in a yearly solar return chart</note>, then there will be the destruction <note>death or great loss</note> of those two parents. Likewise, if the sensitive points of the mother and father are "burnt"—meaning they are overcome by defects such as being combust <note>too close to the Sun</note>—there will be destruction of the parents. || 3 ||

Now he speaks of the planetary combinations causing distress to the parents. In the birth chart <note>original: "janmani"</note>, if the fourth house <note>original: "ambugriha," literally 'water-house,' a synonym for the 4th house</note> and the ruler of that fourth house are occupied by Saturn and Mars <note>original: "shani-arau"</note>, then they bring distress to the parents, unless they are joined with or aspected by benefic planets. || 4 ||

Now he speaks of the combinations for favorable and unfavorable results. Regarding the mother and father: if the ruler of the year’s ascendant <note>original: "tanupa"</note> forms an *Itthasala* <note>A Tajika aspect where a faster planet approaches a slower one, indicating the manifestation of an event</note> or *Muthasila* configuration with the *Sahama* <note>A sensitive mathematical point or 'lot' in the chart</note> of the mother or father, then you should understand that the parents will experience happiness. In this same way, if the ruler of the fourth house forms an *Itthasala* with the ruler of the year’s ascendant, you should know that the father will be happy. This is one favorable combination.

Now for an unfavorable combination: if the ruler of the eighth house <note>The house of death and transformation</note> from the year's ascendant forms an *Itthasala* or *Muthasila* configuration with the *Sahama* of the mother or father, there will be misfortune for the parents.

### If in the birth chart, the fourth house and its ruler are occupied by Saturn and Mars, they bring distress to the parents, unless they are aspected by benefics. || 4 || If the ruler of the ascendant forms an *Itthasala* with the *Sahama* of the mother or father, or if the ruler of the fourth house does so, understand it to mean happiness for the parents.

### If an *Itthasala* is formed by the ruler of the eighth house, it brings a double misfortune to the parents; the same applies if an *Isarafa* configuration <note>A Tajika aspect indicating separation or the fading of an influence</note> is formed by an unfavorable planet. || 5 ||

# This concludes the Fourth House. 
# Now, the Analysis of the Fifth House.

### If the Ruler of the Year is Jupiter, or the Sun, Mars, Mercury, or Venus, and is placed in the house of children, then there is happiness from good children. If they are afflicted by malefics, they bring suffering; these things should be considered regarding one's offspring. || 1 ||

If an unfavorable planet forms an *Isarafa* configuration <note>original: "esarāpha"</note> with the *Sahama* of the mother or father, misfortune will strike the parents. || 5 || This concludes the Fourth House. Now, the Fifth House. Now he speaks of the favorable and unfavorable results regarding children. If the ruler of the year, Jupiter <note>original: "Guru"</note>, is placed in the house of children <note>the 5th or 11th houses</note>, then there will be happiness from a good son. Alternatively, if any of these—the Sun, Mars <note>original: "Ara"</note>, Mercury <note>original: "Saumya"</note>, or Venus <note>original: "Ushanas"</note>—becomes the ruler of the year and is placed in the 5th or 11th house, it similarly brings happiness from a good son. However, if those planets—Jupiter, Sun, Mars, Mercury, or Venus—are oppressed or afflicted by malefic planets, then they should be understood to bring suffering caused by one’s children. || 1 ||

# Now he speaks of the planetary combinations for obtaining a son. He says: "In the son..."

<margin>Tajika Nilakanthi</margin>
<margin>|| 75 ||</margin>
If the fifth house <note>the traditional house of children</note>, or the Sensitive Point of the Son <note>original: "putra-sahama," a mathematical point calculated to predict matters regarding offspring</note> is powerful and strong, then there is the "obtainment of a son" <note>original: "sutapti"</note>, meaning a child will be born. If the Ruler of the Year <note>original: "varshesha"</note> is located in that fifth house and is also aspected by benefics <note>original: "saumya-ishito," meaning viewed by favorable planets like Jupiter or Venus</note>, then there will be great happiness. This is one planetary combination <note>term: "yoga" — a specific alignment of planets that produces a particular result</note>.

### Now another:

If Mercury is joined with Mars <note>original: "sakuja budha"</note>, and if they are placed in a zodiac sign belonging to a benefic planet <note>such as Cancer, Gemini, Virgo, Sagittarius, Pisces, or Libra</note>, and if they are aspected by benefics while placed in the "houses of gain or children" <note>the 5th or 11th houses</note>, then this brings "happiness from children" <note>original: "suta-sukham"</note>. However, if Mercury joined with Mars is weak <note>original: "virala"</note> and is not in the aforementioned favorable positions, then instead of gaining a child, it causes "distress to the child" <note>original: "sutarti," implying illness or suffering for one's offspring</note>. || 2 ||

# Now he speaks of two other combinations for obtaining a son. He says: "Jupiter..."

Whichever zodiac sign Jupiter <note>original: "jiva"</note> occupied at the time of birth, if that same sign appears in the yearly chart <note>original: "abde"</note> in the fifth house and is strong, then it brings happiness regarding children. This is one combination.

### Now another:

If Mars or Mercury becomes the Ruler of the Year and is situated in the fifth house, then they grant the birth of a son. || 3 ||

> If the Sensitive Point of the Son is strong in the fifth house, a child is obtained;
> If the Ruler of the Year is there and aspected by benefics, there is great happiness.
> If Mercury joined with Mars is in the house of the son, aspected by benefics, 
> and placed in the 5th or 11th, there is joy; if weak, there is distress to the child. || 2 ||
> If Jupiter’s birth sign is in the 5th house of the year and is strong,
> it brings happiness. Mars or Mercury as Year Lord here grants a son. || 3 ||
> Whichever house Jupiter occupied at birth, if that is the Year’s Ascendant, 
> it leads to a son; this is also true for Mercury and Venus.
> Whichever sign Saturn and Mars occupied at birth, if that sign is the Year’s
> Ascendant or 5th house, it certainly causes a decrease or distress. || 4 ||

<margin>Section 2</margin>
<margin>|| 75 ||</margin>

# Now he speaks of combinations for the birth of a son and for the suffering of a son. He says: "Where Jupiter..."

Whichever place or sign Jupiter <note>original: "ijya"</note> occupied at the time of birth, if that sign becomes the Ascendant <note>original: "vilagna," the rising sign or 1st house</note> in the yearly chart, then it leads to the "obtainment of a son." In this same way, one should also consider the positions of Mercury and Venus <note>original: "budha-sitayoh"</note>. For example, whichever sign Mercury or Venus occupies at birth, if that sign becomes the Ascendant in the yearly chart, it results in the birth of a child. This is a favorable combination.

# Now for an unfavorable combination:

Whichever zodiac sign Saturn and Mars occupied at the time of birth, if that sign becomes the Ascendant <note>original: "tanu"</note> or the 5th house <note>original: "suta"</note> in the yearly chart, then it causes "a decrease" <note>original: "nyunam," implying loss or lack of success</note>... <meta>catchword: regarding the birth...</meta>

# Combinations for the Birth and Welfare of Children

If the zodiac sign occupied by Saturn in the birth chart, or the sign occupied by Mars in the birth chart, becomes the Yearly Ascendant <note>original: "varsha-lagna," the rising sign for the current year</note>... Or if the sign occupied by Saturn or Mars in the birth chart is located in the fifth house from the Yearly Ascendant... Or if both of these planets are situated together in that house, then it surely and certainly causes "affliction to the son" <note>original: "putrarti"</note> or "suffering to the child" <note>original: "putra-pida"</note>. || 4 ||

### Another Combination for Obtaining a Son

He says: "In the son..." If the Point of Good Fortune <note>original: "punya-sahama," a sensitive point calculated by the positions of the Sun, Moon, and Ascendant</note> is in the fifth house from the Yearly Ascendant and is either viewed by <note>gloss: aspected by</note> or joined with benefic planets <note>original: "shubha-graha," such as Jupiter or Venus</note>, then it leads to the "attainment of a son" <note>original: "putra-prapti"</note>. This is one combination.

Now another: If both the Ruler of the Yearly Ascendant and the Ruler of the Fifth House are strong and are located in the fifth house, then they become the "givers of a son." || 5 ||

### Favorable and Unfavorable Combinations

He says: "The Moon..." If the Moon, or Jupiter <note>original: "vrihaspati"</note>, or Venus <note>original: "shukra"</note> stays in the fifth house from the Yearly Ascendant while being in its sign of exaltation <note>term: "uccha" — the zodiac sign where a planet expresses its maximum positive energy</note>, then it gives a son and ensures the "attainment of a child." This is a favorable combination.

Now for the unfavorable:

-> If the Point of Good Fortune is in the fifth house, it leads to a son if joined with benefics. If the Ruler of the Ascendant and the Lord of the Son are both strong in the fifth, they grant a child. || 5 || The Moon, Jupiter, or Venus in exaltation in the fifth house gives a son. <-

-> If retrograde Mars is in the fifth house, it causes the destruction of the child born. || 6 || If the ruler of the fifth house at birth is Venus, and in the current year it is in the fifth house and forms an *Itthashala* with the Lord of the Ascendant, it grants a child. <-

-> A son is given; however, if Saturn’s position falls in the fifth house and a malefic planet holds authority and aspects it, there is affliction to the child. || 7 || <-

Regarding the unfavorable combination: "Retrograde..." If Mars <note>original: "bhauma"</note> is in retrograde motion <note>term: "vakri" — appearing to move backward in the sky, often considered a sign of unpredictable or unstable energy</note> and is placed in the fifth house from the Yearly Ascendant, then it causes the "destruction of the son who is born" <note>original: "utpanna-suta-nashana"</note>. || 6 ||

### Combinations for the Gain and Loss of Children

He says: "The Lord of the Son..." If at the time of birth, the Lord of the Fifth House was Venus <note>original: "bhargava"</note>, and during the current year it is situated in the fifth house and forms an *Itthashala* yoga <note>term: "itthashala" — a specific Tajika aspect where a faster planet approaches a slower one, indicating the manifestation of an event</note> with the Lord of the Yearly Ascendant, then it grants a son. This is a favorable combination for attainment.

Now for the unfavorable: If the "Point of Saturn" <note>original: "manda-pada," a calculated position associated with Saturn's influence</note> falls in the fifth house this year, and a malefic planet <note>original: "papa-graha," like Mars or Saturn</note> is among the "five authorities" <note>term: "pancha-vargiya" — the five sources of planetary strength used in Tajika astrology</note> and casts its gaze <note>gloss: aspects</note> upon that house, then there will be "affliction to the child" <note>original: "atmajarti"</note>, meaning the son will suffer or face illness. || 7 ||

<summary>This page details specific astrological conditions in the yearly horoscope regarding the birth and health of children, contrasting the positive influence of exalted benefics like Jupiter with the destructive potential of retrograde Mars and malefic aspects on the fifth house.</summary>

He states another rule: "The sign occupied..." In the birth chart <note>original: "sutau," meaning at the time of birth</note>, the zodiac sign in which a planet is located is called its "seat" <note>original: "pada-abhidha," literally 'named as its place'</note>. Regarding the results, he says: "If strong..." If that sign falls in a particular house during the current year and is strong <note>term: "bali" — possessing high planetary strength scores</note>, then it gives the same auspicious results promised by that house in the birth chart. Otherwise, it brings sorrow. If the sign located in that house in the yearly chart is weak, then the good or bad results arising from that house will manifest accordingly. || 8 || This concludes the consideration of the Fifth House.

Now, the consideration of the Sixth House: "When Saturn is the Year-Lord..." <note>term: "abdapa" — the planetary ruler of the entire year</note>. When Saturn is the Year-Lord and is in retrograde motion <note>original: "anruju-gatau," meaning moving backward from Earth's perspective</note>, is afflicted by malefic planets, and is located in the sixth house <note>original: "arige," the house of enemies and illness</note>, then there will be suffering from disease. This includes "triple-humor fever" <note>original: "sannipata," a severe condition involving the imbalance of Vata/wind, Pitta/bile, and Kapha/phlegm</note>. Just as a king's faults affect his subjects, these three humors arise; likewise, there is fear of sharp colic <note>original: "shula"</note>, abdominal tumors <note>original: "gulma"</note>, or stomach ailments.

## **The zodiac sign occupied by a planet at birth is called its "seat." If that sign is strong in the yearly chart, it brings the happiness promised at birth; otherwise, it brings sorrow. || 8 || This ends the Fifth House. Now,**
## **the consideration of the Sixth House: If Saturn is the Year-Lord and is in retrograde motion, or is afflicted, there is suffering from disease: triple-humor fever, colic,**
## **abdominal tumors, eye disease, or irregular fevers. If Jupiter is afflicted by malefics and lacks a "Kamboola" yoga, there are wind-related illnesses. || 1 ||**
## **There may be jaundice, blood disorders, and bile issues if the Sun is the Year-Lord in the sixth; if the Sun is in the twelfth house, there is sharp pain in the eyes. If Venus is in the sixth house of enemies, there is bile; if the Moon is there in a human sign <note>e.g., Gemini, Virgo, Libra, or Aquarius</note> aspected by malefics, there is phlegm. || 2 ||**
