# Collection of 81 Year Old Dina Nath Raina, Jammu. Retired Priest.
<meta>Digitized by eGangotri. CC-0. Courtesy Sanjay Raina, Jammu.</meta>

If Venus is positioned [weakly] and is aspected by or joined with the Lord of the Sixth House <note>The 6th house is traditionally the "House of Disease" in Vedic astrology.</note>, then a disease known as *Shleshma* <note>original: "shleshma," referring to an imbalance of mucus or phlegm</note> will occur. Likewise, if the Moon is situated in the 6th house, a disease related to *Kapha* <note>term: "kapha" — the Ayurvedic humor associated with water and earth elements, manifesting as phlegm</note> will occur. || 2 ||

Now he describes the results of Mercury: "In this way..." In this manner, if Mercury becomes the Year-Lord <note>term: "abdapa" — the planetary ruler of the specific year under review</note> and is located in the 6th house (the house of enemies) joined with malefic planets or is in retrograde motion <note>term: "vakrini" — appearing to move backward in the sky</note>, a disease born of *Vata* <note>term: "vata" — the Ayurvedic humor associated with air and movement; "wind" diseases often refer to joint pain or nervous disorders</note> will occur.

Regarding the "Birth-Lord": The Lord of the Birth Ascendant <note>term: "janilagnanatha" — the planet that ruled the rising sign at the moment of the person's birth</note>, if it is a malefic planet and is aspected by the Year-Lord via a "Kshuta" aspect <note>note: A technical term in Tajika astrology referring to specific aspects, often the 4th, 7th, 1st, or 10th house squares and oppositions</note>, then it will grant disease. If it is further joined with a malefic planet, it will grant death. This means that if the Lord of the Birth Ascendant is a malefic and is joined with another malefic during the year while being aspected by the Year-Lord, it signifies a fatal period. || 3 ||

Now he describes the harmful combinations caused by Saturn: "In the sign of Saturn..." In the birth chart...

->Thus, if Mercury is joined with malefics in the 6th house, a wind-born disease occurs. If the Birth Ascendant Lord<-
->is a malefic and is aspected by the Year-Lord, it brings disease; if joined with a malefic, it brings death. || 3 || If the<-
->sign occupied by Saturn at birth becomes the Ascendant, there is fear of diseases from cold and heat.<-
->If aspected by Saturn and joined with a malefic, one should predict death. || 4 || Similarly, if Mars is so placed,<-
->there are diseases of blood-bile and fire. Otherwise, many other diseases; if aspected by benefics, the illness is slight. || 5 ||<-
->A connection between the Ascendant Lord, the Year-Lord, and the 6th Lord brings disease according to the<-
->planet’s physical humor. If Venus falls in the Sun’s place or the 6th house, there is fear of diseases of desire. || 6 ||<-

When the zodiac sign occupied by Saturn at the time of birth <note>original: "arkibha"</note> becomes the Ascendant <note>term: "lagna" — the rising sign</note> of the yearly chart, there will be fear of diseases caused by "cold and heat" <note>This likely refers to fevers or ague</note>. If that sign is aspected by Saturn in the yearly chart, one should predict death. || 4 ||

Now he describes the harmful combinations caused by Mars: "Similarly..." In this same way, the zodiac sign in which Mars was positioned at the time of birth—if that sign becomes the Ascendant for the current year and is aspected by Mars, then diseases such as "blood-bile" <note>term: "rakta-pitta" — a condition in Ayurveda involving internal bleeding, rashes, or inflammation</note> or fire-related injuries occur. This result is clear. If that Ascendant sign is aspected by benefic planets <note>such as Jupiter or Venus</note>, then the illness will be slight. || 5 ||

Now he describes another harmful combination: "The Ascendant Lord..."

...Lord. <term>Lagnadhipa</term> [means] the master of the Year Ascendant. <term>Abdapati</term> [means] the master of the year. <term>Shasthapati</term> [means] the master of the Sixth House. If the sign where Venus was located at the time of birth becomes the Sixth House <note>The house of disease.</note> in the yearly chart, and the Sun is present in that house, and if Venus has fallen into the 6th, 8th, or 12th house <note>The three "bad" houses associated with loss and suffering.</note>, then a disease born of lust and pride, specifically a <term>Madan-sambandhi-roga</term>, will occur. Now another: If the malefic planets that were in the <term>Sutika-kendra</term> <note>original: "sutika-kendra," the four angular houses (1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th) at the moment of birth.</note> are also present in the Year Ascendant, they result in the acquisition of disease. Now he says another: "By day..." The meaning is: if either the Sun or Mars becomes an authority among the five office-bearers <note>The "Pancha-adhikaris" are the five planets that compete for the title of Year-Lord in the Tajika system.</note> and is in the Ascendant, pain from fever occurs. However, if such an Ascendant is aspected by benefic planets, then in the end, there is the attainment of happiness. || 8 ||

Now he describes the combinations for the destruction or origin of disease: "At night..." If the birth was at night...

<meta>This page continues the technical analysis of the Sixth House in a Tajika (Perso-Arabic influenced Sanskrit astrology) manuscript. It transitions from general rules about planetary aspects to specific "yogas" (combinations) that predict the healing or worsening of diseases, and the onset of specific physical ailments like colic, smallpox, and tumors.</meta>

<page-num>77</page-num>
<header>ता० नी०, प्र०</header> <note>Abbreviation for Tajika Neelakanthi, Prakashika (the commentary).</note>
<sig>२</sig>

Therefore, in that context, if the Moon is in its waxing phase <note>original: "shukla-pakshe upachiyamane," referring to the bright fortnight when the moon's power is increasing</note> and forms a <term>Muthashila</term> or <term>Itthashala</term> yoga <note>Technical Tajika aspects where a faster planet approaches a slower one, indicating the manifestation of an event.</note> with <term>Bhauma</term> <note>The planet Mars.</note> of the yearly chart, the <term>ruk</term> <note>disease</note> will be destroyed; it comes to an end. However, if the waxing Moon in the bright fortnight forms a <term>Muthashila</term> with <term>Shani</term> <note>The planet Saturn, often called "Manda" or the slow one.</note>, the disease will flourish and increase. 

The reverse is also true: if the birth occurred during the day and the Moon is in the dark fortnight <note>original: "krishna-paksha," the waning phase</note> and forms a <term>Muthashila</term> with Mars, the disease will increase. Again, if such a Moon forms a <term>Muthashila</term> with Saturn, the disease will be destroyed. This is the meaning. || 9 ||

Now he states two other combinations. "In the Sun..." etc. If the Sun is joined by <term>Ketu</term> <note>The south node of the Moon, associated with karmic or difficult-to-diagnose issues.</note> and Mercury, and if the Sun in this state forms a <term>Muthashila</term> with Mars, then for the entire year there will be <term>gada</term> <note>illness</note>. This is one combination. Now for the other: if a planet that was a ruler or was strong at the time of birth is joined by <term>Ketu</term> and Mercury in the yearly chart, then it provides disease for the entire year.

->**If the Sun is thus joined by Ketu and Mercury, there is disease for the whole year; so too if the strong birth-ruler is joined by Ketu and Mercury in the year [chart]. || 10 ||**<-

->**When the Muntha is in the fourth or seventh house and is aspected by the hostile gaze of Saturn, there is colic pain; if seen by malefic groups, it results from their transformation. || 11 ||**<-

->**When Mars reaches the sign occupied by Jupiter or Venus at birth and is hidden in the Sun's rays, boils, smallpox, etc., and sluggishness occur; with Mercury and the Moon, leprosy, fistula, and scrofula also occur. || 12 ||**<-

Now he describes the "Shoola" <note>Colic or sharp, piercing pain, literally "a spear."</note> yoga. "In the fourth..." etc. In the yearly chart, if the <term>Muntha</term> <note>A sensitive mathematical point in Tajika astrology that moves one sign per year.</note> is in the fourth house or the "Asta" <note>The seventh house, representing the setting point.</note> and is aspected by Saturn with a hostile gaze <note>original: "shatru-drishtya," specifically the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th house aspects in this system.</note>, then colic pain will occur. If such a <term>Muntha</term> is aspected by malefic planets, the result of that transformation is first the destruction of the pain, and then the return of the colic pain. This is the meaning. || 11 ||

Now he describes combinations for boils and other ailments based on all the planets. "In the birth position..." etc. If <term>Mahija</term> <note>Mars, literally "Son of the Earth."</note> reaches the sign that was occupied by <term>Jiva</term> <note>Jupiter.</note> or <term>Sita</term> <note>Venus.</note> at the time of birth—and what kind of Mars? One that has "gone to the Sun's rays" <note>original: "suryashage," meaning the planet is combust or too close to the Sun to be seen, which weakens its positive qualities.</note>—then boils, smallpox, sluggishness, and diseases arising from cold, heat, or neck glands occur. If Mercury and the Moon are also in that sign which was occupied by birth-Jupiter or Venus...

This page concludes that combination and introduces new formulas for physical disability, the location of ailments in the body, and the debate over the interpretation of "obtaining a woman" in the context of the house of illness.</meta>

<page-num>78</page-num>
<header>TA. NI.</header> <note>Abbreviation for 'Tajika Nilakanthi'</note>
<sig>PR. 2</sig> <note>Abbreviation for 'Prakasha', Section 2</note>

...[from that combination] diseases such as leprosy, fistula, and scrofula <note>original: "gandamaalaa," referring to a swelling of the glands or tumors in the neck</note> will occur. || 12 || 

Now he describes another unfavorable combination <term>yoga</term>. "Birth..." etc. If the lord of the <term>Janma Lagna</term> <note>The Ascendant or rising sign at the moment of birth</note> and the lord of the <term>Munthaha</term> <note>A specific progressed point used in Tajika astrology to indicate the focus of the year</note> are located in the sixth house from the yearly ascendant, and are joined by or aspected by <term>papagrahas</term> <note>malefic planets like Mars or Saturn</note>, or if they are weak, then they will cause great suffering through fever, pain, and physical disability <note>original: "anga-vaikalya," meaning a defect or deformity of the limbs</note>. || 13 ||

Now he states combinations for the onset of disease and "obtaining a woman." The <term>Munthaha</term>, the Ascendant, and their respective lords—if these four are placed between malefic planets, they will grant disease. If even one of these is placed between malefics, it will cause illness. This is an inauspicious combination. 

Now, a favorable combination: If a <term>saumya</term> <note>benefic planet, such as Jupiter or Venus</note> is the lord of the sixth house and is located in the sixth house itself, it is said that "obtaining a woman" <note>original: "striyaah praaptih"</note> occurs. This is how it is understood. Regarding this meaning, the sage Vamana says: "When a benefic is the lord of the sixth and stays in the sixth, there is the obtaining of a woman."

### If the two lords of the birth ascendant are in the sixth house, joined or aspected by malefics and are weak, they cause great suffering through fever, pain, and physical disability. || 13 || If the Munthaha, the Ascendant, and their lords are placed between malefics, they cause disease. If a benefic is the lord of the sixth and is in the sixth, it is said that a woman is obtained. || 14 || The site of that disease should be declared based on the nature of the sign or the division where the strongest disease-causing planet is located. || 15 || If Mars is the lord of the birth's sixth house and is located in the sixth house of the yearly chart, there is illness. If it forms a harsh <term>Itthashala</term> aspect, the disease is great; but if there is a benefic aspect or yoga, the body remains healthy. || 16 || This concludes the Sixth House.

Regarding this, some suggest that "obtaining a woman" is the result. However, that is not correct; because this is the chapter on disease, it is more appropriate that it refers to an "obtaining of disease" related to a woman. Simply saying "obtaining a woman" is contradicted by the context. On this matter, Samarasimha also says: "When a benefic is the lord of the sixth and reaches the sixth, there will be the onset of a disease <note>original: "gada," meaning sickness</note> in a woman." || 14 ||

Now he explains the location of the disease. "The disease-causer..." etc. The planet previously mentioned as the cause of disease will be in a certain sign <term>rashi</term> or division <term>amsha</term> <note>specifically the 'navamsha' or ninth-part division</note>. Whichever is stronger between the sign and the division, the location of that disease should be named according to the nature of that sign—whether caused by Wind, Bile, or Phlegm <note>original: "Vata, Pitta, Kapha," the three doshas or humors of Ayurvedic medicine</note>. || 15 ||

Now he states another point. "Birth..." etc. If Mars <note>original: "Bhauma"</note> is the lord of the sixth house at the time of birth, and in the yearly chart...

<summary>This page outlines astrological combinations (yogas) in the Tajika system that predict chronic illnesses like leprosy, as well as fever and physical disability. It also features a scholarly debate on whether certain placements indicate "obtaining a woman" or, more likely in this context, contracting a disease through or because of a woman.</summary>

<meta>This page transitions from the final considerations of the Sixth House (dealing with illness and enemies) to the detailed analysis of the Seventh House, which governs marriage, partnerships, and physical relationships. It maintains the technical Tajika astrological framework established in the previous pages.</meta>

If the lord is in the sixth house from the ascendant <term>Lagna</term>, it causes illness and disease. If there is a <term>Muthashila</term> <note>A specific astrological configuration or 'aspect' in Tajika astrology where a faster planet moves toward a slower one</note> with a malefic planet <note>original: "papagraha"</note>, there will be extensive and great disease. However, if the body of the planet is aspected by or joined with benefic planets, there will be only minor illness. || 16 ||

### Thus ends the Sixth House.

## Now the Seventh House

"A strong Venus..." etc. If a strong Venus <note>original: "Sita," a common name for Venus meaning 'the white/bright one'</note> becomes the ruler of the year <term>Abdadhipa</term> and is situated in the seventh house <note>known as "Smara," the house of desire and marriage</note>, happiness from the wife’s side should be considered. If this Venus is aspected by Jupiter <note>original: "Ijya"</note>, it creates extreme happiness. If it is aspected by Mars <note>original: "Kuja"</note>, provided Mars is one of the five planetary administrators <term>Pancadhikari</term>, then there will be mutual affection between the couple. || 1 ||

Now he explains the combinations for illicit affairs <note>original: "Jarata"</note> and marriage. "Aspected by Mercury..." etc. When that Venus, being the year-lord, is in the seventh house:

## Now the Seventh House
## If strong Venus is the lord of the year and resides in the seventh house, happiness from the wife’s side should be expected. If aspected by Jupiter, there is extreme happiness; if by Mars (as an administrator), there is mutual affection. || 1 ||
## **If aspected by Mercury, there may be an affair with a young woman; if by Saturn, with an older woman. If aspected by Jupiter, one gets a new wife and offspring quickly follow. || 2 ||**
## **If the lord of the birth ascendant is in the seventh house and is strong, there is happiness from the wife. If Venus is in the sign it occupied at birth, or if Venus is the year-lord, a wife is obtained. || 3 ||**

If aspected by Mercury <note>original: "Budha"</note>, there will be an affair with a woman of young age. The meaning is that he enjoys another's wife. If such a Venus is aspected by Saturn <note>original: "Manda"</note>, the affair will be with an older woman. If such a Venus is aspected by Jupiter <note>original: "Guru"</note>, the wife will be a newly married woman <note>original: "navabharya"</note>. From her, offspring will be produced quickly. 

In this context, the reading "aspected by Jupiter" <note>original: "Guru-drishtya"</note> in the second verse might be a scribal error <note>original: "pramadika," meaning careless or accidental</note>, since the aspect of Jupiter <note>original: "Ijyeksita"</note> was already mentioned in the previous verse. The truth is that the reading "conjoined with Jupiter" <note>original: "Guru-yukte"</note> is better, as it aligns with earlier texts. 

For it is said in the *Essence of Birth Horoscopy* <note>original: "Jatakasara"</note>: "Joined with the one worshipped by the gods [Jupiter], one obtains a new bride born of a good lineage." Vamana says: "Joined with Jupiter, there is a new wife and offspring follow quickly." In the *Ocean of Nectar of Tajika Yogas* <note>original: "Tajika Yoga Sudhanidhi"</note>, it is said: "Even if joined with Jupiter, there is a new beloved, though offspring may not come immediately there." || 2 ||

Now he explains the combinations for happiness from a wife and marriage. "Birth..." etc. If the lord of the birth ascendant...

<meta>This page continues the analysis of the Seventh House (marriage and relationships) from the Tajika Neelakanthi. It builds on the previous page's introduction of the house of "desire" (Smara) and the role of Venus in determining marital happiness or distress.</meta>

<margin>Tajika Neelakanthi</margin>
<margin>|| 79 ||</margin>
<margin>Chapter 2</margin>
<margin>|| 79 ||</margin>

When the seventh house <note>literally "the place of setting," referring to the western horizon where the sun sets</note> is endowed with strength in the year-chart and is highly potent, there is happiness from a wife and joy born from women. When the sign that Venus <note>original: "Shukra"</note> occupied at the time of birth becomes the seventh house in the year-chart, and Venus is the Lord of the Year <term>Abdadhipa</term>, then the "gain of a woman" <note>marriage or a new relationship</note> occurs. This should be carefully noted. Regarding the phrase "birth-Venus sign," if the birth-Venus sign is in the seventh house and Venus is the year-lord, but there is no agreement among authorities on happiness from a wife, then consider other views. As **Samarasimha** says: "When the lord of the birth ascendant is strong in the year-chart, there is marital happiness. When the lord of the year is in the place of Venus, the native marries the person represented by that planet." 

In the ***Tajikalankara***: "When the lord of the birth-ascendant is in the seventh house of the year-chart and is strong, there is great satisfaction from women; marriage occurs in the house joined by Venus." In the ***Tajikayogashudhanidhi***: "When the birth-ascendant lord is in the seventh house of the year-chart and is strong, happiness with women is proclaimed. Marriage occurs when the birth-ascendant lord is strong and enters the sign occupied by Venus at birth." **Vamana** states: "When the lord of the birth-ascendant is in the seventh house from the year-ascendant, and is rising and strong, marital happiness is produced. If Venus and the year-lord occupy that same sign in the seventh house, a man's marriage occurs then." Here, based on previous analysis, "Lord of the Ascendant" refers to the lord of the birth-ascendant. The reading "if both are year-lords" is incorrect, as it contradicts previous verses and lacks the support of authoritative consensus. Therefore, that root text should be considered without foundation. || 3 ||

Now he describes the combination for gaining a woman and for distress concerning a woman:

-> Predict the gain of a wife from the **Itthashala** <note>A specific astrological configuration where a faster planet approaches a slower one, indicating the fulfillment of an event</note> of the lords of the **Ascendant** and the **Seventh House**. If the lord of the **Sahama** <note>A sensitive Arabic part or point in the Tajika system representing specific life themes</note> or the house-lord is "destroyed," it brings suffering to the woman. || 4 || <-

-> When the **Moon** is "destroyed" and located in the sign of **Venus**, predict that sexual union will be minimal. A strong **Mars** in the sign of the natal **Venus** brings the joy of a festival of happiness with women. || 5 || <-

"Destroyed" refers to the lord of the *Sahama* (the point of marriage) or the lord of the seventh house being afflicted. The definition of a "destroyed" planet is given by **Vamana**: "A planet that is occupied by a malefic, joined with a malefic, aspected by a malefic, or has lost its brilliance <note>original: "virashmitam," referring to being combust or weak</note> is known by the wise as 'destroyed'." || 4 ||

Now he describes the combinations for minimal versus great happiness with women. "When the Moon is destroyed": When the Moon <note>original: "Indu"</note> is in the sign occupied by Venus and is "destroyed" (as per the previously mentioned definition), one should predict minimal sexual union.

<summary>This page outlines specific astrological conditions for marriage and marital happiness, citing various authorities like Samarasimha and Vamana to explain how the positions of Venus, Mars, and the Moon determine relationship outcomes.</summary>
<keywords>Tajika astrology, marriage, Seventh House, Venus (Shukra), Itthashala, Sahama, Samarasimha, Vamana, marital happiness, year-chart</keywords>

--- Page 162 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical discussion on the Seventh House, specifically focusing on the planetary combinations (Yogas) that predict marriage and happiness with women in the Tajika system of solar returns. It transitions from general rules to specific configurations involving Mars, Jupiter, and the Sun.</meta>

Furthermore, if Mars <note>original: "Bhauma"</note> occupies the same zodiac sign that Venus <note>original: "Shukra"</note> occupied at the time of birth, and if Mars is strong in the year-chart, then one should predict a celebration of happiness with a woman. || 5 ||

Now, the author explains four planetary combinations <term>Yoga</term> for happiness from a woman. Regarding the phrase "the lord of the birth-seventh house": If the lord of the seventh house <note>the house of partnerships</note> from the birth ascendant is joined with or aspected by the Lord of the Year <term>Abdadhipa</term> or Venus, then a physical union with a woman occurs, characterized by much playfulness and pleasure. This is the first combination.

Regarding the word "Angles" <term>Kendra</term>: If Jupiter <note>original: "Guru"</note> in the year-chart is located in an angle <note>the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses</note> or a trine <note>the 5th or 9th houses</note> while also occupying the same sign that Venus occupied at birth, happiness from a woman is promised. This is the second combination.

Regarding the phrase "Thus, the Term <term>Hadda</term> and the Marriage Part": In this same manner, if the lord of the *Hadda* <term>an Egyptian/Persian system of planetary 'terms' or bounds</term> of the year-ascendant is in the sign occupied by birth-Venus and is situated in an angle or trine, happiness from a woman occurs. This is the third combination.

Likewise, if in the year-chart either the *Hadda Sahama* <term>the Lot or Arabic Part pertaining to these 'terms'</term> or its lord is in the sign occupied by birth-Venus and is in an angle or trine, then happiness with a woman will also occur. This is the fourth combination.

### If the lord of the birth-seventh house is joined or aspected by the Lord of the Year or Venus, sexual union occurs, dominated by much pleasure and play.
### When Jupiter is in an angle or trine and in the sign of birth-Venus, happiness from a woman is stated; the same applies to the lord of the *Hadda* and the Part of Marriage. || 6 ||
### If the Sun is in the position of one of the [five] "Officials," there is constant anxiety caused by women. If the *Inthaha* or the sign of its lord is aspected by Jupiter, it brings about marriage. || 7 ||

Regarding the term "Angles" used here, the authority **Samarasimha** states that excluding the ascendant <note>the 1st house</note>, the 4th, 7th, and 10th houses are intended. His specific statement is: "When Jupiter is in the sign of Venus, or in the 9th, 7th, 5th, 4th, or 10th houses, there is full fruit regarding marriage; the same applies to the Lord of the *Hadda*." It should be understood that the author of this text included the ascendant among these because the results are similar and for the sake of logical brevity. || 6 ||

Now the author states the combinations for distress from women and for marriage. Regarding the word "Official" <term>Adhikari</term>: If the Sun <note>original: "Arka"</note> is in the position belonging to any of the "Five Officials" <term>Pancha-adhikari</term>, there will be unceasing and constant anxiety caused by women.

Similarly, regarding the place of the *Inthaha* <term>also known as the Muntha, the progressed sign for the year</term> or the house of the planet governing that place: if it is aspected by Jupiter <note>original: "Guru"</note>, then that planet/position will become the "marriage-maker." || 7 ||

Now, the author explains the combinations for distress concerning a wife <note>original: "striklesha-yoga"</note>. Regarding the term "Inthiha": if the year-point <term>Inthiha/Muntha</term> <note>a mathematical point that moves one sign per year</note> is located in the seventh house from the year-ascendant and is joined with the Sun and Mars—"Arka" meaning the Sun and "Ara" meaning Mars—then suffering will befall the wife and children. If this combination is aspected by malefic planets <note>such as Saturn or Rahu</note>, the suffering will be especially severe. This is the first combination.

Now for another: If the Lot of the Woman <term>Stri-Sahama</term> <note>an Arabic Part or sensitive point calculated from planetary longitudes</note> is afflicted by malefics and is situated in the seventh house from the year-ascendant, suffering for the wife and children occurs. If it is aspected by malefic planets, the distress is particularly intense. Some commentators say that the phrase "afflicted by malefics" applies to both of these combinations. They suggest that the profection point <term>Inthiha</term> in the seventh house must be joined with the Sun or Mars, but that is not necessarily the case; the presence of the Sun and Mars already implies affliction. Otherwise, defining two separate combinations would be redundant. || 8 ||

Now, the author explains the combination for marriage <note>original: "vivaha-yoga"</note>. Regarding the phrase "In the birth": If at the time of birth, the lord of the seventh house and Venus <note>original: "Shukra"</note> are strong in the year-chart and are situated in the seventh house, and if they form an application <term>Itthashala</term> <note>a Tajika aspect where a faster planet reaches a slower one</note> with the lord of the year-ascendant, then in that year...

->
If the profection point <note>original: "Inthiha"</note> is joined with the Sun or Mars in the seventh house, or if the Lot of the Woman is afflicted, distress comes to the wife and children, especially under malefic aspects. || 8 || If the lord of the seventh house at birth and Venus are strong in the year-chart, and if there is an application with the lord of the ascendant, the gain of a wife is certain. || 9 || If Mars is the Lord of the Year and is aspected by Venus, or if Venus is the Lord of the Year and is aspected by Mars, or if both aspect the Lot of the Wife <term>Dara-Sahama</term>, the gain of a wife is certain. || 10 || Or if at birth the Lot of the Wife is aspected by these two, a woman is obtained. The Lot of the Woman aspected by its own lord or by Venus creates a marriage. || 11 ||
<-

If there is a conjunction-aspect <term>Muthashila/Itthashala</term> with the lord of the house, then the gain of a wife and happiness occur. || 9 || Now, the author explains three combinations for obtaining a wife. Regarding "Mars": First, if Mars is the Lord of the Year <term>Abdadhipa</term> and is aspected by Venus, this is one combination for obtaining a wife. Second, if Venus is the Lord of the Year and is aspected by Mars, this is the second combination. Or, if the Lot of the Wife <note>original: "Dara-sahama"</note> for that year is aspected by both Venus and Mars, the gain of a wife will certainly occur. This is the third combination. || 10 ||

Now, he explains two more combinations for marriage. Regarding "In the birth": Alternatively, if the Lot of the Wife calculated for the birth is aspected by Venus and Mars, a woman/wife is obtained. This was also stated by Samarasimha: "When the Lot of the Woman is aspected in the birth-chart, it indicates the purity of the household." The meaning is this: just as one calculates the Lot of the Woman for the year-chart, one should also do so for the birth-chart or for the time of a query.

<meta>This page continues the technical analysis of the Seventh House (the house of marriage and partners) and transitions into the Eighth House (the house of longevity and death) within the Tajika Neelakanthi. The text continues to use the Persian-influenced Tajika system, specifically referencing the "Lots" (Sahamas) and the annual profection point (Muntha).</meta>

In the *Treatise on Terminology* <note>original: "Sanjnatantra," a foundational Tajika text</note>, in the chapter on Lots <term>Sahama</term> <note>mathematical points calculated for specific life events</note>, it is stated: "This is one combination." Now for another: If the Lord of the Lot of Women <term>Stri-Sahama-swami</term> does not aspect the Lot itself, but Venus <note>the planet of love and marriage</note> does aspect it, then it brings about marriage. || 11 ||

Now, the author explains two combinations for obtaining happiness from a wife. Regarding the phrase "In the birth":

If at the time of birth <note>sūtau</note> the ruler of the seventh house <note>the house of partnership</note> becomes the Lord of the Lot of Women in the yearly chart, then there will be happiness from women, or happiness through a wife. This is one combination. Now for another: If the ruler of the seventh house from the birth chart, the Lord of the Application <term>Itthisha-natha</term> <note>from the Persian 'Ittisāl', referring to the planet the Moon or another planet is moving toward</note>, the ruler of the profection point <term>Muntha</term>, and the Lord of the Year are all located in the tenth house <note>the "sky" or zenith</note> or the seventh house, then there will be happiness from a wife. This is also stated in the *Ocean of Nectar of Tajika Yogas*: "When the birth-seventh lord, the year lord, and the lord of the profection are in the setting [7th] or the sky [10th]..." For this reason, the alternative reading suggesting this only applies to the seventh house in the yearly chart is rejected. || 12 ||

Now, because the seventh house also signifies "the path" or travel, the author describes the combination for going abroad. Regarding the phrase "From the Muntha":

> If the Moon is in its own sign or its sign of exaltation and is placed in the seventh house relative to the profection point <term>Muntha</term>, the person will travel abroad. If this Moon is aspected by malefic planets <note>such as Saturn or Mars</note>, distress will occur during the journey. || 13 || Thus ends the consideration of the Seventh House <note>the House of the Wife</note>.

Now begins the consideration of the Eighth House <note>the House of Death and Longevity</note>. Regarding the phrase "When Mars...":

> If Mars is the Lord of the Year and is "struck" or afflicted by malefic planets and is devoid of strength, there will be a wound from iron or metal <note>original: "ayasa-ghāto"</note>. If Mars is in a fire sign <term>Agnibha</term> <note>Aries, Leo, or Sagittarius</note>, there will be fear of fire. If Mars is in a "two-footed" sign <term>Dvipadabha</term> <note>human signs like Gemini, Virgo, or the first half of Sagittarius</note>, death may occur at the hands of a cruel man or a thief. || 1 ||

If Mars is the Year Lord and is pained by planetary "warfare" <note>yuddha</note> and is weak or poorly placed, a wound from iron occurs. Similarly, if Mars is in a fire sign—which in the *Treatise on Terminology* are defined as Aries, Leo, and Sagittarius—there will be a fear of fire or danger related to fire. Furthermore, if Mars is in a "two-footed" sign—Gemini, Virgo, or the first half of Sagittarius—there will be death or a life-threatening situation caused by a person of cruel nature, such as a thief. || 1 ||

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of planetary positions in a yearly horoscope (Varshaphala). # The Effects of Mars in Specific Houses

Now, the author speaks of the combinations for such a Mars <note>referring to a Mars that is weak or afflicted, as described previously</note> when placed in specific houses, beginning with the phrase "In the sky" <note>original: "viyati," a poetic term for the 10th house or zenith</note>. 

If Mars is the Year Lord <term>Varsha-svami</term> and is weak or afflicted by cruel planets, and if he is located in the sky—that is, the tenth house—then one should predict fear or danger coming from the king's ministers <note>government officials</note>, from enemies, or from thieves. 

Now, if such a Mars is in the "fourth" <note>the fourth house</note>, one should predict fear or danger from the mother's paternal uncle, the maternal uncle, the father, or the preceptor <note>teacher/guru</note>. || 2 ||

# On the Combinations for Great Death

Now he describes the "Great Death Combinations" <term>Maha-mrityu-yoga</term>, starting with the phrase "The Lord of the Ascendant, the Muntha, and the Year..."

If these three—the Ruler of the Year-Ascendant, the Ruler of the Muntha <term>Muntha-pati</term> <note>the sign that progresses one house per year</note>, and the Ruler of the Year—all form an "Application" <term>Itthashala</term> <note>a Tajika aspect where a faster planet approaches a slower one</note> with the Ruler of the Eighth House <note>the house of death</note>, then they bring about the end of life. If this occurs during a period of misfortune <term>Arishta</term> indicated by the planetary cycles <term>Dasha</term>, then death is certain. The meaning is this: if the planetary period of a malefic planet is active at the time of birth—

<margin>
M.

2
</margin>

> Danger arises from the king's ministers, enemies, and thieves when in the sky. In the fourth, fear comes from the mother's paternal uncle, maternal uncle, father, or teacher. || 2 || If the Lord of the Ascendant, the Muntha, and the Year form an application with the Lord of the Eighth, these bring about death. If it occurs during a period of misfortune, death indeed occurs. In that context, if Mars is with the Sun during a daytime year-entry, there is fear of the king. || 3 || If there is a Separation <term>Musaripha</term> <note>a Tajika aspect where the faster planet has moved past the slower one</note> between the Sun and Venus at birth, and that planet becomes the Year-Ruler and is in a center <note>1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th house</note>, there is fear from the king or disease. If Mercury is the ruler of the sign occupied by Mars at birth, and becomes an official in the yearly chart, there is sickness. If Mercury is the ruler and there is a cruel period of Mars, or if the Moon is in a state of "Burnt Bondage" <term>Dagdha-bandha</term> <note>a specific state of weakness or affliction</note> in the 8th or 6th house, they say death occurs in a foreign land. || 4 ||

—if that period is active, then death will surely occur. He explains another point with the word "There": In that yearly chart, if the year begins during the day and Mars is joined with the Sun, there will be fear or danger from the king. || 3 ||

Now he explains four types of death combinations:
1. **The First Combination:** If at the time of birth, the Sun is in a "Separation" <term>Musaripha</term> with Venus, and in the yearly chart that planet becomes one of the five office-bearers <term>Pancha-adhikari</term> and is located in a center, then there will be fear from the king and from disease.
2. **The Second Combination:** If Mercury <note>the "son of the Moon"</note> is one of the five office-bearers in the yearly chart and was the ruler of the sign occupied by Mars at the time of birth, then sickness will occur.
3. **The Third Combination:** If Mercury is the ruler and there is a cruel period of Mars <note>an afflicted planetary cycle</note> occurring in the 4th, 10th, 1st, or 7th house... <note>The text cuts off here, likely continuing with the specific conditions for death abroad mentioned in the verse.</note>

...suffering or a disease of the limbs <note>original: "khu-roga," often referring to hoof disease in animals or foot ailments in humans</note> occurs due to disorders of the blood and similar imbalances.

### The Fourth Combination

Again, if Mercury <term>Budha</term> is the ruler of the year and is under a "cruel aspect" <note>an unfavorable astrological gaze</note> from Mars <term>Bhauma</term>, and is "situated in his rays"—meaning Mercury is within the range of Mars's influence and is "burnt" <term>dagdha</term> <note>a state of being overpowered or combust, usually by the Sun, but here applied to Mars</note>, or oppressed by a "planetary war" <note>a specific calculation where two planets are in close proximity</note> with Mars—then the wise say that imprisonment or death will occur in a foreign land.

This is also stated by Samarasimha <note>a famous 13th-century authority on Tajika astrology</note>: "When [Mercury] is burnt and situated beneath the rays [of Mars], there is imprisonment and death in a foreign land."

In the *Tajika-alamkara* <note>a standard textbook on this system of astrology</note>, it is said: "If Mercury falls in a cruel planetary period <term>Dasha</term> and is damaged by being burnt by those rays, one certainly obtains imprisonment, being killed in a foreign land, and death."

However, some scholars interpret "situated in the rays" as being near the Sun <term>Ina</term>, meaning Mercury is combust by entering the Sun's orb. They argue "burnt" implies proximity to a malefic Mars. Therefore, the sage Vamana says: "When Mercury is joined with Mars and the Sun, there is imprisonment and death in a foreign land."

But this author disagrees. Even if Mercury is in a different sign, it can be combust without being joined with the Sun in the same sign. In Samarasimha's statement, the Sun is not mentioned; therefore, it is incorrect to assume the Sun’s rays are intended. One might ask: then what is the status of Vamana's statement?

-> If the Moon <term>Indu</term> is the ruler and is in the sign occupied by Mars at birth, there is secret fear of the king and disease. If Saturn <term>Manda</term> is the ruler and is in the Tenth House <note>original: "Khe," literally "the sky"</note>, it is remembered as causing pain from being struck by iron. || 5 || <-
-> If Mars is in the Eighth House, there is fear of fire or being struck by the king. If situated on the line <note>likely referring to the horizon or a specific degree of the house</note>, there is a fall from a four-legged animal, sorrow, and disease. || 6 || <-

Since the phrase "Mercury joined with Mars" does not contradict the author's own interpretation, the addition of "and the Sun" is considered an extra detail that does not create a conflict in the core meaning. || 4 ||

### Combinations for Disease

Regarding the verse "In the place of Mars...": If the Moon is the ruler of the year and, at the time of the yearly return, is located in the same zodiac sign that Mars occupied at the moment of birth, there will be "secret" (unseen) fear from the king <note>government or authorities</note> and diseases will arise. This is one combination.

Now for another: If Saturn <term>Shanaishchara</term> becomes the ruler among the five yearly authorities and is placed in the Tenth House, he is remembered as the bringer of pain caused by being struck by iron <note>this could signify weapons, tools, or machinery</note>. || 5 ||

### Combinations for Short Life and Death

Regarding the verse "Mars...": If Mars is in the Eighth House <note>the house of death and longevity</note> relative to the Year Ascendant <term>Varsha-lagna</term>, there is fear from fire, or a "strike" (an injury from a weapon or blow), or fear from the king. This is one...

</margin>

### Now, he describes the general combinations for the year, beginning with: "In which sign..."

In the birth <note>original: "janmani"</note>, meaning at the time of birth, in whichever sign Mars <term>Kuja</term> is located, if that same sign appears as the rising sign of the year <term>Abdalagna</term>, and if Mercury <term>Budha</term> is the Year Lord <term>Varshapatih</term> but is "deprived of strength" <note>original: "nashtabalo," meaning weak due to its position or relationship to other planets</note>, then it will not be auspicious or give good results. ॥ 15 ॥ 

Alternatively, he describes two combinations for falling from a vehicle, beginning with: "With the Sun..." If Saturn <term>Shani</term> is with the Sun <note>original: "sa-arka," meaning conjunct the Sun</note>, and Saturn is also joined with Mars <term>Bhauma</term>, and is located in either the tenth or the eighth house <note>the houses of career and death respectively</note>, then a "duality of vehicles" <note>likely meaning a collision or a change in transport</note> occurs there. This is one combination.

Now another: If Mars is with the Sun and is situated in the eighth house, one should declare a fall from a vehicle. ॥ 16 ॥ 

Now he describes the combinations for "Great Death" <term>Mahamrityu</term>, beginning with: "With Mars as the Year Lord..." If the Year Lord <term>Abdepa</term> is with Mars <note>original: "sāre," from 'Ara' which is a name for Mars</note>, and this Year Lord is situated in the eighth house, death shall occur. Likewise, if the Moon is in the "end, enemy, or misfortune" positions <note>original: "antyāriṣṭatā," referring to the 12th, 6th, and 8th houses</note>, death occurs. This is one combination. Now another:

-> If the sign where Mars was at birth becomes the rising sign of the year, <-
-> and Mercury is the Year Lord but weak, it is not auspicious. ॥ 15 ॥ <-
-> If Saturn is with the Sun and Mars in the tenth or eighth, trouble with <-
-> vehicles occurs. If Mars is with the Sun in the eighth, a fall from a vehicle happens. ॥ 16 ॥ <-

-> If the Lord of the Death-Point is rising but is weak, death occurs while living. ॥ 17 ॥ <-
-> If the Lord of the Virtue-Point is in the eighth from the Virtue-Point, or if <-
-> the birth 8th-lord is at the Virtue-Point and joined by malefic aspects, it gives death. ॥ 18 ॥ <-
-> If the sign that was the eighth at birth is occupied by the Lord of the Virtue-Point, <-
-> or is the eighth sign of the yearly chart, then death will occur. ॥ 19 ॥ <-

When the Lord of the Death-Point <term>Mrityu-sannesha</term> <note>the planetary ruler of the 'Sahama' or sensitive point related to death</note> has "risen" <note>reached the ascendant</note> but is "without strength" <note>nirbale</note>, death occurs during one's lifetime <note>original: "jīvite mṛtiḥ," perhaps implying a vegetative state or extreme suffering</note>. ॥ 17 ॥ 

Now he describes two other "Great Death" combinations: If the Lord of the Virtue-Point <term>Punya-sanneshvara</term> <note>the ruler of the 'Punya Sahama,' a point of fortune</note> is located in the eighth house from the position of the Virtue-Point itself, and is "joined or aspected by malefics" <note>influenced by planets like Mars or Saturn</note>, then death occurs. This is one combination.

If the sign where the Lot of Fortune <term>Punya Saham</term> <note>A sensitive mathematical point in the chart representing prosperity and merit</note> is located in the year's chart happens to be the eighth sign from the birth ascendant <term>Janma Lagna</term>, and if that sign's lord is joined with the lord of the birth ascendant, death may occur. ॥ This is one combination. ॥ Now another: ॥

Similarly, if the eighth sign from the year's ascendant <term>Abda Lagna</term> is joined with the lord of the Lot of Fortune, death may occur. ॥ 19 ॥ Now he describes other combinations for death, beginning with: "If the Lot of Fortune is afflicted..." If the Lot of Fortune <term>Punya Sannam</term> is afflicted, meaning it is joined with malefic planets, and if the Lord of the Eighth House <term>Mritisha</term> <note>The eighth house is traditionally the house of death and longevity</note> goes to the "houses of loss"—the twelfth, sixth, or eighth houses—then one should predict death. ॥ This is one combination. ॥ Now another: the Lord of the Muntha.

If the Lord of the Muntha <term>Munthesha</term> <note>A sensitive point that moves one sign per year in Tajika astrology</note> or the Year Lord <term>Abdapa</term> is likewise afflicted by malefic planets and is placed in the twelfth, sixth, or eighth house, then one should indicate death. ॥ 20 ॥ Now he describes the combinations for "Great Death," beginning with: "If with a malefic..." If the Birth Lord...

->**If the Lot of Fortune is afflicted by malefics, and the Lord of Death is in the twelfth, sixth, or eighth house; or if the Lord of the Muntha or the Year Lord is so placed, death should be predicted there. ॥ 20 ॥**<-

->**If the Birth Lord is with a malefic, and if there is a linkage—**<-

->**—between the Lord of Death and Saturn <term>Akir</term>, and there is an aspect from Mars <term>Bhauma</term>, then death shall occur by one's own hand <note>original: "atmaghatata," referring to suicide or self-inflicted harm</note>. ॥ 21 ॥ Saturn in the eighth in a planetary linkage <term>Itthasala</term> with the Lord of Death is remembered as a bringer of death. But if there is a linkage—**<-

->**—with benefics, then none of these combinations produce inauspicious results. ॥ 22 ॥**<-

If the Year Lord is with a malefic planet, and death <note>meaning the Lord of the Eighth House</note> is situated in the eighth house, death occurs. If the Muntha <term>Itthika</term> <note>likely a scribal variation of Muntha or a specific Tajik term</note> is joined with Saturn <term>Akir</term> and is aspected by Mars—meaning Mars views it with a "hostile" fourth, tenth, first, or seventh aspect—then in such a combination, death occurs by one's own hand. ॥ 21 ॥ Now he describes the Great Death combination and the remedy for all death combinations, beginning with: "Saturn..." If Saturn <term>Manda</term> is in the eighth house from the year's ascendant, and is in a planetary linkage <term>Itthasala</term> with the Lord of the Eighth House, it is said to be a bringer of death. Now he gives the exception to these combinations: All these death-bearing combinations, if they involve a linkage with benefic planets <note>such as Jupiter or Venus</note> instead of malefic ones, do not produce inauspicious or undesirable results. ॥ 22 ॥

Now he describes the **Great Death Yoga**, beginning with the words: **"The Lord of the Eighth House in birth..."** If, in the birth chart <term>Suti</term> <note>the original natal chart</note>, the Lord of the Eighth House <term>Randhrapati</term> <note>the house of death</note> is Saturn <term>Manda</term>, and in the solar return chart <term>Abde</term> <note>the chart for the specific year</note> he is situated in the eighth house from the year's ascendant, and if he forms a <term>Muthashila</term> connection <term>Itthashali</term> <note>a specific Tajika aspect where a faster planet reaches a slower one</note> with the Year Lord <term>Lagnapa</term> through a "cruel" or malefic aspect <note>referring to square or opposition aspects: the 4th, 10th, 1st, or 7th positions</note>, then at that time, in that specific year, he becomes the giver of death. ॥ 23 ॥ 24 ॥

## Thus ends the consideration of the Eighth House. ॥ Now begins the consideration of the Ninth House. ॥

Regarding the Ninth House, the consideration of "Path and Dharma" <note>travel and religious duty</note> begins with the description of the **Yoga for Travel**, starting with the words: **"Mars..."** When Mars is the Year Lord <term>Abdapa</term> and is placed in the third or ninth house, and is joined with malefic planets but remains strong <term>Balanvite</term> and lacks a "Yoga" <note>specifically a connection with a planet that would negate its influence</note>, then at that time, the "Path" or journey will be beneficial <term>Gunavaha</term>, bringing profit and happiness. ॥ If the sign is a movable one <term>Chara</term>, the task is accomplished quickly; if it is a fixed sign <term>Sthira</term>, the result is achieved with delay. ॥ 1 ॥

> If Saturn, the Lord of the Eighth at birth, is in the eighth during the year and connects with the Year Lord ॥
> via a malefic aspect, he becomes a giver of death at that time. ॥ 23 ॥
> If the Moon is in the house of merit <note>the 9th house</note> and a malefic is in the ascendant or the descendant; or if malefics are in the sixth or twelfth houses, ॥
> they give death. Malefic planets in the age-points or at the end of the year-lord's influence bring death. ॥ 24 ॥

## Thus the Eighth House. ॥ Now the consideration of the House of Fortune. ॥

> If the Year Lord is in the third or ninth house, joined by malefics but endowed with strength, ॥
> the journey then brings benefit; if in a movable sign, the work is swift; if in a fixed sign, then slow. ॥ 1 ॥
> If the Sun is the Year Lord, placed in the third or ninth, and possesses the <term>Kambuli</term> Yoga <note>a configuration where the Moon transfers light between two planets</note>, he grants happiness on the path. ॥
> If he is not the "Authorized Lord" <term>Adhikrita</term> <note>one of the five specific lords of the year in Tajika</note>, then the journey may be on behalf of someone else's mission. ॥ 2 ॥
> If Venus is the Year Lord in the third or ninth, there is happiness on the path; if he is retrograde <term>Vilomage</term> ॥
> or combust <term>Aste</term> <note>hidden by the Sun's rays</note>, it is a "bad path" <term>Kugati</term>. If Mercury <term>Saumya</term> is thus placed, a pilgrimage for the gods <term>Devayatra</term> occurs. ॥ 3 ॥

Now he says another thing: **"Placed in the third or ninth..."** If the Sun is the Year Lord and possesses the **Kambuli Yoga**, being placed in the third or ninth house, he then grants happiness on the journey. However, if he is not the "Authorized Lord" <note>one of the five primary office-bearers of the year</note>, then the journey will involve being sent by another <note>missions or errands for others</note>. ॥ 2 ॥ Now he describes other travel yogas in a verse and a half: **"Venus..."** and **"From Malefics..."** If Venus is the Year Lord and is in the third or ninth house, there will be happiness on the path of travel. However, if such a Venus is retrograde or combust, it means a "bad path" <term>Kugati</term>—a miserable journey where the purpose of the work is not achieved.

<meta>This page continues the technical analysis of the Ninth House (Travel and Fortune) in annual horoscopy (Varshaphal). Having established the general conditions for travel in the previous page, the text now specifies how different planets—Mercury, Venus, and Jupiter—act as the "Year Lord" to determine the spiritual or material quality of a journey. It then introduces specific astrological configurations (Yogas) that predict unexpected travel or the acquisition of wealth during a trip.</meta>

Similarly, when **Mercury** is the Year Lord <term>Varshasvamin</term> <note>the planet ruling the entire year in the Tajika system</note> and is strong and placed in the third or ninth house, being a benefic planet, there will be a **journey for divine purposes** <term>Deva-yatra</term> <note>originally: "Deva-uddheshena-yatra," a journey undertaken for a deity or religious reason</note>. This also serves as a general indicator for pilgrimages <term>Tirtha-yatra</term> and similar travels. If **Venus** or **Mercury**, in such a position, is afflicted <term>Adite</term> or joined by "cruel" malefic planets, there will be a **bad journey** <term>Kuyanam</term> <note>a "base" or unpleasant travel experience</note>. In the same manner, when **Jupiter** is the Year Lord and is in the third or ninth house, one should predict a journey for divine purposes; but if that Jupiter is afflicted by malefics, it will result in a bad journey. ॥ 3 ॥

Now, in the second half of the verse, he describes the configuration for an unexpected journey. Regarding the **Itthashala** connection: if there is an <term>Itthashala</term> <note>a specific aspect where a faster planet approaches a slower one, indicating the manifestation of an event</note> between the Lord of the Ascendant <term>Lagnapa</term> and the Lord of the Ninth House <term>Dharmapa</term>, there will be an **unplanned or sudden journey**. ॥ 4 ॥ Now he explains the reversal of the *Muthashila* connection and its extension to other factors: if the Lord of the Ascendant of the year-chart forms an *Itthashala* connection with the Lord of the Ninth House, it indicates a **planned journey**. In this same...

### If afflicted by malefics, it is a bad journey; this is how one should think regarding malefics. If there is a connection between the Lords of the Ascendant and the Ninth House, a planned journey occurs. ॥ 4 ॥ If the Lord of the Ascendant gives its own light to the Lord of the Ninth House, it indicates a planned path. This also applies to the connection between the Ascendant and the Year Lord, and between the Lord of the *Muntha* and the Year Ascendant. ॥ 5 ॥ If Mars is in the Ninth House in the position where Jupiter was at birth, that journey brings servants and wealth. If Mars is in the position where Mercury was at birth and is seen by the Lord of the Ascendant, it gives happiness during travel. ॥ 6 ॥

...manner, when there is a connection between the Year's Ascendant and the Year Lord, or a connection between the Lord of the <term>Muntha</term> <note>a mathematically calculated point that moves one sign per year</note> and the Lord of the Ascendant, the previously mentioned **Muthashila** yoga occurs. ॥ 5 ॥ Now he describes the configurations for a successful journey. Regarding the phrase **"In Jupiter's place"**: if **Mars** <term>Kuja/Bhauma</term>, in the yearly chart, is placed in the Ninth House <term>Dharma</term> and specifically in the zodiac sign where Jupiter was located at the time of birth, it indicates a **good journey** <term>Sadyatra</term>. This brings "servants" <term>Mritya</term> <note>attendants or helpers</note> and "wealth" <term>Vitta</term>, meaning the acquisition of money through one's attendants. This is one configuration. Now for another: **"In Mercury's place"** means in the sign where Mercury was at the time of birth. If, in the yearly chart, Mars is in that sign and is aspected <note>viewed through an astrological aspect</note> by the Lord of the Ascendant, it will be a **giver of happiness during travel**. ॥ 6 ॥

### Combinations for Travel

Now he describes another configuration for a good journey <term>Sad-yatra-yoga</term> in a half-verse: "Standing in its own place" etc. If at the time of birth, **Mars** <note>originally: Bhauma</note> is in its own sign—Aries <note>Mesha</note> or Scorpio <note>Vrishchika</note>—and in the annual chart it is also in Aries or Scorpio, while being strong in the ninth house and aspecting the ascendant <note>Lagna-darshi</note>, then it grants an auspicious journey <note>Suyana-dah</note>.

Now he describes configurations for auspicious and inauspicious journeys in one and a half verses: "The authority of the birth" etc. If **Mercury** <note>originally: Jna/Budha</note>, having been the ruling authority <term>Adhikari</term> at the time of birth, is situated in the zodiac sign occupied by Saturn at the time of birth, and in the annual chart it is positioned in the ninth house joined with "cruel" malefic planets, then there will be travel along a path necessitated by the trouble or conflict <note>jhankatkat</note> involving an enemy.

Regarding the "path of Jupiter": If the **Moon** <note>Indu</note> and **Jupiter** <note>Jiva/Brihaspati</note> act as the birth authorities in this same manner—situated in the sign occupied by Saturn at birth and in the annual chart placed in the ninth house joined by malefic planets—then there will be an arduous path or journey resulting from a quarrel with an enemy.

Regarding the house of fortune <term>Dharma</term>: If **Saturn** is in the ninth house <note>Dharmasthana</note> but lacks authority <note>meaning he does not hold any of the five specific dignities known as Panchadhikari</note>, he will declare an inauspicious path or a "bad journey." ॥ 7 ॥ 8 ॥

->If Mars is in its own sign or is strong and aspects the ascendant, it grants a good journey. When Mercury, as the birth authority, is in the place of Saturn and joined by malefics, ॥ 7 ॥ then travel follows from the trouble of enemies. The same applies to the Moon and Jupiter. If Saturn is in the Ninth house without authority, he speaks of an inauspicious path. ॥ 8 ॥ Thus, when Jupiter is in the Ninth without authority, there is distant travel and association with a king, and from that comes benefit. If Mars is the Year Lord but lacks strength, there is travel far from one's own people. ॥ 9 ॥<-

Now he explains configurations for long-distance travel: "Thus" <note>ittham</note> etc. In this manner, if **Jupiter** is in the ninth house without holding authority in the annual chart, there will be distant travel and a meeting with a king <note>Nripa-sanga</note>, followed by "quality" <note>guna</note>, which refers to the acquisition of wealth <note>Dravya-prapti</note> from that king. This is one configuration.

Now for another: If **Mars** <note>Kuja/Bhauma</note> is the Year Lord <term>Abdapa</term> <note>the planet ruling the specific year in Tajika astrology</note> but is devoid of strength <note>nashta-bale</note> while in the ninth house, there will be movement or travel to a distance away from one's own family members or relatives. ॥ 9 ॥

Now, he explains the configuration for a powerful foreign journey in the first half of the verse: "In the seventh..." etc. If the **Moon** <note>originally: Indu</note> is strong and positioned in the seventh house <note>Yunam</note> or the ninth house <note>Dharma</note>, it serves as a cause for travel to a foreign land; it creates a path for going abroad. 

Now, through the second half of this verse and the first half of the next, he explains the specific results when the "Lord of the Year" <term>Varsha-isha</term> <note>the planet ruling the current year in a Tajika chart</note> is in an angular house <term>Kendra</term>. If the Lord of the Year is strong, joined with a "cruel" malefic planet <note>papayuta</note>, and holds authority—meaning it is one of the five specific planetary controllers <term>Panchadhikari</term>—and is placed in an angular house, then one attains "authority" or "rank." In times of conflict, one might announce a military command or leadership over an army.

He states another result: "In this way..." etc. If **Mercury** <note>Budha</note> and **Mars** <note>Kuja/Bhauma</note> are endowed with strength, are visible <note>originally: akarnirgata; meaning they have emerged from the sun's rays and are not combust</note>, and are joined with **Jupiter** <note>Jiva</note> while occupying an angular house, there will again be a march against an enemy's army. This will bring victory, fame, and happiness.

### If the Moon is strong in the seventh or ninth house, one travels to a foreign land. If the Lord of the Year is strong, joined with a malefic, and holds authority in an angular house: || 10 ||

### One may predict the attainment of rank, or even a military command in battle. Likewise, if Mercury and Mars are joined with Jupiter and are visible: || 11 ||

### There is a march against an enemy's army, bringing victory, fame, and happiness. If Mars is in the ninth house from Jupiter, men will have an auspicious journey. || 12 ||

### Thus ends the consideration of the Ninth House.

### Now begins the consideration of the Tenth House: If the Lord of the Year is strong and situated in the tenth house, there will be royal status, wealth, happiness, and fame. If it is in another angular house, one attains a different location and domestic happiness. || 1 ||

Now he explains the specific details of the second half of the verse: "From Jupiter..." etc. If **Mars** is in the ninth house from the sign occupied by **Jupiter** <note>the "Heavy Planet"</note>, the journeys of men will be of an auspicious nature. || 11 || 12 || 

**Thus ends the consideration of the Ninth House.**

**Now begins the consideration of the Tenth House.** "With strength..." etc. If the Lord of the Year is strong and situated in the tenth house <note>Khastha; literally 'standing in the sky'</note>, there will be royal status, wealth, happiness, and fame. This is one specific configuration <term>yoga</term>. 

Now for another: If the planet is in a different angular house—meaning a center other than the tenth <note>the 1st, 4th, or 7th houses</note>—it results in the attainment of a different place <note>a new posting or residence</note> and the acquisition of domestic happiness. || 1 ||

Now, he describes the specific results of the Sun <note>Surya</note>, when it acts as the Lord of the Year <term>Varshasvami</term>, according to its specific house position. Now, he describes another combination for the loss of wealth. "In Saturn's place..." etc. If, at the time of the yearly chart, Mars <note>original: Kuja/Bhauma</note> is in the tenth house and occupies the same zodiac sign that Saturn <note>original: Shani</note> occupied at the time of birth, and if it aspects the Munthaha <term>Munthaha: a sensitive mathematical point in a yearly horoscope that moves one sign per year</term>, then due to sinful deeds, it will cause fear of the king and the loss of wealth. || 6 ||

Now, he describes the combinations for the increase of sin or merit. "In such..." etc. If Mars is in such a sign (the one occupied by Saturn at birth) and is located in the third or ninth house during the year, and if this Mars is "burnt" <note>original: dagdha; meaning combust or too close to the Sun</note>, destroyed, or lacks strength, there will be an accumulation of sins. This is one combination. Now another: if Saturn <note>original: Manda</note> is the Lord of the Year <term>Abdpah: the planetary ruler of the specific year</term>, holds an official position among the five controllers <note>the Pancha-adhikari</note>, and is placed in the third or ninth house <note>the houses of travel and religion</note>, then it grants an increase in religious merit <note>Dharma</note>. || 7 ||

Now he describes two ill-omened combinations and one auspicious combination. "In that..." etc. If that same Year Lord Saturn is "burnt" or destroyed, the person will perform sinful acts and revile religion. This is one combination. Now another: if the Sun <note>original: Surya</note> is the Year Lord and is in the third or ninth house and is "burnt" or destroyed, the person will similarly be a sinner and a reviler of religion. Now the auspicious combination: "With Jupiter..." etc. In this same manner, if Jupiter <note>original: Guru</note> is the Year Lord in the third or ninth house, even if "burnt" or destroyed, the person attains wealth through righteous means <note>original: naya-marga; the path of ethics or policy</note>. || 8 ||

Now, in the first half of the verse, he describes the auspicious or inauspicious results related to the Munthaha. "Situated there..." etc. The Munthaha, when situated in the third or ninth house, brings the arrival of religious merit. Now he describes the combination for attaining a kingdom <note>high status or office</note>. "In birth..." etc. If the Sun <note>original: Ravi</note> was the lord of the tenth house <note>the house of career</note> at the time of birth, and during the current year it is situated in the tenth house, and if it forms a Muthashila <term>Muthashila: a specific aspect in Tajika astrology between two planets, indicating the fulfillment of a matter</term> with the Lord of the Ascendant of the year, then...

### ->Saturn’s place aspected by Mars from the tenth house, while aspecting the Munthaha, causes fear of the king and loss of wealth due to sinful actions. || 6 || If Mars is in the third or ninth house, burnt or destroyed, sins accumulate. If Saturn is the Year Lord and in the third or ninth, merit increases. || 7 || If Saturn is burnt or destroyed, one becomes a sinner and a hater of religion. If the Sun is in such a state, the same applies. If Jupiter is in this position, one gains wealth through policy. || 8 || The Munthaha in those houses brings merit. If the Sun was the tenth lord at birth and is in the tenth during the year, forming a Muthashila... || 9 ||<-

It highlights that Jupiter remains a positive influence on wealth even when weakened, and introduces the Muthashila yoga for professional success.</summary>

<meta>This page transitions from the analysis of the Tenth House (career and status) to the Eleventh House (gains and income). It continues the commentary on the Tajika Neelakanthi, focusing on how the "burning" (combustion) of specific house lords affects one's life, and how Mercury and Jupiter influence wealth through commerce or scholarship.</meta>

->### Analysis of the Tenth House (Continued) ###<-

By estimating the strength—specifically by inferring the universal strength of the Sun—the attainment of a kingdom <note>original: rajyapti; also refers to high political or professional status</note> is described. Now, the author explains the combinations for the destruction of religious merit and the kingdom in the latter half of the verse.

**Religious merit and career:** If the lords of the house of religious duty <note>the 9th house</note> and the house of action <note>the 10th house</note> are both "burnt" <term>Dagdha: a planet is "burnt" or combust when it is too close to the Sun, losing its power</term>, then they cause the destruction of both religious merit and the kingdom. If the lord of the ninth house is "burnt" and lacks strength, there is a loss of religious merit <note>Dharma</note>. If the lord of the tenth house is "burnt" and lacks strength, there is a loss of the kingdom or career. || 9 || 10 ||

**This ends the consideration of the Tenth House.**

->### Consideration of the Eleventh House ###<-

Now, the author describes the combinations for gain <note>Ayabhava</note>. "If the Year Lord is Mercury..." etc. If Mercury <note>original: Jnya/Budha</note> is the Lord of the Year <term>Abdapa: the planetary ruler of the specific year in the Tajika system</term> and is situated in the house of wealth <note>the 2nd house</note>, and is aspected by or joined with benefic planets, there will be profit from commerce. This is one combination.

Now another: if this same Mercury is the Year Lord, is aspected by or joined with benefic planets, and forms an *Itthasala* <term>Itthasala: a specific Tajika aspect where a faster planet approaches a slower one, indicating the fulfillment of a desire</term> while placed in the Ascendant <note>the 1st house</note> of the yearly chart, there will be profit from reading and writing. || 1 ||

->लग्नाधिपेन राज्यप्तिरुक्ता वीर्यानुमानतः ॥ धर्मकर्माधिपौ दग्धौ धर्मराज्यक्षयावहौ ॥ १० ॥ इति राज्यभावविचारः ॥<-
->अथाऽऽयभावविचारः ॥ अब्देपज्ञेऽर्थगे लाभो वाणिज्याच्छुभदृग्युते ॥ सेंथिहेऽस्मिन्लग्नगते लाभः पठनलेखनात् ॥ १ ॥<-
->अस्मिन्षष्ठाष्टांत्यगते सकूरे नीचकर्म्मकृत् ॥ क्रूरक्षणेन वा लाभोऽस्तंगते लिखनादितः ॥ २ ॥ जीवेऽब्दपे क्रूरहते लग्ने हानिर्भयन्नृपात् ॥ अस्मिन्नधिकृते द्यूने व्यवहाराद्धनक्षयः ॥ ३ ॥<-

Now, the author describes the inauspicious results. "In this..." etc. If this Mercury, as the Year Lord, is placed in the sixth, eighth, or twelfth houses <note>the difficult "Trika" houses</note> and is joined with a malefic planet, the person will perform "lowly deeds" <note>tasks considered menial or socially degrading at the time</note>. If such a Mercury, as Year Lord, is in those same houses and is aspected by malefic planets, it should be said that there is no profit. || 2 || Alternatively, if Mercury is "set" <note>astronomically obscured or combust</note>, there is no profit from writing and similar activities.

Now, he describes a combination of inauspicious and auspicious factors. "If Jupiter..." etc. If Jupiter <note>original: Jive/Guru</note> is the Year Lord and is "struck" or afflicted by malefic planets while in the Ascendant, there will be a loss of wealth and fear from the king <note>government or authorities</note>. This is one combination. Now another: if Jupiter is an official <note>Adhikrita; holding one of the five specific positions of planetary authority</note> and is in the seventh house, there will be a loss of wealth due to business dealings or lawsuits. || 3 ||

<summary>This page concludes the study of the Tenth House, warning that "burnt" (combust) planets destroy career and merit, and begins the Eleventh House (Gains), detailing how Mercury and Jupiter influence income through trade, writing, or legal disputes.</summary>

--- Page 178 ---
<meta>This page continues the analysis of the Eleventh House (Gains) from the Tajika Neelakanthi commentary and transitions into the Twelfth House (Expenditure/Loss). It follows the previous page’s discussion on Mercury's influence on wealth.</meta>

If this Year Lord <term>Abdapa: the planetary ruler of the year in the Tajika system</term> is the designated authority among the five officials <note>the 'Pancha-adhikaris' or five specific planets with governing roles for the year</note> and is situated in the seventh house, there will be the acquisition of wealth through business transactions. || 3 ||

Now, the combinations for attaining wealth are stated. Regarding the **"Itthashala of the Lords of the Ascendant and the Eleventh House"**: if the Lord of the Year Ascendant <note>Lagna</note> and the Lord of the Eleventh House <note>the house of gains</note> form an *Itthashala* <term>Itthashala: a specific aspect in Tajika astrology where a faster planet approaches a slower one, indicating the fulfillment of an event</term>, there will be profit and honor among one's own people. This is one combination.

Now for another: If all planets are endowed with strength and are situated in the eleventh house, they lead to the attainment of great wealth. However, if the planets in the eleventh house are weak, they do not bring about the attainment of wealth. || 4 ||

Now, the combination for obtaining buried treasure is stated. When "Mercury is strong": if Mercury <note>original: Jnya/Budha</note> possesses strength and is associated with the *Muthaha* <term>Muthaha: a sensitive mathematical point in the Tajika system, often linked to the year's primary focus</term> within the Year Ascendant, it means that a strong Mercury is positioned in the Ascendant alongside the *Muthaha*.

->**If there is an Itthashala between the Lords of the Ascendant and the Eleventh House, there is profit and honor among kin. All strong planets in the Eleventh House bring wealth, but weak ones do not. || 4 || Strong Mercury with the Muthaha in the Ascendant or in the Lots of Wealth...**<-

Commentary: In such a case, the gain of buried treasure <term>Nikhatadravya: literally "hidden" or "dug up" wealth; often refers to inheritance or sudden finds</term> occurs, unless there is an aspect from malefic planets. || 5 ||

**This ends the consideration of the Eleventh House.**

### Consideration of the Twelfth House ###

Now, the consideration of expenditure <note>Vyaya Bhava</note> is explained in a verse and a half. If the Lord of the Year Ascendant and the Lord of the Year are stripped of their strength and are placed in the twelfth, sixth, or eighth houses <note>known as 'Dusthanas' or difficult houses</note>, the results should be weighed according to the specific signs they occupy.

When the Lord of the Year is in the sixth house and is located in a "four-legged" sign <note>quadruped signs like Aries, Taurus, Leo, or the latter half of Sagittarius</term>, then the loss of livestock or four-legged animals should be predicted. The same logic applies to their placement in the eighth and twelfth houses.

Now, the author explains the specific influence of Venus <note>original: Bhrigusuta</note> when situated in the sixth house, specifically when it has lost its strength and is aspected by malefics...

<summary>This page explains the conditions for financial prosperity, including the "Itthashala" yoga for honor among kin and specific placements for finding buried treasure. It also begins the analysis of the Twelfth House, focusing on how weak planets in difficult houses can lead to losses, such as the death of livestock.</summary>

<meta>This page continues from the previous discussion on the Eleventh House (gains). It now shifts to the analysis of the Year Lord (Abdapa) when positioned in difficult houses—specifically the Sixth (enemies/debt), Eighth (death/obstacles), and Twelfth (loss/expenditure)—and details the resulting effects on servants, livestock, and mental state.</meta>

<margin>Tajika Neelakanthi</margin>
<margin>|| 88 ||</margin>
<margin>Section 2</margin>
<margin>|| 88 ||</margin>

**Regarding Venus:** If Venus <note>original: Bhrigusuta or Shukra</note> is the <term>Year Lord: the planet designated as the ruler of the current year's chart</term> and is located in the sixth house, and if it is "ruined in strength" <note>meaning it lacks planetary power or 'virya'</note> and is joined by malefic planets, or is aspected by them from the fourth, tenth, first, or seventh positions; and if it is situated in a "human sign" <note>such as Gemini, Virgo, Libra, or Aquarius</note>, then there will be a loss of servants. If such a Venus is situated in a "four-legged sign" <note>such as Aries, Taurus, Leo, or the latter half of Sagittarius</note>, it causes the death of horses. The term "horse" is used here as an illustration; one should understand it to mean the loss of other four-legged livestock as well. This same result—the loss of servants and so on—should be predicted for any other Year Lord who is situated in the sixth, eighth, or twelfth houses if they are in those same types of signs.

Now, the author explains the results of the Year Lord being Mars, based on its specific placement, in the second half of the verse. **Mars in the Tenth:** If Mars <note>original: Kuja or Bhauma</note> is the Year Lord, is joined with the Moon, and is placed in the tenth house <note>referred to as 'kha' or the sky</note>, there will be loss of horses and similar livestock, as well as mental agitation. Likewise, if Mars is joined with the Moon...

->If in a human sign, there is loss of servants; in a four-legged sign, a killer of horses. This same result is spoken of for any Year Lord [in those houses]. If Mars is in the tenth house joined with the Moon, there is loss of horses and the like; there is also mental distress and harm from malefics...<-
->...in the twelfth house. || 2 || If the Sun is in the sixth house, afflicted by malefics and in a four-legged sign, there is a quarrel with servants. This also applies if it is in the eighth or twelfth houses. If Saturn is the Year Lord, is endowed with strength, and stays in the sixth or twelfth houses,<-
->then there is the construction of houses, planting of trees, and building of water reservoirs. || 3 ||<-

...and if Mars is the Year Lord and is positioned in the twelfth house <note>the house of expenditure or 'vyaya'</note> while being afflicted by malefic planets, the loss of horses and similar effects should be predicted. 

Note that the phrase "joined with the Moon" <note>original: shashi-yute</note> was written by the author according to his own judgment. However, in reality, based on earlier authoritative texts, the reading should be "joined with Saturn" <note>original: shani-yute</note> when Mars is in the tenth house. This is confirmed by **Samarasimha**, who states: "If Mars is joined with Saturn in the tenth house, there is imprisonment, mental agitation, and the loss of horses." Similarly, in the <term>Tajika Alankara: a classic decorative or explanatory work on Tajika astrology</term>, it says: "If Mars is joined with Saturn in the tenth house, there will be agitation in the mind and the certain loss of horses." Therefore, when Mars is in the tenth house and joined with Saturn, it results in the loss of horses and mental distress. This is one combination. 

Now for another: The same applies when Mars is in the twelfth house and afflicted by malefics. Alternatively, if the twelfth house is occupied by malefic planets, it results in the loss of horses and so on. || 1 || 2 || 

Next, the author describes the results when the Sun or Saturn are Year Lords and are placed in unfavorable houses. **Regarding the Sun:** If the Sun <note>original: Ravi</note> is the Year Lord and is in the sixth house while being occupied by malefic planets...

<vocab>Tajika: A branch of Indian astrology influenced by Persian traditions. Varshaphala: Annual horoscopy. Shukra: Venus. Bhauma: Mars. Ravi: The Sun. Shani: Saturn. Ripha: The twelfth house. Ashva-nasha: Loss of horses. Samarasimha: An ancient authority on Tajika astrology.</vocab>

<summary>This page describes the negative impacts of Year Lords—Venus, Mars, and the Sun—when placed in the 6th, 10th, or 12th houses, specifically regarding the loss of servants and livestock. Interestingly, it notes that a strong Saturn in these "bad" houses can actually lead to positive construction projects like buildings and reservoirs.</summary>
